这时我便要同老坏蛋道别了。他却自告奋勇，要送我到亨格福德（Hungerford），我们在那儿便可拐上巴斯大道。起初我不好意思应承，全为他那件外衣——蓝的、橙黄的、火红的补丁，早把原本的黑底盖了个严实：但他应承教我骑术——这恰是我大大欠缺的本事——我便与他成了交。等进得亨格福德镇，我已大有长进，跨我那匹老灰马跨得从容自在，出于感激，我提出请他在客栈吃一顿晚饭、宿一夜，只要他肯买一件新外衣：他应承了，说这世道不赖。

这时天已阴云四合，雨下得正急。我的同伴在头一家撞见的旧衣铺里置办齐整之后，寻一家客栈便成了当务之急。我们在「号角」客栈落脚，径直奔旅人堂去，找火烤干衣裳。

===Original===
In this room, at the window, were two men who look’d lazily up at our entrance. They were playing a game, which was no other than to race two snails up a pane of glass and wager which should prove the faster.

“A wet day!” said my comrade, cheerfully.

The pair regarded him. “I’ll lay you a crown it clears within the hour!” said one.

“And I another,” put in the other; and with that they went back to their sport.

Drawing near, I myself was soon as eager as they in watching the snails, when my companion drew my notice to a piece of writing on the window over which they were crawling. ’Twas a set of verses scribbled there, that must have been scratch’d with a diamond: and to my surprise—for I had not guess’d him a scholar—he read them out for my benefit. Thus the writing ran, for I copied it later:

> “Not on the necks of prince or hound,
>
> Nor on a woman’s finger twin’d,
>
> May gold from the deriding ground
>
> Keep sacred that we sacred bind:
>
> Only the heel
>
> Of splendid steel
>
> Shall stand secure on sliding fate,
>
> When golden navies weep their freight.
>
> “The scarlet hat, the laurell’d stave
>
> Are measures, not the springs, of worth;
>
> In a wife’s lap, as in a grave,
>
> Man’s airy notions mix with earth.
>
> Seek other spur
>
> Bravely to stir
>
> The dust in this loud world, and tread
>
> Alp-high among the whisp’ring dead.
>
> “*Trust in thyself*—then spur amain:
>
> So shall Charybdis wear a grace,
>
> Grim Aetna laugh, the Lybian plain
>
> Take roses to her shrivell’d face.
>
> This orb—this round
>
> Of sight and sound—
>
> Count it the lists that God hath built
>
> For haughty hearts to ride atilt.
>
> “**Finis**—Master Tucker’s Farewell.”

===Chinese===
这间屋里，窗前坐着两个人，见我们进来，只懒懒抬眼一望。他们在玩一种游戏，说穿了，就是让两只蜗牛沿一块窗玻璃往上赛跑，赌哪只先到。

「好个雨天！」我的同伴兴致勃勃地搭话。

两人打量他。「我拿一克朗打赌，一个钟头内天就放晴！」一个说。

「我也押一份。」另一个插嘴道；说罢两人重回他们的赛事。

我凑上前去，看蜗牛看得不久便同他们一样起劲；这时我的同伴引我去注意蜗牛爬经的窗上那一方字。那是信手题在上面的几节诗，必是拿钻石划出来的：令我吃惊的是——我原不曾猜他是个识字的人——他竟朗声念给我听。诗是这样写的，我后来抄录了下来：

> 「黄金纵然扣上君王的颈项、猎犬的颈项，
>
> 纵然缠上女人的手指，
>
> 也休想凭这讥诮的尘土，
>
> 守住我们以神圣缔结的神圣：
>
> 唯有那
>
> 灿烂钢靴的鞋跟，
>
> 方能稳立于滑动的命运之上，
>
> 当金色的船队垂泪痛失所载。
>
> 「红衣的高冠，桂冠的诗杖，
>
> 只是价值的量尺，不是价值的源泉；
>
> 妻子的膝头如坟墓，
>
> 男人的虚念归于尘土。
>
> 去另寻一副马刺，
>
> 勇敢地去踏响
>
> 这喧嚣世界的尘土，并且踏得
>
> 同阿尔卑斯一般高，行过窃窃低语的亡者。
>
> 「*信你自己*——而后纵马疾驰：
>
> 卡律布狄斯自会平添一段风韵，
>
> 狰狞的埃特纳会开颜，利比亚荒原
>
> 会把玫瑰贴上她干瘪的脸。
>
> 这寰球——这目与耳
>
> 织成的圆转——
>
> 且当它是上帝筑下的比武场，
>
> 供高傲的心驰马挺枪。
>
> 「**终**——塔克老爷（Master Tucker）的告别。」

===Original===
“And a very pretty moral on four gentlemen that pass their afternoon a setting snails to race!”

At these words, spoken in a delicate foreign voice we all started round: and saw a young lady standing behind us.

Now that she was the one who had passed us in the coach I saw at once. But describe her—to be plain—I cannot, having tried a many times. So let me say only that she was the prettiest creature on God’s earth (which, I hope, will satisfy her); that she had chestnut curls and a mouth made for laughing; that she wore a kirtle and bodice of grey silk taffety, with a gold pomander-box hung on a chain about her neck; and held out a drinking glass toward us with a Frenchified grace.

===Chinese===
「好一篇漂亮的教训，正合适送给四位拿让蜗牛赛跑消磨下午的绅士！」

这话用一副娇柔的外国口音说出，我们全都惊转过身：看见一位年轻小姐立在我们身后。

我一眼便认出，她正是方才马车里掠过的那一位。可是要描摹她——恕我直言——我办不到，已试过许多回了。那么只容我说：她是上帝地上最俊俏的造物（但愿这句能叫她满意）；她生着栗色鬈发，一张生来为了笑的嘴；她穿一身灰色塔夫绸的裙衫与胸衣，颈间链上挂着一只金香盒（pomander）；她朝我们递出一只饮水杯，姿态带着法国式的优雅。

===Original===
“Gentlemen, my father is sick, and will taste no water but what is freshly drawn. I ask you not to brave Charybdis or Aetna, but to step out into the rainy yard and draw me a glassful from the pump there: for our servant is abroad in the town.”

To my deep disgust, before I could find a word, that villainous old pickpocket had caught the glass from her hand and reached the door. But I ran after; and out into the yard we stepp’d together, where I pump’d while he held the glass to the spout, flinging away the contents time after time, till the bubbles on the brim, and the film on the outside, were to his liking.

’Twas he, too, that gain’d the thanks on our return.

===Chinese===
「诸位先生，家父病着，水非新汲的不入口。我不敢劳动二位去闯卡律布狄斯（Charybdis）或埃特纳（Aetna），只求迈步到雨地里，替我从那水泵汲一杯来：我们的仆人进镇办事去了。」

叫我深恶痛绝的是，我还没来得及找着一句话，那老混账扒手已从她手里接过杯子，抢到了门边。我只好追上去；两人一同步入雨中的院子，我压水泵，他把杯口对着水流，一回一回泼掉杯中之水，直到杯沿聚起的细泡和杯壁蒙上的水膜都合了他的心意。

回去时得了道谢的，也是他。

===Original===
“Mistress,” said he with a bow, “my young friend is raw, but has a good will. Confess, now, for his edification—for he is bound on a long journey westward, where, they tell me, the maidens grow comeliest—that looks avail naught with womankind beside a dashing manner.”

The young gentlewoman laughed, shaking her curls.

“I’ll give him in that case three better counsels yet: first (for by his habit I see he is on the King’s side), let him take a circuit from this place to the south, for the road between Marlboro’ and Bristol is, they tell me, all held by the rebels; next, let him avoid all women, even though they ask but an innocent cup of water; and lastly, let him shun thee, unless thy face lie more than thy tongue. Shall I say more?”

“Why, no—perhaps better not,” replied the old rogue hastily, but laughing all the same. “That’s a clever lass,” he added, as the door shut behind her.

===Chinese===
「小姐，」他躬身道，「我这位小朋友生嫩是生嫩，志气却不小。现在请赏一句实话，也好教化教化他——他正要往西边去长途跋涉，听说那边的姑娘出落得最标致——请问在女人心里，同潇洒的做派一比，相貌可就一文不值了，是也不是？」

年轻的小姐摇着鬈发笑了。

「既然如此，我便送他三条更好的忠告：第一（瞧他的装束，我看得出他站在国王一边），让他从此地绕道往南，因为莫尔伯勒（Marlboro’）到布里斯托尔一路，听说全叫叛军占了；第二，让他躲开一切女人，哪怕她们讨的不过是一杯清白的水；第三，让他躲开你老人家——除非你的脸撒谎撒得比你的舌头更厉害。还要我再说么？」

「嗐，不必了——还是不说为妙，」老油子慌忙答道，却仍旧笑着。「这丫头伶俐，」门在她身后关上时，他补了一句。

===Original===
And, indeed, I was fain, next morning, to agree to this. For, awaking, I found my friend (who had shar’d a room with me) already up and gone, and discovered the reason in a sheet of writing pinn’d to my clothes—

> **Young Sir**—I convict myself of ingratitude: but habit is hard to break. So I have made off with the half of thy guineas and thy horse. The residue, and the letter thou bearest, I leave. ’Tis a good world, and experience should be bought early. This golden lesson I leave in return for the guineas. Believe me, ’tis of more worth. Read over those verses on the windowpane before starting, digest them, and trust me, thy obliged,
> Peter, The Jackman.
> Raise not thy hand so often to thy breast: ’tis a sure index of hidden valuables.

===Chinese===
果然，第二天一早，我便不得不赞同这话了。因为醒来时，我发现我的朋友（他与我同住一间房）早已起身去了，缘由就别在我衣裳上的一页字纸——

> **少年郎**——我自首忘恩负义之罪：然积习难改。我已携去你一半的几尼金币和你的马。余下的钱，连同你所带的那封信，留下不动。这世道不赖，而教训宜趁早买下。这金色的一课，权当抵还那几尼。相信我，它更值钱。动身之前，把窗上那几节诗再读一遍，细细消化，并信任我——你感恩的
> 彼得，游荡汉（Peter, The Jackman）。
> 手不要频频抬向胸口：那是有暗藏财货的准信儿。

===Original===
Be sure I was wroth enough: nor did the calm interest of the two snail owners appease me, when at breakfast I told them a part of the story. But I thought I read sympathy in the low price at which one of them offer’d me his horse. ’Twas a tall black brute, very strong in the loins, and I bought him at once out of my shrunken stock of guineas. At ten o’clock, I set out, not along the Bath road, but bearing to the south, as the young gentlewoman had counselled. I began to hold a high opinion of her advice.

By twelve o’clock I was back at the inn door, clamoring to see the man that sold me the horse, which had gone dead lame after the second mile.

“Dear heart!” cried the landlord; “they are gone, the both, this hour and a half. But they are coming again within the fortnight; and I’m expressly to report if you return’d, as they had a wager about it.”

I turn’d away, pondering. Two days on the road had put me sadly out of conceit with myself. For mile upon mile I trudged, dragging the horse after me by the bridle, till my arms felt as if coming from their sockets. I would have turn’d the brute loose, and thought myself well quit of him, had it not been for the saddle and bridle he carried.

===Chinese===
不用说，我气得不轻：早餐时我把事情讲了七八分给那两位蜗牛主人听，他们那副心平气和的兴味也没有让我消气。但其中一位肯贱价把他的马让给我，我自认为在那低价里读出了同情。那是匹高大的黑畜生，腰力极足，我便从我已然缩水的几尼家底里掏钱，当即买了下来。十点钟我动身了，不走巴斯大道，而是照那位年轻小姐的忠告折向南方。我对她的主意，从此敬佩有加。

到十二点钟，我已回到客栈门口，吵嚷着要见卖马的人——那马走出第二英里就彻底瘸了。

「哎呀我的爷！」店主嚷道，「那两位都走啦，走了一个半钟头了。不过半月之内他们还要再来；他们还打了赌，叫我专门留意您回不回来哩。」

我转身走开，一路思量。两天的路途，已把我折腾得自惭形秽。我一英里接一英里地跋涉，缰绳拖着那畜生跟在后头，两条胳膊拽得像要从肩窝里脱出来。若不是它身上还驮着鞍子和辔头，我早把这畜生放了，还自认为甩脱得干净利落。

---

===Original===
’Twas about five in the evening, and I still laboring along, when, over the low hedge to my right, a man on a sorrel mare leap’d easily as a swallow, and alighted some ten paces or less in front of me; where he dismounted and stood barring my path. The muzzle of his pistol was in my face before I could lay hand to my own.

“Good evening!” said I.

“You have money about you, doubtless,” growled the man curtly, and in a voice that made me start. For by his voice and figure in the dusk I knew him for Captain Settle: and in the sorrel with the high white stocking I recognized the mare, Molly, that poor Anthony Killigrew had given me almost with his last breath.

The bully did not know me, having but seen me for an instant at the Crown, and then in very different attire.

===Chinese===
时近傍晚五点，我还在苦苦赶路，忽见右边的矮树篱上，一个人骑一匹栗色母马，燕子似的轻轻一跃，落在了我前方十步上下；他在那里下了马，拦住去路。我还来不及摸自己的枪，他的手枪口已抵到我脸上。

「晚上好！」我说。

「你身上想必带着钱。」那汉子没好气地低吼道，一副嗓音叫我心头一凛。凭暮色里这嗓音和身形，我认出他是塞特尔上尉：而那匹左后腿高白袜的栗色母马，我认出正是莫莉（Molly）——可怜的安东尼·基利格鲁几乎是咽气时把她交给我的。

这恶棍却不认得我：他在「王冠」客栈只同我照过一瞬的面，况且那时我穿的是全然不同的装束。

===Original===
“I have but a few poor coins,” I answer’d.

“Then hand ’em over.”

“Be shot if I do!” said I in a passion; and pulling out a handful from my pocket, I dash’d them down in the road.

For a moment the Captain took his pistol from my face, and stooped to clutch at the golden coins as they trickled and ran to right and left. The next, I had struck out with my right fist, and down he went staggering. His pistol dropped out of his hand and exploded between my feet. I rush’d to Molly, caught her bridle, and leap’d on her back. ’Twas a near thing, for the Captain was rushing toward us. But at the call of my voice the mare gave a bound and turn’d: and down the road I was borne, light as a feather.

A bullet whizz’d past my ear: I heard the Captain’s curse mingle with the report: and then was out of range, and galloping through the dusk.

===Chinese===
「我只有几个可怜的铜子儿。」我答道。

「那就交出来。」

「打死我也不给！」我火冒三丈地说；从兜里掏出一把，狠狠掼在大路上。

上尉一时把枪口从我脸上移开，弯腰去抓那些四下滚散的金币。说时迟，我一记右拳挥出，他踉跄着栽了下去。手枪从他手里脱落，在我两脚之间走了火。我抢到莫莉跟前，抓住缰绳，翻身上了马背。好险——上尉已向我们扑来。但听见我唤她的声音，母马一跃而转：我顺着大路被驮走了，轻得像一片羽毛。

一颗子弹嗖地擦过我的耳朵：我听见上尉的咒骂混着枪声：随后我已出了射程，在暮色里疾驰而去。

## V My Adventure at the Three Cups / 我在「三杯」客栈的历险

===Original===
Secure of pursuit, and full of delight in the mare’s easy motion, I must have travelled a good six miles before the moon rose. In the frosty sky her rays sparkled cheerfully, and by them I saw on the holsters the silver demi-bear that I knew to be the crest of the Killigrews, having the fellow to it engraved on my sword-hilt. So now I was certain ’twas Molly that I bestrode: and took occasion of the light to explore the holsters and saddle flap.

Poor Anthony’s pistols were gone—filched, no doubt, by the Captain: but you may guess my satisfaction, when on thrusting my hand deeper, I touched a heap of coins, and found them to be gold.

’Twas certainly a rare bargain I had driven with Captain Settle. For the five or six gold pieces I scatter’d on the road, I had won close on thirty guineas, as I counted in the moonlight; not to speak of this incomparable Molly. And I began to whistle gleefully, and taste the joke over again and laugh to myself, as we cantered along with the north wind at our backs.

All the same, I had no relish for riding thus till morning. For the night was chill enough to search my very bones after the heat of the late gallop: and, moreover, I knew nothing of the road, which at this hour was quite deserted. So that, coming at length to a tall hill with a black ridge of pine wood standing up against the moon like a fish’s fin, I was glad enough to note below it, and at some distance from the trees, a window brightly lit; and pushed forward in hope of entertainment.

===Chinese===
既已无追兵之虞，又受用着母马轻快的步子，月亮升起之前，我谅必已赶出足足六英里。霜天里月光粼粼，煞是可喜；借着这光，我看见鞍侧枪套上一只银打的半熊（demi-bear）——我知道那是基利格鲁家的族徽，我剑柄上便刻着与它成对的一只。至此我确信胯下正是莫莉：便就着月光，翻检起枪套和鞍翼来。

可怜的安东尼那对手枪不见了——不用问，是叫上尉顺了去：可我把手往深处再一探，摸着一小堆钱币，而且尽是金的，诸位可以想见我那份得意。

同塞特尔上尉做的这桩买卖，实在便宜得出奇。撒在大路上的五六枚金币，换来的是将近三十几尼——这是我在月光下点出来的——更不必说这匹举世无双的莫莉。我不由得欢欢喜喜吹起口哨，把这场玩笑翻来覆去咂摸一遍，独自发笑，任北风在背后吹送，一路策马小跑。

话虽如此，我却无意就这样骑到天明。方才疾驰出的一身热汗散尽，这夜的寒气便直搜进我的骨髓里去；何况这路我人生地不熟，此刻更是四下无人。因此待行到一座高山跟前，见一道黑黢黢的松林脊线衬着月亮竖起，活脱一副鱼鳍模样，山下离树林颇远的地方有一扇窗子灯光明亮，我便欣然催马向前，指望讨个招待。

===Original===
The building was an inn, though a sorry one. Nor, save for the lighted window, did it wear any grace of hospitality, but thrust out a bare shoulder upon the road, and a sign that creaked overhead and look’d for all the world like a gallows. Round this shoulder of the house, and into the main yard (that turn’d churlishly toward the hillside), the wind howled like a beast in pain. I climb’d off Molly, and pressing my hat down on my head, struck a loud rat-tat on the door.

Curiously, it opened at once; and I saw a couple of men in the lighted passage.

===Chinese===
那房子虽是客栈，却寒碜得很。除了那扇亮窗，它通身寻不出半点好客的气象，只把一面光秃秃的山墙肩头探到大路上；招牌悬在头顶吱呀作响，瞧上去活像一架绞架。绕着这面墙肩、拐进正院（那院子别别扭扭地朝向山坡），风嚎得像一头痛极的野兽。我下了莫莉，把帽子往头上按紧，在门上咚咚捶了一通。

说来也怪，门应声便开了；亮着灯的过道里立着两个人。

===Original===
“Heard the mare’s heels on the road, Cap—. Hillo! What in the fiend’s name is this?”

Said I: “If you are he that keeps this house, I want two things of you—first, a civil tongue, and next a bed.”

“Ye’ll get neither, then.”

“Your sign says that you keep an inn.”

“Aye—the Three Cups: but we’re full.”

“Your manner of speech proves that to be a lie.”

I liked the fellow’s voice so little that ’tis odds I would have remounted Molly and ridden away; but at this instant there floated down the stairs and out through the drink-smelling passage a sound that made me jump. ’Twas a girl’s voice singing—

> “Hey nonni—nonni—no!
>
> Men are fools that wish to die!
>
> Is’t not fine to laugh and sing
>
> When the bells of death do ring—”

There was no doubt upon it. The voice belonged to the young gentlewoman I had met at Hungerford. I turned sharply toward the landlord, and was met by another surprise. The second man, that till now had stood well back in the shadow, was peering forward, and devouring Molly with his gaze. ’Twas hard to read his features, but then and there I would have wagered my life he was no other than Luke Settle’s comrade, Black Dick.

===Chinese===
「听见这马的蹄子敲上大路了，上尉——喂！你这算个甚么鬼玩意儿？」

我说：「阁下若就是这店的主人，我要向你讨两样东西——头一样是句人话，第二样是一张床。」

「那你一样也讨不着。」

「你招牌上分明写着开的是客栈。」

「是呀——『三杯』客栈：可我们客满了。」

「你这腔调，便证明这话是谎。」

这汉子的嗓音听得我极不受用，十有八九我已重新跨上莫莉走人了；偏偏这一瞬，一段歌声顺楼梯飘下来，穿过满是酒气的过道，惊得我一跳。那是一个姑娘的嗓音——

> 「嘿，诺尼——诺尼——诺！
>
> 想死的男人才是痴子！
>
> 丧钟敲响的时节，
>
> 有笑又有唱，岂不美哉——」

再无疑问了：那嗓音正属于我在亨格福德遇见的年轻小姐。我猛地转向店主，却又吃了一惊。第二个人，先前一直远远缩在暗影里，这会儿探身向前，目光恨不能把莫莉生吞下去。他的眉眼虽难辨认，可我当场就敢拿性命打赌：此人不是别人，正是卢克·塞特尔的同伙「黑迪克」（Black Dick）。

===Original===
My mind was made up. “I’ll not ride a step further, tonight,” said I.

“Then bide there and freeze,” answer’d the landlord.

He was for slamming the door in my face, when the other caught him by the arm and, pulling him a little back, whisper’d a word or two. I guess’d what this meant, but resolved not to draw back; and presently the landlord’s voice began again, betwixt surly and polite—

“Have ye too high a stomach to lie on straw?”

“Oho!” thought I to myself, “then I am to be kept for the mare’s sake, but not admitted to the house:” and said aloud that I could put up with a straw bed.

“Because there’s the stable loft at your service. As ye hear” (and in fact the singing still went on, only now I heard a man’s voice joining in the catch) “our house is full of company. But straw is clean bedding, and the mare I’ll help to put in stall.”

“Agreed,” I said, “on one condition—that you send out a maid to me with a cup of mulled sack: for this cold eats me alive.”

===Chinese===
我主意已定。「今夜我一步也不再骑了。」我说。

「那你就守在外头冻着吧。」店主答道。

他正要当面把门摔上，另一个却抓住他的胳膊，把他往后拉了拉，咬着耳朵说了两句。我猜得出这是什么意思，却打定主意不退缩；果然，店主的嗓门重新响起，半凶半客气——

「怎么，睡草铺还嫌委屈了你的贵体？」

「哦呵！」我心里暗想，「原来是要看在马的份上留我，人却不许进屋：」嘴上便说，草铺我也将就得住。

「因为马厩顶上的草料棚正闲着，随你用。你也听见了」（歌声确实还在继续，只这会儿又添了一个男人的嗓音加入轮唱）「我们店里宾客盈门。不过草是干净铺盖，母马么，我帮你牵进栏去。」

「一言为定，」我说，「只是有一个条件——你派个侍女出来，给我端一杯烫热的香料酒（sack）：这寒气快把我活活嚼了。」

===Original===
To this he consented: and stepping back into a side room with the other fellow, returned in a minute alone, and carrying a lantern which, in spite of the moon, was needed to guide a stranger across that ruinous yard. The flare, as we pick’d our way along, fell for a moment on an open cart shed and, within, on the gilt panels of a coach that I recogniz’d. In the stable, that stood at the far end of the court, I was surprised to find half a dozen horses standing, ready saddled, and munching their fill of oats. They were ungroom’d, and one or two in a lather of sweat that on such a night was hard to account for. But I asked no questions, and my companion vouchsafed no talk, though twice I caught him regarding me curiously as I unbridled the mare in the only vacant stall. Not a word pass’d as he took the lantern off the peg again, and led the way up a ramshackle ladder to the loft above. He was a fat, lumbering fellow, and made the old timbers creak. At the top he set down the light, and pointed to a heap of straw in the corner.

“Yon’s your bed,” he growled; and before I could answer, was picking his way down the ladder again.

===Chinese===
这一条他应承了：同那汉子退进一间侧屋，转眼便独自回来，提着一盏灯笼——虽有月光，要引生人穿过这破败的院子，这点灯火还是少不得。我们一路拣着道走，灯光有一瞬扫过一座敞棚，照见棚里一辆马车的镀金镶板——那车我认得。院子尽头立着马厩，我在那里吃惊地发现竟有六匹马站成一排，鞍辔齐整，正放开肚皮大嚼燕麦。它们都未经刷洗，还有一两匹浑身汗沫，这样的夜里，这光景颇难自圆其说。但我一句也没问，同伴也吝于开口，只是我在唯一空着的马栏里给母马卸辔时，两番撞见他好奇地打量我。他从钉子上重新摘下灯笼，一言不发地在头里引路，领我爬上一架摇摇欲坠的梯子，进了上头的草料棚。他是个肥胖笨重的汉子，踩得老旧的木料咯吱作响。到得顶上，他放下灯，朝角落里一堆草一指。

「那就是你的铺。」他咕哝道；不等我答话，又摸索着顺梯子下去了。

===Original===
I look’d about, and shiver’d. The eaves of my bedchamber were scarce on speaking terms with the walls, and through a score of crannies at least the wind poured and whistled, so that after shifting my truss of straw a dozen times I found myself still the centre of a whirl of draught. The candle-flame, too, was puffed this way and that inside the horn sheath. I was losing patience when I heard footsteps below; the ladder creak’d, and the red hair and broad shoulders of a chambermaid rose into view. She carried a steaming mug in her hand, and mutter’d all the while in no very choice talk.

The wench had a kind face, though; and a pair of eyes that did her more credit than her tongue.

===Chinese===
我四下一望，直打寒战。我这间卧房，屋檐同墙壁简直连话都搭不上；至少二十来条裂缝里灌着风、嘘嘘作响，害得我把那捆草挪了十来趟，自己仍旧端坐在穿堂风的漩涡正中。牛角罩里的烛火也给吹得东倒西歪。我正要不耐烦，忽听得下面脚步声响；梯子一咯吱，一个红头发、宽肩膀的侍女冒上头来。她手里端着一大杯热气腾腾的酒，嘴里一路嘟囔，措辞可不怎么讲究。

不过这姑娘有张和善的脸；那双眼睛，也比她那条舌头给她添彩得多。

===Original===
“And what’s to be my reward for this, I want to know?” she panted out, resting her left palm on her hip.

“Why, a groat or two,” said I, “when it comes to the reckoning.”

“Lud!” she cried, “what a dull young man!”

“Dull?”

“Aye—to make me ask for a kiss in so many words:” and with the back of her left hand she wiped her mouth for it frankly, while she held out the mug in her right.

“Oh!” I said, “I beg your pardon, but my wits are frozen up, I think. There’s two, for interest: and another if you tell me whom your master entertains tonight, that I must be content with this crib.”

She took the kisses with composure and said—

===Chinese===
「我倒要问问，这趟差事我图个甚么好？」她气喘吁吁地说，左手掌叉在胯上。

「嗐，结账的时候，赏你一两个格罗特银币吧。」我说。

「天老爷！」她嚷道，「好一个没开窍的年轻人！」

「没开窍？」

「可不是——害得人家讨个亲吻，还得把话明明白白说出口：」她说着，左手背大大方方往嘴上一抹，右手把杯子递了过来。

「哦！」我说，「恕罪恕罪，想是我的脑浆冻住了。先来两个，算是利钱：再饶一个，只要你告诉我，今晚你东家招待的都是何等客人，害我只配睡这个窝棚。」

她从容不迫受了那几个吻，说道——

===Original===
“Well—to begin, there’s the gentlefolk that came this afternoon with their own carriage and heathenish French servant: a cranky old grandee and a daughter with more airs than a peacock: Sir Something-or-other Killigew—Lord bless the boy!”

For I had dropp’d the mug and spilt the hot sack all about the straw, where it trickled away with a fragrance reproachfully delicious.

“Now I beg your pardon a hundred times: but the chill is in my bones worse than the ague;” and huddling my shoulders up, I counterfeited a shivering fit with a truthfulness that surpris’d myself.

===Chinese===
「嗯——先说头一拨，是今儿下午自家马车来的贵客，还带着个异教徒似的法国仆人：一位脾气乖张的老太爷，一位比孔雀还多三分架子的千金：叫个基利格—甚么—甚么爵士——老天爷保佑这孩子！」

原来我一失手，杯子跌落，滚烫的香料酒泼了一草铺，四下里淌开，那香气散出来，倒像是专程来责备我的，偏又香得叫人心醉。

「这一回真是一千个对不住：只是这寒气钻进骨头，比疟疾还凶；」我耸起肩膀装出一阵寒战，那份逼真，连我自己都吃了一惊。

===Original===
“Poor lad!”

“—And ’tis first hot and then cold all down my spine.”

“There, now!”

“—And goose flesh and flushes all over my body.”

“Dear heart—and to pass the night in this grave of a place!”

“—And by morning I shall be in a high fever: and oh! I feel I shall die of it!”

“Don’t—don’t!” The honest girl’s eyes were full of tears. “I wonder, now—” she began: and I waited, eager for her next words. “Sure, master’s at cards in the parlor, and’ll be drunk by midnight. Shalt pass the night by the kitchen fire, if only thou make no noise.”

===Chinese===
「可怜的孩子！」

「——而且我脊梁上一阵发热、一阵发冷。」

「哎哟，你瞧瞧！」

「——而且浑身鸡皮疙瘩，一阵阵地发烫。」

「我的心肝儿——还要在这坟坑似的地方过夜！」

「——到明早准要发起高烧：哎呀！我觉得我是要死在这上头了！」

「别——别说了！」这实心眼的姑娘眼里噙满泪水。「我说呀——」她开了腔，我屏息等她的下文。「反正老爷在客厅里耍牌，到半夜也就醉了。你到厨房炉火边过夜吧，只要一点声响也别出。」

===Original===
“But your mistress—what will she say?”

“Is in heaven these two years: and out of master’s speaking distance forever. So blow out the light and follow me gently.”

Still feigning to shiver, I follow’d her down the ladder, and through the stable into the open. The wind by this time had brought up some heavy clouds, and mass’d them about the moon: but ’twas freezing hard, nevertheless. The girl took me by the hand to guide me: for, save from the one bright window in the upper floor, there was no light at all in the yard. Clearly, she was in dread of her master’s anger, for we stole across like ghosts, and once or twice she whisper’d a warning when my toe kick’d against a loose cobble. But just as I seem’d to be walking into a stone wall, she put out her hand, I heard the click of a latch, and stood in a dark, narrow passage.

===Chinese===
「可你们的太太——她要说甚么呢？」

「两年前就上了天堂：从此永远出了老爷嗓门能够着的地界。吹了灯，放轻脚步跟我来。」

我仍旧装着寒战，随她下了梯子，穿过马厩走到露天里。这工夫风已卷来沉沉的乌云，团团围住月亮：天却照旧冻得结结实实。姑娘牵着我的手引路：因为除了楼上那扇亮窗，院子里再无一点光。她分明怕东家动怒，我们像两个鬼魂似的蹑足穿过；我的脚尖踢着活动的圆石，她还一两次低声警示。正当我像要一头撞上石墙时，她伸手一推，我听得门闩咔哒一响，已立身在一条漆黑狭窄的过道里。

===Original===
The passage led to a second door that open’d on a wide, stone-pav’d kitchen, lit by a cheerful fire, whereon a kettle hissed and bubbled as the vapor lifted the cover. Close by the chimney corner was a sort of trap, or buttery hatch, for pushing the hot dishes conveniently into the parlor on the other side of the wall. Besides this, for furniture, the room held a broad deal table, an oak dresser, a linen press, a rack with hams and strings of onions depending from it, a settle and a chair or two, with (for decoration) a dozen or so of ballad sheets stuck among the dish covers along the wall.

“Sit,” whisper’d the girl, “and make no noise, while I brew a rack-punch for the menfolk in the parlor.” She jerked her thumb toward the buttery hatch, where I had already caught the murmur of voices.

I took up a chair softly, and set it down between the hatch and the fireplace, so that while warming my knees I could catch any word spoken more than ordinary loud on the other side of the wall. The chambermaid stirr’d the fire briskly, and moved about singing as she fetch’d down bottles and glasses from the dresser—

> “Lament ye maids an’ darters
>
> For constant Sarah Ann,
>
> Who hang’d hersel’ in her garters
>
> All for the love o’ man,
>
> All for the—”

===Chinese===
过道尽头是第二道门，通向一间宽大的石板地厨房，一炉欢腾的火照得亮堂，火上坐着的水壶嘶嘶滚沸，蒸气把壶盖顶得一掀一掀。烟囱角近旁开着一扇活板小窗，也就是配膳窗（buttery hatch），好把热菜顺手推进墙那面的客厅。除这个之外，屋里的家什有一张宽大的松木餐桌、一座橡木餐具柜、一具亚麻布柜、一排挂架垂着火腿和一串串洋葱，一张高背长椅和一两把椅子；点缀么，是沿墙碗盖之间插着的十几张歌谣篇子。

「坐，」姑娘悄声道，「别出声，等我给客厅里的爷们调一盆拉克潘趣酒（rack-punch）。」她朝配膳窗一撇大拇指，那边的人声嗡嗡，早钻进我耳朵里了。

我轻轻端过一把椅子，安在配膳窗和壁炉之间，这样一边烘着膝盖，一边墙那边但凡有谁的嗓门高过寻常，我一个字也漏不掉。侍女利落地拨旺炉火，一面从餐具柜上取瓶拿杯，一面走一面唱——

> 「姑娘媳妇们都来哭一哭
>
> 多情的萨拉·安（Sarah Ann），
>
> 她拿吊袜带子自尽了呀，
>
> 全为了个男人，
>
> 全为了——」

===Original===
She was pausing, bottle in hand, to take the high note: but hush’d suddenly at the sound of the voices singing in the room upstairs—

> “Vivre en tout cas
>
> C’est le grand soulas
>
> Des honnêtes gens!”

“That’s the foreigners,” said the chambermaid, and went on with her ditty—

> “All for the love of a souljer
>
> Who christening name was Jan.”

A volley of oaths sounded through the buttery hatch.

“—And that’s the true-born Englishmen, as you may tell by their speech. ’Tis pretty company the master keeps, these days.”

===Chinese===
她手里举着瓶子，正歇一口气要拔高音：楼上屋里传来的歌声却叫她猛地收了声——

> “Vivre en tout cas
>
> C’est le grand soulas
>
> Des honnêtes gens!”
>
> （法语：「不论好歹活着，／才是正人君子的／大快活！」）

「那是外邦人，」侍女说，接着唱她的歌——

> 「全为了个当兵的，
>
> 他领洗的教名叫扬（Jan）。」

一阵咒骂的连珠炮从配膳窗里轰出来。

「——这些才是地道的英国人，听听那口音便知。如今我们老爷结交的，可真是好宾朋。」

===Original===
She was continuing her song, when I held up a finger for silence. In fact, through the hatch my ear had caught a sentence that set me listening for more with a still heart.

“D——n the Captain,” the landlord’s gruff voice was saying; “I warn’d ’n agen this fancy business when sober, cool-handed work was toward.”

“Settle’s way from his cradle,” growl’d another; “and times enough I’ve told ’n: ‘Cap’n,’ says I, ‘there’s no sense o’ proportions about ye.’ A master mind, sirs, but ’a’ll be hang’d for a hen-roost, so sure as my name’s Bill Widdicomb.”

“Ugly words—what a creeping influence has that same mention o’ hanging!” piped a thinner voice.

“Hold thy complaints, Old Mortification,” put in a speaker that I recogniz’d for Black Dick; “sure the pretty maid upstairs is tender game. Hark how they sing!”

And indeed the threatened folk upstairs were singing their catch very choicely, with a girl’s clear voice to lead them—

> “Comment dit papa—
>
> Margoton, ma mie?”

===Chinese===
她还要往下唱，我竖起一根指头请她噤声。原来透过配膳窗，我耳朵已捕到一句话，吓得我屏息静气，只等下文。

「上尉真该死，」店主的粗嗓门正在说；「早先我就劝过他，别弄这些花哨勾当，明明该干的是沉着冷静、手上稳当的活计。」

「塞特尔打摇篮里出来就是这路数，」另一个瓮声瓮气地说；「我不知同他讲过多少回：『上尉，』我说，『你这人做事就是没有个轻重分寸。』诸位，他是帅才，可他到头来准得为掏鸡窝似的小勾当给吊死，就像我叫比尔·威迪科姆（Bill Widdicomb）一样板上钉钉。」

「难听话——一提吊死两个字，那股阴惨惨的劲儿就往人骨头缝里爬！」一个更细的嗓音尖声道。

「省省你的怨言吧，老苦修（Old Mortification），」插话的嗓音我听出是黑迪克；「楼上的俏姑娘分明是块嫩肉。听听她们唱的！」

果然，楼上那些身陷险境的人们，正把轮唱曲唱得极雅致，由一个姑娘清亮的嗓音领着——

> “Comment dit papa—
>
> Margoton, ma mie?”
>
> （法语：「爸爸怎么说——／玛戈彤，我的亲亲？」）

===Original===
“Heathen language, to be sure,” said the thin voice again, as the chorus ceased: “thinks I to mysel’ ‘they be but Papisters,’ an’ my doubting mind is mightily reconcil’d to manslaughter.”

“I don’t like beginning ’ithout the Cap’n,” observed Black Dick: “though I doubt something has miscarried. Else, how did that young spark ride in upon the mare?”

“An’ that’s what thy question should ha’ been, Dick, with a pistol to his skull.”

“He’ll keep till the morrow.”

“We’ll give Settle half-an-hour more,” said the landlord: “Mary!” he push’d open the hatch, so that I had barely time to duck my head out of view, “fetch in the punch, girl. How did’st leave the young man i’ the loft?”

“Asleep, or nearly,” answer’d Mary—

> “Who hang’d hersel’ in her garters,
>
> All for the love o’ man—”

“—Anon, anon, master: wait only till I get the kettle on the boil.”

===Chinese===
「异教话，错不了，」轮唱一停，那细嗓门又说：「我心里就想：『不过是些天主教徒罢了』，我这颗满腹疑虑的心，便同杀人放火的营生大大言归于好了。」

「上尉不到，我不喜欢动手，」黑迪克说：「不过我怕是出了岔子。不然，那匹马怎会叫个轻狂小子骑着进来？」

「你该问的本就是这句，迪克，只消拿枪顶住他的脑壳问。」

「他搁不坏，留到明天再算账。」

「再给塞特尔半个钟头，」店主说：「玛丽！」他把配膳窗推开，我堪堪来得及把头缩出视线之外，「把潘趣酒端进来，丫头。阁楼上那位年轻人，你撂下他时是个甚么光景？」

「睡着了，差不离儿。」玛丽答道——

> 「她拿吊袜带子自尽了呀，
>
> 全为了个男人——」

「——这就来，这就来，老爷：只等我把水壶坐滚。」

===Original===
The hatch was slipp’d to again. I stood up and made a step toward the girl.

“How many are they?” I ask’d, jerking a finger in the direction of the parlor.

“A dozen all but one.”

“Where is the foreign guests’ room?”

“Left hand, on the first landing.”

“The staircase?”

“Just outside the door.”

“Then sing—go on singing for your life.”

“But—”

“Sing!”

“Dear heart, they’ll murder thee! Oh! for pity’s sake, let go my wrist—

> “‘Lament, ye maids an’ darters—’”

===Chinese===
配膳窗重新掩上。我站起身，朝姑娘挪近一步。

「他们一共几个？」我朝客厅那边弹了弹手指，问。

「差一个一打。」

「外国客人的房间在哪里？」

「左手，上了楼梯头一进。」

「楼梯呢？」

「就在门外。」

「那就唱——接着唱，豁出性命唱下去。」

「可是——」

「唱！」

「我的心肝儿，他们会杀了你的！哎呀！行行好，放开我的手腕——

> 『姑娘媳妇们都来哭一哭——』」

===Original===
I stole to the door and peep’d out. A lantern hung in the passage, and showed the staircase directly in front of me. I stay’d for a moment to pull off my boots, and, holding them in my left hand, crept up the stairs. In the kitchen, the girl was singing and clattering the glasses together. Behind the door, at the head of the stairs, I heard voices talking. I slipp’d on my boots again and tapp’d on the panel.

“Come in!”

===Chinese===
我潜到门边，向外窥探。过道里挂着一盏灯笼，照出正对我面前的楼梯。我停了一停，脱下靴子提在左手，蹑手蹑脚爬上楼去。厨房里，姑娘唱着歌，把杯子碰得叮当作响。楼梯顶头那扇门后，我听得有人说话。我重新蹬上靴子，在门板上叩了两下。

「进来！」

===Original===
Let me try to describe that on which my eyes rested as I push’d the door wide. ’Twas a long room, wainscoted half up the wall in some dark wood, and in daytime lit by one window only, which now was hung with red curtains. By the fireplace, where a brisk wood fire was crackling, lean’d the young gentlewoman I had met at Hungerford, who, as she now turn’d her eyes upon me, ceas’d fingering the guitar or mandoline that she held against her waist, and raised her pretty head not without curiosity.

But ’twas on the table in the centre of the chamber that my gaze settled; and on two men beside it, of whom I must speak more particularly.

===Chinese===
且让我试着描一描，推开门时映入眼帘的这间屋子。那是一间长屋，半墙镶着深色木料的壁板，白天只靠一扇窗子采光，此刻窗上挂着红帘。壁炉里一炉柴火烧得正欢，噼啪作响；炉边倚着的，正是我在亨格福德遇见的那位年轻小姐——她这时把目光转到我身上，停了手，不再拨弄贴在腰间的吉他（又像是曼陀林），抬起俏丽的头，不无好奇。

但我的目光钉住的，是屋子正中的那张桌子，和桌边的两个人；这两位，我须得更细地说一说。

===Original===
The elder, who sat in a high-back’d chair, was a little, frail, deform’d gentleman of about fifty, dress’d very richly in dark velvet and furs, and wore on his head a velvet skullcap, round which his white hair stuck up like a ferret’s. But the oddest thing about him was a complexion that any maid of sixteen would give her ears for—of a pink and white so transparent that it seem’d a soft light must be glowing beneath his skin. On either cheek bone this delicate coloring centred in a deeper flush. This is as much as I need say about his appearance, except that his eyes were very bright and sharp, and his chin stuck out like a vicious mule’s.

===Chinese===
年长的那位坐在一把高背椅里，是个瘦小孱弱、身形残陋的绅士，年纪约莫五十，一身深色天鹅绒加裘皮的衣裳，穿得极是阔气；头上扣一顶天鹅绒小帽，白发绕着帽檐根根支棱，活像雪貂的毛。但他身上最奇的是那副脸色——天底下随便哪位十六岁的姑娘，拿一对耳朵来换都甘愿——粉里透白，白得莹澈，仿佛皮肤底下正透着一层柔光；两边颧骨上，这娇嫩的颜色又各凝成一团更深的红晕。他的相貌，我只消再补一句：眼光极亮极尖，下巴前凸，像头使性子踢人的骡子。

===Original===
The table before him was cover’d with bottles and flasks, in the middle of which stood a silver lamp burning, and over it a silver saucepan that sent up a rare fragrance as the liquid within it simmer’d and bubbled. So eager was the old gentleman in watching the progress of his mixture, that he merely glanc’d up at my entrance, and then, holding up a hand for silence, turn’d his eyes on the saucepan again.

The second man was the broad-shouldered lackey I had seen riding behind the coach: and now stood over the saucepan with a twisted flask in his hand, from which he pour’d a red syrup very gingerly, drop by drop, with the tail of his eye turn’d on his master’s face, that he might know when to cease.

===Chinese===
他面前的桌上摆满了大小瓶罐，正当中燃着一盏银灯，灯上坐着一只银小锅，锅里的汁水咕嘟咕嘟地熬着滚着，腾起一股罕有的香气。老绅士盯着他的调配物进展，专注得很，我进来他只抬眼一扫，便举手示意噤声，又把目光收回锅上。

第二个人，正是我见过骑马跟在车后的那位阔肩膀仆人：此刻立在银锅跟前，手里擎一只歪脖细颈的瓶子，正把红色的糖浆一滴一滴、小心翼翼地斟进去，眼角的余光瞟着主人的脸色，好知道几时该住手。

===Original===
Now it may be that my entrance upset this experiment in strong drinks. At any rate, I had scarce come to a stand about three paces inside the door, when the little old gentleman bounces up in a fury, kicks over his chair, hurls the nearest bottles to right and left, and sends the silver saucepan spinning across the table to my very feet, where it scalded me clean through the boot, and made me hop for pain.

“Spoil’d—spoil’d!” he scream’d: “drench’d in filthy liquor, when it should have breath’d but a taste!”

And, to my amazement, he sprang on the strapping servant like a wildcat, and began to beat, cuff, and belabor him with all the strength of his puny limbs.

===Chinese===
也许是我这一进门，搅砸了这场烈酒的实验。总之，我进门才站定三步，那小老头便火冒三丈地蹦起来，一脚踢翻椅子，把跟前的瓶瓶罐罐左右乱掷，又把银锅掼得滴溜溜转、飞过桌面，恰恰落到我脚前——滚烫的锅底隔着靴子把我烫了个透，痛得我单脚直蹦。

「毁了——毁了！」他尖叫道：「叫下流的酒水淹了个透，本该只让它沾一沾口味的！」

更叫我瞠目的是，他竟像只野猫扑上那魁梧的仆人，用他孱弱肢体的全部气力，又打又掴又捶。

===Original===
’Twas like a scene out of Bedlam. Yet all the while the girl lean’d quietly against the mantelshelf, and softly touched the strings of her instrument; while the servant took the rain of blows and slaps as though ’twere a summer shower, grinning all over his face, and making no resistance at all.

Then, as I stood dumb with perplexity, the old gentleman let go his hold of the fellow’s hair, and, dropping on the floor, began to roll about in a fit of coughing, the like of which no man can imagine. ’Twas hideous. He bark’d, and writhed, and bark’d again, till the disorder seem’d to search and rack every innermost inch of his small frame. And in the intervals of coughing his exclamations were terrible to listen to.

===Chinese===
这简直是贝德拉姆（Bedlam）疯人院里搬出来的一幕。而那姑娘自始至终静静倚着炉台，轻拢慢捻她的琴弦；那仆人挨着雨点般的拳掌，倒仿佛沐着一场夏日阵雨，满脸堆笑，全不还手。

接着，正当我讷讷发怔，老绅士松开攥着那汉子头发的手，瘫倒在地板上，打起一阵谁也想象不出的咳嗽。那样子怕人极了。他像犬吠似地咳着、扭着，又咳，直咳得那场大乱仿佛要把他小小躯壳里最深处的每一寸都翻出来上刑；咳嗽的间隙里，他那一声声呼号，更是惨不忍闻。

===Original===
“He’s dying!” I cried; and ran forward to help.

The servant pick’d up the chair, and together we set him in it. By degrees the violence of the cough abated, and he lay back, livid in the face, with his eyes closed, and his hands clutching the knobs of the chair. I turn’d to the girl. She had neither spoken nor stirr’d, but now came forward, and calmly ask’d my business.

===Chinese===
「他要死了！」我叫道；抢步上前救助。

仆人拾起椅子，我们两人把他扶坐进去。咳嗽的势头渐渐平息，他仰面靠好，脸色青灰，双目紧闭，两手死死抠住椅子扶手的雕球。我转向那姑娘。她始终一言未发、一动未动，此刻才走上前来，平平静静地问我的来意。

===Original===
“I think,” said I, “that your name is Killigrew?”

“I am Delia Killigrew, and this is my father, Sir Deakin.”

“Now on his way to visit his estates in Cornwall?”

She nodded.

“Then I have to warn you that your lives are in danger.” And, gently as possible, I told her what I had seen and heard downstairs. In the middle of my tale, the servant stepp’d to the door, and return’d quietly. There was no lock on the inside. After a minute he went across, and drew the red curtains. The window had a grating within, of iron bars as thick as a man’s thumb, strongly clamp’d in the stonework, and not four inches apart. Clearly, he was a man of few words; for, returning, he merely pull’d out his sword, and waited for the end of my tale.

===Chinese===
「我想，」我说，「您府上姓基利格鲁？」

「我叫德利娅·基利格鲁，这是我父亲迪肯爵士。」

「眼下正要去康沃尔巡视自家的田庄？」

她点了点头。

「那么我必须警告二位：你们的性命堪虞。」于是我把楼下所见所闻，拣着最和缓的措辞讲给她听。讲到一半，那仆人走到门边，又悄没声地转了回来。门内侧没有上锁。过了一分钟，他又踱过去，把红窗帘拉严。窗子里头嵌着一道格栅，铁条有人的拇指粗细，用铁卡牢牢铆在石工里，条与条相隔不到四英寸。看得出，这是个惜字如金的人；他回身之后，只管拔出剑来，静等我讲完。

===Original===
The girl, also, did not interrupt me, but listen’d in silence. As I ceas’d, she said—

“Is this all you know?”

“No,” answer’d I, “it is not. But the rest I promise to tell you if we escape from this place alive. Will this content you?”

She turn’d to the servant, who nodded. Whereupon she held out her hand very cordially.

===Chinese===
那姑娘也不打断我，只默默听着。我话音一落，她便说——

「你知道的，就这些么？」

「不，」我答道，「不止这些。但其余的，我答应二位：只消我们活着逃出此地，便一一奉告。这样，二位可满意？」

她转向仆人，仆人点头。于是她极诚恳地伸出手来。

===Original===
“Sir, listen: we are travelers bound for Cornwall, as you know, and have some small possessions, that will poorly reward the greed of these violent men. Nevertheless, we should be hurrying on our journey did we not await my brother Anthony, who was to have ridden from Oxford to join us here, but has been delayed, doubtless on the King’s business—”

She broke off, as I started: for below I heard the main door open, and Captain Settle’s voice in the passage. The arch villain had return’d.

===Chinese===
「先生，请听：如您所知，我们是取道康沃尔去的旅人，随身有些许薄产，实在填不满这伙强徒的贪壑。若不是等我兄弟安东尼，我们本该早已兼程上路；他原该从牛津骑马来此与我们会合，如今想必是为着国王的公务耽搁了——」

我悚然一惊，她的话也断了：因为我听见楼下正门洞开，过道里传来塞特尔上尉的嗓音。这元凶巨恶回来了。

===Original===
“Mistress Delia,” I said hurriedly, “the twelfth man has enter’d the house, and unless we consider our plans at once, all’s up with us.”

“Tush!” said the old gentleman in the chair, who (it seems) had heard all, and now sat up brisk as ever. “I, for my part shall mix another glass, and leave it all to Jacques. Come, sit by me, sir, and you shall see some pretty play. Why, Jacques is the neatest rogue with a small sword in all France!”

“Sir,” I put in, “they are a round dozen in all, and your life at present is not worth a penny’s purchase.”

“That’s a lie! ’Tis worth this bowl before me, that, with or without you, I mean to empty. What a fool thing is youth! Sir, you must be a dying man like myself to taste life properly.” And, as I am a truthful man, he struck up quavering merrily—

> “Hey, nonni—nonni—no!
>
> Men are fools that wish to die!
>
> Is’t not fine to laugh and sing
>
> When the bells of death do ring?
>
> Is’t not fine to drown in wine,
>
> And turn upon the toe,
>
> And sing, hey—nonni—no?
>
> Hey, nonni—nonni—”

===Chinese===
「德利娅小姐，」我急道，「第十二个人已经进屋了，咱们若不立时定计，一切都完了。」

「呸！」椅子里的老绅士说话了——他似乎方才全听见了——这会儿又精神抖擞地坐直。「我么，自去再调一杯，天塌下来有雅克。来，坐到我身边，先生，请你瞧一场好戏。嗐，论耍小剑，雅克是全法兰首届一指的利落角色！」

「爵士，」我插话道，「他们拢共足足一打，您这条命眼下的行情，连一枚便士都不值。」

「那是瞎话！它值我面前这一锅——不管有没有你们搭手，我都打算把它喝干。青春真是天下第一蠢物！先生，你须得像我一样做个将死之人，才咂摸得出活着的真味。」而且我句句属实——他竟颤悠悠地欢唱起来——

> 「嘿，诺尼——诺尼——诺！
>
> 想死的男人才是痴子！
>
> 丧钟敲响的时节，
>
> 有笑又有唱，岂不美哉？
>
> 沉进酒里淹死岂不美哉，
>
> 踮起脚尖打个旋，
>
> 再唱一声：嘿——诺尼——诺？
>
> 嘿，诺尼——诺尼——」

===Original===
“—Come and sit, sir, nor spoil sport. You are too raw, I’ll wager, to be of any help; and boggling I detest.”

“Indeed, sir,” I broke in, now thoroughly anger’d, “I can use the small sword as well as another.”

“Tush! Try him, Jacques.”

===Chinese===
「——来坐下，先生，别败了兴致。要打赌么，你太生嫩，帮不上手；而缩手缩脚的人，我最见不得。」

「说真的，爵士，」我这时恼透了，抢着说，「使小剑我不输给任何人。」

「呸！雅克，试试他。」

===Original===
Jacques, still wearing a stolid face, brought his weapon to the guard. Stung to the quick, I wheel’d round, and made a lunge or two, that he put aside as easily as though I were a babe. And then—I know not how it happened, but my sword slipp’d like ice out of my grasp, and went flying across the room. Jacques, sedately as on a matter of business, stepp’d to pick it up, while the old gentleman chuckled.

I was hot and asham’d, and a score of bitter words sprang to my tongue-tip, when the Frenchman, as he rose from stooping, caught my eye, and beckon’d me across to him.

He was white as death, and pointed to the hilt of my sword and the demi-bear engrav’d thereon.

===Chinese===
雅克仍旧一副木然面孔，举剑立了个门户。我羞愤攻心，霍地回身，抢进一两剑，他却像对付襁褓婴孩一般轻轻拨开。接着——我也说不清是怎么一回事，我那柄剑竟像冰棱似的从掌心里滑脱，直飞过半个房间。雅克神色安详，仿佛在办一桩正经差事，踱步过去拾剑，老绅士在一旁呵呵窃笑。

我又臊又恼，二十句毒话直涌到舌尖；恰在此时，那法国人俯身拾剑、直起身来，同我目光一撞，朝我招手，唤我过去。

他面白如死，指着我这柄剑的剑柄，指着柄上镌刻的半熊。

===Original===
“He is dead,” I whisper’d: “hush!—turn your face aside—killed by those same dogs that are now below.”

I heard a sob in the true fellow’s throat. But on the instant it was drown’d by the sound of a door opening and the tramp of feet on the stairs.

===Chinese===
「他死了，」我悄声道：「别作声——把脸偏过去——杀他的，正是此刻楼下那群狗东西。」

我听见这条忠义汉子的喉咙里哽咽了一声。但转瞬之间，那哽咽便被开门声和踏上楼梯的一阵脚步声淹没了。

## VI The Flight in the Pine Wood / 松林夜奔

===Original===
By the sound of their steps I guess’d one or two of these dozen rascals to be pretty far gone in drink, and afterward found this to be the case. I look’d round. Sir Deakin had pick’d up the lamp and was mixing his bowl of punch, humming to himself without the least concern﻿—

> “Vivre en tout cas
 

 C’est le grand soulas”﻿—

with a glance at his daughter’s face, that was white to the lips, but firmly set.

“Hand me the nutmeg yonder,” he said, and then, “why, daughter, what’s this?﻿—a trembling hand?”

And all the while the footsteps were coming up.

There was a loud knock on the door.

“Come in!” call’d Sir Deakin.

===Chinese===
从脚步声里我听出，这一打恶棍中已有一两个醉得相当深了，事后证明果然如此。我环顾四周。迪肯爵士已端起银灯，正调着他那一盆潘趣酒，自顾自哼着小曲，浑不把祸事放在心上——

> “Vivre en tout cas
 
 C’est le grand soulas”——（法语：「不论好歹活着，／才是大快活！」）

一边却瞟着女儿的脸：那张脸白到了唇边，神色却绷得纹丝不动。

「把那边的肉豆蔻递给我。」他说，随即又道：「嗳，女儿，这是怎么？——手也打起颤来了？」

而那些脚步声，自始至终一步步逼上楼来。

门上擂鼓似的一阵重响。

「进来！」迪肯爵士扬声应道。

===Original===
At this, Jacques, who stood ready for battle by the entrance, wheeled round, shot a look at his master, and dropping his point, made a sign to me to do the same. The door was thrust rudely open, and Captain Settle, his hat cock’d over one eye, and sham drunkenness in his gait, lurched into the room, with the whole villainous crew behind him, huddled on the threshold. Jacques and I stepp’d quietly back, so as to cover the girl.

“Would you mind waiting a moment?” inquir’d Sir Deakin, without looking up, but rubbing the nutmeg calmly up and down the grater: “a fraction too much, and the whole punch will be spoil’d.”

===Chinese===
话音未落，守在门口、严阵以待的雅克霍地转身，朝主人急急递了个眼色，随即垂下剑尖，又示意我也照办。门被粗暴地推开，塞特尔上尉歪戴着帽子、压住一只眼，装出一副醉态，踉踉跄跄闯进屋来；他身后那整整一伙恶棍，乱糟糟挤在门槛上。雅克和我悄然退后几步，好把姑娘挡在身后。

「劳驾，稍候片刻可好？」迪肯爵士问道，头也不抬，只管拿肉豆蔻在擦板上不紧不慢地来回擦着：「分量多出一星半点，这一整盆潘趣酒就全糟蹋了。」

===Original===
It took the Captain aback, and he came to a stand, eyeing us, who look’d back at him without saying a word. And this discomposed him still further.

There was a minute during which the two parties could hear each other’s breathing. Sir Deakin set down the nutmeg, wiped his thin white fingers on a napkin, and address’d the Captain sweetly﻿—

“Before asking your business, sir, I would beg you and your company to taste this liquor, which, in the court of France”﻿—the old gentleman took a sip from the mixing ladle﻿—“has had the extreme honor to be pronounced divine.” He smack’d his lips, and rising to his feet, let his right hand rest on the silver foot of the lamp as he bowed to the Captain.

Captain Settle’s bravado was plainly oozing away before this polite audacity: and seeing Sir Deakin taste the punch, he pull’d off his cap in a shamefaced manner and sat down by the table with a word of thanks.

===Chinese===
这一下大出上尉意料，他收步站定，眼睛打量着我们；我们也回望着他，一言不发。这使他愈发乱了方寸。

有那么一分钟，两边的人只听得见彼此的呼吸。迪肯爵士放下肉豆蔻，用餐巾拭了拭那双瘦白的手指，随即甜滋滋地向上尉开口——

「先生，还未请教贵干，先请阁下与您的伙伴们尝一尝这酒——它在法兰西宫廷里，」老绅士就着调酒的长勺呷了一口，「可是享过莫大的荣幸，被品评为天赐神品呢。」他咂了咂嘴，站起身来，右手搭在银灯的灯座上，向上尉微微一躬。

面对这份彬彬有礼的胆大包天，塞特尔上尉那股虚张声势的凶焰，分明正一分一分地泄掉：又见迪肯爵士亲口尝了潘趣酒，他竟有些讪讪地摘下帽子，道了声谢，在桌边坐下了。

===Original===
“Come in, sirs﻿—come in!” call’d the old gentleman; “and follow your friend’s example. ’Twill be a compliment to make me mix another bowl when this is finish’d.” He stepped around the table to welcome them, still resting his hand on the lamp, as if for steadiness. I saw his eye twinkle as they shuffled in and stood around the chair where the Captain was seated.

“Jacques, bring glasses from the cupboard yonder! And, Delia, fetch up some chairs for our guests﻿—no, sirs, pray do not move!”

He had waved his hand lightly to the door as he turned to us: and in an instant the intention as well as the bright success of this comedy flash’d upon me. There was now no one between us and the stairs, and as for Sir Deakin himself, he had already taken the step of putting the table’s width between him and his guests.

===Chinese===
「进来呀，诸位先生——都进来！」老先生喊道：「都学学你们朋友的榜样。等这盆见了底，承诸位赏脸，我再调上一盆。」他绕过桌子去迎客，那只手依旧搭在银灯上，仿佛借它站稳身子。看他们拖着脚步挨挨蹭蹭进来，围着上尉坐的那把椅子站定，我瞧见他眼里狡黠地一闪。

「雅克，从那边的橱柜里取酒杯来！还有，德利娅，去给客人们搬几把椅子——不，诸位先生，千万别动！」

他转身吩咐我们时，手已朝门口那边轻轻一挥：转瞬之间，这出喜剧的用意、连同它已然得手的妙处，一齐在我心头闪亮。此刻我们与楼梯之间已空无一人；至于迪肯爵士自己，他早迈出了那一步，把一张桌子的宽度隔在了自己与客人之间。

===Original===
I touch’d the girl’s arm, and we made as if to fetch a couple of chairs that stood against the wainscot by the door. As we did so, Sir Deakin push’d the punch bowl forward under the Captain’s nose.

“Smell, sir,” he cried airily, “and report to your friends on the foretaste.”

Settle’s nose hung over the steaming compound. With a swift pass of the hand, the old gentleman caught up the lamp and had shaken a drop of burning oil into the bowl. A great blaze leap’d to the ceiling. There was a howl﻿—a scream of pain; and as I push’d Mistress Delia through the doorway and out to the head of the stairs, I caught a backward glimpse of Sir Deakin rushing after us, with one of the stoutest among the robbers at his heels.

“Downstairs, for your life!” I whisper’d to the girl, and turning, as her father tumbled past me, let his pursuer run on my sword, as on a spit. At the same instant, another blade pass’d through the fellow transversely, and Jacques stood beside me, with his back to the lintel.

===Chinese===
我碰了碰姑娘的臂膀，两人做出要去搬椅子的模样——那几把椅子，正靠在门边的壁板上。便在这时，迪肯爵士把潘趣酒盆往前一送，端到了上尉的鼻子底下。

「闻一闻，先生，」他轻快地喊道，「再把这先尝的滋味，向你的朋友们报一报。」

塞特尔的鼻子正俯在那盆腾着热气的东西上。说时迟，那时快，老绅士一把抓起银灯，甩进去一滴燃着的灯油。轰然一声，一大蓬火苗蹿上了天花板。顿时一片嚎叫——一声痛极的惨嗥；我推着德利娅小姐挤出房门、推到楼梯口时，回头瞥了一眼：迪肯爵士正朝我们随后赶来，一个最强壮的强盗已紧紧咬住他的脚跟。

「下楼去，快逃命！」我急促地低喝；待她父亲跌跌撞撞从我身边冲过，我便回身站定，让那追兵一头扎上我的剑，如同扎上一根烤叉。同一瞬间，另一柄剑横里穿透了那家伙——雅克立在我身旁，脊背抵着门楣。

===Original===
As we pull’d our swords out and the man dropp’d, I had a brief view into the room, where now the blazing liquid ran off the table in a stream. Settle, stamping with agony, had his palms press’d against his scorch’d eyelids. The fat landlord, in trying to beat out the flames, had increased them by upsetting two bottles of aqua vitae, and was dancing about with three fingers in his mouth. The rest stood for the most part dumbfound’d: but Black Dick had his pistol lifted.

Jacques and I sprang out for the landing and round the doorway. Between the flash and the report I felt a sudden scrape, as of a red-hot wire, across my left thigh and just above the knee.

“*Tenez, camarade*,” said Jacques’ voice in my ear; “*à moi la porte﻿—à vous le maître, là-bas*:” and he pointed down the staircase, where, by the glare of the conflagration that beat past us, I saw the figures of Sir Deakin and his daughter standing.

===Chinese===
我们拔剑出鞘，那人扑地倒下；就这一瞥的工夫，我望见屋内光景：烧着的酒液已顺着桌沿淌成一道火溪。塞特尔痛得双脚乱跺，两掌死死捂住烫坏的眼皮。胖店主本想扑灭火焰，却撞翻了两瓶烈酒（aqua vitae），反倒把火喂得更旺，此刻正把三根指头塞在嘴里，团团乱转。其余的人多半呆若木鸡：唯有黑迪克举起了手枪。

雅克和我蹿上楼梯平台，闪到门侧。就在火光乍闪、枪声炸响的一瞬，我只觉左腿膝头上被狠狠剐了一下，像有一根烧红的铁丝抽过。

「*Tenez, camarade*，」雅克的声音在我耳边响起；「*à moi la porte—à vous le maître, là-bas*：」（法语：「拿稳，同伴——门口归我，那边老爷归你。」）他朝楼梯下一指；借着从我们身边腾腾卷过的火光，我看见迪肯爵士和他女儿的身影，正立在楼梯脚下。

===Original===
“But how can you keep the door against a dozen?”

The Frenchman shrugg’d his shoulders with a smile﻿—

“*Mais﻿—comme ça!*”

For at this moment came a rush of footsteps within the room. I saw a fat paunch thrusting past us, a quiet pass of steel, and the landlord was wallowing on his face across the threshold. Jacques’ teeth snapp’d together as he stood ready for another victim: and as the fellows within the room tumbled back, he motion’d me to leave him.

===Chinese===
「可你一个人守门，怎么敌得过一打人？」

那法国人耸耸肩，微微一笑——

「*Mais—comme ça!*」（法语：「可是——喏，就这样！」）

恰在此时，屋里响起一阵急促的脚步。我看见一个肥肚皮从我们身边挤将出来，剑光不声不响地一递，那店主便一头栽过门槛，俯面乱滚。雅克牙关咬紧，立在那里候着下一个送上门的；待屋里那伙人跌跌撞撞缩了回去，他挥手催我快走。

===Original===
I sprang from his side, and catching the rail of the staircase, reach’d the foot in a couple of bounds.

“Hurry!” I cried, and caught the old baronet by the hand. His daughter took the other, and between us we hurried him across the passage for the kitchen door.

Within, the chambermaid was on her knees by the settle, her face and apron of the same hue. I saw she was incapable of helping, and hasten’d across the stone floor, and out toward the back entrance.

A stream of icy wind blew in our faces as we stepp’d over the threshold. The girl and I bent our heads to it, and stumbling, tripping, and panting, pull’d Sir Deakin with us out into the cold air.

===Chinese===
我从雅克身边一跃而出，一把攀住楼梯栏杆，只两蹦便落到了楼底。

「快走！」我喊道，一把攥住老从男爵的手。他女儿攥住另一只，我们两个夹着他匆匆穿过过道，直奔厨房门。

厨房里，那侍女跪在高背长椅旁，脸和围裙白成了一个颜色。我见她帮不上忙，急忙奔过石板地，朝后门冲去。

跨出门槛，一股冰冷的疾风迎面灌来。姑娘和我低头迎风，跌跌撞撞、踉踉跄跄、气喘吁吁，拽着迪肯爵士一道冲进了寒气里。

===Original===
The yard was no longer dark. In the room above someone had push’d the casement open, letting in the wind: and by this ’twas very evident the room was on fire. Indeed, the curtains had caught, and as we ran, a pennon of flame shot out over our heads, licking the thatch. In the glare of it the outbuildings and the yard gate stood clearly out from the night. I heard the trampling of feet, the sound of Settle’s voice shouting an order, and then a dismal yell and clash of steel as we flung open the gate.

“Jacques!” scream’d the old gentleman: “my poor Jacques! Those dogs will mangle him with their cut and thrust﻿—”

’Twas very singular and sad, but as if in answer to Sir Deakin’s cry, we heard the brave fellow’s voice; and a famous shout it must have been to reach us over the roaring of the flames﻿—

“*Mon maître﻿—mon maître!*” he call’d twice, and then “*Sauve toi!*” in a fainter voice, yet clear. And after that only a racket of shouts and outcries reach’d us. Without doubt the villains had overpower’d and slain this brave servant. In spite of our peril (for they would be after us at once), ’twas all we could do to drag the old man from the gate and up the road: and as he went he wept like a child.

===Chinese===
院子里不再黑暗。楼上屋里不知谁推开了窗扉，放进了风：单凭这一点便很明白，屋里已经起了火。果然，窗帘烧着了；我们奔逃之际，一条燕尾旗似的火舌从头顶蹿出，舔着茅草屋顶。火光之下，附属的棚舍和院门从夜色里清清楚楚地凸现出来。我听见杂沓的脚步声、塞特尔发号施令的呼喝，随后，就在我们撞开院门的一刻，又是一声凄厉的喊叫、一阵钢铁相击。

「雅克！」老绅士失声尖叫：「我可怜的雅克！那些狗东西刀劈剑刺，要把他剁烂了——」

说来既奇异又伤心：仿佛专为应答迪肯爵士这声呼喊似的，我们竟听见了那条好汉的嗓音；那必是一声了不得的大喊，才能压过烈火的咆哮，传到我们耳边——

「*Mon maître—mon maître*！」他喊了两遍，接着又用弱了些、却依然清楚的声音喊道：「*Sauve toi*！」（法语：「我的主人——我的主人！」「你快逃命！」）这以后，传到我们耳中的便只剩一片嘈杂的呼喝喊杀。毫无疑问，恶棍们已一拥而上，杀害了这条忠勇的仆人。我们自身危在旦夕（他们转眼便要追来），却仍旧费尽气力，才把老人从栅门口拖开、拖上大路：他一路走，一路哭得像个孩子。

===Original===
After about fifty yards, we turn’d in at a gate, and began to cut across a field: for I hop’d thus not only to baffle pursuit for a while, but also to gain the wood that we saw dimly ahead. It reach’d to the top of the hill, and I knew not how far beyond: and as I was reflecting that there lay our chance of safety, I heard the inn door below burst open with loud cries, and the sound of footsteps running up the road after us.

Moreover, to complete our fix, the clouds that had been scurrying across the moon’s face, now for a minute left a clear interval of sky about her: so that right in our course there lay a great patch brilliantly lit, whereon our figures could be spied at once by anyone glancing into the field. Also, it grew evident that Sir Deakin’s late agility was but a short and sudden triumph of will over body: for his poor crooked legs began to trail and lag sadly. So turning sharp about, we struck for the hedge’s shadow, and there pull’d him down in a dry ditch, and lay with a hand on his mouth to stifle his ejaculations, while we ourselves held our breathing.

The runners came up the road, pausing for a moment by the gate. I heard it creak, and saw two or three dark forms enter the field﻿—the remainder tearing on up the road with a great clatter of boots.

===Chinese===
跑出大约五十码，我们拐进一道栅门，开始斜穿一块田：我指望这样不但能暂且甩脱追兵，还能赶到前面影影绰绰的那片树林。树林一直连到山顶，再往远处还有多远，我无从知道；我正暗自盘算，我们的生机正系于那片林子，忽听山下客栈大门砰然撞开，喊声四起，一串脚步顺着大路朝我们追来。

更把我们窘境凑齐的是：方才还在月脸上疾驰的乌云，这一阵偏偏让开了，月亮四周露出一小片清明的夜空：恰在我们去的路上，横着一大片明晃晃的亮地，谁只要往田里瞟一眼，立时便能发现我们的身形。同样显而易见的是，迪肯爵士方才那阵矫捷，不过是意志对病体一场短暂而突发的胜利：他那双可怜的弯腿，已经开始凄凄惶惶地拖沓、迟滞。于是我们猛然折转，直扑树篱的阴影，把他按倒在一条干沟里，一只手捂住他的嘴，止住他的呼号，我们自己也都屏住了呼吸。

追兵沿大路赶到，在栅门前略停了停。我听见门轴吱呀一响，看见两三条黑影进了田——其余的人马靴声隆隆，径直往大路上追去了。

===Original===
“Alas, my poor Jacques!” moan’d Sir Deakin: “and to be butcher’d so, that never in his days kill’d a man but as if he lov’d him!”

“Sir,” I whisper’d harshly, “if you keep this noise I must gag you.” And with that he was silent for awhile.

There was a thick tangle of brambles in the ditch where we lay: and to this we owe our lives. For one of the men, coming our way, pass’d within two yards of us, with the flat of his sword beating the growth over our heads.

“Reu‑ben! Reuben Gedges!” call’d a voice by the gate.

The fellow turn’d; and peeping between the bramble twigs, I saw the moonlight glittering on his blade. A narrow, light-hair’d man he was, with a weak chin: and since then I have paid him out for the fright he gave us.

===Chinese===
「唉，我可怜的雅克！」迪肯爵士呜咽道：「竟遭这般屠戮——他这一辈子杀人，哪一回不是像疼着爱着一般下手的呀！」

「爵士，」我狠声悄道，「您再这样出声，我就只得堵上您的嘴了。」这一来，他果然静了一小会儿。

我们卧身的沟里盘结着一丛浓密的荆棘：我们这条命，正是欠它的。因为有一个汉子偏向我们这边走来，离我们不足两码，拿剑脊啪啪抽打我们头顶的草木。

「鲁——本！鲁本·吉杰斯（Reuben Gedges）！」栅门边有人喊。

那汉子回过身去；我从荆棘枝的缝隙里窥望，见月光在他的剑刃上熠熠闪烁。他是个瘦窄条儿的淡黄头发汉子，下巴绵软：后来，他让我们受的这场惊吓，我已连本带利找他清偿了。

===Original===
“What’s the coil?” he shouted back.

“The stable roof’s ablaze﻿—for the Lord’s sake come and save the hosses!”

He strode back, and in a minute the field was clear. Creeping out with caution, I grew aware of two mournful facts: first, that the stable was indeed afire, as I perceiv’d by standing on tiptoe and looking over the hedge; and second, that my knee was hurt by Black Dick’s bullet. The muscles had stiffened while we were crouching, and now pain’d me badly. Yet I kept it to myself as we started off again to run.

But at the stile that, at the top of the field, led into the woods, I pull’d up﻿—

===Chinese===
「那边闹甚么乱子？」他扬声回问。

「马棚顶着火啦——千万看主的份上，快来救马儿！」

他大步赶了回去，不消一分钟，田里便净了。我小心翼翼爬出来，才察觉两件令人心沉的事：其一，马棚果真起了火——我踮起脚尖、越过树篱望见的；其二，我的膝盖中了黑迪克那一枪。蹲伏的工夫肌肉早僵住了，这时痛得厉害。可等我们重新拔腿再跑，我把这疼一字没提。

但到了田头那座通向树林的梯磴前，我收住了脚——

===Original===
“Sorry I am to say it, but you must go on without me.”

“O﻿—oh!” cried the girl.

“ ’Tis for your safety. See, I leave a trail of blood behind me, so that when day rises they will track us easily.”

And sure enough, even by the moon, ’twas easy to trace the dark spots on the grass and earth beside the stile. My left boot, too, was full of blood.

She was silent for awhile. Down in the valley we could hear the screams of the poor horses. The light of the flames lit up the pine trunks about us to a bright scarlet.

===Chinese===
「这话我实在不愿说出口，可你们必须丢下我，自己往前走了。」

「哦——哦！」姑娘叫出声来。

「这是为了你们的安全。瞧，我在身后留下一道血迹，等天一亮，他们便能顺着它轻易追来。」

果然不假：哪怕只借着月光，梯磴旁草叶上、泥土上的黑点也一目了然。我左脚的靴子里，也灌满了血。

她沉默了一阵。谷底传来可怜的马儿的嘶鸣。火焰的光把我们四周的松树干照得一片猩红。

===Original===
“Sir, you hold our gratitude cheaply.”

She unwound the kerchief from her neck, and making me sit on the stile, bound up my knee skillfully, twisting a short stick in the bandage to stop the bleeding.

I thank’d her, and we hurried on into the depths of the wood, treading silently on the deep carpet of pine needles. The ground rose steeply all the way: and all the way, though the light grew feebler, the roar and outcries in the valley follow’d us.

Toward the hill’s summit the trees were sparser. Looking upward, I saw that the sky had grown thickly overcast. We cross’d the ridge, and after a minute or so were in thick cover again.

===Chinese===
「先生，您未免把我们的感激之情看得太贱了。」

她解下颈间的围巾，叫我坐在梯磴上，手脚利落地替我包扎膝盖，又把一小截木棍绞进绷带里，替我止住血。

我谢过她，我们又匆匆往树林深处赶，踏着厚厚的松针地毡，悄然无声。地势一路陡峭上行；一路行去，火光虽愈来愈弱，谷中的咆哮与喊杀却始终缀在我们身后。

将近山顶，树木渐渐稀疏。我仰头一望，天已阴得厚厚实实。我们翻过山脊，约莫一分钟过后，重又钻进密林。

===Original===
’Twas here that Sir Deakin’s strength gave out. Almost without warning, he sank down between our hands, and in a second was taken with that hateful cough, that once already this night had frightened me for his life.

“Ah, ah!” he groaned, between the spasms, “I’m not fit﻿—I’m not fit for it!” and was taken again, and roll’d about barking, so that I fear’d the sound would bring all Settle’s gang on our heels. “I’m not fit for it!” he repeated, as the cough left him, and he lay back helpless, among the pine needles.

Now, I understood his words to bear on his unfitness for death, and judg’d them very decent and properly spoken: and took occasion to hint this in my attempts to console him.

===Chinese===
正是在这里，迪肯爵士的气力用尽了。几乎全无先兆，他从我们搀扶的手中间瘫软下去，转眼便犯起那阵可恨的咳嗽——今夜早些时候，它已吓得我为他性命担忧过一回。

「啊，啊！」他在阵阵痉挛之间呻吟：「我不中用——我干不来这个啦！」说罢又是一阵咳，咳得他像犬吠似地满地翻滚，我怕这动静要把塞特尔一伙全数引到我们脚跟上来。「我干不来这个啦！」咳势退去后，他又说了一遍，无力地仰倒在松针之中。

我呢，把这话理解成他自认不堪就死，还觉得这话说得极体面、极有分寸：便趁着劝慰他，把这层意思点了他一句。

===Original===
“Why, bless the boy!” he cried, sitting up and staring, “for what d’ye think I’m unsuited?”

“Why, to die, sir﻿—to be sure!”

“Holy Mother!” he regarded me with surprise, contempt and pity, all together: “was ever such a dunderhead! If ever man were fit to die, I am he﻿—and that’s just my reasonable complaint. Heart alive! ’tis unfit to *live* I am, tied to this absurd body!”

===Chinese===
「老天爷保佑这孩子！」他直起身子瞪着我，嚷道：「你当我干不来的，是甚么呀？」

「嗐，自然是死呀，爵士——这还用问！」

「圣母马利亚！」他望着我，又是吃惊，又是鄙夷，又是怜悯，三样俱全：「天底下竟有这等榆木脑瓜！若说世上有人够格一死，那人正是我——而这恰恰是我那在情在理的抱怨。哎呀呀！我不中用的是*活*，是叫这副荒唐的皮囊捆住了活！」

===Original===
I suppose my attitude express’d my lack of comprehension, for he lifted a finger and went on﻿—

“Tell me﻿—can you eat beef, and drink beer, and enjoy them?”

“Why, yes.”

“And fight﻿—hey? and kiss a pretty girl, and be glad you’ve done it? Dear, dear, how I do hate a fool and a fool’s pity! Lift me up and carry me a step. This night’s work has kill’d me: I feel it in my lungs. ’Tis a pity, too; for I was just beginning to enjoy it.”

===Chinese===
想是我那副神情写足了懵懂，他竖起一根指头，接着说——

「我问你——牛肉，你吃得香么？啤酒，你喝得痛快么？」

「这个自然。」

「还能厮杀一场——嗯？还能亲一亲俏姑娘，亲过之后心里欢喜？哎呀呀，我最恨的就是蠢材，还有蠢材的怜悯！扶我起来，抱我走一步。今夜这场营生已经要了我的命：我肺里觉出来了。可惜，实在可惜；我才刚刚咂出味儿来呢。」

===Original===
I lifted him as I would a babe, and off we set again, my teeth shutting tight on the pain of my hurt. And presently, coming to a little dingle, about half a mile down the hillside, well hid with dead bracken and blackberry bushes, I consulted with the girl. The place was well shelter’d from the wind that rock’d the treetops, and I fear’d to go much further, for we might come on open country at any moment and so double our peril. It seem’d best, therefore, to lay the old gentleman snugly in the bottom of this dingle and wait for day. And with my buff-coat, and a heap of dried leaves, I made him fairly easy, reserving my cloak to wrap about Mistress Delia’s fair neck and shoulders. But against this at first she protested.

===Chinese===
我像抱婴孩一般抱起他，大家重新上路，我咬紧牙关，死死忍着腿伤的痛。不多时，来到山坡下方约半英里处的一小片幽谷，谷上枯蕨与黑莓丛掩覆得严严实实，我便同姑娘商量起来。这地方避风，任凭风怎样摇撼树梢，谷里纹丝不动；我又不敢再多赶路，因为随时可能撞上开阔的旷野，把凶险再添上一倍。因此，上策莫如让老绅士在谷底舒舒服服躺下，静候天明。我用自己那件皮甲，加上一堆干叶，把他铺垫得相当妥帖，又把自己的斗篷省下来，好裹住德利娅小姐那姣美的颈项与肩头。只是起初，她执意不肯。

===Original===
“For how are you to manage?” she ask’d.

“I shall tramp up and down, and keep watch,” answer’d I, strewing a couch for her beside her father: “and ’tis but fair exchange for the kerchief you gave me from your own throat.”

At last I persuaded her, and she crept close to her father, and under the edge of the buff-coat for warmth. There was abundance of dry bracken in the dingle, and with this and some handfuls of pine needles, I cover’d them over, and left them to find what sleep they might.

===Chinese===
「那你自己怎么办呢？」她问。

「我来回踱步，替你们守夜，」我答道，一面在她父亲身旁给她铺一个铺位：「这也不过是公平交易——你把贴着咽喉的围巾解给了我，我拿斗篷还你。」

我终于劝住了她。她挨紧父亲，钻到皮甲衣缘下面取暖。幽谷里干蕨多的是，我便拿干蕨和几把松针，把他们两个掩好，由他们去寻各自的睡，能睡多少是多少。

===Original===
For two hours and more after this, I hobbled to and fro near them, as well as my wound would allow, looking up at the sky through the pine tops, and listening to the sobbing of the wind. Now and then I would swing my arms for warmth, and breathe on my fingers, that were sorely benumb’d; and all the while kept my ears on the alert, but heard nothing.

’Twas, as I said, something over two hours after, that I felt a soft cold touch, and then another, like kisses on my forehead. I put up my hand, and looked up again at the sky. As I did so, the girl gave a long sigh, and awoke from her doze﻿—

“Sure, I must have dropp’d asleep,” she said, opening her eyes, and spying my shadow above her: “has aught happened?”

“Aye,” replied I, “something is happening that will wipe out our traces and my bloody track.”

===Chinese===
这以后两个多钟头，我拖着伤，一瘸一拐在他们近旁来回踱步，能撑多快是多快；仰头从松梢间望天，听着风的呜咽。不时甩动双臂取暖，往冻得发僵的指头上呵气；耳朵自始至终警醒着，却甚么也没有听见。

如我所说，约莫两个多钟头过后，我觉出额头上轻轻一触，又是一触，凉凉的，像两个吻。我抬起手，又仰头望天。恰在此时，姑娘长叹一声，从小睡中醒来——

「可不，我准是打了个盹。」她说着睁开眼睛，瞥见我的影子罩在头顶：「出了甚么事么？」

「嗯，」我答道，「有一件事正在发生，会把我们的行踪、连同我那条血路，统统抹掉。」

===Original===
“And what is that?”

“Snow: see, ’tis falling fast.”

She bent over, and listen’d to her father’s breathing.

“ ’Twill kill him,” she said simply.

I pull’d some more fronds of the bracken to cover them both. She thank’d me, and offer’d to relieve me in my watch: which I refus’d. And indeed, by lying down I should have caught my death, very likely.

===Chinese===
「那是甚么事？」

「下雪了：瞧，下得正紧呢。」

她俯下身去，听父亲的呼吸。

「这会送了他的命。」她平平地说。

我又扯了些蕨叶，把父女两个盖上。她谢过我，提出替我守一程，我辞了。说实在的，这时候若躺倒，十之八九要把命躺没了。

===Original===
The big flakes drifted down between the pines: till, as the moon paled, the ground about me was carpeted all in white, with the foliage black as ink above it. Time after time, as I tramp’d to and fro, I paus’d to brush the fresh-forming heap from the sleepers’ coverlet, and shake it gently from the tresses of the girl’s hair. The old man’s face was covered completely by the buff-coat: but his breathing was calm and regular as any child’s.

Day dawn’d. Awaking Mistress Delia, I ask’d her to keep watch for a time, while I went off to explore. She crept out from her bed with a little shiver of disgust.

“Run about,” I advis’d, “and keep the blood stirring.”

She nodded: and looking back, as I strode down the hill, I saw her moving about quickly, swinging her arms, and only pausing to wave a hand to me for goodspeed.

===Chinese===
大片大片的雪花在松树之间飘摇而下：待月亮失了颜色，我四周的地面已铺成一幅白毡，头顶的枝叶黑得像墨。我来回踱着，一趟又一趟地停下，拂去酣睡者被衾上新积起的一小堆，再轻轻抖去姑娘发绺上的雪。老人的脸整个儿蒙在皮甲底下：呼吸却平稳匀净，赛过任何一个小孩子。

天破晓了。我唤醒德利娅小姐，请她守望片刻，我好出去探一探路。她带着一丝嫌恶的寒颤，从铺上爬了出来。

「多跑动跑动，」我给她出主意，「好叫血脉活络。」

她点点头：我大步下山时回头一望，见她正快步走动，抡着双臂，只停下来一回，朝我挥手送行。

---

===Original===
’Twas an hour before I return’d: and plenty I had to tell. Only at the entrance to the dingle the words failed from off my tongue. The old gentleman lay as he had lain throughout the night. But the bracken had been toss’d aside, and the girl was kneeling over him. I drew near, my step not arousing her. Sir Deakin’s face was pale and calm: but on the snow that had gather’d by his head, lay a red streak of blood. ’Twas from his lungs, and he was quite dead.

===Chinese===
过了一个钟头我才回来：满肚子的话要讲。可刚到幽谷口，那些话便从我舌头上溜了个干净。老绅士还像整夜那样躺着。只是蕨叶已被拨到一旁，姑娘跪在他身侧。我走近前去，脚步也没惊动她。迪肯爵士的脸苍白而安详：但在他头边积起的雪上，躺着一道殷红的血。那是从他肺里涌出来的，他已经死透了。

## VII I Find a Comrade / 我找到一位同伴

===Original===
But I must go back a little and tell you what befell in my expedition.

===Chinese===
不过我还得倒回去一点儿，把此番探险的经过讲给诸位听。

===Original===
I had scarce trudged out of sight of my friends, down the hill, when it struck me that my footprints in the snow were in the last degree dangerous to them, and might lead Settle and his crew straight to the dingle. Here was a fix. I stood for some minutes nonpluss’d, when above the stillness of the wood (for the wind had dropp’d) a faint sound as of running water caught my ear, and help’d me to an idea.

The sound seem’d to come from my left. Turning aside I made across the hill toward it, and after two hundred paces or so came on a tiny brook, not two feet across, that gush’d down the slope with a quite considerable chatter and impatience. The bed of it was mainly earth, with here and there a large stone or root to catch the toe: so that, as I stepped into the water and began to thread my way down between the banks of snow, ’twas necessary to look carefully to my steps.

===Chinese===
我下山刚走得望不见友人的身影，忽然省悟过来：我留在雪上的脚印，对他们实在是天大的祸患，没准会把塞特尔和他的手下径直引到幽谷里去。这可难办了。我怔怔地立了好几分钟，一筹莫展；忽然，在松林的一片寂静之上（风已经停了），一缕流水似的轻响传进我耳朵，替我解出了个主意。

那声音似乎来自左边。我偏转方向，横过山坡朝它走去，约莫两百步之后，遇见一条小小的溪流，宽不足两英尺，顺着坡面哗哗地冲下来，那份喧腾急躁的劲儿倒颇为可观。溪床大半是泥土，东一处西一处横着大石头或树根，正好绊人的脚尖：因此我一脚踏进水里、开始在两岸积雪之间穿行而下时，便不得不步步留神。

===Original===
Here and there the brook fetch’d a leap down a sharper declivity, or shot over a hanging stone: but, save for the wetting I took in these places, my progress was easy enough. I must have waded in this manner for half a mile, keeping the least possible noise, when at an angle ahead I spied a clearing among the pines, and to the right of the stream, on the very verge, a hut of logs standing, with a wood rick behind it.

’Twas a low building, but somewhat long, and I guess’d it to be, in summer time, a habitation for the woodcutters. But what surpris’d me was to hear a dull, moaning noise, very regular and disquieting, that sounded from the interior of the hut. I listen’d, and hit on the explication. ’Twas the sound of snoring.

===Chinese===
溪水时不时从更陡的坡面纵身一跃，或从一块悬空的石头上激射出去：不过除了在这些地方吃水受湿，我一路走得还算顺当。就这样蹚水而行、尽量不弄出半点声响，约莫走了半英里，前方一处拐角，我发现松林间有片空地，溪流右侧、紧贴着边上立着一座圆木小屋，屋后堆着一垛柴。

那屋子低矮，却有些长，我猜到了夏天，便是伐木工们的住处。可叫我吃惊的是，屋子里面传出一阵沉闷的、呻吟似的声响，节奏均匀，听得人心里发毛。我侧耳细听，恍然悟出了缘故。那是打鼾的声音。

===Original===
Drawing nearer with caution, I noticed, in that end of the hut which stood over the stream, a gap, or window hole. The sound issued through this like the whirring of a dozen looms. “He must be an astonishing fellow,” thought I, “that can snore in this fashion. I’ll have a peep before I wake him.” I waded down till I stood under the sill, put both hands upon it, and pulling myself up quiet as a mouse, stuck my face in at the window﻿—and then very nearly sat back into the brook for fright.

===Chinese===
我小心翼翼地凑近，看见屋子横跨溪流的那一头有个豁口，或者说窗洞。那声音从洞里涌出来，活像十几台织机一齐嗡鸣。「能这样打鼾的，必是位了不得的人物，」我心想，「趁没叫醒他，且先窥上一眼。」我蹚水而下，到窗台底下站定，双手按住台沿，像老鼠一般悄没声儿把身子撑上去，将脸探进窗口——随即吓得差点儿一屁股坐回溪水里去。

===Original===
For I had gazed straight down into the upturn’d faces of Captain Settle and his gang.

===Chinese===
因为我一眼望下去，正对着塞特尔上尉和他那伙人仰起的脸。

===Original===
How long I stood there, with the water rushing past my ankles and my body turning from cold to hot, and back again, I cannot tell you. But ’twas until, hearing no pause in the sleepers’ chorus, I found courage for another peep: and that must have been some time.

There were but six rascals beside the Captain (so that Jacques must have died hard, thought I), and such a raffle of arms and legs and swollen upturn’d faces as they made I defy you to picture. For they were pack’d close as herrings; and the hut was fill’d up with their horses, ready saddled, and rubbing shoulder to loin, so narrow was the room. It needed the open window to give them air: and even so, ’twas not over-fresh inside.

===Chinese===
我在那里立了多久——溪水从我脚踝边奔流而过，我的身子忽而由冷转热、忽而由热转冷——我说不上来。只知道自己一直立着，直到听出酣睡者们的大合唱没有半刻停顿，才鼓起勇气又窥了第二眼：这中间想必过了好一阵子。

上尉之外只有六个恶棍（可见雅克临死之前必曾死战一场，我想），至于他们弄出的那副手脚纠缠、脸膛肿胀朝天的乱象，我倒要请诸君自己想象看。因为他们挤得像桶里的鲱鱼一般紧密；屋子又让他们的马塞满了——鞍辔齐备，肩擦着胯，屋里就是这么窄。全凭敞开的窗口透空气：饶是如此，屋内的气味也说不上多新鲜。

===Original===
I had no mind to stay: but before leaving found myself in the way of playing these villains a pretty trick. To right and left of the window, above their heads, extended two rude shelves that now were heap’d with what I conjectured to be the spoils of the larder of the Three Cups. Holding my breath and thrusting my head and shoulders into the room, I ran my hand along and was quickly possess’d of a boil’d ham, two capons, a loaf, the half of a cold pie, and a basket holding three dozen eggs. All these prizes I filched one by one, with infinite caution.

I was gently pulling the basket through the window hole, when I heard one of the crew yawn and stretch himself in his sleep. So, determining to risk no more, I quietly pack’d the basket, slung it on my right arm, and with the ham grasp’d by the knuckle in my left, made my way up the stream.

===Chinese===
我无意久留：可临走之前，却起意要同这帮恶棍玩一手漂亮的把戏。窗洞两侧、就在他们头顶上方，各架着一条粗陋的搁板，上面堆着的，我猜想必是「三杯」客栈伙房里掳来的吃食。我屏住呼吸，把头和肩膀探进屋内，伸手沿着搁板一路摸过去，顷刻之间便到手了一只煮火腿、两只阉鸡、一条面包、半块冷馅饼，还有一篮子三打鸡蛋。这些战利品，我一件一件、以十二万分的小心逐一偷了出来。

我正轻轻把篮子往窗外拖，忽听那伙人中有一个打着呵欠、在睡梦里伸起懒腰来。于是我不敢再冒风险，悄悄把篮子收拾停当，挎上右臂，左手攥住火腿的关节骨，便顺溪而上了。

===Original===
’Twas thus laden that I enter’d the dingle, and came on the sad sight therein. I set down the ham as a thing to be asham’d of, and bar’d my head. The girl lifted her face, and turning, all white and tragical, saw me.

“My father is dead, sir.”

I stoop’d and pil’d a heap of fresh snow over the blood stains. There was no intent in this but to hide the pity that chok’d me. She had still to hear about her brother, Anthony. Turning, as by a sudden thought, I took her hand. She look’d into my eyes, and her own filled with tears. ’Twas the human touch that loosen’d their flow, I think: and sinking down again beside her father, she wept her fill.

===Chinese===
我就这样满载而归，进了幽谷，撞见谷中那幅伤心的光景。我把火腿放下来，仿佛它是一件叫人羞愧的东西，又摘下帽子。姑娘抬起脸，转过身来——满面苍白，一片惨戚——望见了我。

「我父亲死了，先生。」

我俯下身去，捧起一堆干净的新雪，盖住那些血迹。这样做没有别的用意，只为掩住那哽在我喉头的哀怜。关于她兄弟安东尼的下落，她还有待听闻。我像是蓦然想起什么似的转过身，握住她的手。她望进我的眼睛里，自己的眼里便涨满了泪水。我想，正是这一点人情的触碰，松开了泪水的闸门：她重新在父亲身旁跪坐下去，痛痛快快哭了个够。

===Original===
“Mistress Killigrew,” I said, as soon as the first violence of her tears was abated, “I have still some news that is ill hearing. Your enemies are encamp’d in the woods, about a half mile below this”﻿—and with that I told my story.

“They have done their worst, sir.”

“No.”

She looked at me with a question on her lip.

Said I, “you must believe me yet a short while without questioning.”

Considering for a moment, she nodded. “You have a right, sir, to be trusted, though I know not so much as your name. Then we must stay close in hiding?” she added very sensibly, though with the last word her voice trail’d off, and she began again to weep.

===Chinese===
「基利格鲁小姐，」等她头一阵倾泻的眼泪稍见平息，我便开口道，「我还有一些消息，听着只会叫人难受。你的仇人们就扎营在树林里，离此地约半英里。」——接着我便把自己的经历讲了一遍。

「他们最毒的手段已经使尽了，先生。」

「不。」

她望着我，唇边挂着一个疑问。

我说：「你还得再信我一小会儿，暂且不要问。」

她思忖片刻，点了点头。「你有这个权利，先生，值得人家信任，虽说我连你的名字都还不知道。那么，我们须得紧紧藏起来才是？」她加上这一句，极有见地；只是说到末一个字，声音渐渐低了下去，又重新哭了起来。

===Original===
But in time, having cover’d the dead baronet’s body with sprays of the wither’d bracken, I drew her to a little distance and prevail’d on her to nibble a crust of the loaf. Now, all this while, it must be remembered, I was in my shirt sleeves, and the weather bitter cold. Which at length her sorrow allow’d her to notice.

“Why, you are shivering, sore!” she said, and running, drew my buff-coat from her father’s body, and held it out to me.

“Indeed,” I answer’d, “I was thinking of another expedition to warm my blood.” And promising to be back in half an hour, I follow’d down my former tracks toward the stream.

===Chinese===
不过到得后来，我们用枯萎的蕨叶把已故从男爵的遗体掩好，我把她引到稍远的地方，劝她啃了几口面包皮。诸君须记得，这半天工夫，我身上始终只穿一件衬衫，而天气又冷得砭骨。她的悲伤总算容她注意到了这一点。

「哎呀，瞧你抖得多厉害！」她说着跑过去，从她父亲遗体上取来那件皮甲，递到我面前。

「可不是，」我答道，「我正想着再来一次远征，好叫血脉暖和暖和。」于是我答应半个钟头内回来，便顺着原先的脚印，朝溪边走去。

===Original===
Within twenty minutes I was back, running and well-nigh shouting with joy.

“Come!” I cried to her, “come and see for yourself!”

===Chinese===
不到二十分钟我就回来了，一路奔跑，欢喜得几乎要放声喊叫。

「来！」我朝她喊道，「来亲眼看看！」

===Original===
What had happen’d was this:﻿—Wading cautiously down the brook, I had cause suddenly to prick up my ears and come to a halt. ’Twas the muffled tramp of hoofs that I heard, and creeping a bit further, I caught a glimpse, beyond the hut, of a horse and rider disappearing down the woods. He was the last of the party, as I guess’d from the sound of voices and jingling of bits further down the slope. Advancing on the hut with more boldness, I found it deserted. I scrambled up on the bank and round to the entrance. The snow before it was trampled and sullied by the footmarks of men and horses: and as I noted this, came Settle’s voice calling up the slope﻿—

===Chinese===
事情的经过是这样的：——我蹑手蹑脚顺溪而下，忽然不得不竖起耳朵、收住脚步。我听见的是一阵沉闷的马蹄声；再往前爬了几步，便瞥见小屋那一头，一人一骑正没入林子深处。那是这伙人的殿后，再往坡下去些，那人语声和马嚼子的叮当声便是明证。我胆子大了些，向小屋逼上前去，发现它已人去屋空。我爬上溪岸，绕到门口。门前的积雪被人脚马蹄践踏得一片乌糟：正当我留意到这一点，坡上头传来塞特尔的喊声——

===Original===
“Jerry﻿—Jerry Toy!”

A nearer voice hail’d in answer.

“Where’s Reuben?”

“Coming, Captain﻿—close behind!”

“Curse him for a loitering idiot! We’ve wasted time enough, as ’tis,” called back the Captain. “How in thunder is a man to find the road out of this cursed wood?”

“Straight on, Cap’n﻿—you can’t miss it,” shouted another voice, not two gunshots below.

A volcano of oaths pour’d up from Settle. I did not wait for the end of them: but ran back for Mistress Delia.

===Chinese===
「杰里——杰里·托伊！」

近处一个声音高声应答。

「鲁本呢？」

「来了，上尉——就在后头！」

「这个磨磨蹭蹭的蠢货，真该死！我们已经耽误得够久了。」上尉回喊道。「这该死的林子里，鬼叫人怎么找得出路？」

「一直往前，上尉——错不了。」另一个声音喊道，就在坡下不出两枪远近的地方。

塞特尔那里顿时喷起一座火山似的连篇咒骂。我没有等它喷完，便跑回去接德利娅小姐了。

===Original===
Together we descended to the hut. By this time the voices had faded away in distance. Yet to make sure that the rascals had really departed, we follow’d their tracks for some way, beside the stream; and suddenly came to a halt with cries of joyful surprise.

The brook had led us to a point where, over a stony fall veil’d with brown bracken, it plunged into a narrow ravine. Standing on the lip, where the water took a smoother glide before leaping, we saw the line of the ravine mark’d by a rift in the pines, and through this a slice of the country that lay below. ’Twas a level plain, well watered, and dotted here and there with houses. A range of wooded hills clos’d the view, and toward them a broad road wound gently, till the eye lost it at their base. All this was plain enough, in spite of the snow that cover’d the landscape. For the sun had burst out above, and the few flakes that still fell looked black against his brilliance and the dazzling country below.

===Chinese===
我们一同下到小屋那里。这时人声已在远处消散。不过，为确证这伙恶棍真的去了，我们还是沿着溪边、跟随他们的足迹走了一程；忽然双双止步，发出又惊又喜的欢呼。

溪水把我们引到一处地方：它从一道蒙着褐色蕨衣的石瀑上翻落，一头扎进狭窄的峡谷。我们立在崖唇上——水在这儿先舒成一道平缓的滑流，再纵身跃下——只见峡谷的走向由松林间一道裂隙标出，透过裂隙，望得见下方乡野的一角。那是一片平畴，水道纵横，四处点缀着屋舍。一带林木蓊郁的丘陵收住了视野，一条宽阔的大路朝丘陵蜿蜒而去，走势从容，直到目光在山脚下把它跟丢。尽管积雪覆着整片原野，这一切仍看得清清楚楚。因为太阳已在头顶破云而出，仍在飘落的几片雪花，映着他的光辉和下方耀眼的乡野，看上去竟是黑的。

===Original===
But what caus’d our joy was to see, along the road, a small cavalcade moving away from us, with many bright glances of light and color, as their steel caps and sashes took the sunshine﻿—a pretty sight, and the prettier because it meant our present deliverance.

The girl beside me gave a cry of delight, then sigh’d; and after a minute began to walk back toward the hut: where I left her, and ran up hill for the basket and ham. On my return, I found her examining a heap of rusty tools that, it seem’d, she had found on a shelf of the building. ’Twas no light help to the good fellowship that afterward united us, that from the first I could read her thoughts often without words; and for this reason, that her eyes were as candid as the noonday.

===Chinese===
而令我们欢喜的，是大路上望见的一小队人马正离我们远去，钢盔与肩带迎着日光，闪出点点明亮的光与色——一幅好看的景象，而且加倍好看，因为它意味着我们眼下的解救。

我身旁的姑娘欢叫一声，随即又叹了口气；过了一分钟，便转身朝小屋走去：我把她留在那里，跑上山坡去取篮子和火腿。回来时，见她正在察看一堆生了锈的工具——看那情形，是从屋里搁板上翻出来的。日后把我们联结在一起的那份美好情谊，从一开始便得了这个不小的助力：我常常无须她开口，便能读出她的心思；而所以能够如此，是因为她的眼睛坦白得如同正午的日光。

===Original===
So now I answer’d her aloud﻿—

“This afternoon we may venture down to the plain, where no doubt we shall find a clergyman to sell us a patch of holy ground﻿—”

“Holy ground?” She look’d at me awhile and shook her head. “I am not of your religion,” she said.

“And your father?”

“I think no man ever discovered my father’s religion. Perhaps there was none to discover: but he was no bad father,” she steadied her voice and went on:﻿—“He would prefer the hillside to your ‘holy ground.’ ”

===Chinese===
于是此刻我便出声回答她——

「今天下午我们就可以冒险下到平原上去，在那边，毫无疑问，我们能找到一位牧师，卖给我们一小块圣地——」

「圣地？」她望了我一阵，摇了摇头。「我不奉你们的教。」她说。

「那么你父亲呢？」

「我想，从没有人窥破过我父亲的宗教。也许根本无物可窥破：但他决不是个坏父亲。」她稳住声音，接着说：——「比起你们的『圣地』来，他更中意这片山坡。」

===Original===
So, an hour later, I delv’d his grave in the frosty earth, close by the spot where he lay. Somehow, I shiver’d all the while, and had a cruel shooting pain in my wound that was like to have mastered me before the task was ended. But I managed to lower the body softly into the hole and to cover it reverently from sight: and afterward stood leaning on my spade and feeling very light in the head, while the girl knelt and pray’d for her father’s soul.

And the picture of her as she knelt is the last I remember, till I open’d my eyes, and was amazed to find myself on my back, and staring up at darkness.

===Chinese===
于是，一个钟头之后，我便在他倒卧之处的近旁，从冻土里为他掘墓。不知怎么，我自始至终抖个不住，伤口里更有一阵阵火辣辣的剧痛，险些没等活计干完就把我击垮。但我总算把遗体轻轻放进了穴里，又恭而敬之地掩上土，不叫它再见天日：随后便拄着铁锹立着，只觉头重脚轻，而这时姑娘已跪了下去，为她父亲的灵魂祈祷。

她跪着祈祷的那幅画面，是我记得的最后一幕；待我再睁开眼，惊愕地发现自己仰面躺着，正对着一片黑暗向上凝望。

===Original===
“What has happen’d?”

“I think you are very ill,” said a voice: “can you lean on me, and reach the hut?”

“Why, yes: that is, I think so. Why is everything dark?”

“The sun has been down for hours. You have been in a swoon first, and then talk’d﻿—oh, such nonsense! Shame on me, to let you catch this chill!”

She help’d me to my feet and steadied me: and how we reached the hut I cannot tell you. It took more than one weary hour, as I now know; but, at the time, hours and minutes were one to me.

===Chinese===
「出了什么事？」

「我想你病得不轻，」一个声音说：「能靠着我，走到小屋去吗？」

「唔，能：就是说，我想能吧。怎么四下里一片漆黑？」

「太阳落下去好几个钟头了。你先是昏厥，后来又说胡话——哎呀，好一派胡言！都怨我不好，竟让你招了这场寒气！」

她扶我起身，撑稳了我：我们是怎么到的小屋，我说不上来。如今我才知道，那足足耗了一个多钟头，疲惫不堪的钟头；可在当时，钟头与分钟于我本是一样。

===Original===
In that hut I lay four nights and four days, between ague fit and fever. And that is all the account I can give of the time, save that, on the second day, the girl left me alone in the hut and descended to the plain, where, after asking at many cottages for a physician, she was forced to be content with an old woman reputed to be amazingly well skill’d in herbs and medicines; whom, after a day’s trial, she turn’d out of doors. On the fourth day, fearing for my life, she made another descent, and coming to a wayside tavern, purchased a pint of aqua vitae, carried it back, and mix’d a potion that threw me into a profuse sweat. The same evening I sat up, a sound man.

===Chinese===
在那间小屋里，我躺了四天四夜，疟寒与高烧轮番来袭。关于那段时日，我所能交代的也尽在于此；只是：第二天，姑娘把我独自留在屋里，下到平原去了，在许多村舍挨门挨户打听医生，末了不得不将就一位老妇人——据说她在草药汤剂上神通大得惊人；才试了一天，便被她请出了门。第四天，她怕我性命不保，又下去了一趟，走到一家路旁酒馆，买了一品脱烈酒（aqua vitae，「生命之水」），带回来调了一剂药，灌得我大汗淋漓。当天晚上，我便坐了起来，好端端一条汉子了。

===Original===
Indeed, so thoroughly was I recover’d that, waking early next morning, and finding my sweet nurse asleep from sheer weariness, in a corner of the hut, I stagger’d up from my bed of dried bracken, and out into the pure air. Rare it was to stand and drink it in like wine. A footstep arous’d me. ’Twas Mistress Delia: and turning, I held out my hand.

“Now this is famous,” said she: “a day or two will see you as good a man as ever.”

===Chinese===
的确，我康复得再彻底不过：第二天一清早醒来，见我那可爱的看护因过度的劳累，正在屋角酣睡，我便从干蕨铺就的床上一骨碌爬起，摇摇晃晃走出屋去，到了清爽的空气里。立在那儿像饮酒一般把它一口口饮下，真是罕有的享受。一阵脚步声惊动了我。是德利娅小姐：我转过身，向她伸出手。

「这下可妙极了，」她说：「一两天之内，包你又是条齐整的好汉。」

===Original===
“A day or two? Tomorrow at latest, I shall make trial to start.” I noted a sudden change on her face, and added: “Indeed, you must hear my reasons before setting me down for an ingrate;” and told her of the King’s letter that I carried. “I hoped that for a while our ways might lie together,” said I; and broke off, for she was looking me earnestly in the face.

“Sir, as you know, my brother Anthony was to have met me﻿—nay, for pity’s sake, turn not your face away! I have guess’d﻿—the sword you carry﻿—I mark’d it. Sir, be merciful, and tell me!”

===Chinese===
「一两天？最迟明天，我就要试着动身了。」我察觉她脸上蓦地一变，便补充道：「说真的，你总得先听听我的理由，再把我当作忘恩负义之徒不迟；」于是把身上带着的国王的密信对她说知。「我原本指望，我们俩的路可以结伴同行一程，」我说；话到半截却收住了，因为她正急切地端详着我的脸。

「先生，你也知道，我的兄弟安东尼原是要来迎我的——不，看在怜悯的份上，别把脸别开！我已经猜到了——你随身佩的那柄剑——我认出来了。先生，行行好，告诉我吧！」

===Original===
I led her a little aside to the foot of a tall pine; and there, though it rung my heart, told her all; and left her to wrestle with this final sorrow. She was so tender a thing to be stricken thus, that I who had dealt the blow crept back to the hut, covering my eyes. In an hour’s time I look’d out. She was gone.

At nightfall she return’d, white with grief and fatigue; yet I was glad to see her eyes red and swol’n with weeping. Throughout our supper she kept silence; but when ’twas over, look’d up and spoke in a steady tone﻿—

===Chinese===
我把她引到一旁，走到一株高松的脚下；在那里，虽然字字剜心，我还是把一切都告诉了她；随后便丢下她，让她独自去与这最后的悲伤角力。她这么一个柔嫩的人儿，竟遭此重击；我这个亲手递出打击的人，只得掩着双眼，一步一步挨回小屋。过了一个钟头，我向外张望。她已经不在了。

暮色四合时她回来了，悲伤与劳瘁使她脸色煞白；然而望见她双眼哭得又红又肿，我倒觉得欢喜。整个晚饭她一言不发；但饭一吃完，便抬起头来，声气平稳地说——

===Original===
“Sir, I have a favor to ask, and must risk being held importunate﻿—”

“From you to me,” I put in, “all talk of favors had best be dropp’d.”

“No﻿—listen. If ever it befell you to lose father or mother or dearly loved friend, you will know how the anguish stuns﻿—Oh sir! today the sun seem’d fallen out of heaven, and I a blind creature left groping in the void.
