Indeed, sir, ’tis no wonder: I had a father, brother, and servant ready to die for me﻿—three hearts to love and lean on: and today they are gone.”

I would have spoken, but she held up a hand.

===Chinese===
「先生，我有一事相求，哪怕被当作纠缠不休，也顾不得了——」

「从你到我，」我插言道，「恩惠二字，最好还是免谈。」

「不——你听着。倘若你也曾遭逢丧父、丧母或失去挚友之事，你便知道这剧痛何等灼人发懵——啊，先生！今天，太阳仿佛从天上跌落了下去，只剩我这瞎了眼的生灵，在虚空里摸索。真的，先生，这不足为怪：我原有一个父亲、一个兄弟、一个仆人，个个都甘愿为我而死——有三颗心可以爱、可以倚靠：而今天，他们都没有了。」

我本想说话，她却举起一只手来。

===Original===
“Now when you spoke of Anthony﻿—a dear lad!﻿—I lay for some time dazed with grief. By little and little, as the truth grew plainer, the pain grew also past bearing. I stood up and stagger’d into the woods to escape it. I went fast and straight, heeding nothing, for at first my senses were all confus’d: but in a while the walking clear’d my wits, and I could think: and thinking, I could weep: and having wept, could fortify my heart. Here is the upshot, sir﻿—though ’tis held immodest for a maid to ask even far less of a man. We are both bound for Cornwall﻿—you on an honorable mission, I for my father’s estate of Gleys, wherefrom (as your tale proves) some unseen hands are thrusting me. Alike we carry our lives in our hands. You must go forward: I may not go back. For from a King who cannot right his own affairs there is little hope; and in Cornwall I have surer friends than he. Therefore take me, sir﻿—take me for a comrade! Am I sad? Do you fear a weary journey? I will smile﻿—laugh﻿—sing﻿—put sorrow behind me. I will contrive a thousand ways to cheat the milestones. At the first hint of tears, discard me, and go your way with no prick of conscience. Only try me﻿—oh, the shame of speaking thus!”

===Chinese===
「方才你说起安东尼——多好的一个孩子！——我悲恸得痴了好一阵。一点一点地，真相越分明，痛楚也越发难当。我站起身，跌跌撞撞逃进树林，想躲开它。我走得又快又直，甚么也不顾，因为起初我的神智全乱了：可走了一阵，脚步让我的头脑清醒过来，我能想了：既能想，便能哭：哭过了，便能把心重新撑住。事情的结局就是这样，先生——虽说一个闺中女子向男子开口相求，哪怕所求远不及此，也要算不知羞了。我们俩都是往康沃尔去——你身负一桩体面的使命，我去我父亲的格莱斯庄园，那里（正如你的遭遇所证）正有看不见的手把我往前推。我们一样，都把性命提在手里赶路。你必须向前：我不能后退。指望一位连自家事都摆不平的国王，本没有多少盼头；在康沃尔，我有比他靠得住的朋友。所以带上我吧，先生——收我为同伴！难道我愁苦么？难道你怕路途劳顿么？我会微笑——会大笑——会唱歌——会把悲伤丢在身后。我会想出一千种法子，骗过一块块里程石。头一回见我掉泪，你就只管丢下我，问心无愧地走你的路。只求你试我一试——啊，说这样的话，多叫人羞煞！」

===Original===
Her voice had grown more rapid toward the close: and now, breaking off, she put both hands to cover her face, that was hot with blushes. I went over and took them in mine:

“You have made me the blithest man alive,” said I. She drew back a pace with a frighten’d look, and would have pull’d her hands away.

“Because,” I went on quickly, “you have paid me this high compliment, to trust me. Proud was I to listen to you; and merrily will the miles pass with you for comrade. And so I say﻿—Mistress Killigrew, take me for your servant.”

===Chinese===
说到末尾，她的语声越来越急：此刻戛然收住，她双手掩住那张烧得通红的脸。我走过去，把那双手握进自己掌中：

「你使我成了世上最快活的人。」我说。她带着受了惊吓的神色退后一步，直要把手抽回去。

「因为，」我赶紧接下去，「你给了我这份崇高的嘉许——信任我。听你说话，我心中自豪；有你作同伴，一程程路也自会走得欢欢喜喜。所以我请求——基利格鲁小姐，收下我作你的仆人吧。」

===Original===
To my extreme discomposure, as I dropp’d her hands, her eyes were twinkling with laughter.

“Dear now; I see a dull prospect ahead if we use these long titles!”

“But﻿—”

“Indeed, sir, please yourself. Only as I intend to call you ‘Jack’ perhaps ‘Delia’ will be more of a piece than ‘Mistress Killigrew.’ ” She dropp’d me a mock curtsey. “And now, Jack, be a good boy, and hitch me this quilt across the hut. I bought it yesterday at a cottage below here﻿—”

She ended the sentence with the prettiest blush imaginable; and so, having fix’d her screen, we shook hands on our comradeship, and wish’d each other good night.

===Chinese===
等我松开她的手，却见她眼里已闪着笑意，弄得我狼狈至极。

「哎哟，往后若拿这些长长的名头你来我往，可要闷死人啦！」

「可是——」

「真的，先生，随你的便。只是，我既打算唤你『杰克』，那么比起『基利格鲁小姐』来，『德利娅』想必更为相称。」她朝我戏谑地屈膝一福。「好了，杰克，做个乖孩子，替我把这床被帘拉在屋里那头。是我昨天在下面一处村舍买的——」

话没说完，她脸上已飞起所能想象的最动人的红晕；于是，张挂好她的被帘，我们便为这同伴之谊握了握手，互道了晚安。

## VIII I Lose the King's Letter; and Am Carried to Bristol / 我遗失了国王的密信；并被押往布里斯托尔

===Original===
Almost before daylight we were afoot, and the first ray of cold sunshine found us stepping from the woods into the plain, where now the snow was vanished and a glistening coat of rime spread over all things. Down here the pines gave way to bare elms and poplars, thickly dotted, and among them the twisting smoke of farmstead and cottage, here and there, and the morning stir of kitchen and stable very musical in the crisp air.
Delia stepped along beside me, humming an air or breaking off to chatter. Meeting us, you would have said we had never a care. The road went stretching away to the northwest and the hills against the sky there; whither beyond, we neither knew nor (being both young, and one, by this time, pretty deep in love) did greatly care. Yet meeting with a wagoner and his team, we drew up to enquire.

===Chinese===
天还没亮我们就上路了，第一缕冷冽的阳光照到我们身上时，我们正从树林里走出来，踏上平原。这时雪已经化尽，一层亮晶晶的白霜铺满了万物。松树到了这里便让位给光秃秃的榆树和白杨，稀疏地散布着，其间升起了农庄和小屋的袅袅炊烟，零零落落。厨房和马厩清晨的声响在冷冽的空气里格外悦耳。
德利娅走在我身旁，哼着一支小曲，不时停下来喋喋不休。若是有人遇见我们，准会说我们无忧无虑。大路向西北延伸，那边的山丘衬着天空；再往何处去，我们既不知道，也不大在意——毕竟我们两个都年轻，其中一个此刻已深深坠入了爱河。然而遇上一个赶车的和他的一队牲口，我们还是停下来打听。

===Original===
The wagoner had a shock of whitish hair and a face purple-red above, by reason of the cold, and purple-black below, for lack of a barber. He purs'd up his mouth and look'd us slowly up and down.
"Come," said I, "you are not deaf, I hope, nor dumb."
"Send I may niver!" the fellow ejaculated, slowly and with contemplation: "'tis an unseemly sight, yet tickling to the mirthfully minded. Haw—haw!" He check'd his laughter suddenly and stood like a stone image beside his horses.

===Chinese===
那车夫长着一头灰白的乱发，脸的上半截因为受冻而发紫发红，下半截因为久未剃须而紫黑。他噘起嘴巴，慢悠悠地上下打量我们。
"好了，"我说，"但愿你不是聋子，也不是哑巴。"
"老天保佑别叫我遇上！"那家伙拖长了声音，慢条斯理地说，"这可真是一副不太好看的样子，不过倒逗得人想笑。哈——哈！"他突然刹住笑声，像个石头雕像似的立在马旁边。

===Original===
"Good sir," said Delia, laying a hand on my arm (for I was growing nettled), "your mirth is a riddle: but tell us our way and you are free to laugh."
"Oh, Scarlet—Scarlet!" answer'd he: "and to me, that am a man o' blushes from my cradle!"
Convinced by this that the fellow must be an idiot, I told him so, and left him staring after us; nor heard the sound of his horses moving on again for many minutes.

===Chinese===
"好心的先生，"德利娅把手搭在我胳膊上说道（因为我正要恼火起来），"您的笑真是个谜——不过只要告诉我们路怎么走，您尽可以笑个够。"
"噢，红的——红的！"他回答，"对我这个打从娘胎出来就脸红的汉子来说！"
我断定这人准是个傻子，便直言相告，然后撇下他瞪着我们的背影；好半天才听到他的马车又动起来的声音。

===Original===
After this we met about a dozen on the road, and all paus'd to stare. But from one—an old woman—we learn'd we were walking toward Marlboro', and about noon were over the hills and looking into the valley beyond.
'Twas very like the other vale; only a pleasant stream wound along the bottom, by the banks of which the road took us. Here, by a bridge, we came to an inn bearing the sign of the Broad Face, and entered: for Captain Settle's stock of victuals was now done. A sour-fac'd woman met us at the door.
"Do you stay here," Delia advis'd me, "and drink a mug of beer while I bargain with the hostess for fresh food." She follow'd the sour-fac'd woman into the house.
But out she comes presently with her cheeks flaming and a pair of bright eyes. "Come!" she commanded, "come at once!" Setting down my half emptied mug, I went after her across the bridge and up the road, wondering. In this way we must have walk'd for a mile or more before she turn'd and stamp'd her little foot—
"Horrible!" she cried. "Horrible—wicked—shameful! Ugh!" There were tears in her eyes.

===Chinese===
之后我们在路上遇到了大约一打人，个个都停下来看我们。但有一位——一个老妇人——告诉我们，我们正朝马尔伯勒（Marlboro'）走去。将近中午时，我们翻过山丘，望见了那边的山谷。
那山谷跟先前经过的很像，只有一条宜人的溪流蜿蜒在谷底，大路就沿着河岸走。在一座桥边，我们看见一家挂着"宽脸"招牌的旅店，便走了进去——因为塞特尔上尉（Captain Settle）的食物已经吃完了。一个板着脸的女人在门口迎住了我们。
"你在这儿等着，"德利娅建议道，"喝杯麦酒，我跟女东家讨点新鲜食物去。"她跟着那板脸女人进了屋。
可是她很快就出来了，双颊绯红，一双眼睛亮闪闪的。"来！"她命令道，"快来！"我放下喝了半杯的麦酒，满腹疑惑地跟着她穿过桥走上大路。这样走了大约一英里多路，她才转过身来，跺着小脚——
"太可怕了！"她叫道，"太可怕了——邪恶——无耻！呸！"她眼里含着泪水。

===Original===
"What is shameful?"
She made no reply, but walk'd on again quickly.
"I am getting hungry, for my part," sigh'd I, after a little.
"Then you must starve!"
"Oh!"
She wheel'd round again.
"Jack, this will never do. If you are to have a comrade, let it be a boy."
"Now, I am very passably content as things are."
"Nonsense: at Marlboro', I mean, you must buy me a suit of boy's clothes. What are you hearkening to?"
"I thought I heard the noise of guns—or is it thunder?"
"Dear Jack, don't say 'tis thunder! I do mortally fear thunder—and mice."
"'Twouldn't be thunder at this time of year. No, 'tis guns firing."
"Where?—not that I mind guns."
"Ahead of us."

===Chinese===
"什么东西无耻？"
她没有回答，只是快步往前走。
"我倒是越来越饿了，"过了一会儿我叹道。
"那你只好挨饿！"
"噢！"
她又转过身来。
"杰克，这可不行。你若要找个同伴，不如找个男孩。"
"依我说，眼下这样我已经相当满意了。"
"胡说：我是说，到了马尔伯勒，你得给我买一套男孩子的衣服。你在听什么？"
"我好像听到了枪声——还是打雷？"
"亲爱的杰克，别说是打雷！我顶害怕打雷了——还有老鼠。"
"这个季节不会打雷的。不，那是枪声。"
"在哪儿？——不过枪声我不怕。"
"就在我们前面。"

===Original===
On the far side of the valley we enter'd a wood, thinking by this to shorten our way: for the road here took a long bend to eastward. Now, at first this wood seem'd of no considerable size, but thicken'd and spread as we advanced. 'Twas only, however, after passing the ridge, and when daylight began to fail us, that I became alarm'd. For the wood grew denser, with a tangle of paths crisscrossing amid the undergrowth. And just then came the low mutter of cannon again, shaking the earth. We began to run forward, tripping in the gloom over brambles, and stumbling into holes.
For a mile or so this lasted: and then, without warning, I heard a sound behind me, and look'd back, to find Delia sunk upon the ground.
"Jack, here's a to-do!"
"What's amiss?"
"Why, I am going to swoon!"

===Chinese===
在山谷的另一头，我们走进一片树林，想抄近路——因为这条路在这里向东绕了很大一个弯。起初这林子似乎不大，但越往里走越密越深。不过，翻过山脊、天色开始暗下来之后，我才着了慌。林子越来越密，灌木丛中纵横交错着各种小径。就在这时，低沉的炮声又响起来，震得地面发颤。我们开始往前跑，在暮色中被荆棘绊倒，跌进坑里。
这样跑了大约一英里路：突然间，我听到身后有声响，回头一看，发现德利娅瘫倒在地上。
"杰克，出事了！"
"怎么了？"
"唉，我要晕过去了！"

===Original===
The words were scarce out, when there sounded a crackling and snapping of twigs ahead, and two figures came rushing toward us—a man and a woman. The man carried an infant in his arms: and though I call'd on them to stop, the pair ran by us with no more notice than if we had been stones. Only the woman cried, "Dear Lord, save us!" and wrung her hands as she pass'd out of sight.
"This is strange conduct," thought I: but peering down, saw that Delia's face was white and motionless. She had swoon'd, indeed, from weariness and hunger. So I took her in my arms and stumbled forward, hoping to find the end of the wood soon. For now the rattle of artillery came louder and incessant through the trees, and mingling with it, a multitude of dull shouts and outcries. At first I was minded to run after the man and woman, but on second thought, resolv'd to see the danger before hiding from it.
The trees, in a short while, grew sparser, and between the stems I mark'd a ruddy light glowing. And then I came out on an open space upon the hillside, with a dip of earth in front; and beyond, a long ridge of pines standing up black, because of a red glare behind them; and saw that this came not from any setting sun, but was the light of a conflagration.

===Chinese===
话音未落，前方就传来了树枝噼啪折断的声响，两个人影朝我们冲来——一男一女。那男人怀里抱着一个婴儿：我虽然喊他们停下，两人却像我们是石头似的径直跑过去了。只有那女人叫了一声："天主啊，救救我们！"绞着双手消失在视野之外。
"这可真怪，"我想：但低头一看，发现德利娅脸色苍白，一动不动。她确实是又累又饿晕过去了。我于是把她抱起来，蹒跚着往前走，希望快些走出这片林子。因为此时炮火的隆隆声穿过树林传来，越来越响，连绵不断，夹杂着一片沉闷的呐喊和叫骂。起初我想去追那对男女，但转念一想，决定先看清危险再躲避。
没过多久，树木稀疏了，在树干之间我瞥见了一片红光。接着我来到山腰上的一片空地，面前是个凹地；再远处是一长排黑黝黝的松树，因为后面映着一片红光；我看出那光并非落日，而是一场大火的光焰。

===Original===
The glare danced and quiver'd in the sky, as I cross'd the hollow. It made even Delia's white cheek seem rosy. Up amid the pines I clamor'd, and along the ridge to where it broke off in a steep declivity. And lo! in a minute I look'd down as 'twere into the infernal pit.
There was a whole town burning below. And in the streets men were fighting, as could be told by their shouts and the rattle and blaze of musketry. For a garment of smoke lay over all and hid them: only the turmoil beat up as from a furnace, and the flames of burning thatches, and quick jets of firearms like lightning in a thundercloud. Great sparks floated past us, and over the trees at our back. A hot blast breath'd on our cheeks. Now and then you might hear a human shriek distinct amid the din, and this spoke terribly to the heart.
Now the town was Marlboro', and the attacking force a body of royal troops sent from Oxford to oust the garrison of the Parliament, which they did this same night, with great slaughter, driving the rebels out of the place, and back on the road to Bristol. Had we guess'd this, much ill luck had been spared us; but we knew nought of it, nor whether friends or foes were getting the better. So (Delia being by this time recover'd a little) we determined to pass the night in the woods, and on the morrow to give the place a wide berth.

===Chinese===
那红光在我穿过凹地时在天空中跳跃、摇曳。它甚至把德利娅苍白的脸颊映得泛起了红晕。我沿着松林往上爬，顺着山脊走到一处陡峭的断崖边。看哪！不一会儿我就往下望去，仿佛看到了地狱的深渊。
底下有一整座镇子在燃烧。街上人们在厮杀，可以从他们的呐喊和火枪的噼啪射击声中听出来。因为一层烟幕笼罩了一切，把他们遮住了：只有骚乱像从熔炉里升腾而起，茅草屋顶燃烧的火焰，以及枪口喷出的阵发火光，恰似雷雨云中的闪电。大团的火星从我们身边飘过，飘到身后的树顶上。一股热风吹到我们脸上。不时能听到一声尖厉的惨叫从嘈杂中传来，令人心惊胆战。
那镇子正是马尔伯勒，进攻的部队是王军从牛津派来驱逐议会军驻军的。就在这个晚上，他们以极大的伤亡夺回了此地，把叛军赶出去，往布里斯托尔方向溃退。我们若猜到此节，就能少受许多罪了；但我们对此一无所知，也不知道哪一方占了上风。因此（德利娅此时已经恢复了一些）我们决定在树林里过一夜，第二天一早远远绕开这地方。

===Original===
Retreating, then, to the hollow (that lay on the lee side of the ridge, away from the north wind), I gather'd a pile of great stones, and spread my cloak thereover for Delia. To sleep was impossible, even with the will for it. For the tumult and fighting went on, and only died out about an hour before dawn: and once or twice we were troubled to hear the sound of people running on the ridge above. So we sat and talked in low voices till dawn; and grew more desperately hunger'd than ever.
With the chill of daybreak we started, meaning to get quit of the neighborhood before any espied us; and fetch'd a compass to the south without another look at Marlboro'. At the end of two hours, turning northwest again, we came to some water meadows beside a tiny river (the Kennet, as I think), and saw, some way beyond, a high road that cross'd to our side (only the bridge was now broken down), and further yet, a thick smoke curling up; but whence this came I could not see. Now we had been avoiding all roads this morning, and hiding at every sound of footsteps. But hunger was making us bold. I bade Delia crouch down by the stream's bank, where many alders grew, and set off toward this column of smoke.

===Chinese===
于是我们退到那个凹地（在山脊的背风面，远离北风），我搬了一堆大石头，把斗篷铺在上面给德利娅。想睡觉也是不可能的，即便有这个意愿。骚乱和战斗还在继续，直到黎明前一小时左右才渐渐平息：我们还一两次被山脊上有人奔跑的声响惊扰。我们就那样坐着低声说话，一直说到天亮；肚子比先前更饿得厉害了。
清晨的寒意中我们动身了，打算在被人发现之前离开这一带。我们折向南方，再没有回头去看马尔伯勒。走了大约两个小时后，又转向西北，来到一条小河（我想是肯尼特河 Kennet）边的水草牧场，看见远处有一条大路横穿过来（不过桥已经断了），更远处有浓烟升起；但看不出烟从哪里来。这一早上我们一直在回避所有大路，一有脚步声就躲藏。但饥饿让我们壮了胆。我让德利娅蹲在长满赤杨的河岸上，自己朝那柱浓烟走去。

===Original===
By the spot where the road cross'd I noted that many men and horses had lately pass'd hereby to westward, and, by their footmarks, at a great speed. A little further, and I came on a broken musket flung against the hedge, with a nauseous mess of blood and sandy hairs about the stock of it; and just beyond was a dead horse, his legs sticking up like bent poles across the road. 'Twas here that my blood went cold on a sudden, to hear a dismal groaning not far ahead. I stood still, holding my breath, and then ran forward again.
The road took a twist that led me face to face with a small whitewashed cottage, smear'd with black stains of burning. For seemingly it had been fir'd in one or two places, only the flames had died out: and from the back, where some outbuilding yet smoulder'd, rose the smoke that I spied. But what brought me to a stand was to see the doorway all crack'd and charr'd, and across it a soldier stretch'd—a green-coated rebel—and quite dead. His face lay among the burn'd ruins of the door, that had woefully singed his beard and hair. A stain of blood ran across the door stone and into the road.
I was gazing upon him and shuddering, when again I heard the groans. They issued from the upper chamber of the cottage. I stepped over the dead soldier and mounted the ladder that led upstairs.

===Chinese===
在大路交汇处，我注意到不久前有许多人马从这里经过，往西而去，从脚印来看速度极快。再往前走一点，我发现一支断了的火枪靠在树篱上，枪托周围沾着一片令人作呕的血迹和沙色毛发；再往前是一匹死马，四条腿像弯杆子一样竖在路当中。就在这个地方，我的血一下子凉了，因为我听到了前面不远处传来痛苦的呻吟。我屏住呼吸站住，然后又往前跑去。
大路拐了一个弯，迎面出现了一座粉刷过的小屋，墙上有烧焦的黑斑。看来它曾被从一两处点燃，但火焰已经熄灭了：后面某处附属建筑还在冒烟，那正是我看到的烟柱来源。但令我站住脚的，是看到门洞全都烧焦开裂，一个士兵横陈在门口——一个穿绿衣的叛军——已经死了。他的脸埋在烧毁的门板碎片里，胡子和头发都被烧焦了。一道血迹从门槛一直流到路上。
我正盯着他发抖，又听到了呻吟声。声音从楼上传来。我跨过那个死去的士兵，爬上通向楼上的梯子。

===Original===
The upper room was but a loft. In it were two beds, whereof one was empty. On the edge of the other sat up a boy of sixteen or thereabouts, stark naked and moaning miserably. With one hand he seem'd trying to cover a big wound that gaped in his chest: the other, as my head rose over the ladder, he stretch'd out with all the fingers spread. And this was his last effort. As I stumbled up, his fingers clos'd in a spasm of pain; his hands dropp'd, and the body tumbled back on the bed, where it lay with the legs dangling.
The poor lad must have been stabb'd as he lay asleep. For by the bedside I found his clothes neatly folded and without a speck of blood. They were clean, though coarse; so thinking they would serve for Delia, I took them, albeit with some scruples at robbing the dead, and covering the body with a sheet, made my way downstairs.
Here, on a high shelf at the foot of the ladder, I discover'd a couple of loaves and some milk, and also, lying hard by, a pair of shepherd's shears, which I took also, having a purpose for them. By this time, being sick enough of the place, I was glad to make all speed back to Delia.
She was still waiting among the leafless alders, and clapp'd her hands to see the two loaves under my arm.
Said I, flinging down the clothes, and munching at my share of the bread—
"Here is the boy's suit that you wish'd for."

===Chinese===
楼上不过是个阁楼。里面有两张床，一张是空的。另一张床上坐着一个大约十六岁的少年，赤身裸体，痛苦地呻吟着。他一只手似乎想捂住胸口上一个张开的大伤口：另一只手在我探头上来时张开了五指伸向我。这是他最后的动作。我踉跄着爬上来时，他的手指因剧痛痉挛了一下；双手垂落，身体仰面倒回床上，两腿悬在床边。
这可怜的少年准是在熟睡时被刺的。因为我在床边找到了他的衣服，叠得整整齐齐，一滴血迹也没有。衣服虽粗，却很干净；我想德利娅可以穿，便拿走了，尽管有些于心不安——从死人身上拿东西。我用一张被单盖住尸体，下了楼。
在梯脚旁的高架板上，我发现了两条面包和一些牛奶，旁边还放着一把牧羊人的大剪刀，我也拿了——我正用得着。这时我对这地方已经厌恶透了，便赶紧回去找德利娅。
她还在光秃秃的赤杨树丛中等着，看到我腋下夹着两条面包，高兴得拍起手来。
我把衣服往地上一扔，一边嚼着自己的那份面包，一边说：
"这就是你想要的那套男孩衣服。"

===Original===
"Oh, dear! 'tis not a very choice one." Her face fell.
"All the better for escaping notice."
"But—but I *like* to be noticed!"
Nevertheless, when breakfast was done, she consented to try on the clothes. I left her eyeing them doubtfully, and stroll'd away by the river's bank. In a while her voice call'd to me—
"Oh, Jack—they do not fit at all!"
"Why, 'tis admirable!" said I, returning, and scanning her. Now this was a lie: but she took me more than ever, so pretty and comical she look'd in the dress.
"And I cannot walk a bit in them!" she pouted, strutting up and down.
"Swing your arms more, and let them hang looser."
"And my hair. Oh, Jack, I have such beautiful hair!"
"It must come off," said I, pulling the shears out of my pocket.
"And look at these huge boots!"
Indeed, this was the main trouble, for I knew they would hurt her in walking: yet she made more fuss about her hair, and only gave in when I scolded her roundly. So I took the shears and clipp'd the chestnut curls, one by one, while she cried for vexation; and took occasion of her tears to smuggle the longest lock inside my doublet.

===Chinese===
"噢，天哪！可不太好看呢。"她脸沉了下来。
"越不起眼越好。"
"可是——可是我就*喜欢*引人注意！"
尽管如此，早餐吃完后，她还是同意试穿那身衣服。我留她在那里犹犹豫豫地打量衣服，自己沿着河岸溜达了一会儿。没多久她的声音传了过来——
"噢，杰克——一点也不合身呀！"
"哪里，好极了！"我走回去打量她说。其实这是谎话：可她穿着这身衣服实在太好看、太滑稽了，让我更喜欢她了。
"我穿着根本走不了路！"她撅着嘴，来回走着摆出架势。
"胳膊甩大点，让它们更松散些。"
"还有我的头发。噢，杰克，我这头发多漂亮啊！"
"必须剪掉，"我说着，从口袋里掏出剪刀。
"还有这双大靴子！"
这倒确实是主要问题，我知道走路会磨脚的：可她对头发闹得更凶，直到我狠狠训了她一通才罢休。于是我拿起剪刀，一缕一缕地剪掉她栗色的卷发，她又气又恼地哭起来；我趁她落泪之际，把最长的一绺偷偷藏进了我的紧身上衣里。

===Original===
---
But, an hour after, she was laughing again, and had learned to cock the poor country lad's cap rakishly over one eye: and by evening was walking with a swagger and longing (I know) to meet with folks. For, to spare her the sight of the ruin'd cottage, I had taken her round through the fields, and by every bypath that seem'd to lead westward. 'Twas safer to journey thus; and all the way she practic'd a man's carriage and airs, and how to wink and whistle and swing a stick. And once, when she left one of her shoes in a wet ditch, she said "d——n!" as natural as life: and then—
We jump'd over a hedge, plump into an outpost of rebels, as they sat munching their supper.

===Chinese===
---
可是，一小时之后，她又笑了起来，已经学会了把那顶可怜的乡下小子帽歪斜地扣在一只眼上方：到傍晚，她已经走得趾高气扬，巴不得（我知道）遇见什么人。因为我不想让她看到那座烧毁的小屋，所以绕着田间小路走，专挑那些似乎通向西边的羊肠小道。这样走安全些；一路上她一直练习男人的步态和派头，学怎么使眼色、怎么吹口哨、怎么甩棍子。有一次，她把一只鞋掉进了水沟里，竟然骂了一声"见鬼"，活灵活现：接着——
我们翻过一道树篱，正好撞进叛军的一个前哨，他们正坐在那里啃晚饭。

===Original===
They were six in all, and must have been sitting like mice: for all I know of it is this. I had climb'd the hedge first, and was helping Delia over, when out of the ground, as it seem'd, a voice shriek'd, "Run—run!—the King's men are on us!" and then, my foot slipping, down I went on to the shoulders of a thickset man, and well-nigh broke his neck as he turn'd to look up at me.
At first, the whole six were for running, I believe. But seeing only a lad stretch'd on his face, and a second on the hedge, they thought better of it. Before I could scramble up, one pair of hands was screw'd about my neck, another at my heels, and in a trice there we were pinion'd.
"Fetch the lantern, Zacchaeus."

===Chinese===
一共六个人，一定是像老鼠一样安安静静坐着的：因为我所知道的就是这些。我先爬过树篱，正在帮德利娅翻过去，突然仿佛从地底下冒出一个声音尖叫："快跑——快跑！——王军来了！"接着我脚下一滑，扑通摔到一个壮汉的肩膀上，差点把他的脖子压断，他一抬头看我。
起初，我相信那六个人都想逃。但看到不过是一个脸朝下趴在地上的小子，另一个还挂在树篱上，他们就改变了主意。我还没来得及爬起来，一双手就扼住了我的脖子，另一双手抓住了我的脚跟，转眼间我们就被捆住了。
"把灯笼拿来，撒该（Zacchaeus）。"

===Original===
'Twas quickly lit, and thrust into my face; and very foolish I must have look'd. The fellows were all clad in green coats, much soil'd with mud and powder. And they grinn'd in my face till I long'd to kick them.
"Search the malignant!" cried one. "Question him," call'd out another; and forthwith began a long interrogatory concerning the movements of his Majesty's troops, from which, indeed, I learn'd much concerning the late encounter: but of course could answer nought. 'Twas only natural they should interpret this silence for obstinacy.
"March 'em off to Captain Stubbs!"
"Halloa!" shouted a pockmarked trooper, that had his hand thrust in on my breast: "bring the lantern close here. What's this?"
'Twas, alas! the King's letter: and I bit my lip while they cluster'd round, turning the lantern's yellow glare upon the superscription.

===Chinese===
灯笼很快点着了，举到我面前；我那副模样一定很狼狈。那些人全都穿着绿色军衣，沾满了泥污和火药。他们冲我咧嘴笑着，我恨不得踢他们。
"搜那个保王党！"一个喊道。"审问他，"另一个叫道；随即开始了一长串关于国王陛下的军队动向的盘问，从那里面我确实了解到不少关于最近那场遭遇战的情况：但当然什么也不能回答。他们把这种沉默当作顽固，也在情理之中。
"押他们去见斯塔布斯上尉（Captain Stubbs）！"
"嘿！"一个麻脸骑兵叫道，他的手已经伸进了我怀里："把灯笼拿近点。这是什么？"
哎呀，正是国王的密信！他们围着我，把灯笼的黄光打在信封上，我咬着嘴唇。

===Original===
"Lads, there's promotion in this!" shouted the thickset man I had tumbled on (who, it seem'd, was the sergeant in the troop): "hand me the letter, there! Zacchaeus Martin and Tom Pine—you two bide here on duty: t'other three fall in about the prisoners—quick march! 'The wicked have digged a pit—'"
The rogue ended up with a tag from the Psalmist.
We were march'd down the road for a mile or more, till we heard a loud bawling, as of a man in much bodily pain, and soon came to a small village, where, under a tavern lamp, by the door, was a man perch'd up on a tub, and shouting forth portions of the Scripture to some twenty or more green-coats assembled round. Our conductor pushed past these, and enter'd the tavern. At a door to the left in the passage he halted, and knocking once, thrust us inside.
The room was bare and lit very dimly by two tallow candles, set in bottles. Between these, on a deal table, lay a map outspread, and over it a man was bending, who look'd up sharply at our entrance.
He was thin, with a blue nose, and wore a green uniform like the rest: only his carriage proved him a man of authority.

===Chinese===
"弟兄们，这可有升官的机会了！"把我撞倒的那个壮汉（看样子是队里的军士）嚷道，"把信给我！撒该·马丁和汤姆·派恩——你们俩留下值勤：其他三个押犯人——快步走！'恶人挖了陷阱——'"
这个无赖还用了一段《诗篇》来收尾。
我们被押着沿大路走了一英里多，直到听见一阵大声嚎叫，像是有人疼痛难忍，不久就到了一个小村庄。旅店门外一盏灯下，一个人蹲在一只木桶上，对着围成一圈的二十多个绿衣兵高声念诵圣经。我们的押送人从他们中间挤过去，走进了旅店。在走廊左边的一扇门前停下，敲了一下，把我们推进去。
屋里空荡荡的，只有两根插在瓶子里的脂烛发出昏暗的光。中间一张松木桌上摊开一幅地图，一个人正俯身看图，我们一进去，他便猛然抬起头来。
他很瘦，鼻子发青，穿着跟其他人一样的绿军衣：但他的气派说明他是个当官的。

===Original===
This Captain Stubbs listened, you may be sure, with a bright'ning eye to the sergeant's story; and at the close fix'd an inquisitive gaze on the pair of us, turning the King's letter over and over in his hands.
"How came this in your possession?" he ask'd at length.
"That," said I, "I must decline to tell."
He hesitated a moment; then, reseating himself, broke the seal, spread the letter upon the map, and read it slowly through. For the first time I began heartily to hope that the paper contain'd nothing of moment. But the man's face was no index of this. He read it through twice, folded it away in his breast, and turn'd to the sergeant—
"Tomorrow at six in the morning we continue our march. Meanwhile keep these fellows secure. I look to you for this."

===Chinese===
这个斯塔布斯上尉听军士报告时，你可想而知，眼睛越听越亮；最后他用一种好奇的目光审视着我们两个，把国王的密信翻来覆去地看。
"这信怎么到了你手里？"他终于问道。
"这个，"我说，"恕我不能奉告。"
他犹豫了一下；然后重新坐下，拆开封口，把信摊在地图上，慢慢读了一遍。我第一次衷心希望这封信里没有什么要紧的东西。但这人的脸上看不出什么。他读了两遍，把信折好塞进胸口，然后转向军士——
"明天早上六点继续行军。在此期间，把这两个人看好了。我指望你办妥这件事。"

===Original===
The sergeant saluted and we were led out. That night we pass'd in handcuffs, huddled with fifty soldiers in a hayloft of the inn and hearkening to their curious talk, that was half composed of Holy Writ and half of gibes at our expense. They were beaten men and, like all such, found comfort in deriding the greater misfortunes of others.
Before daylight the bugles began to sound, and we were led down to the green before the tavern door, where already were close upon five hundred gather'd, that had been billeted about the village and were now forming in order of march—a soil'd, batter'd crew, with torn ensigns and little heart in their movements. The sky began a cold drizzle as we set out, and through this saddening whether we trudged all day, Delia and I being kept well apart, she with the vanguard and I in the rear, seeing only the winding column, the dejected heads bobbing in front as they bent to the slanting rain, the cottagers that came out to stare as we pass'd; and hearing but the hoarse words of command, the low mutterings of the men, and always the monotonous tramp-tramp through the slancheing rain.

===Chinese===
军士敬了个礼，我们被带了出去。那天晚上我们戴着手铐，跟五十个士兵一起挤在旅店的干草阁楼里，听着他们奇怪的谈话——一半是《圣经》经文，一半是拿我们开涮的俏皮话。他们是一群打了败仗的人，就像所有败军一样，靠嘲笑别人更大的不幸来寻求安慰。
天亮之前，军号响了，我们被押到旅店门前的草地上，那里已经聚集了将近五百人。他们是驻扎在村子里的部队，现在正列队行军——一支肮脏破烂的队伍，旗帜破破烂烂，行军无精打采。我们出发时天空开始飘起冷雨，在这种令人沮丧的天气里我们走了一整天。德利娅和我被远远隔开，她在前队，我在后队，只看见蜿蜒的队伍，前头的人在斜雨中低垂着沮丧的脑袋晃动，路旁有农舍里的人出来看着我们走过；耳边只有嘶哑的口令声，士兵低声的嘟囔，以及泥泞道路上那单调的踏——踏——声。

===Original===
'Tis like a bad dream to me, and I will not dwell on it. That night we pass'd at Chippenham—a small market town—and on the morrow went tramping again through worse weather, but always amid the same sights and sounds. There were moments when I thought to go mad, wrenching at my cords till my wrists bled, yet with no hope to escape. But in time, by good luck, my wits grew deaden'd to it all, and I march'd on with the rest to a kind of lugubrious singsong that my brain supplied. For hours I went thus, counting my steps, missing my reckoning, and beginning again.
Daylight was failing when the towers of Bristol grew clear out of the leaden mist in front; and by five o'clock we halted outside the walls and beside the ditch of the castle, waiting for the drawbridge to be let down. Already a great crowd had gather'd about us, of those who had come out to learn news of the defeat, which, the day before some fugitives had carried to Bristol. To their questions, as to all else, I listen'd like a man in a trance: and recall this only—that first I was shivering out in the rain and soon after was standing beside Delia, under guard of a dozen soldiers, and shaking with cold, beneath a gateway that led between the two wards of the castle. And there, for an hour at least, we kick'd our heels, until from the inner ward Captain Stubbs came striding and commanded us to follow.
Across the court we went in the rain, through a vaulted passage, and passing a screen of carved oak found ourselves suddenly in a great hall, near forty yards long (as I reckon it), and rafter'd with oak. At the far end, around a great marble table, were some ten or more gentlemen seated, who all with one accord turn'd their eyes upon us, as the captain brought us forward.

===Chinese===
这对我就像一场噩梦，我不愿多费笔墨。那天晚上我们在奇彭纳姆（Chippenham）——一个小镇——过夜，第二天又在更糟糕的天气里跋涉，但景色和声音始终如一。有那么几次我以为自己要疯了，拼命拉扯绳索直到手腕出血，却毫无逃脱的希望。但慢慢地，运气不错，我的神智变得麻木了，我随着队伍行进，脑子里自动响起一种凄凉的哼唱。我就这样走了几个小时，数着步数，数乱了，又从头再来。
天色渐暗时，布里斯托尔的塔楼从前方铅灰色的薄雾中清晰显现；五点钟时我们在城墙外、城堡壕沟边停下，等着放下吊桥。已经有大群人围住了我们，他们是来打听败讯的——前一天有些溃兵逃到了布里斯托尔。对于他们的问题，以及其他一切，我都像梦游一样听着：我只记得先是在雨中发抖，不久之后就和德利娅一起站在十二个士兵的看守下，在通往城堡两道内院的门楼下瑟瑟发抖。我们在那里至少等了一个小时，直到斯塔布斯上尉从内院大步走出来，命令我们跟他走。
我们在雨中穿过庭院，走过一条拱廊通道，经过一面雕花橡木屏风，突然间来到一座大厅——长约四十码（据我估计），屋顶是橡木椽子。在大厅尽头，一张巨大的大理石桌旁坐着十来位绅士，上尉把我们带过去时，他们不约而同地把目光投向了我们。

===Original===
The table before them was litter'd with maps, warrants, and papers; and some of the gentlemen had pens in their hands. But the one on whom my eyes fastened was a tall, fair soldier that sat in the centre, and held his Majesty's letter, open, in his hand: who rose and bow'd to me as I came near.
"Sir," he said, "the fortune of war having given you into our hands, you will not refuse, I hope, to answer our questions."
"Sir, I have nought to tell," answer'd I, bowing in return.
With a delicate white hand he wav'd my words aside. He had a handsome, irresolute mouth, and was, I could tell, of very different degree from the merchants and lawyers beside him.

===Chinese===
他们面前的桌上散乱着地图、令状和文书；几位绅士手里还拿着笔。但我目光紧盯着的，是坐在正中央的一个高个子、浅色头发的军人，他手里拿着国王那封拆开了的信。我走近时，他站起来向我鞠了一躬。
"先生，"他说，"战争的命运把你交到了我们手里，希望你不至于拒绝回答我们的问题。"
"先生，我无话可说，"我鞠了一躬答道。
他伸出一只纤细白皙的手，把我的话挡开了。他有一张英俊而优柔寡断的嘴，我可以看出，他与身旁那些商人和律师的身份截然不同。

===Original===
"You act under orders from the—the—"
"Antichrist," put in a snappish little fellow on his right.
"I do nothing of the sort," said I.
"Well, then, sir, from King Charles."
"I do not."
"Tush!" exclaim'd the snappish man, and then straightening himself up—"That boy with you—that fellow disguis'd as a countryman—look at his boots!—he's a Papist spy!"
"There, sir, you are wrong!"
"I saw him—I'll be sworn to his face—I saw him, a year back, at Douai, helping at the mass! I never forget faces."
"Why, what nonsense!" cried I, and burst out laughing.
"Don't mock at me, sir!" he thunder'd, bringing down his fist on the table. "I tell you the boy is a Papist!" He pointed furiously at Delia, who, now laughing also, answer'd him very demurely—
"Indeed, sir—"
"I saw you, I say."
"You are bold to make so certain of a Papist—"
"I saw you!"
"That cannot even tell maid from man!"
"What is meant by that?" asks the tall soldier, opening his eyes.
"Why, simply this, sir: I am no boy at all, but a girl!"
There was a minute, during which the little man went purple in the face, and the rest star'd at Delia in blank astonishment.
"Oh, Jack," she whisper'd in my ear, "I am so very, very sorrow: but I *cannot* wear these hateful clothes much longer."
She fac'd the company with a rosy blush.
"What say you to this?" ask'd Colonel Essex—for 'twas he—turning round on the little man.
"Say? What do I say? That the fellow is a Papist, too. I knew it from the first, and this proves it!"

===Chinese===
"你是受命于——受命于——"
"敌基督（Antichrist），"他右边一个脾气暴躁的矮个子插嘴道。
"我绝无此事，"我说。
"好吧，那么先生，是受命于查理国王。"
"也不是。"
"呸！"暴躁的小个子叫了一声，然后挺直身子——"你身边那个小子——那个装成乡下人的家伙——看看他的靴子！他是个天主教间谍！"
"先生，你弄错了！"
"我见过他——我可以指着他的脸发誓——一年前在杜埃（Douai），我见过他帮忙做弥撒！我从不会认错脸。"
"胡说八道！"我大喊一声，放声大笑。
"别嘲笑我，先生！"他一拳砸在桌上吼道，"我告诉你那小子是天主教徒！"他怒气冲冲地指着德利娅，德利娅这时也笑起来，非常矜持地回答他——
"确实，先生——"
"我看见你了，我说。"
"你居然敢对一个天主教徒如此确定——"
"我看见你了！"
"连女的男的都分不清！"
"这是什么意思？"高个子军人睁大了眼睛问道。
"很简单，先生：我根本不是什么男孩，我是个姑娘！"
沉默了一分钟，那小个子的脸涨成了紫色，其余的人都目瞪口呆地盯着德利娅。
"噢，杰克，"她在我耳边低语，"我真是非常、非常抱歉：可是我*再也不能*穿这身该死的衣服了。"
她满脸绯红地面对众人。
"对此你怎么说？"埃塞克斯上校（Colonel Essex）——正是他——转过身问那小个子。
"说什么？我说什么？那家伙也是天主教徒。我从一开始就看出来了，这下可证实了！"

## IX I Break Out of Prison / 第九章 越狱

===Original===
You are now to be ask’d to pass over the next four weeks in as many minutes: as would I had done at the time! For I spent them in a bitter cold cell in the main tower of Bristol keep, with a chair and a pallet of straw for all my furniture, and nothing to stay my fast but the bread and water that the jailer﻿—a sour man, if ever there were one﻿—brought me twice a day.

===Chinese===
诸君此刻须将接下来的四个星期压缩成几分钟读过——我那时何尝不想如此！只因这四个星期全耗在布里斯托尔主塔那间冰冷牢房里：一张椅子、一捆稻草便是全部家当，狱卒——天底下怕再没比他更阴沉的人了——每日两回送来面包和清水，除此之外再无别物果腹。

===Original===
This keep lies in the northwest corner of the outer ward of the castle﻿—a mighty tall pile and strongly built, the walls (as the jailer told me) being a full twenty-five feet thick near the foundations, though by time you ascended to the towers this thickness had dwindled to six feet and no more. In shape ’twas a quadrilateral, a little shorter from north to south than from east to west (in which latter direction it measured sixty feet, about), and had four towers standing at the four corners, whereof mine was five fathoms higher than the rest.

===Chinese===
这塔楼坐落在城堡外庭西北角——楼身极高，筑造坚固，狱卒告诉我，墙基处厚达整整二十五英尺，往上至塔顶便只剩六英尺不到了。整座塔呈四边形，南北略短于东西（东西方向约六十英尺），四角各立一座小塔，我所囚的那座比其余高出五寻。

===Original===
Guess, then, how little I thought of escape, having but one window, a hundred feet (I do believe) above the ground, and that so narrow that, even without the iron bar across it, ’twould barely let my shoulders pass. What concern’d me more was the cold that gnaw’d me continually these winter nights, as I lay thinking of Delia (whom I had not seen since our examination), or gazing out on the patch of frosty heaven that was all my view. ’Twas thus I had heard Bristol bells ringing for Christmas in the town below.

===Chinese===
由此不难想见，我对越狱这事有多不抱指望：整间牢房只有一扇窗，离地怕有百英尺之高，窗口窄得即便拆了那根铁条，我的肩膀也勉勉强强才能挤过去。更叫人难熬的是冬夜的寒气，它整夜整夜地啮噬着我，我躺在草铺上，不是思念德利娅（自从审讯之后就再没见过她），便是凝望那一小块结霜的天空——那便是我全部的风景。布里斯托尔的圣诞钟声便是这样从下方镇子里传进我耳中的。

===Original===
Colonel Essex had been thrice to visit me, and always offer’d many excuses for my treatment; but when he came to question me, why of course I had nothing to tell, so that each visit but served to vex him more. Clearly I was suspected to know a great deal beyond what appear’d in the letter: and no doubt poor Anthony Killigrew had receiv’d some verbal message from His Majesty which he lived not long enough to transmit to me. As ’twas, I kept silence; and the Colonel in return would tell me nothing of what had befallen Delia.

===Chinese===
埃塞克斯上校（Colonel Essex）来看过我三回，每回都对我的待遇百般致歉；可他一开口讯问，我自然无话可说，于是每来一次他便多恼一分。显而易见，他们怀疑我知道的远不止信上所写：可怜的安东尼·基利格鲁（Anthony Killigrew）想必还从国王陛下那里接到了什么口信，只是没来得及转达给我便已不在人世。我缄口不言，上校也从不告诉我德利娅的遭遇。

===Original===
One fine, frosty morning, then, when I had lain in this distress just four weeks, the door of my cell open’d, and there appear’d a young woman, not uncomely, bringing in my bread and water. She was the jailer’s daughter, and wore a heavy bunch of keys at her gird.

===Chinese===
如此熬了整整四个星期，在一个晴朗而霜冻的清晨，牢门开了，走进一个不算难看的年轻女子，手里端着我的面包和水。她是狱卒的女儿，腰带上挂着一大串钥匙。

===Original===
"Oh, good morning!" said I: for till now her father only had visited me, and this was a welcome change.

===Chinese===
"哦，早上好！"我说道——此前一直是她父亲来送饭，换个人来总归是件叫人高兴的事。

===Original===
Instead of answering cheerfully (as I look’d for), she gave a little nod of the head, rather sorrowful, and answered:﻿—

===Chinese===
她并未如我所期待的那样爽快答话，而是略带忧伤地点了点头，答道——

===Original===
"Father’s abed with the ague."

===Chinese===
"父亲卧病在床，发了寒热。"

===Original===
"Now you cannot expect me to be sorry."

===Chinese===
"这你可别指望我会难过。"

===Original===
"Nay," she said; and I caught her looking at me with something like compassion in her blue eyes, which mov’d me to cry out suddenly﻿—

===Chinese===
"不会的，"她说。我瞥见她那双蓝眼睛里带着几分怜悯，便脱口喊道——

===Original===
"I think you are woman enough to like a pair of lovers."

===Chinese===
"我想你是个通情达理的姑娘，总不至于反对成全一对有情人吧。"

===Original===
"Oh, aye: but where’s t’other half of the pair?"

===Chinese===
"哦，那是自然——可另一半在哪儿呢？"

===Original===
"You’re right. The young gentlewoman that was brought hither with me﻿—I know not if she loves me: but this I do know﻿—I would give my hand to learn her whereabouts, and how she fares."

===Chinese===
"你说得对。和我一同被押来的那位年轻小姐——我不知她是否爱我：但有一件事我确知——我愿拿一只手去换她的下落，哪怕只得知她过得好不好。"

===Original===
"Eat thy loaf," put in the girl very suddenly, setting down the plate and pitcher.

===Chinese===
"吃了你的面包吧，"那姑娘突兀地插了一句，放下盘子和水罐。

===Original===
’Twas odd, but I seem’d to hear a sob in her voice. However, her back was toward me as I glanc’d up. And next moment she was gone, locking the iron door behind her.

===Chinese===
说来也怪，我似乎听见她声音里带着一丝哽咽。可我抬头望去时，她已背过身去。转眼间她便出去了，随手锁上铁门。

===Original===
I turn’d from my breakfast with a sigh, having for the moment tasted the hope to hear something of Delia. But in a while, feeling hungry, I pick’d up the loaf beside me, and broke it in two.

===Chinese===
我叹了口气，搁下早餐，方才那一瞬的希望——或许能听到德利娅的消息——转眼便散了。过了一会儿，腹中饥饿，便拿起身边的面包掰成两半。

===Original===
To my amaze, out dropp’d something that jingled on the stone floor.

===Chinese===
令我大吃一惊的是，有什么东西掉出来，叮当一声落在石板地上。

===Original===
’Twas a small file: and examining the loaf again, I found a clasp-knife also, and a strip of paper, neatly folded, hidden in the bread.

===Chinese===
是一把小锉刀！我再翻检面包，又找到一把折叠小刀，以及一条叠得整整齐齐的纸条，都藏在面包里。

===Original===
> Deare Jack,
> Colonel Essex, finding no good come of his interrogatories, hath set me at large; though I continue under his eye, to wit, with a dowager of his acquaintance, a Mistress Finch. Wee dwell in a private house midway down St. Thomas his street, in Redcliffe: and she hath put a dismal dress upon me (Jack, ’tis *hideous*), but otherwise uses me not ill. But take care of thyself, my deare friend: for though the Colonel be a gentilman, he is press’d by them about him, and at our last interview I noted a mischief in his eye. Canst use this file?﻿—(but take care: all the gates I saw guarded with troopers today.) This by one who hath been my friend: for whose sake tear the paper up. And beleeve your cordial, loving comrade
> D. K.

===Chinese===
> 亲爱的杰克：
> 埃塞克斯上校见讯问无果，已将我释放，只是仍在他监视之下——暂住他一位相识的寡妇芬奇太太（Mistress Finch）家中。我们住在雷德克利夫区圣托马斯街中段的一所私宅里。她给我套了一件难看至极的衣裳（杰克，那简直*没法看*），不过旁的倒还过得去。你千万保重，我亲爱的朋友：上校虽是个绅士，却被身边人裹挟着，上次见面我注意到他眼里藏着恶意。这把锉刀你用得上吗？——（但要当心：我今天看见各道城门都有骑兵把守。）写这信的人一直是你的朋友——为了她，请将此信销毁。相信你真诚的、爱你的同伴。
> D. K.

===Original===
After reading this a dozen times, till I had it by heart, I tore the letter into small pieces and hid them in my pocket. This done, I felt lighter-hearted than for many a day, and (rather for employment than with any farther view) began lazily to rub away at my window bar. The file work’d well. By noon the bar was half sever’d, and I broke off to whistle a tune. ’Twas﻿—

===Chinese===
我把信读了十几遍，直到倒背如流，才将它撕成碎片藏进口袋。做完这些，我心头久违地轻松起来，便——与其说有什么打算，不如说是为了打发时间——懒洋洋地锉起窗上的铁条。锉刀很好使。到中午时铁条已断了一半，我停下来吹了支小曲。曲子是——

===Original===
> "Vivre en tout cas
>  C’est le grand soulas"﻿—

===Chinese===
> "无论如何活着
>  这便是莫大的慰藉"——

===Original===
and I broke off to hear the key turning in my lock.

===Chinese===
正吹着，忽听钥匙在锁孔里转动，我便住了口。

===Original===
The jailer’s daughter enter’d with my second meal. Her eyes were red with weeping.

===Chinese===
狱卒的女儿端着第二顿饭进来了。她双眼哭得通红。

===Original===
Said I, "Does your father beat you?"

===Chinese===
我问："你父亲打你了？"

===Original===
"He has, before now," she replied: "but not today."

===Chinese===
"以前是打过，"她答道，"不过今天没有。"

===Original===
"Then why do you weep?"

===Chinese===
"那你为什么哭？"

===Original===
"Not for that."

===Chinese===
"不是为了这个。"

===Original===
"For what then?"

===Chinese===
"那为了什么？"

===Original===
"For you﻿—oh, dear, dear! How shall I tell it? They are going to﻿—to﻿—" She sat down on the chair, and sobb’d in her apron.

===Chinese===
"为了你——哦，天哪，天哪！我该怎么说？他们要——要——"她一屁股坐到椅子上，用围裙捂着脸抽泣。

===Original===
"What is’t they are going to do?"

===Chinese===
"他们要干什么？"

===Original===
"To﻿—to﻿—h‑hang you."

===Chinese===
"要——要——绞——绞死你。"

===Original===
"The devil! When?"

===Chinese===
"见鬼！什么时候？"

===Original===
"Tut‑tut‑tomorrow mo‑horning!"

===Chinese===
"明——明天早——早上！"

===Original===
I went suddenly very cold all over. There was silence for a moment, and then I heard the noise of someone dropping a plank in the courtyard below.

===Chinese===
我浑身骤然冰冷。沉默了片刻，我听见下面院子里有人在放木板的声响。

===Original===
"What’s that?"

===Chinese===
"那是什么？"

===Original===
"The gug‑gug﻿—"

===Chinese===
"绞——绞架——"

===Original===
"Gallows?"

===Chinese===
"绞架？"

===Original===
She nodded.

===Chinese===
她点了点头。

===Original===
"You are but a weak girl," said I, meditating.

===Chinese===
"你不过是个弱女子，"我思忖着说。

===Original===
"Aye: but there’s a dozen troopers on the landing below."

===Chinese===
"是啊：可楼下楼梯口还有十几个骑兵呢。"

===Original===
"Then, my dear, you must lock me up," I decided gloomily, and fell to whistling﻿—

===Chinese===
"那么，亲爱的，你只好把我锁起来了，"我阴郁地做了决定，随即又吹起口哨——

===Original===
> "Vivre en tout cas
>  C’est le grand soulas"﻿—

===Chinese===
> "无论如何活着
>  这便是莫大的慰藉"——

===Original===
A workman’s hammer in the court below chim’d in, beating out the tune, and driving the moral home. I heard a low sob behind me. The jailer’s daughter was going.

===Chinese===
下面院子里一个工匠的锤声应和着，一下一下敲出曲调，仿佛在为这歌词配注脚。身后传来一声低低的抽泣。狱卒的女儿要走了。

===Original===
"Lend me your bodkin, my dear, for a memento."

===Chinese===
"把你的簪子借我做个念想吧，亲爱的。"

===Original===
She pull’d it out and gave it to me.

===Chinese===
她拔下簪子递给我。

===Original===
"Thank you, and now goodbye! Stop: here’s a kiss to take to my dear mistress. They shan’t hang me, my dear."

===Chinese===
"多谢你，再见了！等一下——这一吻请你转交给我的好小姐。他们休想绞死我，亲爱的。"

===Original===
The girl went out, sobbing, and lock’d the door after her.

===Chinese===
那姑娘哭着出去了，随手锁上了门。

===Original===
I sat down for a while, feeling doleful. For I found myself extremely young to be hang’d. But soon the *whang﻿—whang!* of the hammer below rous’d me. "Come," I thought, "I’ll see what that rascal is doing, at any rate," and pulling the file from my pocket, began to attack the window bar with a will. I had no need for silence, at this great height above the ground: and besides, the hammering continued lustily.

===Chinese===
我坐了一会儿，满心凄凉——毕竟年纪轻轻就要被绞死，未免太不值了。可下面那"砰——砰——！"的锤声很快把我惊醒。"好吧，"我心想，"无论如何我得看看那家伙在捣什么鬼。"我从口袋里掏出锉刀，铆足了劲继续对付那根铁条。在这离地百尺的高处，完全不必顾忌声响——何况底下锤声正起劲呢。

===Original===
Daylight was closing as I finish’d my task and, pulling the two pieces of the bar aside, thrust my head out at the window.

===Chinese===
完工时天色已暗，我把断成两截的铁条拨到一旁，将头探出窗外。

===Original===
Directly under me, and about twenty feet from the ground, I saw a beam projecting, about six feet long, over a sort of doorway in the wall. Under this beam, on a ladder, was a carpenter fellow at work, fortifying it with two supporting timbers that rested on the sill of the doorway. He was merry enough over the job, and paused every now and again to fling a remark to a little group of soldiers that stood idling below, where the fellow’s workbag and a great coil of rope rested by the ladder’s foot.

===Chinese===
正下方离地约二十英尺处，有一根约六英尺长的横梁从墙上一扇门洞上方伸出来。一个木匠正站在梯子上忙着给横梁加两根撑木，撑木搁在门洞的石槛上。他干得挺来劲，不时停下来冲下面闲站着的几个士兵说笑几句——他的工具袋和一大盘绳子就搁在梯脚边。

===Original===
"Reckon, Sammy," said one, pulling a long tobacco pipe from his mouth and spitting, " ’tis a long while since thy last job o’ the sort."

===Chinese===
"我说，萨米，"一个士兵从嘴里拔出长烟斗，吐了口唾沫，"你上回干这活儿怕是有年头了吧。"

===Original===
"Aye, lad: terrible disrepair this place has fall’n into. But send us a cheerful heart, say I! Instead o’ the viper an’ owl, shall henceforward be hangings of men an’ all manner o’ diversion."

===Chinese===
"可不是嘛，伙计：这地方破败得不成样子了。不过我说，打起精神来！往后这儿可没有蛇和猫头鹰看了，换成了吊死人，保准比什么都有趣。"

===Original===
I kept my head out of sight and listen’d.

===Chinese===
我把头缩回暗处，继续听着。

===Original===
"What time doth ’a swing?" ask’d another of the soldiers.

===Chinese===
"几点开绞？"另一个士兵问。

===Original===
"I heard the Colonel give orders for nine o’clock tomorrow," answer’d the first soldier, spitting again.

===Chinese===
"我听上校下令，明天九点，"第一个士兵又吐了口唾沫，答道。

===Original===
The clock over the barbican struck four: and in a minute was being answer’d from tower after tower, down in the city.

===Chinese===
城堡门楼上钟敲了四点：片刻之后，城中塔楼的钟声便此起彼伏地应和起来。

===Original===
"Four o’clock!" cried the man on the ladder: "time to stop work, and here goes for the last nail!" He drove it in and prepar’d to descend.

===Chinese===
"四点了！"梯子上那木匠叫道，"该收工了，钉上最后一颗钉子走人！"他把钉子敲进去，准备下来。

===Original===
"Hi!" shouted a soldier, "you’ve forgot the rope."

===Chinese===
"喂！"一个士兵喊道，"你把绳子忘了。"

===Original===
"That’ll wait till tomorrow. There’s a staple to drive in, too. I tell you I’m dry, and want my beer."

===Chinese===
"明天再说。还有个铁环要打呢。实话说我嗓子都冒烟了，得喝杯啤酒去。"

===Original===
He whipp’d his apron round his waist, and gathering up his nails, went down the ladder. At the foot he pick’d up his bag, shoulder’d the ladder, and loung’d away, leaving the coil of rope lying there. Presently the soldiers saunter’d off also, and the court was empty.

===Chinese===
他把围裙往腰上一缠，拾起钉子，走下梯子。到了梯脚，他拎起工具袋，扛起梯子，晃晃悠悠地走了，那盘绳子就丢在原地。不一会儿士兵们也闲逛散去，院子空了。

===Original===
Now up to this moment I had but one idea of avoiding my fate, and that was to kill myself. ’Twas to this end I had borrow’d the bodkin of the maid. Afterward I had a notion of flinging myself from the window as they came for me. But now, as I look’d down on that coil of rope lying directly below, a prettier scheme struck me. I sat down on the floor of my cell and pull’d off my boots and stockings.

===Chinese===
在此之前，我一心想着的逃避厄运的法子不过是一死了之——向那姑娘借簪子便是为此。后来又想过等他们来提人时纵身跳窗。但此刻，当我低头看见那盘绳子就扔在正下方，一个更好的主意忽然冒了出来。我在牢房地板上坐下，脱掉靴子和袜子。

===Original===
’Twas such a pretty plan that I got into a fever of impatience. Drawing off a stocking and picking out the end of the yarn, I began to unravel the knitting for dear life, until the whole lay, a heap of thread, on the floor. I then serv’d the other in the same way: and at the end had two lines, each pretty near four hundred yards in length: which now I divided into eight lines of about a hundred yards each.

===Chinese===
这主意实在太妙，我急得坐立不安。我扯下一只袜子，找到线头，拼命往下拆线，直到整只袜子变成地板上一堆毛线。另一只也照此办理：最终我得到两根各近四百码长的线——再把它们分成八股，每股约一百码。

===Original===
With these I set to work, and by the end of twenty minutes had plaited a rope﻿—if rope, indeed, it could be called﻿—weak to be sure, but long enough to reach the ground with plenty to spare. Then, having bent my bodkin to the form of a hook, I tied it to the end of my cord, weighted it with a crown from my pocket, and clamber’d up to the window. I was going to angle for the hangman’s rope.

===Chinese===
我动手编绳，二十分钟后便搓出一根——姑且叫它绳子吧——虽然肯定不结实，但长度绰绰有余，够垂到地面还有余。然后我把簪子弯成钩状，系在绳头，从口袋里摸出一枚克朗当坠子，爬到窗口。我要去钓那刽子手的绳索了。

===Original===
’Twas near dark by this; but I could just distinguish it on the paving stones below, and looking about the court, saw that no one was astir. I wriggled first my head, then a shoulder, through the opening, and let the line run gently through my hand. There was still many yards left, that could be paid out, when I heard my coin tinkle softly on the pavement.

===Chinese===
此时天色已近全黑；但我还能勉强辨认出地上的绳盘，四下张望，院中无人走动。我先把头，再把一个肩膀挤过窗洞，让绳子从手中缓缓滑出。还有好几码余量时，我听见坠子轻轻叮当落在石板路上。

===Original===
Then began my difficulty. A dozen times I pull’d my hook across the coil before it hitch’d; and then a full three score of times the rope slipped away before I had rais’d it a dozen yards. My elbow was raw, almost, with leaning on the sill, and I began to lose heart and head, when, to my delight, the bodkin caught and held. It had fasten’d on a kink in the rope, not far from the end. I began to pull up, hand over hand, trembling all the while like a leaf.

===Chinese===
真正的难处来了。我的钩子在绳盘上刮了十几回才钩住一次；好不容易钩住了，又足足有六七十回绳子在拉起不到十二英尺时滑脱。我的胳膊肘在窗槛上磨得几乎破了皮，正当我心灰意冷之际，簪子终于咬住不放了——它钩住了绳头附近的一个绳结。我双手交替往上拉，浑身抖得像片树叶。

===Original===
For I had two very reasonable fears. First, the rope might slip away and tumble before it reach’d my grasp. Secondly, it might, after all, prove a deal too short. It had look’d to me a new rope of many fathoms, not yet cut for tomorrow’s purpose; but eyesight might well deceive at that distance, and surely enough I saw that the whole was dangling off the ground long before it came to my hand.

===Chinese===
因为我有两重十足合理的担忧：其一，绳子可能在到手之前滑脱坠地；其二，它或许根本不够长。在我眼里那是一根崭新的多寻长绳，还没为明天的事剪断过；但隔着这么远，眼力难免出错——果然，我看见整根绳子离地还老远就没影了，远在我够得着之前。

===Original===
But at last I caught it, and slipping back into the room, pull’d it after me, yard upon yard. My heart went loud and fast. There was nothing to fasten it to but an iron staple in the door, that meant losing the width of my cell, some six feet. This, however, must be risk’d, and I made the end fast, lower’d the other out of window again, and climbing to a sitting posture on the window sill, thrust out my legs over the gulf.

===Chinese===
但终于还是够着了。我缩回房间，一码一码地把绳子拽进来。心跳得又响又急。牢房里唯一能系绳子的东西是门上的铁环，这意味着要白搭上整个牢房的宽度——约六英尺。不过顾不得那么多了。我把绳头系牢，将另一头重新垂出窗外，在窗台上坐好，把两条腿伸向深渊。

===Original===
Thankful was I that darkness had fallen before this, and hidden the giddy depths below me. I gripp’d the rope and push’d myself inch by inch through the window, and out over the ledge. For a moment I dangled, without courage to move a hand. Then, wreathing my legs round the rope, I loosed my left hand, and caught with it again some six inches lower. And so, down I went.

===Chinese===
谢天谢地，黑暗此时已经降临，遮住了脚下令人眩晕的深渊。我抓住绳子，一寸一寸地把自己推出窗外，悬在岩台之上。有那么一瞬，我吊在那里，连动一根手指的勇气都没有。随后我两腿盘住绳子，松开左手，在下方约六英寸处重新抓牢，就这样一寸一寸地往下滑。

===Original===
Minute follow’d minute, and left me still descending, six inches at a time, and looking neither above nor below, but always at the grey wall that seem’d sliding up in front of me. The first dizziness was over, but a horrible aching of the arms had taken the place of it. ’Twas growing intolerable, when suddenly my legs, that sought to close round the rope, found space only. I had come to the end.

===Chinese===
一分钟接一分钟过去，我仍在往下挪，每次六英寸，既不敢看上方也不敢看下方，只死死盯着眼前似乎在向上滑动的灰墙。起初的眩晕过去了，取而代之的是双臂可怕的酸痛，正越来越难以忍受时，忽然两腿想盘住绳子，却只盘住了空气——到底了。

===Original===
I look’d down. A yard below my feet the beam of the gallows gleam’d palely out of the darkness. Here was my chance. I let my hands slip down the last foot or so of rope, hung for a moment, then dropp’d for the beam.

===Chinese===
我往下望去。脚下约一码处，绞架的横梁在黑暗中泛着惨白的光。机会来了。我让双手沿最后一英尺左右的绳子滑下去，悬了片刻，然后纵身朝横梁扑去。

===Original===
My feet miss’d it, as I intended they should; but I flung both arms out and caught it, bringing myself up with a jerk. While yet I hung clawing, I heard a footstep coming through the gateway between the two wards.

===Chinese===
我的脚故意没踩中横梁，双臂却猛扑过去紧紧抱住，猛地把自己拽了上去。我正悬在上面攀抓着，忽听两道庭院之间的门道里传来脚步声。

===Original===
Here was a fix. With all speed and silence I drew myself up to the beam, found a hold with one knee upon it, got astride, and lay down at length, flattening my body down against the timber. Yet all the while I felt sure I must have been heard.

===Chinese===
这下可麻烦了。我飞快而无声地爬上横梁，单膝跪稳，骑了上去，再慢慢趴平，把整个身子紧贴在木梁上。可我总觉得自己一定被听见了。

===Original===
The footsteps drew nearer, and pass’d almost under the gallows. ’Twas an officer, for, as he pass’d, he called out﻿—

===Chinese===
脚步声越来越近，几乎从绞架正下方走过。那是个军官，因为他一边走一边喊道——

===Original===
"Sergeant Downs! Sergeant Downs!"

===Chinese===
"唐斯中士！唐斯中士！"

===Original===
A voice from the guardroom in the barbican answer’d him through the darkness.

===Chinese===
门楼卫兵室里一个声音在黑暗中应答。

===Original===
"Why is not the watch set?"

===Chinese===
"为什么还没换岗？"

===Original===
"In a minute, sir: it wants a minute to six."

===Chinese===
"马上就好，长官：离六点还差一分钟。"

===Original===
"I thought the Colonel order’d it at half past five?"

===Chinese===
"我以为上校下令五点半换岗？"

===Original===
In the silence that follow’d, the barbican clock began to strike, and half a dozen troopers tumbled out from the guardroom, some laughing, some grumbling at the coldness of the night. The officer return’d to the inner ward as they dispersed to their posts: and soon there was silence again, save for the *tramp-tramp* of a sentry crossing and recrossing the pavement below me.

===Chinese===
话音落处一阵沉默，门楼的钟开始敲响，六七个骑兵从卫兵室涌出来，有的笑着，有的抱怨夜里太冷。军官折回内庭，他们各自散去上岗：很快四下又恢复了寂静，只有脚下一个哨兵来回踱步的"踏——踏——"声。

===Original===
All this while I lay flatten’d along the beam, scarce daring to breathe. But at length, when the man had pass’d below for the sixth time, I found heart to wriggle myself toward the doorway over which the gallows protruded. By slow degrees, and pausing whenever the fellow drew near, I crept close up to the wall: then, waiting the proper moment, cast my legs over, dangled for a second or two swinging myself toward the sill, flung myself off, and, touching the ledge with one toe, pitch’d forward in the room.

===Chinese===
我始终趴在横梁上，几乎不敢呼吸。终于，当那人在下面第六次走过时，我鼓起勇气朝绞架伸出的那扇门洞方向蠕动过去。一点点挪，那人一靠近我就停，我终于贴近了墙壁：然后瞅准时机，把腿甩过去，荡了一两下朝窗台靠去，纵身一跳，脚尖点在岩台上，扑进了房间。

===Original===
The effect of this was to give me a sound crack as I struck the flooring, which lay about a foot below the level of the sill. I pick’d myself up and listen’d. Outside, the regular tramp of the sentry prov’d he had not heard me; and I drew a long breath, for I knew that without a lantern he would never spy, in the darkness, the telltale rope dangling from the tower.

===Chinese===
落地时结结实实摔了一跤——地板比窗台低了约一英尺。我爬起来侧耳倾听。外面哨兵的脚步声规律如常，说明他没有听见；我长舒一口气，因为我知道他没有灯笼，绝不可能在黑暗中看见那根从塔上垂下的泄密绳索。

===Original===
In the room where I stood all was right. But the flooring was uneven to the foot, and scatter’d with small pieces of masonry. ’Twas one of the many chambers in the castle that had dropp’d into disrepair. Groping my way with both hands, and barking my shins on the loose stones, I found a low vaulted passage that led me into a second chamber, empty as the first. To my delight, the door of this was ajar, with a glimmer of light sliting through the crack. I made straight toward it, and pull’d the door softly. It open’d, and show’d a lantern dimly burning, and the staircase of the keep winding past me, up into darkness.

===Chinese===
我所在的这间屋子倒还完好，但脚下地面凹凸不平，散落着小块碎石。这是城堡里许多失修房间中的一间。我双手摸索着往前走，胫骨在松动的石头上磕得生疼，终于摸到一条低矮的拱顶通道，通向另一间空屋子。令我欣喜的是，这间屋子的门虚掩着，门缝里透出一丝微光。我径直走过去，轻轻拉门。门开了，里面点着一盏昏暗的提灯，塔楼的旋梯从我身旁蜿蜒向上，没入黑暗。

===Original===
My chance was, of course, to descend: which I did on tiptoe, hearing no sound. The stairs twisted down and down, and ended by a stout door with another lamp shining above it. After listening a moment I decided to be bold, and lifted the latch. A faint cry saluted me.

===Chinese===
我的出路当然是往下走。我踮着脚尖往下走，没有听到任何声响。旋梯弯弯曲曲一路向下，尽头是一扇厚实的木门，门上方另一盏灯亮着。我侧耳听了一会儿，决定放手一搏，推开了门闩。一声低低的惊叫迎面而来。

===Original===
I stood face to face with the jailer’s daughter.

===Chinese===
我与狱卒的女儿面对面站着。

===Original===
The room was a small one, well lit, and lin’d about the walls with cups and bottles. ’Twas, as I guess’d, a taproom for the soldiers: and the girl had been scouring one of the pewter mugs when my entrance startled her. She stood up, white as if painted, and gasp’d﻿—

===Chinese===
这是一间不大的屋子，灯火通明，四壁摆满了杯瓶。依我猜，这是士兵们的酒馆：那姑娘刚才正在擦一只锡镴酒杯，我的闯入把她吓了一跳。她站起身来，脸白得像涂了粉，倒吸一口气——

===Original===
"Quick﻿—quick! Down here behind the counter for your life!"

===Chinese===
"快——快！钻到柜台后面去，快！"

===Original===
There was scarce time to drop on my knees before a couple of troopers loung’d in, demanding mull’d beer. The girl bustled about to serve them, while the pair lean’d their elbows on the counter, and in this easy attitude began to chat.

===Chinese===
我刚来得及跪倒在柜台后面，两个骑兵就晃进来要热啤酒。姑娘忙着招呼他们，两人把胳膊肘支在柜台上，就这么闲聊起来。

===Original===
"A shrewd night!"

===Chinese===
"今晚可真冷！"

===Original===
"Aye, a very freezing frost! Lucky that soldiering is not all sentry work, or I for one ’ud ensue my natural trade o’ plumbing. But let’s be cheerful: for the voice o’ the turtle is heard i’ the land."

===Chinese===
"可不是，冻死人了！幸亏当兵不全是站岗，不然我可得回去重操水管工的旧业了。不过咱们高兴点——田间的斑鸠已经叫了嘛。"

===Original===
"Hey?"

===Chinese===
"嗯？"

===Original===
The man took a pull at his hot beer before explaining.

===Chinese===
那人先灌了口热啤酒，才接着说。

===Original===
"The turtle signifieth the Earl o’ Stamford, that is tonight visiting Colonel Essex in secret: an’ this is the import﻿—war, bloody war. Mark me."

===Chinese===
"田间的斑鸠就是指斯坦福德伯爵（Earl of Stamford），他今晚秘密拜访埃塞克斯上校——意思就是：要打仗了，血腥的战争。记住我的话。"

===Original===
"Stirring, striving times!"

===Chinese===
"风起云涌的年头啊！"

===Original===
"You may say so! ’A hath fifteen thousand men, the Earl, no farther off than Taunton﻿—why, my dear, how pale you look, to be sure!"

===Chinese===
"可不是嘛！伯爵手下一万五千人，就驻在汤顿附近——哎呀，我的小姐，你脸色怎么这么白！"

===Original===
" ’Tis my head that aches," answer’d the girl.

===Chinese===
"是我头疼，"姑娘答道。

===Original===
The men finish’d their drink, and saunter’d out. I crept from under the counter, and look’d at her.

===Chinese===
两人喝完酒，晃晃悠悠地出去了。我从柜台下爬出来，看着她。

===Original===
"Father’ll kill me for this!"

===Chinese===
"父亲会打死我的！"

===Original===
"Then you shall say﻿—Is it forward or back I must go?"

===Chinese===
"那你说——我是往前走还是退回去？"

===Original===
"She pull’d up a trap close beside her feet, and pointed out a ladder leading down to the darkness. "The courts are full of troopers," she added.

===Chinese===
她掀起脚边一块活板门，露出一架通向黑暗的梯子。"院子里全是骑兵，"她又补了一句。

===Original===
"The cellar?"

===Chinese===
"地窖？"

===Original===
She nodded.

===Chinese===
她点了点头。

===Original===
"Quick! There’s a door at the far end. It leads to the crypt of St. John’s Chapel. You’ll find the key beside it, and a lantern. Here is flint and steel." She reach’d them down from a shelf beside her. "Crouch down, or they’ll spy you through the window. From the crypt a passage takes you to the governor’s house. How to escape then, God knows! ’Tis the best I can think on."

===Chinese===
"快！地窖尽头有一扇门，通向圣约翰礼拜堂的地穴。钥匙和提灯都在门边。这是火石和火镰。"她从身旁架子上够下来递给我。"蹲低些，别让他们从窗户看见你。从地穴有一条通道通往总督府。之后怎么逃，只有天知道了——这是我能想到的最好法子。"

===Original===
I thank’d her, and began to step down the ladder. She stood for a moment to watch, leaving the trap open for better light. Between the avenue of casks and bins I stumbled toward the door and lantern that were just to be discern’d at the far end of the cellar. As I struck steel on flint, I heard the trap close: and since then have never set eyes on that kindhearted girl.

===Chinese===
我谢过她，开始踏下梯子。她站在上面看了一会儿，活板门敞着好照些亮。我在两排酒桶和货箱之间磕磕绊绊地朝地窖尽头走去，门和提灯隐约可辨。我打出火镰点灯时，听见活板门合上了——自那以后，我再也没见过这位好心的姑娘。

===Original===
The lantern lit, I took the key and fitted it to the lock. It turned noisily, and a cold whiff of air struck my face. Gazing round this new chamber, I saw two lines of squat pillars, supporting a low arch’d roof. ’Twas the crypt beneath the chapel, and smelt vilely. A green moisture trickled down the pillars, and dripp’d on the tombs beneath them.

===Chinese===
灯点着后，我拿起钥匙插进锁孔。钥匙转动时嘎嘎作响，一股阴冷的风扑面而来。我环顾这新进入的空间，只见两排矮墩墩的柱子撑着低矮的拱顶。这就是礼拜堂下面的地穴了，气味恶臭难闻。绿色的潮气顺着柱子往下淌，滴落在下方的墓棺上。

===Original===
At the end of this dreary place was a broken door, consisting only of a plank or two, that I easily pull’d away: and beyond, a narrow passage, over which I heard the tread of troopers plainly, as they pac’d to and fro; also the muffled note of the clock, sounding seven.

===Chinese===
这阴森地方的尽头是一扇破门，不过一两块木板拼成，我轻轻一拉便开了：门外是一条窄通道，头顶传来骑兵来回踱步的清晰脚步声，还有沉闷的钟声——报了七点。

===Original===
The passage went fairly straight, but was block’d here and there with fallen stones, over which I scrambled as best I could. And then, suddenly I was near pitching down a short flight of steps. I held the lantern aloft and look’d.

===Chinese===
通道大致笔直，但不时被落下的石块堵住，我只能尽量翻过去。忽然，我差点一头栽下一段短短的台阶。我举起提灯照看。

===Original===
At the steps’ foot widen’d out a low room, whereof the ceiling, like that of the crypt, rested on pillars. Between these, every inch of space was pil’d with barrels, chests, and great pyramids of round shot. In each corner lay a heap of rusty pikes. Of all this the signification was clear. I stood in the munition room of the Castle.

===Chinese===
台阶脚下是一个低矮的房间，天花板和地穴一样由柱子支撑。柱子之间每一寸空地都堆满了木桶、箱子和成金字塔形的圆形炮弹。每个角落里都堆着一垛锈迹斑斑的长矛。这一切的含义不言自明：我站在城堡的军火库里。

===Original===
But what chiefly took my notice was a great door, studded with iron nails, that barr’d all exit from the place. Over the barrels I crept toward it, keeping the lantern high, in dread of firing any loose powder. ’Twas fast lock’d.

===Chinese===
但最引我注意的是一扇钉满铁铆钉的大门，堵住了此处唯一的出口。我踩着木桶朝它爬去，把提灯举得高高的，生怕引燃散落的火药。门锁得很死。

===Original===
I think that, for a moment or two, I could have wept. But in a while the thought struck me that with the knife in my pocket ’twas possible to cut away the wood around the lock. "Courage!" said I: and pulling it forth, knelt down to work.

===Chinese===
我想，那一两秒钟里我简直要哭出来了。但转念一想，口袋里还有把小刀，或许能把锁周围的木头剜掉。"鼓起勇气！"我对自己说，掏出刀跪下来动手。

===Original===
Luck in life has always used me better than my deserts. At an hour’s end there I was, hacking away steadily, yet had made but little progress. And then, pressing the knife deep, I broke the blade off short. The door upon the far side was cas’d with iron.

===Chinese===
我这辈子的运气总比我应得的好。整整一个小时后，我还在一刀一刀地锯，进展却微乎其微。接着，我用力一按，刀刃"啪"地断了——门的另一面包着铁皮。

===Original===
*Tramp﻿—tramp!*

===Chinese===
*踏——踏——！*

===Original===
’Twas the sound of man’s footfall, and to the ear appear’d to be descending a flight of steps on the other side of the door. I bent my ear to the keyhole: then stepp’d to a cask of bullets that stood handy by. I took out a dozen, felt in my pocket for Delia’s kerchief that she had given me, caught up a pike from the pile stack’d in the corner, and softly blowing out my light, stood back to be conceal’d by the door, when it open’d.

===Chinese===
是人的脚步声，听起来像是在门另一边的台阶上往下走。我把耳朵凑近锁眼：然后走到旁边一桶子弹前，摸出十几发，从口袋里掏出德利娅给我的手帕，从角落里那垛长矛中抽出一支，轻轻吹灭灯火，退到门后藏好，等门开。

===Original===
The footsteps still descended. I heard an aged voice muttering﻿—

===Chinese===
脚步声仍在往下走。一个苍老的声音喃喃抱怨——

===Original===
"Shrivel my bones﻿—ugh!﻿—ugh! Wintry work﻿—wintry work! Here’s an hour to send a grandfatherly man a-groping for a keg o’ powder!"

===Chinese===
"冻死我这把老骨头了——呃！呃！大冷天的——大冷天的！偏偏叫我这么个上了年纪的人来摸黑找火药桶！"

===Original===
A wheezy cough clos’d the sentence, as a key was with difficulty fitted in the lock.

===Chinese===
一阵气喘吁吁的咳嗽结束了这句话，同时钥匙费劲地插进了锁孔。

===Original===
"Ugh﻿—ugh! Sure, the lock an’ I be a pair, for stiff joints."

===Chinese===
"呃——呃！这锁跟我倒真是一对儿——都关节僵硬。"

===Original===
The door creak’d back against me, and a shaft of light pierc’d the darkness.

===Chinese===
门嘎吱一声朝我这边开了，一道光柱刺破黑暗。

===Original===
Within the threshold, with his back to me, stood a grey-bearded servant, and totter’d so that the lantern shook in his hand. It sham’d me to lift a pike against one so weak. Instead, I dropp’d it with a clatter, and leap’d forward. The old fellow jumped like a boy, turn’d, and fac’d me with dropp’d jaw, which gave me an opportunity to thrust four or five bullets, not over roughly, into his mouth. Then, having turn’d him on his back, I strapp’d Delia’s kerchief tight across his mouth, and took the lantern from his hand.

===Chinese===
门槛里站着一个灰白胡须的仆人，背对着我，身子摇摇晃晃，提灯在他手里直颤。对这样一个衰弱之人举起长矛，实在有愧。我索性把长矛"哗啦"一扔，猛扑上前。老头像孩子一样跳了起来，转过身，张大了嘴瞪着我——我趁机不怎么粗暴地往他嘴里塞了四五颗子弹。接着，我把他翻了个仰面朝天，用德利娅的手帕紧紧勒住他的嘴，从他手里拿过提灯。

===Original===
Not a word was said. Sure, the poor old man’s wits were shaken, for he lay meek as a mouse, and star’d up at me, while I unstrapp’d his belt and bound his feet with it. His hands I truss’d up behind him with his own neckcloth; and catching up the lantern, left him there. I lock’d the door after me, and slip’d the key into my pocket as I sprang up the stairs beyond.

===Chinese===
他一声没吭。可怜的老头准是吓傻了，温顺得像只老鼠，仰面瞪着我。我解下他的皮带捆住他的脚，又用他自己的围巾反绑了他的双手。提起提灯，把他丢在原地。我锁上门，把钥匙揣进口袋，纵身跳上通往上方的楼梯。

===Original===
But here a light was shining, so once more I extinguish’d my lantern. The steps ended in a long passage, with a handsome lamp hanging at the uttermost end, and beneath this lamp I stepp’d into a place that fill’d me with astonishment.

===Chinese===
但这里有灯光，于是我再次吹灭提灯。楼梯尽头是一条长长的走廊，尽头挂着一盏考究的灯，我走到灯下，眼前出现一处令我大吃一惊的地方。

===Original===
’Twas, I could not doubt, the entrance hall of the governor’s house. An oak door, very massive, fronted me; to left and right were two smaller doors, that plainly led into apartments of the house. Also to my left, and nigher than the door on that side, ran up a broad staircase, carpeted and brightly lit all the way, so that a very blaze fell on me as I stood. Under the first flight, close to my left shoulder, was a line of pegs with many cloaks and hats depending therefrom. Underfoot, I remember, the hall was richly tiled in squares of red and white marble.

===Chinese===
毫无疑问，这是总督府的门厅。正对面是一扇厚重的橡木大门；左右各有一扇小门，显然是通往府内各房间的。左手边、比那扇小门更近处，有一道宽阔的楼梯向上延伸，铺着地毯，一路灯火通明，我站的地方简直被照得雪亮。楼梯第一段下方、紧靠我左肩处，有一排挂钩，挂着许多斗篷和帽子。脚下的地面，我记得，是红白大理石方砖铺就的，十分华贵。

===Original===
Now clearly, this was a certain place wherein to be caught. "But," thought I, "behind one of the two doors, to left or to right, must lie the governor’s room of business; and in that room﻿—as likely as not﻿—his keys." Which door, then, should I choose? For to stay here was madness.

===Chinese===
这地方明摆着是个一来就抓个正着的所在。"不过，"我暗忖，"左边或右边那两扇门背后，必有一间是总督的书房；而那书房里——十有八九——放着他的钥匙。"该选哪扇门呢？在这儿傻站可不是办法。

===Original===
While I stood pondering, the doubt was answer’d for me. From behind the right-hand door came a burst of laughter and clinking of glasses, on top of which a man’s voice﻿—the voice of Colonel Essex﻿—call’d out for more wine.

===Chinese===
正当我踌躇时，右边那扇门替我做了选择——门后爆发出一阵笑声和碰杯声，紧接着一个男人的声音——埃塞克斯上校的声音——嚷着要添酒。

===Original===
I took a step to the door on the left, paus’d for a second or two with my hand on the latch, and then cautiously push’d it open. The chamber was empty.

===Chinese===
我向左迈了一步，手搭在门闩上停了一两秒，然后小心翼翼地推开门。屋里没人。

===Original===
’Twas a long room, with a light burning on a square centre table, and around it a mass of books, loose papers and documents strewn, seemingly without order. The floor too was litter’d with them. Clearly this was the Colonel’s office.

===Chinese===
这是一间长条形的房间，正中一张方桌上点着灯，四周堆满了书，散落着成堆的文件和卷宗，似乎毫无条理。地上也到处都是。这显然是上校的办公室。

===Original===
I gave a rapid glance around. The lamp’s scarce illumin’d the far corners; but in one of these stood a great leathern screen, and over the fireplace near it a rack was hanging, full of swords, pistols, and walking canes. Stepping toward it I caught sight of Anthony’s sword, suspended there amongst the rest (they had taken it from me on the day of my examination); which now I took down and strapp’d at my side. I then chose out a pistol or two, slipped them into my sash, and advanced to the centre table.

===Chinese===
我迅速扫了一眼。灯光几乎照不到远处的角落；但其中一个角落立着一面巨大的皮屏风，旁边的壁炉上方挂着一个架子，上面插满了剑、手枪和手杖。我朝它走去，一眼看见了安东尼的剑——他们在我受审那天从我身上搜走的——和其他武器挂在一起。我把它摘下来挎在腰间，又挑了两三把手枪别进腰带，走到方桌前。

===Original===
Under the lamplight lay His Majesty’s letter, open.

===Chinese===
灯光下摊着国王陛下的信，已经拆开了。

===Original===
My hand was stretch’d out to catch it up, when I heard across the hall a door open’d, and the sound of men’s voices. They were coming toward the office.

===Chinese===
我伸手正要去抓那封信，忽听门厅那边一扇门开了，传来男人的说话声。他们正朝办公室走来。

===Original===
There was scarce time to slip back, and hide behind the screen, before the door latch was lifted, and two men enter’d, laughing yet.

===Chinese===
我刚来得及溜回去藏到屏风后面，门闩就被抬起，两个男人说笑着走了进来。

===Original===
"Business, my lord﻿—business," said the first (’twas Colonel Essex): "I have much to do tonight."

===Chinese===
"谈正事吧，阁下——谈正事，"头一个开口的正是埃塞克斯上校，"今晚我还有许多事要忙。"

===Original===
"Sure," the other answer’d, "I thought we had settled it. You are to lend me a thousand out of your garrison﻿—"

===Chinese===
"不错，"另一个答道，"我以为我们已经谈妥了。你从守军中拨一千人给我——"

===Original===
"Which, on my own part, I would willingly do. Only I beg you to consider, my lord, that my position here hangs on a thread. The extreme men are already against me: they talk of replacing me by Fiennes﻿—"

===Chinese===
"我个人是很乐意的。只是恳请阁下体谅，我在此地的处境岌岌可危。激进分子已经反对我了：他们扬言要用法因斯（Fiennes）来替换我——"

===Original===
"Nat Fiennes is no soldier."

===Chinese===
"纳特·法因斯（Nat Fiennes）可不是块当兵的料。"

===Original===
"No: but he’s a bigot﻿—a stronger recommendation. Should this plan miscarry, and I lose a thousand men﻿—"

===Chinese===
"不错：可他是个狂热分子——这才是更有分量的推荐。倘若此计落败，我折了一千人——"

===Original===
"Heavens alive, man! It *cannot* miscarry. Hark ye: there’s Ruthen of Plymouth will take the south road with all his forces. A day’s march behind I shall follow﻿—along roads to northward﻿—parallel for a way, but afterward converging. The Cornishmen are all in Bodmin. We shall come on them with double their number, aye, almost treble. Can you doubt the issue?"

===Chinese===
"老天爷，老兄！这*不可能*失败。你听我说：普利茅斯的鲁森（Ruthen）会率全部兵力走南路。我在他后面一天路程跟进——走北面的路——先平行一段，然后合拢。康沃尔人全在博德明（Bodmin）。我们的人数是他们的两倍，甚至将近三倍。结局还有什么可怀疑的？"

===Original===
"Scarcely, with the Earl of Stamford for General."

===Chinese===
"有斯坦福德伯爵（Earl of Stamford）挂帅，自然没什么可怀疑的。"

===Original===
The Earl was too far occupied to notice this compliment.

===Chinese===
伯爵正忙着别的，没注意到这句恭维。

===Original===
" ’Twill be swift and secret," he said, "as Death himself﻿—and as sure. Let be the fact that Hopton is all at sixes and sevens since the Marquis shipp’d for Wales: and at daggers drawn with Mohun."

===Chinese===
"此役将如死神一般迅疾隐秘，"他说，"也如死神一般十拿九稳。更何况霍普顿（Hopton）自从侯爵渡海赴威尔士以来一直六神无主，又跟莫恩（Mohun）势同水火。"

===Original===
Said the Colonel slowly﻿—"Aye, the notion is good enough. Were I not in this corner, I would not think twice. Listen now: only this morning they forc’d me to order a young man’s hanging, who might if kept alive be forc’d in time to give us news of value. I dar’d not refuse."

===Chinese===
上校缓缓说道——"嗯，这主意确实不错。若不是我处境窘迫，我连想都不会想第二遍。你听好了：就今天早上，他们逼我下令绞死一个年轻人，留着他活着，说不定迟早能从他嘴里掏出有价值的消息。我不敢不从。"

===Original===
"He that you caught with the King’s letter?"

===Chinese===
"就是你们抓到的那个带着国王密信的人？"

===Original===
"Aye﻿—a trumpery missive, dealing with naught but summoning of the sheriff’s posse and the like. There is more behind, could we but wait to get at it."

===Chinese===
"对——一封不值一提的信，不过是调集郡治安官的民团之类。背后还有更大的文章，只要我们耐得住性子去挖。"

===Original===
"The gallows may loosen his tongue. And how of the girl that was taken too?"

===Chinese===
"绞架或许能让他开口。那姑娘怎么样了？也抓到了？"

===Original===
"I have her in safe keeping. This very evening I shall visit her, and make another trial to get some speech. Which puts me in mind﻿—"

===Chinese===
"她在我手里，看管得很严。今晚我就去再试一回，看能不能从她嘴里套出点话来。这倒提醒我了——"

===Original===
The Colonel tinkled a small hand bell that lay on the table.

===Chinese===
上校摇了摇桌上的一只小手铃。

===Original===
The pause that follow’d was broken by the Earl.

===Chinese===
沉默片刻后，伯爵打破了寂静。

===Original===
"May I see the letter?"

===Chinese===
"我能看看那封信吗？"

===Original===
The Colonel handed it, and tinkled the bell again, more impatiently. At length steps were heard in the hall, and a servant open’d the door.

===Chinese===
上校把信递过去，又摇了一遍铃，这回更不耐烦了。终于，门厅里传来脚步声，一个仆人开了门。

===Original===
"Where is Giles?" ask’d the Colonel. "Why are you taking his place?"

===Chinese===
"贾尔斯（Giles）在哪儿？"上校问，"你怎么替他当差？"

===Original===
"Giles can’t be found, your honor."

===Chinese===
"贾尔斯找不着了，大人。"

===Original===
"Hey?"

===Chinese===
"嗯？"

===Original===
"He’s a queer oldster, your honor, an’ maybe gone to bed wi’ his aches and pains."

===Chinese===
"他是个怪老头，大人，兴许浑身疼，回屋睡去了。"

===Original===
(I knew pretty well that Giles had done no such thing: but be sure I kept the knowledge safe behind my screen.)

===Chinese===
（我心知贾尔斯才没干这事：但我把这秘密稳稳藏在了屏风后面。）

===Original===
"Then go seek him, and say﻿—No, stop: I can’t wait. Order the coach around at the barbican in twenty minutes from now﻿—twenty minutes, mind, without fail. And say﻿—’twill save time﻿—the fellow’s to drive me to Mistress Finch’s house in St. Thomas’ Street﻿—sharp!"

===Chinese===
"那你去找他，就说——不，等等：我等不及了。叫马车二十分钟后到门楼候着——记住，二十分钟，不得有误。顺便说一句——省得传错话——叫他把我送到圣托马斯街芬奇太太家——要快！"

===Original===
As the man departed on his errand, the Earl laid down His Majesty’s letter.

===Chinese===
那仆人领命而去，伯爵放下了国王陛下的信。

===Original===
"Hang the fellow," he said, "if they want it: the blame, if any, will be theirs. But, in the name of Heaven,Colonel, don’t fail in lending me this thousand men! ’Twill finish the war out of hand."

===Chinese===
"他们要绞死那家伙就绞吧，"他说，"若有骂名也该他们担。可是，看在老天份上，上校，这一千人你可千万得借我！此役一毕，战争就收场了。"

===Original===
"I’ll do it," answered the Colonel slowly.

===Chinese===
"我会借的，"上校慢悠悠地答道。

===Original===
"And I’ll remember it," said the Earl. "Tomorrow, at six o’clock, I set out."

===Chinese===
"我记着你的情，"伯爵说。"明天六点，我出发。"

===Original===
The two men shook hands on their bargain and left the room, shutting the door after them.

===Chinese===
两人就这笔交易握了手，走出房间，随手带上了门。

===Original===
I crept forth from behind the screen, my heart thumping on my ribs. Thus far it had been all fear and trembling with me; but now this was chang’d to a kind of panting joy. ’Twas not that I had spied the prison keys hanging near the fireplace, nor that behind the screen lay a heap of the Colonel’s riding boots, whereof a pair, ready spurr’d, fitted me choicely well; but that my ears tingled with news that turn’d my escape to a matter of public welfare: and also that the way to escape lay plann’d in my head.

===Chinese===
我从屏风后面溜出来，心在肋骨间砰砰直跳。在此之前我始终战战兢兢；此刻这种恐惧变成了一种喘不过气来的狂喜——倒不是因为我瞥见了挂在壁炉旁的监狱钥匙，也不是因为屏风后面堆着上校的几双骑马靴，其中一双带马刺的正合我脚——而是因为我的耳朵听到了足以将我的逃亡变成攸关大局之事的消息：而且，逃走的路线已经在我脑子里计划好了。

===Original===
Shod in the Colonel’s boots, I advanc’d again to the table. With sealing-wax and the Governor’s seal, that lay handy, I clos’d up the King’s letter, and sticking it in my breast, caught down the bunch of keys and made for the door.

===Chinese===
蹬上上校的靴子，我再次走到桌前。用蜡和随手可得的总督印章把国王的信封好，塞进怀中，抄起那串钥匙，朝门口走去。

===Original===
The hall was void. I snatch’d down a cloak and heavy broad-brimm’d hat from one of the pegs, and donning them, slipp’d back the bolts of the heavy door. It opened without noise. Then, with a last hitch of the cloak, to bring it well about me, I stepp’d forth into the night, shutting the door quietly on my heels.

===Chinese===
门厅空无一人。我从挂钩上抓下一领斗篷和一顶厚实的宽边帽戴上，拨开大门的门栓。门无声地开了。最后我把斗篷裹裹紧，迈步走进夜色，轻轻带上了身后的门。

===Original===
My feet were on the pavement of the inner ward. Above, one star only broke the blackness of the night. Across the court was a sentry tramping. As I walk’d boldly up, he stopped short by the gate between the wards and regarded me.

===Chinese===
我踏上了内庭的石板路。头顶上，一颗孤星刺破漆黑的夜空。对面院子里一个哨兵正在踱步。我大步走上前去，他在两道庭院之间的门口停住脚步，打量着我。

===Original===
Now was my danger. I knew not the right key for the wicket: and if I fumbled, the fellow would detect me for certain. I chose one and drew nearer; the fellow look’d, saluted, stepp’d to the wicket, and open’d it himself.

===Chinese===
此时最危险。我不知道哪把钥匙能开那扇小门：万一我摸索起来，这家伙肯定会起疑。我随手挑了一把走近；那人看了看，行了个礼，走到小门前亲自打开了门。

===Original===
"Good night, Colonel!"

===Chinese===
"晚安，上校！"

===Original===
I did not trust myself to answer: but passed rapidly through to the outer ward. Here, to my joy, in the arch’d passage of the barbican gate, was the carriage waiting, the porter standing beside the door; and here also, to my dismay, was a torch alight, and under it half a dozen soldiers chatting. A whisper pass’d on my approach﻿—

===Chinese===
我不敢答话，快步穿过门洞来到外庭。令我高兴的是，在门楼拱道里，马车正等着，门房站在车门旁；但令我沮丧的是，旁边还点着一支火把，下面围着五六个士兵在聊天。我一走近，便有人低声传话——

===Original===
"The Colonel!" and they hurried into the guardroom.

===Chinese===
"上校来了！"他们赶紧闪进卫兵室。

===Original===
"Good evening, Colonel!" The porter bow’d low, holding the door wide.

===Chinese===
"晚上好，上校！"门房深深鞠了一躬，把车门大敞开。

===Original===
I pass’d him rapidly, climb’d into the shadow of the coach, and drew a long breath.

===Chinese===
我从他身边匆匆走过，钻进马车的阴影里，长舒了一口气。

===Original===
Then ensued a hateful pause, as the great gates were unbarr’d. I gripp’d my knees for impatience.

===Chinese===
接着是令人煎熬的等待——大门正在拔门栓。我焦急得双手攥紧了膝盖。

===Original===
The driver spoke a word to the porter, who came round to the coach door again.

===Chinese===
车夫对门房说了句什么，门房又绕回车门旁。

===Original===
"To Mistress Finch’s, is it not?"

===Chinese===
"是去芬奇太太家吧？"

===Original===
"Ay," I muttered; "and quickly."

===Chinese===
"嗯，"我低声说，"越快越好。"

===Original===
The coachman touched up his pair. The wheels mov’d; went quicker. We were outside the Castle.

===Chinese===
车夫扬鞭催马。车轮转动，越转越快。我们出了城堡。

===Original===
With what relief I lean’d back as the Castle gates clos’d behind us! And with what impatience at our slow pace I sat upright again next minute! The wheels rumbl’d over the bridge, and immediately we were rolling easily down hill, through a street of some importance: but by this time the shutters were up along the shop fronts and very few people abroad. At the bottom we turn’d sharp to the left along a broader thoroughfare: and then suddenly drew up.

===Chinese===
城堡大门在身后合上时，我多么舒畅地往后一靠！可下一分钟我又焦躁地坐直了——车速太慢了！车轮隆隆滚过桥面，随即我们便顺着山坡轻松滑下，穿过一条还算像样的大街：但此时沿街店铺都已上了门板，行人寥寥。到了坡底，我们猛地左转，驶上一条更宽的主道：接着忽然停住了。

===Original===
"Are we come?" I wonder’d. But no: ’twas the city gate, and here we had to wait for three minutes at least, till the sentries recogniz’d the Colonel’s coach and open’d the doors to us. They stood on this side and that, presenting arms, as we rattled through; and next moment I was crossing a broad bridge, with the dark Avon on either side of me, and the vessels thick thereon, their lanterns casting long lines of yellow on the jetty water, their masts and cordage looming up against the dull glare of the city.

===Chinese===
"到了？"我纳闷。并没有：是城门到了，我们至少等了三分钟，哨兵才认出上校的马车，开门放行。两旁的卫兵举枪敬礼，马车哗啦啦驶过；转眼间我便驶上一座宽阔的桥，两侧是黑沉沉的河面，河上泊满了船，船灯在墨黑的水面上投下一道道昏黄的光，桅杆和缆绳在城市暗淡的灯火映衬下影影绰绰。

===Original===
Soon we were between lines of building once more, shops, private dwellings and warehouses intermix’d; then pass’d a tall church; and in about two minutes more drew up again. I look’d out.

===Chinese===
很快我们又穿行在两旁夹道的建筑之间——店铺、民宅、仓库交错；经过一座高大的教堂；大约两分钟后又停了下来。我向外张望。

===Original===
Facing me was a narrow gateway leading to a house that stood somewhat back from the street, as if slipping away from between the lines of shops that wedg’d it in on both sides. Over the grill a link was burning. I stepp’d from the coach, open’d the gate, and crossing the small court, rang at the house bell.

===Chinese===
正对面是一条窄门道，通向一所略微退离街面的房子，仿佛正从两旁紧逼的店铺之间悄悄溜走。铁栅上方一盏火把燃着。我跳下车，推开栅门，穿过小院子，按响了门铃。

===Original===
At first there was no answer. I rang again: and now had the satisfaction to hear a light footfall coming. A bolt was pull’d and a girl appear’d holding a candle high in her hand. Quick as thought, I stepped past her into the passage.

===Chinese===
起初没人应门。我又按了一遍：终于满意地听到轻盈的脚步声传来。门闩拉开，一个姑娘举着一支蜡烛出现在门口。我闪电般跨过她身边，闪进门厅。

===Original===
"Delia!"

===Chinese===
"德利娅！"

===Original===
"Jack!"

===Chinese===
"杰克！"

===Original===
"Hist! Close the door. Where is Mistress Finch?"

===Chinese===
"嘘！关门。芬奇太太在哪儿？"

===Original===
"Upstairs, expecting Colonel Essex. Oh, the happy day! Come﻿—" she led me into a narrow back room and setting down the light regarded me﻿—"Jack, my eyes are red for thee!"

===Chinese===
"楼上，正等着埃塞克斯上校。哦，今天真是好日子！来——"她把我领进一间窄小的后屋，放下烛台，看着我——"杰克，我为你哭红了眼睛！"

===Original===
"I see they are. Tomorrow I was to be hang’d."

===Chinese===
"我看见了。明天我就要被绞死了。"

===Original===
She put her hands together, catching her breath: and very lovely I thought her, in her straight grey gown and Puritan cap.

===Chinese===
她双手合十，倒吸一口气：我觉得她穿着笔挺的灰袍、戴着清教徒小帽的样子美极了。

===Original===
"They have been questioning me. Didst get my letter?"

===Chinese===
"他们一直在审我。你收到我的信了吗？"

===Original===
The answer was on my lip when there came a sound that made us both start.

===Chinese===
我正要回答，忽听一声响，我们俩都吓了一跳。

===Original===
’Twas the dull echo of a gun firing, up at the Castle.

===Chinese===
那是城堡方向一声闷闷的枪响。

===Original===
"Delia, what lies at the back here?"

===Chinese===
"德利娅，这后面是什么？"

===Original===
"A garden and a garden door: after these a lane leading to Redcliff Street."

===Chinese===
"一座花园和一扇园门：园门外面是一条通往雷德克利夫街（Redcliff Street）的小巷。"

===Original===
"I must go, this moment."

===Chinese===
"我此刻必须走。"

===Original===
"And I?"

===Chinese===
"我呢？"

===Original===
She did not wait my answer, but running out into the passage, she came swiftly back with a heavy key. I open’d the window.

===Chinese===
她没等我回答，跑出门厅，很快拿着一把沉重的钥匙回来了。我打开了窗户。

===Original===
"Delia! De‑lia!" ’Twas a woman’s voice calling her, at the head of the stairs.

===Chinese===
"德利娅！德利——娅！"楼梯口传来一个女人的呼唤声。

===Original===
"Aye, Mistress Finch."
