Joan lay beside me, her chin propp’d on one earth-stain’d hand, her great solemn eyes wide open as she listened. Till that moment I had regarded her rather as a man comrade than a girl, but now some feminine trick of gesture awoke me perhaps, for my fancy began to contrast her with Delia, and I broke off my story and sigh’d.

===Chinese===
大病初愈的头几天，有消息传到我们这儿（怎么传来的，我忘了）：德文与康沃尔两地的各派之间已共领庄严圣事、立下和约，乡间复归太平。我当时并不大在意：如今春天既到，我只管在突岩间信步闲逛，一面看琼恩做活，一面思念德利娅。虽说再见到她的希望微乎其微，我那位不久前的美丽同伴却整日整日盘踞在我心头。这份心事我谁也没告诉：因为虽说我在昏迷里多半漏过些口风，琼恩却从未露过一丝口风，我也始终不曾察觉。

我身边便只剩琼恩做伴：她父亲那天下午救下我的性命之后，言语举动上再没理会过我；那猫——简·特加格尔（Jan Tergagle，得名于据说常在这一带荒原出没的一个精灵）——也同样的淡漠。于是我便同琼恩一天天闲混：或看羊，或在她犁地时跟在她身边，或直挺挺躺在山坡上，同她谈论战争与战役。这是她唯一感到好奇的话题（我提出教她认印刷字，她还嗤笑我），亚历山大与汉尼拔、恺撒与圣女贞德，还有我记得生平的其他名将的故事，她一听就是几个钟头。

一天傍晚——那是五月初——我们爬上坦普尔上方那座灰色突岩的岩顶，从那里望得见两面海峡（英吉利与布里斯托尔两湾）上移动的白帆；我摊开身子躺在短草皮上，讲着我惯常讲的故事。琼恩卧在我身旁，一只沾着泥土的手支着下巴，那双庄重的大眼睛睁得圆圆地听着。直到那一刻为止，我一直拿她当个男同伴多过当个姑娘；可这会儿，也许是她哪个女儿家的小动作惊醒了我，我的心念竟拿她同德利娅两下比较起来，于是故事讲到一半，我住了口，叹了口气。

===Original===
“Art longing to be hence?” she asked.

I felt ashamed to be thus caught, and was silent. She look’d at me and went on﻿—

“Speak out, lad.”

“Loth would I be to leave you, Joan.”

“And why?”

“Why, we are good friends, I hope: and I am grateful.”

“Oh, aye﻿—wish thee’d learn to speak the truth, Jack. Art longing to be hence, and shalt﻿—soon.”

“Why, Joan, you would not have me dwell here always?”

She made no answer for a while, and then with a change of tone﻿—

“Shalt ride wi’ me to Bodmin Fair tomorrow for a treat, an’ see the Great Turk and the Fat ’Ooman and hocus-pocus. So tell me more ’bout Joan the Frenchwoman.”

===Chinese===
「你是不是盼着离开这儿？」她问。

心事这样被人当场逮住，我羞得没了言语。她看了看我，接着说——

「照实说吧，小伙子。」

「琼恩，要我离开你，我是舍不得的。」

「那是为啥？」

「呃，我们是好朋友哇，我希望：我心里感激你呢。」

「嗐，是呀——杰克，就盼你学学说真话。你正是盼着离开这儿，也真就要走了——快了。」

「嗐，琼恩，你总不至于要我在这儿待上一辈子吧？」

她半晌没有答话，随后换了一副口气——

「明儿个跟我骑马去博德明集市乐一乐，看大土耳其人、胖女人，还有变戏法。再给我讲讲那法兰西女人琼的事儿。」

===Original===
On the morrow, about nine in the morning, we set off﻿—Joan on the strawberry, balanced easily on an old sack, which was all her saddle; and I on Molly, that now was sound again and chafing to be so idle. As we set out, Joan’s father for the first time took some notice of me, standing at the door to see us off and shouting after us to bring home some account of the wrestling. Looking back at a quarter mile’s distance I saw him still fram’d in the doorway, with the cat perch’d on his shoulder.

Bodmin town is naught but a narrow street, near on a mile long, and widening toward the western end. It lies mainly along the south side of a steep vale, and this May morning as Joan and I left the moors and rode down to it from northward, already we could hear trumpets blowing, the big drum sounding, and all the bawling voices and hubbub of the fair. Descending, we found the long street lin’d with booths and shows, and nigh blocked with the crowd: for the revel began early and was now in full swing. And the crew of gipsies, whifflers, mountebanks, fortune tellers, cutpurses and quacks, mix’d up with honest country faces, beat even the rabble I had seen at Wantage.

===Chinese===
第二天早晨九点来钟，我们动身了——琼恩骑着那匹草莓红马，身子安安稳稳，鞍子不过是条旧麻袋，那便是她全部的坐垫；我骑莫莉，它这时伤已痊愈，闲了这么久，正躁得难受。出发的当口，琼恩的父亲头一回对我有了表示，站在门口送我们，还在后面喊，叫我们捎些摔跤比赛的消息回家。跑出四分之一英里回头一望，他还嵌在门口那框框里，肩上蹲着那只猫。

博德明镇不过一条窄街，将近一英里长，朝西头渐渐放宽。它大体卧在一道陡谷的南坡上。这日五月的早晨，我和琼恩出了荒原，从北面骑马下坡进城，老远便听见号角齐鸣、大鼓咚咚，还有集市上鼎沸的人声与喧嚷。下了坡，只见长街两旁排满货摊和戏棚，人群几乎把街道堵死：因为这场欢会开得早，此刻正是最热闹的当口。那起子吉普赛人、卖艺人、江湖骗子、算命先生、扒手和假药郎中，掺在一群老实的乡下面孔中间，比我在旺蒂奇（Wantage）见过的那伙乱民还要庞杂。

===Original===
Now my own first business was with a tailor: for the clothes I wore when I rode into Temple, four months back, had been so sadly messed with blood, and afterward cut, to free them from my wound, that now all the tunic I wore was of sackcloth, contrived and stitch’d together by Joan. So I made at once for a decent shop, where luckily I found a suit to fit me, one taken (the tailor said) off a very promising young gentleman that had the misfortune to be kill’d on Braddock Down. Arrayed in this, I felt myself again, and offered to take Joan to see the Fat Woman.

We saw her, and the Aethiop, and the Rhinoceros (which put me in mind of poor Anthony Killigrew), and the Pig-fac’d Baby, and the Cudgel play; and presently halted before a Cheap Jack, that was crying his wares in a prodigious loud voice, near the town wall.

===Chinese===
说到我自己，头一桩事是寻裁缝：四个月前我骑马进坦普尔时穿的那身衣裳，先是叫血污得一塌糊涂，后来又为着处理伤口给铰开了，弄得我如今身上这件上衣，还是琼恩拿麻袋布裁裁拼拼缝出来的。于是我径直奔一家像样的铺子，运气也好，寻着一身合我尺寸的行头——照裁缝说，是从一位前程远大的年轻绅士身上取下来的，他有这个不幸，在布拉多克荒原上送了性命。这身装束一上身，我便又找回了原先的自己，遂提议带琼恩去看胖女人。

胖女人看了，埃塞俄比亚人、犀牛（它让我想起可怜的安东尼·基利格鲁）、猪脸婴儿、棍棒对打，也都一一看了；不多时，我们在一个流动摊贩（Cheap Jack）跟前站住了脚，他正在镇墙附近，扯着一副惊天动地的嗓门叫卖货物。

===Original===
’Twas a meagre, sharp-visag’d fellow with a grey chin beard like a billy goat’s; and (as fortune would have it) spying our approach, he picked out a mirror from his stock and holding it aloft, addressed us straight﻿—

“What have we here,” cries he, “but a pair o’ lovers coming? and what i’ my hand but a lover’s hourglass? Sure the stars of heav’n must have a hand in this conjuncture﻿—and only thirteen pence, my pretty fellow, for a glass that will tell the weather i’ your sweetheart’s face, and help make it fine.”

===Chinese===
那是个瘦削的、面相尖刻的家伙，下巴上一撮灰胡子，活像公山羊的髯；也是合当有事，他一眼瞧见我们走近，便从货堆里拣出一面镜子，高高举起，冲着我们直讲起来——

「这不是打远儿来了什么，」他喊道，「不是一对儿小情人么？再瞧我手里拿的是什么，不正是恋人的沙漏么？遇上这等巧合，定是天上的星宿也搭了手——只要十三便士，我的漂亮小哥，买一面镜子，它能报出你心上人脸上的阴晴，还能帮着把天变晴。」

===Original===
There were many country fellows with their maids in the crowd, that turned their heads at this address; and as usual the women began.

“ ’Tis Joan o’ the Tor!”

“Joan’s picked up wi’ a sweetheart﻿—tee-hee!﻿—an’ us reckoned her’d forsworn mankind!”

“Who is he?”

“Some furriner, sure: that likes garlic.”

“He’s bought her no ribbons yet.”

“How should he, poor lad; that can find no garments upon her to fasten ’em to?”

===Chinese===
人群里有许多带着姑娘的乡下小伙子，听了这话都回过头来；而照例，是娘儿们先开的头。

「那不是托尔的琼恩么！」

「琼恩相好了个小情郎——嘻嘻！——咱们还当她赌咒一辈子不沾男人呢！」

「他是谁呀？」

「准是个外乡人：爱吃大蒜的那号。」

「他还没给她买过一根缎带呢。」

「他怎么买得起哟，可怜的小伙子；她身上寻得出一件衣裳，好把缎带别上去么？」

===Original===
And so on, with a deal of spiteful laughter. Some of these sayings were half truth, no doubt: but the truthfullest word may be infelix. So noting a dark flush on Joan’s cheek, I thought to end the scene by taking the Cheap Jack’s mirror on the spot, to stop his tongue, and then drawing her away.

But in this I was a moment too late; for just as I reached up my hand with the thirteen pence, and the grinning fellow on the platform bent forward with his mirror, I heard a coarser jest, a rush in the crowd, and two heads go *crack!* together like eggs. ’Twas two of Joan’s tormentors she had taken by the hair and served so: and dropping them the next instant had caught the Cheap Jack’s beard, as you might a bell rope, and wrench’d him head-foremost off his stand, my thirteen pence flying far and wide. Plump he fell into the crowd, that scatter’d on all hands as Joan pummelled him: and *whack, whack!* fell the blows on the poor idiot’s face, who scream’d for mercy, as though Judgment Day were come.

===Chinese===
诸如此类，还夹着一阵阵恶毒的哄笑。这些话里有几句算得半真，无疑：可最真的话也可能不祥（infelix）。我瞥见琼恩脸颊上升起一团黑红，心想不如当场买下那流动摊贩的镜子，堵住他的嘴，再把她拽走了事。

可这一着，我慢了一拍；我攥着十三便士刚伸手递上去，台板上那嘻嘻赔笑的家伙正端着镜子俯身过来，就听得一句更下流的浑话、人群里一阵骚动，随即两颗脑袋咔嚓！一声磕在一处，活像磕鸡蛋。原来是琼恩揪住了两个捉弄她的家伙的头发，这般款待了他们：下一瞬她撒开这俩，又一把攥住那流动摊贩的胡子，就像攥钟绳一般，把他头朝前猛地拽下台来，我的十三便士撒了个漫天遍地。他扑通栽进人群，琼恩拳脚交加，众人向四面八方躲散：啪！啪！巴掌一下下落在那可怜的蠢货脸上，他杀猪也似的讨饶，仿佛审判日到了。

===Original===
No one, for the minute, dared to step between them: and presently Joan looking up, with arm raised for another buffet, spied a poor Astrologer close by, in a red and yellow gown, that had been reading fortunes in a tub of black water beside him, but was now broken off, dismayed at the hubbub. To this tub she dragged the Cheap Jack and sent him into it with a round souse. The black water splashed right and left over the crowd. Then, her wrath sated, Joan faced the rest, with hands on hips, and waited for them to come on.

Not a word had she spoken, from first to last: but stood now with hot cheeks and bosom heaving. Then, finding none to take up her challenge, she strode out through the folk, and I after her, with the mirror in my hand; while the Cheap Jack picked himself out of the tub, whining, and the Astrologer wip’d his long white beard and soil’d robe.

===Chinese===
一时间，没人敢上前夹在两人中间：不多时，琼恩抬起头来，胳膊还举着正要再赏一记耳光，瞥见近旁一位可怜的占星术士，穿着红黄相间的长袍，方才一直在身边一盆黑水里为人算命，此刻被这场喧腾吓断了营生。琼恩便把那流动摊贩拖到这盆黑水跟前，闷头一按，把他整个儿浸了进去。黑水朝左右的人群泼溅开来。这时琼恩怒气已泄，便双手叉腰，面对余下那些人，单等他们上前。

从头至尾她一句话也没有说：只是站在那里，两颊发烫，胸口起伏。随后见没人应她的阵，她便大步穿过人群走出去，我手里攥着那面镜子跟在后头；那流动摊贩哼哼唧唧地从盆里往外爬，占星术士则揩着他那把长白胡子和弄污了的袍子。

===Original===
Outside the throng was a carriage, stopp’d for a minute by this tumult, and a servant at the horses’ heads. By the look of it, ’twas the coach of some person of quality; and glancing at it I saw inside an old gentleman with a grave venerable face, seated. For the moment it flash’d on me I had seen him before, somewhere: and cudgell’d my wits to think where it had been. But a second and longer gaze assured me I was mistaken, and I went on down the street after Joan.

She was walking fast and angry; nor when I caught her up and tried to soothe, would she answer me but in the shortest words. Woman’s justice, as I had just learn’d, has this small defect﻿—it goes straight enough, but mainly for the wrong object. Which now I proved in my own case.

===Chinese===
人群外头停着一辆马车，被这场乱子耽搁了片刻，一个仆人拉住马头。看那气派，是哪位有身份贵人的车驾；我朝车里一瞥，见里头端坐着一位老先生，面容庄重威严。刹那间我心里忽地一闪，觉得从前在哪儿见过他：便绞尽脑汁去想究竟是在哪里。可再定睛多瞧了两眼，我确信是自己认错了，便顺着街追琼恩去了。

她走得又快又气；我赶上她、百般宽慰，她也只肯用最简短的字眼答我。女人的公道，我方才算是领教了，有这么一个小小的缺陷——路子倒是直，只是多半认错了对象。这一点，如今轮到我拿自身来作证了。

===Original===
“Where are you going, Joan?”

“To Fifteen Balls stable, for my horse.”

“Art not leaving the fair yet, surely!”

“That I be, though. Have had fairing enow﻿—wi’ a man!”

===Chinese===
「你去哪儿，琼恩？」

「上『十五球』（Fifteen Balls）客栈的马棚，牵我的马。」

「你总不至于这就离开集市了吧！」

「就是要走。集市的乐子可受够喽——跟个男人一道！」

===Original===
Nor for a great part of the way home would she speak to me. But meeting, by Pound Scawens (a hamlet close to the road), with some friends going to the fair, she stopp’d for a while to chat with them, whilst I rode forward: and when she overtook me, her brow was clear again.

===Chinese===
回家的一路上，大半程她也不肯同我讲话。可在庞德斯考恩斯（Pound Scawens，紧挨大路的一个小村子）一带，遇见几位正赶集去的朋友，她便停下同他们聊了一阵，我先往前骑：等她重新赶上来，她眉头又开朗了。

===Original===
“Am a hot headed fool, Jack, and have spoil’d thy day for thee.”

“Nay, that you have not,” said I, heartily glad to see her humble, for the first time in our acquaintance: “but if you have forgiven me that which I could not help, you shall take this that I bought for you, in proof.”

And pulling out the mirror, I lean’d over and handed it to her.

===Chinese===
「杰克，我这急性子的蠢货，把你这一天给搅黄了。」

「哪里，并没有，」我说——相识以来头一回见她服软，心里着实欢喜——「不过，你既宽恕了我那桩由不得自己的事，就请收下这件我给你买的东西，做个凭证。」

说着，我掏出那面镜子，俯过身去，递到她手里。

===Original===
“What i’ the world be this?” she ask’d, taking and looking at it doubtfully.

“Why, a mirror.”

“What’s that?”

“A glass to see your face in,” I explained.

“Be this my face?” She rode forward, holding up the glass in front of her. “Why, what a handsome looking gal I be, to be sure! Jack, art certain ’tis my very own face?”

“To be sure,” said I amazed.

“Well!” There was silence for a full minute, save for our horses’ tread on the high road. And then﻿—

“Jack, I be powerful dirty!”

This was true enough, and it made me laugh. She looked up solemnly at my mirth (having no sense of a joke, then or ever) and bent forward to the glass again.

===Chinese===
「这是个啥玩意儿？」她问，接过去，将信将疑地端详着。

「嗐，一面镜子呀。」

「镜子是啥？」

「一面照得见你脸的玻璃，」我解释道。

「这是我的脸？」她把镜子举在面前，继续往前骑。「嗐，瞧我这姑娘，敢情长得还真俊哩！杰克，你敢保这真是我自个儿的脸？」

「自然是真的，」我说，暗自吃惊。

「哎！」接下来足足一分钟没人作声，只有两匹马的蹄声踏在大路上。然后——

「杰克，我脏得厉害！」

这话千真万确，逗得我放声大笑。她见我发笑，神情庄重地抬眼看我（她那时也罢，往后也罢，全没有玩笑的神经），又低头凑向那面镜子。

===Original===
“By the way,” said I, “did you mark a carriage just outside the crowd, by the Cheap Jack’s booth?﻿—with a white-hair’d gentleman seated inside?”

Joan nodded. “Master Hannibal Tingcomb: steward o’ Gleys.”

“What!”

I jumped in my saddle, and with a pull at the bridle brought Molly to a standstill.

“Of Gleys?” I cried. “Steward of Sir Deakin Killigrew that was?”

“Right, lad, except the last word. ‘That *is*,’ should’st rather say.”

“Then you are wrong, Joan: for he’s dead and buried, these five months. Where is this house of Gleys? for tomorrow I must ride there.”

“ ’Tis easy found, then: for it stands on the south coast yonder, and no house near it: five mile from anywhere, and sixteen from Temple, due south. Shall want thee afore thou startest, Jack. Dear, now! who’d ha’ thought I was so dirty?”

===Chinese===
「对了，」我说，「你留意到人群外头那辆马车没有，就在那流动摊贩的棚子近旁？——车里坐着一位白头发的老先生？」

琼恩点了点头。「汉尼拔·廷科姆老爷：格莱斯的管家。」

「什么！」

我在鞍上猛地一震，一把勒住缰绳，让莫莉站定了。

「格莱斯的管家？」我叫道。「是已故迪肯·基利格鲁爵士的管家？」

「说得对，小伙子，只是末了一个词不对。你该说『现在还是』。」

「那你可就错了，琼恩：他死了下葬都五个月了。格莱斯宅在哪儿？明天我就得骑马去。」

「那好找：它就立在南边那片海岸上，左近没有别的人家：离哪儿都是五英里，正南离坦普尔十六英里。你动身前我有事找你，杰克。哎呀，瞧瞧！谁能想到我竟这么脏？」

===Original===
The cottage door stood open as we rode into the yard, and from it a faint smoke came curling, with a smell of peat. Within I found the smould’ring turves scattered about as on the day of my first arrival, and among them Joan’s father stretch’d, flat on his face: only this time the cat was curl’d up quietly, and lying between the old man’s shoulder blades.

===Chinese===
我们骑进院子时，小屋的门敞着，一缕淡淡的烟从门里袅袅卷出，带着泥炭的气味。进屋一看，只见闷燃的泥炭砖四下散着，同我头一回来的那天一样，而琼恩的父亲就直挺挺趴在当间，脸朝下：只是这一回，那猫安安静静蜷成一团，卧在老人的两片肩胛之间。

===Original===
“Drunk again,” said Joan shortly.

But looking more narrowly, I marked a purplish stain on the ground by the old man’s mouth, and turned him softly over.

“Joan,” said I, “he’s not drunk﻿—he’s dead!”

She stood above us and looked down, first at the corpse, then at me, without speaking for a time: at last﻿—

“Then I reckon he may so well be buried.”

“Girl,” I call’d out, being shocked at this callousness, “ ’tis your father﻿—and he is dead!”

“Why that’s so, lad. An he were alive, shouldn’t trouble thee to bury ’n.”

===Chinese===
「又喝醉了。」琼恩简短地说。

可凑近细看，我看见老人嘴边的地上有一摊发紫的污渍，便轻轻把他翻了过来。

「琼恩，」我说，「他不是醉了——他是死了！」

她立在我们身旁，低头望着，先望那尸身，再望我，半晌不作声：末了才说——

「那我看，正好这就把他埋了。」

「姑娘，」我喊了出来，被这份狠心冷漠惊得不行，「这是你父亲——他死了！」

「说的是，小伙子。他要是还活着，还犯不着劳你的神来埋他。」

===Original===
And so, before night, we carried him up to the bleak tor side, and dug his grave there; the black cat following us to look. Five feet deep we laid him, having dug down to solid rock; and having covered him over, went silently back to the hovel. Joan had not shed a single tear.

===Chinese===
就这样，天黑之前，我们把他抬上荒凉的突岩坡，在那里掘了墓穴；黑猫也跟了去看。一直掘到见着硬石，我们把他安放在五英尺深处；掩埋停当，便默默回到那间窝棚。琼恩自始至终没有掉一滴眼泪。

## XIV I Do No Good in the House of Gleys / 我在格莱斯宅邸一无所获

===Original===
Very early next morning I awoke, and hearing no sound in the loft above (whither, since my coming, Joan had carried her bed), concluded her to be still asleep. But in this I was mistaken: for going to the well at the back to wash, I found her there, studying her face in the mirror.
"Luckily met, Jack," she said, when I was cleansed and freshly glowing: "Now fill another bucket and sarve me the same."
"Cannot you wash yourself?" I ask'd, as I did so.

===Chinese===
第二天一早我就醒了，听到阁楼上面（自我来了之后，琼恩就把床搬到了那里）没有声响，以为她还在睡觉。但我错了：我去后面的井边洗脸时，发现她在那里，正对着镜子端详自己的脸。
"正好碰见你，杰克，"我洗得干干净净、容光焕发时她说，"现在再打一桶水，也给我浇上。"
"你自己不会洗吗？"我一边照做一边问。

===Original===
"Lost the knack, I reckon. Stand thee so, an' slush the water over me."
"But your clothes!" I cried out, "they'll be soaking wet!"
"Clothes won't be worse for a wash, neither. So slush away."
Therefore, standing at three paces' distance, I sent a bucketful over her, and then another and another. Six times I filled and emptied the bucket in all: and at the end she was satisfied, and went, dripping, back to the kitchen to get me my breakfast.

===Chinese===
"大概是忘了怎么洗了。你就那么站着，把水浇到我身上。"
"可是你的衣服！"我叫道，"会全湿透的！"
"衣服淋了水也不会更糟。浇就是了。"
于是我站在三步开外，一桶水浇到她身上，又一桶，又一桶。前前后后一共打了六桶：最后她满意了，浑身滴着水回到厨房去给我做早饭。

===Original===
"Art early abroad," she said, as we sat together over the meal.
"Yes, for I must ride to Gleys this morning."
"Shan't be sorry to miss thee for a while. Makes me feel so shy—this cleanliness." So, promising to be back by nightfall, I went presently to saddle Molly: and following Joan's directions and her warnings against quags and pitfalls, was soon riding south across the moor and well on my road to the House of Gleys.

===Chinese===
"你出门可真早，"我们坐在一起吃饭时她说。
"是的，因为我今天上午得骑马去格莱斯。"
"少看你一会儿我也不难过。这种干净劲儿——叫人怪不好意思的。"我答应天黑前就回来，然后去给莫莉（Molly）备鞍：按照琼恩的指路和关于沼泽陷阱的告诫，很快就骑马向南穿过荒原，踏上了去格莱斯宅邸（House of Gleys）的路。

===Original===
My way leading me by Braddock Down, I turned aside for a while to examine the ground of the late fight (though by now little was to be seen but a piece of earthwork left unfinish'd by the rebels, and the fresh mounds where the dead were laid); and so 'twas high noon—and a dull, cheerless day—before the hills broke and let me have sight of the sea. Nor, till the noise of the surf was in my ears, did I mark the chimneys and naked grey walls of the house I was bound for.

===Chinese===
我的路经过布拉多克荒原（Braddock Down），我拐过去看了看最近那场战斗的战场（虽然眼下能看到的不过是一段叛军未完工的工事，和新堆起的坟丘）；因此到了正午时分——天色阴沉，毫无生气——山峦才豁然开朗，大海映入眼帘。直到涛声入耳，我才注意到我要去的那座宅邸的烟囱和光秃秃的灰色墙壁。

===Original===
'Twas a gloomy, savage pile of granite, perch'd at the extremity of a narrow neck of land, where every wind might sweep it, and the waves beat on three sides the cliff below. The tide was now at the full, almost, and the spray flying in my face, as we crossed the head of a small beach, forded a stream, and scrambled up the rough road to the entrance gate.
A thin line of smoke blown level from one chimney was all the sign of life in the building: for the narrow lights of the upper story were mostly shuttered, and the lower floor was hid from me by a high wall enclosing a courtlage in front. One stunted ash, with boughs tortured and bent toward the mainland, stood by the gate, which was lock'd. A smaller door, also lock'd, was let into the gate, and in this again a shuttered iron grating. Hard by, dangled a rusty bellpull, at which I tugg'd sturdily.

===Chinese===
那是一座阴沉、粗犷的花岗岩建筑，矗立在一处狭窄的海岬尽头，四面来风都可扫过，崖下三面是海浪拍击。潮水几乎涨满，浪花扑打在我脸上。我们穿过一小片海滩的尽头，趟过一条小溪，攀上粗糙的石路来到入口大门前。
只有从一根烟囱里被吹平的一缕细烟是这建筑里有生命迹象的证明：楼上狭窄的窗洞大多装了百叶窗，楼下则被一道高墙挡住了视线，墙围出前院。一棵矮小的白蜡树，枝条向陆地方向扭曲弯折，长在大门旁边。大门上了锁。门上开了一扇小门，也上了锁，小门上又有一扇带铁栅的百叶窗。旁边挂着一个生锈的铃绳，我使劲拽了几下。

===Original===
On this, a crack'd bell sounded, far in the house, and scared a flock of starlings out of a disused chimney. Their cries died away presently, and left no sound but that of the gulls wailing about the cliff at my feet. This was all the answer I won.
I rang again, and a third time: and now at last came the sound of footsteps shuffling across the court within. The shutter of the grating was slipp'd back, and a voice, crack'd as the bell, asked my business.

===Chinese===
这时，宅子深处响起了一声沙哑的铃声，惊起一群椋鸟从一个废弃的烟囱里飞出来。它们的叫声很快就消失了，只剩下脚下悬崖上海鸥的哀鸣。这就是我得到的全部回答。
我又拉了第二次、第三次：终于听到院子里有拖着脚步走路的声音。铁栅的百叶窗滑开了，一个和铃声一样沙哑的声音问我的来意。

===Original===
"To see Master Hannibal Tingcomb," answered I.
"Thy name?"
"He shall hear it in time. Say that I come on business concerning the estate."
The voice mutter'd something, and the footsteps went back. I had been kicking my heels there for twenty minutes or more when they returned, and the voice repeated the question—
"Thy name?"
Being by this time angered, I did a foolish thing; which was, to clap the muzzle of my pistol against the grating, close to the fellow's nose. Singular to say, the trick serv'd me. A bolt was slipp'd hastily back and the wicket door opened stealthily.

===Chinese===
"我来见汉尼拔·廷科姆（Hannibal Tingcomb）老爷，"我答道。
"你叫什么名字？"
"他自会知道的。就说我是来谈庄园事务的。"
那声音嘟囔了几句，脚步声便退去了。我在那里空等了二十多分钟，脚步声才又回来，那声音又问了一遍：
"你叫什么名字？"
这时我被惹恼了，做了一件蠢事：把枪口顶在铁栅上，正对着那家伙的鼻子。说来也怪，这招居然管用了。门闩慌忙拉开，小门悄悄打开了。

===Original===
"I want," said I, "room for my horse to pass."
Thereupon more grumbling follow'd, and a prodigious creaking of bolts and chains; after which the big gate swung stiffly back.
"Sure, you must be worth a deal," I said, "that shut yourselves in so careful."
Before me stood a strange fellow—extraordinary old and bent, with a wizen'd face, one eye only, and a chin that almost touched his nose. He wore a dirty suit of livery, that once had been canary-yellow; and shook with the palsy.

===Chinese===
"我需要，"我说，"让我马过去的地方。"
接着又是嘟囔，门闩和铁链嘎吱嘎吱响得厉害；然后大门慢慢地往后推开了。
"你们可真够金贵的，"我说，"把自己关得这么严实。"
眼前站着一个怪人——老得出奇，弯腰驼背，一张皱巴巴的脸，只有一只眼睛，下巴几乎碰到了鼻子。他穿着一套脏兮兮的号衣，原本是金丝雀黄色的；浑身打着哆嗦。

===Original===
"Master Tingcomb will see the young man," he squeak'd, nodding his head; "but is a-reading just now in his Bible."
"A pretty habit," answered I, leading in Molly—"if unseasonable. But why not have said so?"
He seem'd to consider this for a while, and then said abruptly—
"Have some pasty and some good cider?"
"Why yes," I said, "with all my heart, when I have stabled the sorrel here."
He led the way across the court, well paved but chok'd with weeds, toward the stable. I found it a spacious building, and counted sixteen stalls there; but all were empty save two, where stood the horses I had seen in Bodmin the day before. Having stabled Molly, I left the place (which was thick with cobwebs) and follow'd the old servant into the house.

===Chinese===
"廷科姆老爷会见这位年轻人，"他尖声说着点了点头；"不过正在读《圣经》呢。"
"好习惯，"我牵着莫莉进来答道，"——虽然不太合时宜。不过你怎么不早说？"
他似乎想了一会儿，然后突然说道：
"要不要来点馅饼和好苹果酒？"
"那当然，"我说，"求之不得，等我把这匹枣红马安顿好。"
他领着我穿过铺得整整齐齐但杂草丛生的院子，朝马厩走去。我发现马厩很宽敞，数了一下有十六个隔间；但只有两个里面有马，正是前一天在博德明（Bodmin）看到的那两匹。把莫莉安顿好后，我离开了马厩（里面蛛网密布），跟着老仆人进了宅子。

===Original===
He took me into a great stone kitchen, and brought out the pasty and cider, but poured out half a glass only.
"Have a care, young man: 'tis a luscious, thick, seductive drink," and he chuckled.
"'Twould turn the edge of a knife," said I, tasting it and looking at him: but his one blear'd eye was inscrutable. The pasty also was mouldy, and I soon laid it down.
"Hast a proud stomach that cometh of faring sumptuously: the beef therein is our own killing," said he. "Young sir, art a man of blood, I greatly fear, by thy long sword and handiness with the firearms."
"Shall be presently," answered I, "if you lead me not to Master Tingcomb."

===Chinese===
他把我领进一间大石砌厨房，拿出馅饼和苹果酒，但只倒了半杯。
"当心，年轻人：这酒又浓又稠，很迷人，"他嘿嘿笑道。
"连刀刃都给泡钝了，"我尝了一口看着他说：但他那只浑浊的眼睛深不可测。馅饼也发了霉，我很快就放下了。
"你那胃口是大鱼大肉养出来的娇贵胃口：里面的牛肉是我们自己宰的，"他说。"少爷，看你带着长剑，玩火枪又那么利落，我怕你是个杀人的人。"
"马上就是了，"我答道，"如果你不带我去见廷科姆老爷的话。"

===Original===
He scrambled up briskly and totter'd out of the kitchen into a stone corridor, I after him. Along this he hurried, muttering all the way, and halted before a door at the end. Without knocking he pushed it open, and motioning me to enter, hasten'd back as he had come.
"Come in," said a voice that seem'd familiar to me.

===Chinese===
他麻利地站起来，蹒跚着走出厨房，进了石头走廊，我跟在后面。他一路小跑，嘴里嘟嘟囔囔，在尽头一扇门前停下。没敲门就推开了，示意我进去，自己急匆匆按原路回去了。
"请进，"一个我似乎熟悉的声音说道。

===Original===
Though, as you know, 'twas still high day, in the room where now I found myself was every appearance of night: the shutters being closed, and six lighted candles standing on the table. Behind them sat the venerable gentleman whom I had seen in the coach, now wearing a plain suit of black, and reading in a great book that lay open on the table. I guess'd it to be the Bible; but noted that the candles had shades about them, so disposed as to throw the light, not on the page, but on the doorway where I stood.
Yet the old gentleman, having bid me enter, went on reading for a while as though wholly unaware of me: which I found somewhat nettling, so began—

===Chinese===
虽然你知道，当时还是大白天，但我走进的这间屋子里却完全是夜晚的景象：百叶窗关着，桌上点着六支蜡烛。蜡烛后面坐着那位我在马车里见过的老绅士，此刻穿着一身素黑，正在读一本摊开在桌上的大书。我猜是《圣经》；但注意到蜡烛周围有遮光罩，光线不是照在书页上，而是照在我站的门廊处。
可是这位老绅士请我进来之后，又继续读了一会儿，仿佛完全没注意到我：这让我有点恼火，于是开口道——

===Original===
"I speak, I believe, to Master Hannibal Tingcomb, steward to Sir Deakin Killigrew."
He went on, as if ending his sentence aloud: "… And my darling from the power of the dog." Here he paused with finger on the place and looked up. "Yes, young sir, that is my name—steward to the late Sir Deakin Killigrew."
"The late?" cried I: "Then you know—"
"Surely I know that Sir Deakin is dead: else should I be but an unworthy steward." He open'd his grave eyes as if in wonder.
"And his son, also?"
"Also his son Anthony, a headstrong boy, I fear me, a consorter with vile characters. Alas! that I should say it."
"And his daughter, Mistress Delia?"
"Alas!" and he fetched a deep sigh.
"Do you mean, sir, that she too is dead!"
"Why, to be sure—but let us talk on less painful matters."
"In one moment, sir: but first tell me—where did she die, and when?"

===Chinese===
"我想，我是在跟汉尼拔·廷科姆（Hannibal Tingcomb）老爷说话吧，迪肯·基利格鲁爵士（Sir Deakin Killigrew）的管家。"
他接着念，像是在大声读完那句话："……将我的爱女从恶犬的爪下救出。"他把手指按在那一页上停住，抬起头来。"是的，少爷，那就是我的名字——已故迪肯·基利格鲁爵士的管家。"
"已故？"我叫道，"那么您知道——"
"我当然知道迪肯爵士已经去世了：否则我就不是个称职的管家。"他睁开庄重的眼睛，仿佛有些惊讶。
"他的儿子呢？"
"还有他的儿子安东尼，一个任性的孩子，我怕他交的都是些下流人。唉！我真不该说这话。"
"他的女儿，德利娅（Delia）小姐呢？"
"唉！"他深深叹了口气。
"您的意思是，她也死了！"
"这个嘛，确实如此——不过我们还是谈点不那么伤心的事吧。"
"请稍等，先生：但先告诉我——她是在哪里、什么时候死的？"

===Original===
For my heart stood still, and I was fain to clutch the table between us to keep me from falling. I think this did not escape him, for he gave me a sharp look, and then spoke very quiet and hush'd,
"She was cruelly kill'd by highwaymen, at the Three Cups inn, some miles out of Hungerford. The date given me is the 3rd of December last."
With this a great rush of joy came over me, and I blurted out, delighted—
"There, sir, you are wrong! Her father was kill'd on the night of which you speak—cruelly enough, as you say: but Mistress Delia Killigrew escaped, and after the most incredible adventures—"
I was expecting him to start up with joy at my announcement; but instead of this, he gaz'd at me very sorrowfully and shook his head; which brought me to a stand.

===Chinese===
我的心一下子停跳了，不得不抓住我们之间的桌子才没有倒下。我想他没有漏掉这个细节，因为他锐利地看了我一眼，然后压低声音说：
"她在亨格福德（Hungerford）几英里外的'三杯'客栈（Three Cups inn）被拦路强盗残忍杀害了。我得到的日期是去年十二月三日。"
一听这话，一阵巨大的喜悦涌上心头，我脱口而出：
"先生，您错了！她的父亲是在您说的那个晚上被杀的——确实很残忍，正如您所说：但德利娅·基利格鲁小姐逃了出来，经历了最不可思议的奇遇——"
我本以为他听到我的消息会欣喜若狂地站起来；但相反，他非常悲伤地看着我，摇了摇头；我一时愣住了。

===Original===
"Sir," I said, changing my tone, "I speak but what I know: for 'twas I had the happy fortune to help her to escape, and, under God's hand, to bring her safe to Cornwall."
"Then, where is she now?"
Now this was just what I could not tell. So, standing before him, I gave him my name and a history of all my adventures in my dear comrade's company, from the hour when I saw her first in the inn at Hungerford. Still keeping his finger on the page, he heard me to the end attentively, but with a curling of the lips toward the close, such as I did not like. And when I had done, to my amaze he spoke out sharply, and as if to a whipp'd schoolboy.

===Chinese===
"先生，"我换了语气说，"我所说的都是我亲眼所见：因为我有幸帮助她逃出来，并且托上帝的福，把她安全送到了康沃尔。"
"那她现在在哪里？"
这正是我无法回答的问题。于是我站在他面前，报上我的名字，从在亨格福德客栈第一次见到她说起，讲述了我与亲爱的同伴（comrade）在一起的全部冒险经历。他手指一直按在书页上，专心地听我说完，但到最后嘴唇微微撇了一下，我不太喜欢那样。我讲完后，令我惊讶的是，他厉声说道，像是在训斥一个挨了鞭子的小学生。

===Original===
"'Tis a cock-and-bull story, sir, of which I could hope to make you ashamed. Six weeks in your company? and in boy's habit? Surely 'twas enough the pure unhappy maid should be dead—without such vile slander on her reputation, and from you, that were known, sir, to have been at that inn, and on that night, with her murderers. Boy, I have evidence that, taken with your confession, would weave you a halter; and am a Justice of the Peace. Be thankful, then, that I am a merciful man; yet be abash'd."
Abash'd, indeed, I was; or at least taken aback, to see his holy indignation and the flush on his waxen cheek. Like a fool I stood staggered, and wondered dimly where I had heard that thin voice before. In the confusion of my senses I heard it say solemnly—
"The sins of her fathers have overtaken her, as the Book of Exodus proclaim'd: therefore is her inheritance wasted, and given to the satyr and the wild ass."

===Chinese===
"先生，这是一派胡言，我真希望你能为此感到羞耻。跟你在一起六周？还穿着男孩子的衣服？一个纯洁不幸的姑娘死了已经够惨了——还要加上你对她名誉的卑鄙诽谤，而你，先生，众所周知，那天晚上就在那家客栈里，和她的凶手在一起。小子，我手里的证据加上你刚才的口供，足以给你编一条绞索；我又是治安法官。所以你该谢天谢地，我是个仁慈的人；但你该感到羞愧。"
我确实羞愧了——至少是大吃一惊，看到他那义愤填膺的样子和蜡黄脸上的红晕。我像个傻子似的愣在那里，隐隐约约觉得以前在哪里听过那个尖细的声音。在一片混乱中，我听到他庄重地说——
"她父亲的罪孽已经追上了她，正如《出埃及记》所宣告的：因此她的产业荒废，交给了山羊和野驴。"

===Original===
"And which of the twain be you, sir?"
I cannot tell what forced this violent rudeness from me, for he seem'd an honest, good man; but my heart was boiling that any should put so ill a construction on my Delia. As for him, he had risen, and was moving with dignity to the door—to show me out, as I guess. When suddenly I, that had been staring stupidly, leap'd upon him and hurled him back into his chair.
For I had marked his left foot trailing, and, by the token, knew him for the white hair'd man of the bowling-green.
"Master Hannibal Tingcomb," I spoke in his ear, "—dog and murderer! What did you in Oxford last November? And how of Captain Lucius Higgs, otherwise Captain Luke Settle, otherwise Mr. X.? Speak, before I serve you as the dog was served that night!"

===Chinese===
"那您是这两样中的哪一样呢，先生？"
我说不出是什么逼出了这句粗暴无礼的话，因为他看上去是个老实好人；可是一想到有人如此诋毁我的德利娅，我就气得要炸。至于他，已经站起来，威严地朝门口走去——大概是想送我出去。可就在这时，一直傻瞪着眼的我猛然扑向他，把他摔回了椅子。
因为我注意到他的左脚拖着步子，从这个特征，我认出他就是滚木球场上那个白发老头。
"汉尼拔·廷科姆（Hannibal Tingcomb）老爷，"我凑在他耳边说，"——狗东西，杀人犯！去年十一月你在牛津干什么？卢修斯·希格斯上尉（Captain Lucius Higgs），又名卢克·塞特尔上尉（Captain Luke Settle），又名X先生（Mr. X.），又是怎么回事？说吧，不然我就叫你尝尝那天晚上那条狗的下场！"

===Original===
I dream yet, in my sick nights, of the change that came over the vile, hypocritical knave at these words of mine. To see his pale venerable face turn green and livid, his eyeball start, his hands clutch at air—it frighten'd me.
"Brandy!" he gasped. "Brandy! there—quick—for God's sake!"
And the next moment he had slipp'd from my grasp, and was wallowing in a fit on the floor. I ran to the cupboard at which he had pointed, and finding there a bottle of strong waters, forced some drops between his teeth; and hard work it was, he gnashing at me all the time and foaming at the mouth.
Presently he ceased to writhe and bite: and lifting, I set him in his chair, where he lay, a mere limp bundle, staring and blinking. So I sat down facing him, and waited his recovery.

===Chinese===
即使在病中的夜晚，我还会梦见那个卑鄙虚伪的无赖听到我这番话后的变化。看到他那张苍白庄重的脸变成青绿色，眼珠子几乎要瞪出来，双手在空中乱抓——真把我吓坏了。
"白兰地！"他喘着气叫道。"白兰地！快——快——看在上帝的份上！"
下一刻他已经从我手中滑脱，在地上痉挛着打滚。我跑到他指的那个柜子前，找到一瓶烈酒，撬开他的牙齿灌了几滴进去；费了好大劲，他一直咬牙切齿地冲我咆哮，口吐白沫。
渐渐地他不再挣扎咬人了：我把他扶起来放到椅子上，他瘫在那里，像一捆软塌塌的东西，呆呆地眨着眼睛。我便在对面坐下，等他缓过来。

===Original===
"Dear young sir," he began at length feebly, his fingers searching the Bible before him, from force of habit. "Kind young sir—I am an old, dying man, and my sins have found me out. Only yesterday, the physician at Bodmin told me that my days are numbered. This is the second attack, and the third will kill me."
"Well?" said I.
"If—if Mistress Delia be alive (as indeed I did not think), I will make restitution—I will confess—only tell me what to do, that I may die in peace."

===Chinese===
"亲爱的少爷，"他终于有气无力地开口，手指习惯性地摸索着面前的《圣经》。"好心的少爷——我是一个快死的老人了，我的罪孽已经被揭露出来。就在昨天，博德明的医生告诉我，我的日子不多了。这是第二次发作，第三次就会要了我的命。"
"嗯？"我说。
"如果——如果德利娅小姐还活着（我确实以为她死了），我会弥补——我会忏悔——只要告诉我怎么做，我好死得安宁。"

===Original===
Indeed, he look'd pitiable, sitting there and stammering: but I harden'd my heart to say—
"I must have a confession, then, written before I leave the room."
"But, dear young friend, you will not use it if I give up all? You will not seek my life? that already is worthless, as you see."
"Why, 'tis what you deserve. But Delia shall say when I find her—as I shall go straight to seek her. If she be lost, I shall use it—never fear: if she be found, it shall be hers to say what mercy she can discover in her heart; but I promise you I shall advise none."

===Chinese===
确实，他坐在那里结结巴巴的样子很可怜：但我硬起心肠说——
"那我必须有一份书面忏悔，趁我还没离开这间屋子。"
"但是，亲爱的好心少爷，如果我把一切交出来，你不会用它的吧？你不会要我的命吧？我的命已经一文不值了，你也看到了。"
"这本来就是你罪有应得。但等我找到德利娅——我会直接去找她——由她来决定。如果她死了，我一定会用这份忏悔——不用怕：如果她还活着，就把这份忏悔交给她，由她决定能发多大的慈悲；但我保证不会替她出主意。"

===Original===
The tears by this were coursing down his shrunken cheeks, but I observ'd him watch me narrowly, as though to find out how much I knew. So I pull'd out my pistol, and setting pen and paper before him, obtained at the end of an hour a very pretty confession of his sins, which lies among my papers to this day. When 'twas written and sign'd, in a weak, rambling hand, I read it through, folded it, placed it inside my coat, and prepared to take my leave.
But he called out an order to the old servant to saddle my mare, and stood softly praying and beseeching me in the courtyard till the last moment. Nor when I was mounted would anything serve but he must follow at my stirrup to the gate. But when I had briefly taken leave, and the heavy doors had creaked behind me, I heard a voice calling after me down the road—
"Dear young sir! Dear friend!—I had forgotten somewhat."
Returning, I found the gate fastened, and the iron shutter slipp'd back.

===Chinese===
泪水这时已经顺着他的皱纹往下淌了，但我注意到他紧盯着我，好像在试探我到底知道多少。我掏出手枪，把笔和纸摆在他面前，一个小时后拿到了一份相当详尽的罪行忏悔——直到今天还在我手里。写好之后，他用颤抖散乱的字迹签了名。我读了一遍，折好放进内袋，准备告辞。
但他叫来老仆人给我的马备鞍，在院子里一直低声祈祷、恳求我，直到最后一刻。我上了马，他还非得跟着走到大门边。我匆匆告辞之后，沉重的大门在我身后嘎吱关上，我听见一个声音在后面追着喊——
"亲爱的少爷！亲爱的朋友！——我忘了点事。"
我转身回去，发现大门已经关上了，铁栅的百叶窗又滑开了。

===Original===
"Well?" I asked, leaning toward it.
"Dear young friend, I pity thee, for thy paper is worthless. Today, by my advices, the army of our most Christian Parliament, more than twenty thousand strong, under the Earl of Stamford, have overtaken thy friends, the malignant gentry, near Stratton Heath, in the northeast. They are more than two to one. By this hour tomorrow, the Papists all will be running like conies to their burrows, and little chance wilt thou have to seek Delia Killigrew, much less to find her. And remember, I know enough of thy late services to hang thee: mercy then will lie in my friends' hands; but be sure I shall advise none."
And with a mocking laugh he clapp'd-to the grating in my face.

===Chinese===
"怎么？"我凑过去问道。
"亲爱的好心少爷，我可怜你，因为你那份忏悔一文不值。今天根据我得到的情报，我们最虔诚的议会军在斯坦福德伯爵（Earl of Stamford）率领下，两万多人，在东北方向的斯特拉顿荒原（Stratton Heath）追上了你的朋友们，那些保王党的绅士们。兵力对比超过二比一。到明天这个时候，天主教徒们全都会像野兔一样逃回洞里，你找德利娅·基利格鲁的机会少之又少，更不用说找到她了。还有，记住，我掌握着足以绞死你的证据：到时候你只能听凭我朋友们发落了；但请放心，我绝不会替你求情。"
他嘲弄地哈哈一笑，当着我的脸把铁栅拍上了。

## XV I Leave Joan and Ride to the Wars / XV 我告别琼恩，投身战场

===Original===
You may guess how I felt at being thus properly fooled. And the worst was I could see no way to mend it; for against the barricade between us I might have beat myself for hours, yet only hurt my fists: and the wall was so smooth and high, that even by standing on Molly’s back I could not﻿—by a foot or more﻿—reach the top to pull myself over.

There was nothing for it but to turn homewards, down the hill: which I did, chewing the cud of my folly, and finding it bitter as gall. What consoled me somewhat was the reflection that his threats were, likely enough, mere vaporing: for of any breach of the late compact between the parties I had heard nothing, and never seem’d a country more wholly given up to peace than that through which I had ridden in the morning. So recalling Master Tingcomb’s late face of terror, and the confession in my pocket, I felt more cheerful. “England has grown a strange place, if I cannot get justice on this villain,” thought I; and rode forward, planning a return-match and a sweet revenge.

There is no more soothing game, I believe, in the world than this of holding imaginary triumphant discourse with your enemy. Yet (oddly) it brought me but cold comfort on this occasion, my wound being too recent and galling. The sky, so long clouded, was bright’ning now, and growing serener every minute: the hills were thick with foxgloves, the vales white with hawthorn, smelling very sweetly in the cool of the day: but I, with the bridle flung on Molly’s neck, pass’d them by, thinking only of my discomfiture, and barely rousing myself to give back a “Good day” to those that met me on the road. Nor, till we were on the downs and Joan’s cottage came in sight, did I shake the brooding off.

===Chinese===
诸君可以想见，我被人如此戏耍之后是何等滋味。最糟的是我看不到任何补救之法：对着隔在我们之间的那堵高墙，我就是捶上几个小时，也不过伤了自己的拳头。那墙光滑高耸，即便踩在莫莉背上，我也差了足足一英尺够不着墙头翻过去。

无奈之下，我只好转身下山往回走，一路咀嚼着自己的愚蠢，滋味比胆汁还苦。聊以自慰的是，那家伙的威胁多半只是虚张声势：近来两方之间并无违约的消息，我早晨骑马经过的那片乡野，似乎比任何地方都更彻底地沉醉在和平之中。想到廷科姆老爷那张惊恐万状的脸，再摸摸口袋里的供词，我的心情好了些。「若治不了这个恶棍，那英格兰真成了一片怪地方，」我想；于是策马前行，盘算着如何来一场漂亮的还击，讨回一笔痛快的账。

我相信，世上再没有比与想象中的敌人展开一番得意洋洋的辩论更令人宽慰的游戏了。然而（说来也怪）这次它只给了我冷冰冰的慰藉，因为伤口太新、太疼。久阴的天空此刻放了晴，每过一刻便更加澄澈：山坡上长满了毛地黄，山谷里山楂花一片雪白，在清凉的暮色中散发着幽香。但我只是将缰绳搭在莫莉颈上，任它从身边流过，满脑子只想着自己的败绩，连路上遇见行人也勉强才应一声「日安」。直到踏上荒原、琼恩的小屋映入眼帘，我才从这沉思中挣脱出来。

===Original===
Joan was not in the kitchen when I arrived, nor about the buildings; nor yet could I spy her anywhere moving on the hills. So, after calling to her once or twice, I stabled the mare, and set off up the tor side to seek her.

Now I must tell you that since the day of my coming I had made many attempts to find the place where Joan had then hidden me, and always fruitlessly: though I knew well whereabouts it must be. Indeed, I had thought at first I had only to walk straight to the hole: yet found after repeated trials but solid earth and boulders for my pains.

But today as I climb’d past the spot, something very bright flashed in my eyes and dazzled me, and rubbing them and looking, I saw a great hole in the hill﻿—facing to the sou’-west﻿—in the very place I had search’d for it; and out of this a beam of light glancing.

Creeping near on tiptoe, I found one huge block of granite that before had seemed bedded, among a dozen fellow-boulders, against the turf﻿—the base resting on another well-nigh as big﻿—was now rolled back; having been fixed to work smoothly on a pivot, yet so like nature that no eye, but by chance, could detect it. Now, who in the beginning designed this hiding place I leave you to consider; and whether it was the Jews or Phoenicians﻿—nations, I am told, that once work’d the hills around for tin. But inside ’twas curiously paved and lined with slabs of granite, the specks of ore in which, I noted, were the points of light that had once puzzled me. And here was Joan’s bower, and Joan herself inside it.

===Chinese===
我到时，琼恩不在厨房，也不在屋舍附近，山坡上也看不到她的身影。喊了她一两声后，我将母马牵进马厩，便沿着突岩山坡上去寻她。

须知自我到来那天起，我多次尝试寻找琼恩当初藏我的那个地方，却屡屡徒劳，尽管我大致知道它该在何处。起初我以为只需径直走到洞口便成，结果反复试探，除了坚实的泥土和乱石，一无所获。

但今天当我攀过那处地点时，一道极亮的光闪入眼中，晃得我睁不开眼。揉了揉眼再看，山坡上赫然有一个大洞——朝向西南——正是我苦寻未果之处；一道光柱从洞中射出。

我蹑手蹑脚凑近，发现一块巨大的花岗岩——此前它看似与其他十来块巨石一起，牢牢嵌在草皮之中，底下还压着另一块几乎同样大的石头——此刻已被推开。原来它装在枢轴上，可以顺畅转动，却与天然岩石别无二致，若非偶然，绝无人能识破。至于这藏身之所最初由谁设计，诸君不妨推想：是犹太人还是腓尼基人——据人说，他们曾在这周围的山中开采锡矿。洞内铺设精巧，四壁嵌着花岗岩板，板上的矿石斑点，我注意到，正是当年令我困惑的那些光点。此处便是琼恩的闺阁，琼恩本人就在里面。

===Original===
She was sitting with her back to me, in her left hand holding up the mirror, that caught the rays of the now sinking sun (and thus had dazzled me), while with her right she tried to twist into some form of knot her tresses﻿—black, and coarse as a horse’s mane﻿—that already she had roughly braided. A pail of water stood beside her; and around lay scatter’d a score or more of long thorns, cut to the shape of hair pins.

’Tis probable that after a minute’s watching I let some laughter escape me. At any rate Joan turned, spied me, and scrambled up, with an angry red on her cheek. Then I saw that her bodice was neater lac’d than usual, and a bow of yellow ribbon (fish’d up heaven knows whence) stuck in the bosom. But the strangest thing was to note the effect of this new tidiness upon her: for she took a step forward as if to cuff me by the ear (as, a day agone, she would have done), and then stopp’d, very shy and hesitating.

“Why, Joan,” said I, “don’t be anger’d. It suits you choicely﻿—it does indeed.”

“Art scoffing, I doubt.” She stood looking heavily and askance at me.

“On my faith, no: and what a rare tiring-bower the Jew’s Kitchen makes! Come, Joan, be debonair and talk to me, for I am out of luck today.”

===Chinese===
她背对着我坐着，左手举着那面镜子，映着西沉的夕阳光芒（正是它晃了我的眼），右手试图将一绺绺粗硬如马鬃的黑发——她已草草编过一遍——拧成某种发髻的形状。身旁放着一桶水，四周散落着二十多根长荆棘，削成了簪子的模样。

我看了约莫一分钟，大概笑出了声。琼恩闻声回头，发现了我，一骨碌爬起来，脸上泛起一抹羞恼的红晕。我这才看清她的胸衣系得比平日齐整得多，领口还别着一条黄丝带结（天知道从哪儿翻出来的）。最令我惊异的是这身新整洁对她的影响：她上前一步，仿佛要揪我耳朵（搁在昨天她准会这么干），却又停下，羞涩而迟疑。

「哎呀，琼恩，」我说，「别生气。这身打扮太好看了——真的。」

「你准在笑话我。」她站着，沉沉地斜睨着我。

「我发誓，绝没有：犹太人的厨房（Jews' Kitchen）成了多好的梳妆间啊！来，琼恩，高兴些，跟我说说话吧，我今天倒霉透了。」

===Original===
“Forgit it, then” (and she pointed to the sun), “whiles yet some o’t is left. Tell me a tale, an thou’rt minded.”

“Of what?”

“O’ the bloodiest battle thou’st ever heard tell on.”

So, sitting by the mouth of the Jew’s Kitchen, I told her as much as I could remember out of Homer’s Iliad, wondering the while what my tutor, Mr. Josias How, of Trinity College, would think to hear me so use his teaching. By-and-by, as I warm’d to the tale, Joan forgot her new smartness; and at length, when Hector was running from Achilles round the walls, clapp’d her hands for excitement, crying, “Church an’ King, lad! Oh, brave work!”

“Why, no,” answered I, “ ’twas not for that they were fighting;” and looking at her, broke off with, “Joan, art certainly a handsome girl: give me a kiss for the mirror.”

Instead of flying out, as I look’d for, she fac’d round, and answered me gravely﻿—

“That I will not: not to any but my master.”

“And who is that?”

“No man yet; nor shall be till one has beat me sore: him will I love, an’ follow like a dog﻿—if so be he whack me often enow’.”

===Chinese===
「那就忘了吧，」她指着太阳说，「趁日头还没落尽。给我讲个故事，你若愿意的话。」

「讲什么？」

「讲你听过的最血腥的一仗。」

于是我坐在犹太人的厨房洞口，将荷马《伊利亚特》中能记住的部分讲给她听，一边讲一边想，若三一学院的约西亚斯·豪先生（Mr. Josias How）听到我这般活用他的教诲，不知作何感想。渐渐地，我越讲越投入，琼恩也忘了新梳的发髻。最后，当赫克托耳绕着城墙被阿喀琉斯追赶时，她兴奋地拍起手来，喊道：「教会与国王，小伙子！哦，好一场恶战！」

「哎，不对，」我答道，「他们可不是为这个打仗的；」我看着她，话锋一转：「琼恩，你确实是个漂亮姑娘：把那面镜子送我的人情，该给我一个吻。」

她没有像我预料的那样发火，而是转过身来，郑重答道——

「这个我不给：除了我的主人，谁也不给。」

「谁是你的主人？」

「还没找到呢。得有人把我狠狠打服了：我就爱他，像狗一样跟着他——只要他揍我揍得够勤就行。」

===Original===
“A strange way to love,” laughed I. She look’d at me straight, albeit with an odd gloomy light in her eyes.

“Think so, Jack? then I give thee leave to try.”

I think there is always a brutality lurking in a man to leap out unawares. Yet why do I seek excuses, that have never yet found one? To be plain, I sprang fiercely up and after Joan, who had already started, and was racing along the slope.

Twice around the tor she led me: and though I strain’d my best, not a yard could I gain upon her, for her bare feet carried her light and free. Indeed, I was losing ground, when coming to the Jew’s Kitchen a second time, she tried to slip inside and shut the stone in my face.

Then should I have been prettily bemock’d, had I not, with a great effort, contrived to thrust my boot against the door just as it was closing. Wrenching it open, I laid hand on her shoulder; and in a moment she had gripp’d me, and was wrestling like a wildcat.

Now being Cumberland-bred I knew only the wrestling of my own county, and nothing of the Cornish style. For in the north they stand well apart, and try to wear down one another’s strength: whereas the Cornish is a brisker lighter play﻿—and (as I must confess) prettier to watch. So when Joan rush’d in and closed with me, I was within an ace of being thrown, pat.

===Chinese===
「这爱法可真怪，」我笑了。她直直地看着我，眼中却有一抹异样的阴翳。

「觉得怪，杰克？那我准你试试。」

我想每个男人心底都潜伏着一种暴戾，会猝不及防地跳出来。可我何必找借口呢？我从未找到过一个。说白了，我猛地跃起，朝琼恩追去——她已抢先跑开，沿着斜坡飞奔而去。

她绕着突岩跑了两圈：我使出浑身解数，却一步也追不上她，因为她赤着脚，跑得轻盈自在。我确实在落后，当第二次跑到犹太人的厨房时，她试图闪身进去，把石门在我面前关上。

若非我奋力一脚，在石门即将合拢时将靴子卡了进去，我就要被好好嘲弄一番了。我扳开门，一把抓住她的肩头；转瞬间她已扣住我，像一头野猫般扭打起来。

我是坎伯兰（Cumberland）人，只懂本郡的摔跤功夫，对康沃尔式全然陌生。北方摔跤讲究站得远远的，靠耐力磨垮对方；康沃尔式则轻快灵巧——而且（我得承认）看着更好看。所以当琼恩扑上来贴身肉搏时，我差点就被干净利落地摔了出去。

===Original===
But recovering, I got her at arm’s length, and held her so, while my heart ach’d to see my fingers gripping her shoulders and sinking into the flesh. I begg’d off; but she only fought and panted, and struggled to lock me by the ankles again. I could not have dream’d to find such fierce strength in a girl. Once or twice she nearly overmastered me: but at length my stubborn play wore her out. Her breath came short and fast, then fainter: and in the end, still holding her off, I turned her by the shoulders, and let her drop quietly on the turf. No thought had I any longer of kissing her; but stood back, heartily sick and ashamed of myself.

For awhile she lay, turn’d over on her side, with hands guarding her head, as if expecting me to strike her. Then gathering herself up, she came and put her hand in mine, very meekly.

“Had lik’d it better had’st thou stamped the life out o’ me, a’most. But there, lad﻿—am thine forever!”

’Twas like a buffet in the face to me. “What!” I cried.

She look’d up in my face﻿—dear Heaven, that I should have to write it!﻿—with eyes brimful, sick with love; tried to speak, but could only nod: and broke into a wild fit of tears.

I was standing there with her hand in mine, and a burning remorse in my heart, when I heard the clear notes of a bugle blown, away on the road to Launceston.

Looking that way, I saw a great company of horse coming down over the crest, the sun shining level on their arms and a green standard that they bore in their midst.

Joan spied them the same instant, and check’d her sobs. Without a word we flung ourselves down full length on the turf to watch.

===Chinese===
但我稳住了，将她推到一臂之外，紧紧抓住。看着我的手指深深嵌进她的肩肉，我心如刀绞。我求她罢手；她却只是喘息着挣扎，试图再次锁住我的脚踝。我万没想到一个姑娘竟有如此凶猛的力气。一两次她几乎将我制服；但我那死缠烂打的招式终于耗尽了她的气力。她的呼吸越来越急促，然后越来越微弱：最终，我仍架着她的肩膀，轻轻将她放到草地上。我再也无心去吻她了；退后几步，对自己厌恶至极、羞愧难当。

她侧身躺了一会儿，双手护着头，仿佛等着我动手打她。然后她撑起身子，走过来，温顺地将手放在我掌中。

「我倒宁愿你一脚把我踩死呢。可是算了，小伙子——我永远是你的人了！」

这话如同一记耳光。「什么！」我喊道。

她仰起脸看着我——天哪，我竟不得不写下这一句！——眼中满是泪水，因爱而心碎；想说话，却只能点头；接着号啕大哭起来。

我握着她的手站在那里，心中燃烧着悔恨，这时朗塞斯顿方向传来一阵清亮的号角声。

望去，只见一大队骑兵正从山脊上下来，阳光平射在他们武器和队伍中央那面绿旗上。

琼恩同时发现了他们，止住了啜泣。我们二话不说，双双趴在草地上观望。

===Original===
They were more than a thousand, as I guess’d, and came winding down the road very orderly, till, being full of them, it seem’d a long serpent writhing with shiny scales. The tramp of hoofs and jingling of bits were pretty to hear.

“Rebels!” whisper’d I. Joan nodded.

There were three regiments in all, whereof the first (and biggest) was of dragoons. So clear was the air, I could almost read the legend on their standard, and the calls of their captains were borne up to us extremely distinct.

As they rode leisurely past, I thought of Master Tingcomb’s threat, and wonder’d what this array could intend. Nor, turning it over, could I find any explanation: for the Earl of Stamford’s gathering, he had said, was in the northeast, and I knew such troops as the Cornish generals had to be quarter’d at Launceston. Yet here, on the near side of Launceston, was a large body of rebel horse marching quietly to the sou’-west. Where was the head or tail to it?

Turning my head as the last rider disappear’d on the way to Bodmin, I spied a squat oddly shap’d man striding down the hill very briskly: yet he look’d about him often and kept to the hollows of the ground; and was crossing below us, as it appeared, straight for Joan’s cottage.

Cried I: “There is but one man in the world with such a gait﻿—and that’s Billy Pottery!”

And jumping to my feet (for he was come directly beneath us) I caught up a great stone and sent it bowling down the slope.

===Chinese===
估计有一千多人，队伍沿大路蜿蜒而下，秩序井然，密密匝匝，宛如一条闪亮鳞甲的长蛇在蠕动。马蹄声和马嚼子的叮当声甚是悦耳。

「叛军！」我低声道。琼恩点了点头。

共三个团，最前面（也是最大的）是龙骑兵。空气如此澄澈，我几乎能读出旗上的字迹，军官们的口令声也清晰可闻。

他们悠然骑过时，我想起廷科姆老爷的威胁，猜测这支兵马意欲何为。翻来覆去也想不出个所以然：据他说，斯坦福德伯爵（Earl of Stamford）的集结在东北方，而且我知道康沃尔将领们手中的部队都驻扎在朗塞斯顿。然而就在朗塞斯顿近侧，一大队叛军骑兵正悄然向西南行进。这到底是怎么回事？

最后一个骑兵消失在去博德明（Bodmin）的路上时，我扭头望去，瞥见一个矮胖奇形的男人快步从山坡上走下来：他不时张望，专拣洼地走；看样子正朝琼恩的小屋直奔而来。

我喊道：「天下只有一个人走路是这副架势——比尔·波特里（Billy Pottery）！」

我跳起来（他正走到我们下方），捡起一块大石头朝坡下滚去。

===Original===
Bounce it went past him, missing his legs by a foot or less. The man turn’d, and catching sight of me as I stood waving, made his way up the hill. ’Twas indeed Captain Billy: and coming up, the honest fellow almost hugg’d me for joy.

“Was seeking thee, Jack,” he bawled: “learn’d from Sir Bevill where belike I might find thee. Left his lodging at Launceston this mornin’, and trudged ivery foot o’ the way. A thirsty land, Jack﻿—neither horse’s meat nor man’s meat therein, nor a chair to sit down on: an’ three women only have I kiss’d this day!” He broke off and look’d at Joan. “Beggin’ the lady’s pardon for sea manners and way o’ speech.”

“Joan,” said I, “this is Billy Pottery, a good mariner and friend of mine: and as deaf as a haddock.”

Billy made a leg; and as I pointed to the road where the cavalry had just disappeared, went on with a nod﻿—

“That’s so: old Sir G’arge Chudleigh’s troop o’ horse sent off to Bodmin to seize the High Sheriff and his posse there. Two hour agone I spied ’em, and ha’ been ever since playin’ spy.”

“Then where be the King’s forces?” I made shift to enquire by signs.

“March’d out o’ Launceston today, lad﻿—an’ but a biscuit a man between ’em, poor dears﻿—for Stratton Heath, i’ the nor’-east, where the rebels be encamp’d. Heard by scouts o’ these gentry bein’ sent to Bodmin, and were minded to fight th’ Earl o’ Stamford whiles his dragooners was away. An’ here’s the long an’ short o’t: thou’rt wanted, lad, to bear a hand wi’ us up yonder﻿—an the good lady here can spare thee.”

And here we both look’d at Joan﻿—I shamefacedly enough, and Billy with a puzzled air, which he tried very delicately to hide.

She put her hand in mine.

“To fight, lad?”

I nodded my head.

“Then go,” she said without a shade in her voice; and as I made no answer, went on﻿—“Shall a woman hinder when there’s fightin’ toward? Only come back when thy wars be over, for I shall miss thee, Jack.”

And dropping my hand she led the way down to the cottage.

===Chinese===
石头从他身旁弹过，离他的腿不到一尺。那人转过身来，看见我站着挥手，便朝山上走来。果然是比尔船长：这个老实人上来时高兴得差点把我搂住。

「正找你呢，杰克，」他嚷道，「从贝维尔爵士那儿打听出你大概在这一带。今早从朗塞斯顿他住处出发，一路全是用脚量过来的。这地方渴死人，杰克——马没草料，人没饭食，连把椅子也没有：我今天才亲了三个娘们！」他住了口，看看琼恩。「请姑娘原谅水手的做派和说话方式。」

「琼恩，」我说，「这位是比尔·波特里，我的好朋友，一个好水手：耳朵比黑线鳕还聋。」

比尔行了个礼；我指了指刚才骑兵经过的大路，他点头道——

「没错：乔治·查德利老爵士（Sir G'arge Chudleigh）的骑兵被派去博德明抓那里的郡长大人和他的警卫队。两个钟头前我瞧见他们，之后就一直当探子。」

「那国王的军队呢？」我比划着问。

「今天从朗塞斯顿开拔了，小伙子——每人只有一块饼干充饥，可怜的家伙——往东北的斯特拉顿荒原（Stratton Heath）去，叛军就在那儿扎营。探子听说这帮家伙被派去了博德明，就想趁龙骑兵不在，跟斯坦福德伯爵干一仗。长话短说：人家要你去，小伙子，跟我们一起在那边搭把手——只要这位好姑娘舍得放你走。」

我和比尔都看向琼恩——我满面羞惭，比尔则一脸困惑，还极力掩饰。

她将手放在我掌中。

「去打仗吗，小伙子？」

我点点头。

「那就去吧，」她语气平淡；见我不答，又说道——「打仗的事，女人家何必拦着？只是打完了就回来，我会想你的，杰克。」

她松开我的手，领着我们朝小屋走去。

===Original===
Now Billy, of course, had not heard a word of this: but perhaps he gathered some import. Anyway, he pull’d up short midway on the slope, scratched his head, and thunder’d﻿—

“What a good lass!”

Joan, some paces ahead, turn’d at this and smil’d: whereat, having no idea he’d spoken above a whisper, Billy blush’d red as any peony.

’Twas but a short half hour when, the mare being saddled and Billy fed, we took our leave of Joan. Billy walked beside one stirrup, and the girl on the other side, to see us a few yards on our way. At length she halted﻿—

“No leave-takin’s, Jack, but ‘Church and King!’ Only do thy best and not disgrace me.”

And “Church and King!” she call’d thrice after us, standing in the road. For me, as I rode up out of that valley, the drums seem’d beating and the bugles calling to a new life ahead. The last light of day was on the tors, the air blowing fresher as we mounted: and with Molly’s every step the past five months appear’d to dissolve and fall away from me as a dream.

On the crest, I turn’d in the saddle. Joan was yet standing there, a black speck on the road. She waved her hand once.

Billy had turn’d too, and, uncovering, shouted so that the hilltops echoed.

“A good lass﻿—a good lass! But what’s become o’ t’other one?”

===Chinese===
比尔当然一个字也没听见：但也许他猜到了几分。总之，他在半山坡猛地站住，挠了挠头，大喝一声——

「多好的姑娘啊！」

走在几步开外的琼恩闻声回头，微微一笑。比尔全然不知自己喊得有多响，脸红得像朵牡丹。

不过半小时，母马备好鞍，比尔也吃饱了，我们便向琼恩道别。比尔走在一侧马镫旁，姑娘走在另一侧，送了我们一段路。最后她停下脚步——

「不说再见了，杰克，只喊'教会与国王！'尽你的力，别给我丢脸。」

她站在路上连喊了三声「教会与国王！」目送我们远去。对我而言，策马驶出山谷时，鼓声仿佛在响，号角仿佛在吹，召唤着前方崭新的人生。夕阳最后一缕光落在突岩上，越往高处骑风越清新：莫莉每迈一步，过去五个月便如一场梦般在我身后消融散尽。

在山脊上，我勒马回望。琼恩仍站在路上，化作一个小小的黑点。她挥了一次手。

比尔也回过头，脱下帽子高声喊道，山巅为之回响——

「多好的姑娘——多好的姑娘！可另一个呢？」

## XVI The Battle of Stamford Heath / XVI 斯坦福德荒原之战

===Original===
Night came, and found us but midway between Temple and Launceston: for though my comrade stepp’d briskly beside me, ’twas useless to put Molly beyond a walk; and besides, the mare was new from her day’s journey. This troubled me the less by reason of the moon (now almost at the full), and the extreme whiteness of the road underfoot, so that there was no fear of going astray. And Billy engaged that by sunrise we should be in sight of the King’s troops.

“Nay, Jack,” he said, when by signs I offered him to ride and tie: “never rode o’ horseback but once, and then ’pon Parson Spinks his red mare at Bideford. Parson i’ those days was courtin’ the Widow Hambly, over to Torrington: an’ I, that wanted to fare to Barnstaple, spent that mornin’ an’ better part o’ th’ afternoon, clawin’ off Torrington. And th’ end was the larboard halyards broke, an’ the mare gybed, an’ to Torrington I went before the wind, wi’ an unseemly bloody nose. ‘Lud!’ cries the widow, ‘ ’tis the wrong man ’pon the right horse!’ ‘Pardon, mistress,’ says I, ‘the man is well enow, but ’pon the wrong horse, for sure.’ ”

Now and then, as we went, I would dismount and lead Molly by the bridle for a mile or so: and all the way to Launceston Billy was recounting his adventures since our parting. It appeared that, after leaving me, they had come to Plymouth with a fair passage: but before they could unlade, had advertisement of the Governor’s design to seize all vessels then riding in the Sound, for purposes of war; and so made a quick escape by night into Looe Haven, where they had the fortune to part with the best part of their cargo at a high profit. ’Twas while unlading here that Billy had a mind to pay a debt he ow’d to a cousin of his at Altarnun, and, leaving Matt Soames in charge, had tramped northward through Liskeard to Launceston, where he found the Cornish forces, and was met by the news of the Earl of Stamford’s advance in the northeast. Further meeting, in Sir Bevill’s troop, with some north coast men of his acquaintance, he fell to talking, and so learn’d about me and my ride toward Braddock, which (it seem’d) was now become common knowledge. This led him to seek Sir Bevill, with the result that you know: “for,” as he wound up, “ ’tis a desirable an’ rare delight to pay a debt an’ see some fun, together.”

===Chinese===
夜幕降临，我们才行到坦普尔与朗塞斯顿之间的半路：我的同伴虽在我身旁步履轻快，可要莫莉加快步子也是徒劳；况且这匹母马白天刚跑完一整天的路程。好在明月（此时将近满月）当空，脚下道路又白得分明，全无迷路之虞，我也就不甚焦心。比尔·波特里（Billy Pottery）还打包票，说日出之前我们准能望见国王的军队。

我打手势提议两人轮流骑乘、一骑一程，他说：「不成，杰克。我这辈子统共骑过一回马，还是骑的斯平克斯牧师（Parson Spinks）那匹红母马，在比迪福德（Bideford）。那些日子牧师正追求托灵顿（Torrington）那头的汉布利寡妇（Widow Hambly）：而我呢，本要往巴恩斯特珀（Barnstaple）去，却搭上一整个上午外加大半个下午，才抢风驶离了托灵顿。到头来左舷升降索断了，母马迎风掉了个樯，我便顺风一路直冲托灵顿而去，鼻血淋漓，不成体统。『天哪！』寡妇嚷道，『错的人骑了对的马！』『恕罪，太太，』我说，『人是顶好的，只是骑错了马，千真万确。』」

一路行去，我不时下马，替莫莉牵着笼头走上一英里左右：去朗塞斯顿的一路上，比尔絮絮讲起我们分别后的种种经历。原来他们离开我之后，一帆风顺到了普利茅斯：可不待卸货，便得到风声，说总督打算为战事扣押当时停泊在普利茅斯湾的所有船只；于是连夜出逃，一溜烟进了卢港（Looe Haven），在那里运气颇佳，把大半货物高价脱了手。正是在卢港卸货的当口，比尔想起还欠着阿尔塔农（Altarnun）一个表亲的债，便把船交给马特·索姆斯（Matt Soames）照管，自己徒步北上，经利斯卡德走到朗塞斯顿，在那里赶上了康沃尔的兵马，也迎面听到斯坦福德伯爵（Earl of Stamford）向东北推进的消息。其后又在贝维尔爵士队中遇到几个相熟的北岸汉子，攀谈之间，便听说了我、以及我骑马奔布拉多克（Braddock）的事——这事（看来）如今已成尽人皆知的谈资。于是他便去寻贝维尔爵士，结果如何，诸君已经知道：「因为，」他如此收尾，「还债兼看热闹，一趟能凑齐两桩乐事，实在难得又痛快。」

===Original===
We had some trouble at Launceston gate, where were a few burghers posted for sentries, and, as I could see, ready to take fright at their own shadows. But Billy gave the watchword (“One and All”), and presently they let us through. As we pass’d along the street we marked a light in every window almost, though ’twas near midnight; and the people moving about behind their curtains. There were groups too in the dark doorways, gather’d there discussing, that eyed us as we went by, and answered Billy’s “Good night, honest men!” very hoarse and doubtfully.

But when we were beyond the town, and between hedges again, I think I must have dozed off in my saddle. For, though this was a road full of sharp memories, being the last I had traveled with Delia, I have no remembrance to have felt them; or, indeed, of noting aught but the fresh night air, and the constellation of the Bear blazing ahead, and Billy’s voice resonant beside me.

And after this I can recall passing the tower of Marham Church, with the paling sky behind it, and some birds chattering in the carved courses: and soon (it seem’d) felt Billy’s grip on my knee, and open’d my eyes to see his finger pointing.

===Chinese===
在朗塞斯顿城门口我们遇到点小麻烦：那里站着几个充任哨兵的市民，而且我看得出，他们连自己的影子都能吓一跳。好在比尔报了口令（「One and All」，即康沃尔人的「万众一心」），他们很快便放行了。沿街走过时，虽已近午夜，几乎家家窗口都亮着灯，窗帘后有人影晃动。昏暗的门洞里也聚着一堆堆人，在那里议论纷纷，眼睛盯着我们经过，对比尔那声「晚安，各位老实人！」的招呼，答得又沙哑又迟疑。

可一出城，重新走在两道树篱之间，我想我准是在马鞍上打起了盹。因为这条路虽满是刺心的旧忆——它是我同德利娅一道走过的最后一段路——我却记不起自己曾为之动过什么情；说实在的，除了清爽的夜风、前方辉耀的大熊星座、身边比尔洪亮的嗓音，我什么也不记得了。

此后我记得行经马勒姆教堂（Marham Church）的塔楼，背后天色渐白，雕砌的线脚间有几只鸟儿啁啾；随即（仿佛转眼间）我觉得比尔一把攥住我的膝盖，睁眼一看，他的手指正指向前方。

===Original===
We stood on a ridge above a hollow vale into which the sun, though now bright, did not yet pierce, but passing over to a high, conical hill beyond, smote level on line after line of white tents﻿—the prettiest sight! ’Twas the enemy there encamped on the top and some way down the sides, the smoke of their trampled watch fires still curling among the gorsebushes. I heard their trumpets calling and drums beating to arms; for though, glancing back at the sun, I judged it to be hardly past four in the morning, yet already the slopes were moving like an anthill﻿—the regiments gathering, arms flashing, horsemen galloping to and fro, and the captains shouting their commands. In the distance this had a sweet and cheerful sound, no more disquieting than a ploughboy calling to his team.

Looking down into the valley at our feet, at first I saw no sign of our own troops﻿—only the roofs of a little town, with overmuch smoke spread above it, like a morning mist. But here also I heard the church bells clashing and a drum beating, and presently spied a gleam of arms down among the trees, and then a regiment of foot moving westward along the base of the hill. ’Twas evident the battle was at hand, and we quicken’d our pace down into the street.

It lay on the slope, and midway down we pass’d some watch fires burn’d out; and then a soldier or two running and fastening their straps; and last a little child, that seem’d wild with the joy of living amid great events, but led us pretty straight to the sign of the Tree, which indeed was the only tavern.

===Chinese===
我们立在一道山脊上，俯临一处凹陷的谷地：太阳虽已明亮，却还照不进谷中，而是越过谷地，平平正正地射在远处一座高耸的圆锥形山丘上，照得一行行白色帐篷雪亮——好看极了！敌人就扎营在那山顶上，并顺着山坡向下延展了颇远一段，被踩踏过的守夜篝火，青烟还在荆豆丛间袅袅盘绕。我听见他们的号角声声召唤、战鼓催动集合；因为虽回头看看日头，我判断不过清晨刚过四点，山坡却已像蚁冢一般骚动起来——各团在集结，兵器闪着光，骑兵来来往往疾驰，队长们高声传着口令。这些声音远远听来竟有几分甜美悦耳，不比农家少年吆喝他的牛队更叫人心慌。

俯看脚下的谷地，起初我看不到我们自己军队的踪影——只有一座小镇的屋顶，镇上腾起过多的烟，像一层晨雾铺展开来。但在那里我也听见教堂钟声急响、一面鼓咚咚敲着，不多时便窥见树影之间兵器一闪，继而又见一个步兵团沿着山脚向西开进。显然大战已迫在眉睫，我们加快脚步，下坡进了街市。

小镇坐落在山坡上，走到半途，我们经过几堆烧尽的篝火；接着是一两个边跑边系皮带的士兵；最后是一个小孩子，看模样为大事件中活着的热闹欢喜得发了狂，却领着我们走得相当顺当，径直找到了「树」字号招牌（the sign of the Tree）——那确乎是镇上唯一的客栈。

===Original===
It stood some way back from the street, with a great elm before the porch: where by a table sat two men, with tankards beside them, and a small company of grooms and soldiers standing round. Both men were more than ordinary tall and soldier like: only the bigger wore a scarlet cloak very richly lac’d, and was shouting orders to his men; while the other, dress’d in plain buff suit and jack boots, had a map spread before him, which he studied very attentively, writing therein with a quill pen.

===Chinese===
客栈自街面向后退进去一段，门廊前立着一棵大榆树：树旁一张桌边坐着两个人，手边摆着大酒杯，周围站着一小群马夫和士兵。两人都生得异乎寻常地高大，一派军人气概：只是那块头更大的一人披一领绦饰华贵的猩红披风，正朝部下大声下令；另一人穿一身素色皮甲、蹬着长筒马靴，面前摊开一幅地图，正潜心细究，用鹅毛笔在上面写着什么。

===Original===
“What a plague have we here?” cries the big man, as we drew up.

“Recruits if it please you, sir,” said I, dismounting and pulling off my hat, though his insolent tone offended me.

“S’lid! The boy speaks as if he were a regiment,” growls he, half aloud: “Can’st fight?”

“That, with your leave, sir, is what I am come to try.”

===Chinese===
我们收步站定，那大个子嚷道：「这儿来的是什么货色？」

「回大人的话，是来投军的，」我说着翻身下马、摘下帽子，尽管他那傲慢的口气冒犯了我。

「见鬼！这小子开口倒像自己就带着一个团，」他半自言自语地咕哝：「你会打仗吗？」

「大人容禀，这正是我前来一试的。」

===Original===
“And this rascal?” He turned on Billy.

Billy heard not a word, of course, yet answered readily﻿—

“Why, since your honor is so pleasantly minded﻿—let it be cider.”

Now the first effect of this, deliver’d with all force of lung, was to make the big man sit bolt upright and staring: recovering speech, however, he broke into a volley of blasphemous curses.

===Chinese===
「这个泼皮呢？」他转向比尔。

比尔自然一个字也没听见，却应声便答——

「呃，既然大人您兴致这么好——那就来苹果酒吧。」

这话扯足了嗓门吼将出来，头一个效果，便是叫那大个子猛地坐直、瞪眼呆望：待回过神来能开口了，他劈头便是一连串渎神的咒骂。

===Original===
All this while the man in buff had scarce lifted his eyes off the map. But now he looks up﻿—and I saw at the first glance that the two men hated each other.

“I think,” said he quietly, “my Lord Mohun has forgot to ask the *gentleman’s* name.”

“My name is Marvel, sir﻿—John Marvel.” I answer’d him with a bow.

“Hey!”﻿—and dropping his pen he starts up and grasps my hand﻿—“Then ’tis you I have never thanked for His Gracious Majesty’s letter.”

“The General Hopton?” cried I.

===Chinese===
这一通闹腾里，那穿皮甲的人几乎没有从地图上抬过眼。可此刻他抬起头来——我一眼便看出，这两个人彼此相恶。

「我想，」他不动声色地说，「莫恩勋爵（Lord Mohun）忘了请教这位*绅士*的尊姓大名。」

「在下名叫马弗尔，大人——约翰·马弗尔。」我躬身答道。

「哎呀！」——他丢下鹅毛笔，霍地起身，一把攥住我的手——「那么，替圣明的国王陛下递送密信、而我始终不曾道谢的，正是您了。」

「霍普顿将军？」我叫道。

===Original===
“Even so, sir. My lord,” he went on, still holding my hand and turning to his companion, “let me present to you the gentleman that in January sav’d your house of Bocconnoc from burning at the hands of the rebels﻿—whom God confound this day!” He lifted his hat.

“Amen,” said I, as his lordship bowed, exceedingly sulky. But I did not value his rage, being hot with joy to be so beprais’d by the first captain (as I yet hold) on the royal side. Who now, not without a sly triumph, flung the price of Billy’s cider on the table and, folding up his map, address’d me again﻿—

“Master Marvel, the fight today will lie but little with the horse﻿—or so I hope. You will do well, if your wish be to serve us best, to leave your mare behind. The troop which my Lord Mohun and I command together is below. But Sir Bevill Grenville, who has seen and is interested in you, has the first claim: and I would not deny you the delight to fight your first battle under so good a master. His men are, with Sir John Berkeley’s troop, a little to the westward: and if you are ready I will go some distance with you, and put you in the way to find him. My lord, may we look for you presently?”

===Chinese===
「正是，先生。」他仍握着我的手，转向他的同伴，继续道：「大人，请容我引见这位绅士：今年一月，正是他从叛军手里救下了您的博科诺克宅邸（Bocconnoc），使它免遭焚毁——愿上帝今日便叫那帮叛军溃灭！」他举了举帽子。

「阿门，」我说；勋爵大人躬了躬身，一脸愠怒。我却不在乎他的火气，因为被王党一方（我至今仍这般认为）首屈一指的统帅当面如此称许，我心头正热烘烘地欢喜。这位将军此刻不无狡黠地带着几分得意，把比尔那份苹果酒钱抛在桌上，收起地图，又向我开口——

「马弗尔先生，今日之战，骑兵派不上多少用场——至少我希望如此。您若想为我们效力至大，最好把母马留下。莫恩大人与我共同指挥的那队骑兵就在坡下。只是贝维尔·格伦维尔爵士（Sir Bevill Grenville）见过您、对您颇为属意，他有优先之权：我也不忍夺去您头一仗便在如此良将麾下效命的乐趣。他的人马与约翰·贝克利爵士（Sir John Berkeley）的骑兵队一道，在西边不远：您若准备好了，我陪您走上一程，把寻他的路径指点给您。大人，稍后阵前可有望见到您吗？」

===Original===
The Lord Mohun nodded, surly enough: so, Billy’s cider being now drunk and Molly given over to an ostler, we set out down the hill together, Billy shouldering a pipe and walking after with the groom that led Sir Ralph’s horse. Be sure the General’s courtly manner of speech set my blood tingling. I seem’d to grow a full two inches taller; and when, in the vale, we parted, he directing me to the left, where through a gap I could see Sir Bevill’s troop forming at some five hundred paces’ distance, I felt a very desperate warrior indeed; and set off at a run, with Billy behind me.

’Twas an open space we had to cross, dotted with gorsebushes; and the enemy’s regiments, plain to see, drawn up in battalia on the slope above, which here was gentler than to the south and west. But hardly had we gone ten yards than I saw a puff of white smoke above, then another, and then the summit ring’d with flame; and heard the noise of it roaring in the hills around. At the first sound I pull’d up, and then began running again at full speed: for I saw our division already in motion, and advancing up the hill at a quick pace.

===Chinese===
莫恩勋爵点了点头，阴沉依旧：于是比尔的苹果酒也喝干了，莫莉也交托给了马夫，我们便一道动身下山；比尔扛着一杆烟斗，跟在替拉尔夫爵士牵马的马僮一旁走。将军那温文有礼的谈吐，确实叫我热血发麻。我仿佛登时长高了整整两英寸；到谷中分手时，他指点我向左，透过一道豁口，我望见五百步开外贝维尔爵士的队伍正在列队，那一刻我自觉真真成了一员亡命的勇士；于是撒腿便跑，比尔跟在我身后。

我们要穿过的是一片开阔地，星点散布着荆豆丛；上面的山坡上，敌军的各团列阵分明，看得清清楚楚——这一面的坡势比南面和西面都平缓些。可我们才走出十码，就见上方腾起一缕白烟，接着又是一缕，随即整个山顶环着一圈火焰轰然炸响；那声浪在四周群山间滚滚回荡。头一声响时我猛地收步，随即又拔腿全速奔跑：因为我看见我们的部队已经出动，正以急促的步伐向山上推进。

===Original===
The curve of the slope hid all but the nearest: but above them I saw a steep earthwork, and thereon three or four brass pieces of ordnance glittering whenever the smoke lifted. For here the artillery was plying the briskest, pouring down volley on volley; and four regiments at least stood mass’d behind, ready to fall on the Cornishmen; who, answering with a small discharge of musketry, now ran forward more nimbly.

To catch up with them, I must now turn my course obliquely up the hill, where running was pretty toilsome. We were panting along when suddenly a shower of sand and earth was dash’d in my face, spattering me all over. Half-blinded, I look’d and saw a great round shot had ploughed a trench in the ground at my feet, and lay there buried.

At the same moment, Billy, who was running at my shoulder, plumps down on his knees and begins to whine and moan most pitiably.

“Art hurt, dear fellow?” asked I, turning.

“Oh, Jack, Jack﻿—I have no stomach for this! A cool, wet death at sea I do not fear; only to have the great hot shot burning in a man’s belly﻿—’tis terrifying. I *hate* a swift death! Jack, I be a sinner﻿—I will confess: I lied to thee yesterday﻿—never kiss’d the three maids I spoke of﻿—never kiss’d but one i’ my life, an’ her a tap-wench, that slapp’d my face for ’t, an’ so don’t properly count. I be a very boastful man!”

Now I myself had felt somewhat cold inside when the guns began roaring: but this set me right in a trice. I whipp’d a pistol out of my sash and put the cold ring to his ear: and he scrambled up; and was a very lion all the rest of the day.

But now we had again to change our course, for to my dismay I saw a line of sharpshooters moving down among the gorsebushes, to take the Cornishmen in flank. And ’twas lucky we had but a little way further to go; for these skirmishers, thinking perhaps from my dress and our running thus that we bore some message open’d fire on us: and though they were bad marksmen, ’twas ugly to see their bullets pattering into the turf, to right and left.

===Chinese===
坡面的弧度遮住了远处，只看得见最近的部队：但在他们上方，我望见一道陡峭的土垒，垒上三四门铜炮，每当烟散便闪着寒光。因为此处炮兵打得最是急切，一排排炮弹倾泻而下；土垒后至少集结着四个团，只待扑杀康沃尔人；而康沃尔人以一阵稀落的排枪回敬，此刻向前奔得愈加轻捷。

要追上他们，我这时只得斜切着向山上跑，这段路跑起来相当吃力。我们正喘息前行，忽然一阵沙土劈头盖脸砸在我脸上，溅了我满身。我半睁半闭地看去，只见一颗大铁弹已在我脚边犁出一道沟，正埋在那儿。

与此同时，跑在我肩旁的比尔扑通一声跪倒在地，开始呜咽哀号，可怜到了极点。

「你受伤了吗，亲爱的伙计？」我转身问。

「哦，杰克，杰克——我可没胃口应付这个！在海上凉丝丝、湿漉漉地死一场，我不怕；唯独叫那滚烫的大弹丸在人肚肠里烧着——那才吓人。我*恨*的就是这种快死法！杰克，我是个罪人——我要忏悔：昨天我对你撒了谎——我先前夸口的那三个娘们，压根儿一个也没亲过——这辈子统共只亲过一个，还是个酒馆掌酒的丫头，为这事还挨了她一记耳光，所以严格算不得数。我是个顶爱吹牛的人！」

说实在的，大炮轰鸣起来时，我自己心里也发过一阵凉：可他这一闹，顿时把我治好了。我唰地从腰带里拔出一支手枪，把那冰冷的枪口圈贴上他的耳朵：他一骨碌爬起来；这一天余下的时辰里，他都勇猛得像头狮子。

可这时我们只得再度改道，因为令我惊愕的是，我看见一列狙击兵正沿着荆豆丛向下移动，要去侧击康沃尔人。所幸我们只剩一小段路要走；因为那些散兵，许是从我的装束和我们这般奔跑的模样，推断我们身负传令的差事，竟朝我们开了火：他们枪法虽然蹩脚，可眼见子弹啪嗒啪嗒落进左右两侧的草皮，终究不是滋味。

===Original===
We caught up the very last line of the ascending troop﻿—lean, hungry looking men, with wan faces, but shouting lustily. I think they were about three hundred in all. “Come on, lad,” called out a bearded fellow with a bandage over one eye, making room for me at his side; “there’s work for plenty more!”﻿—and a minute after, a shot took him in the ribs, and he scream’d out “Oh, my God!” and flinging up his arms, leap’d a foot in air and fell on his face.

Pressing up, I noted that the first line was now at the foot of the earthwork; and, in a minute, saw their steel caps and crimson sashes swarming up the face of it, and their pikes shining. But now came a shock, and the fellow in front was thrust back into my arms. I reeled down a pace or two and then, finding foothold, stood pushing. And next, the whole body came tumbling back on me, and down the hill we went flying, with oaths and cries. Three of the rebel regiments had been flung on us and by sheer weight bore us before them. At the same time the sharpshooters pour’d in a volley: and I began to see how a man may go through a battle, and be beat, without striking a blow.

But in the midst of this scurry I heard the sound of cheering. ’Twas Sir John Berkeley’s troop (till now posted under cover of the hedges below) that had come to our support; and the rebels, fearing to advance too far, must have withdrawn again behind their earthwork, for after a while the pressure eas’d a bit, and, to my amaze, the troop which but a minute since was a mere huddled crowd, formed in some order afresh, and once more began to climb. This time, I had a thickset pikeman in front of me, with a big wen at the back of his neck that seem’d to fix all my attention. And up we went, I counting the beat of my heart that was already going hard and short with the work; and then, amid the rattle and thunder of their guns, we stopp’d again.

===Chinese===
我们赶上了登山队伍的最后一线——都是些精瘦、面带饥色的汉子，脸色苍白，喊声却响亮。我想他们总共约三百人。「来吧，小伙子，」一个独眼蒙着绷带的大胡子汉子扬声喊道，在身旁给我腾出位置；「活儿多着呢，不嫌人多！」——一分钟后，一颗子弹打中他的肋部，他惨叫一声「哦，我的上帝！」双臂向上一扬，蹦起一英尺高，脸朝下栽倒在地。

奋力向上挤时，我注意到第一线已到了土垒脚下；转眼间，便见他们的铁盔和绯红饰带密密麻麻涌上垒壁，长矛闪亮。可这时猛然一撞，前面的汉子被直挺挺搡进我怀里。我踉跄着倒退一两步，随即找到立足处，站定死顶。紧接着，整支队伍哗啦啦朝我压塌下来，我们骂着、喊着，一路飞跌下山。叛军三个团朝我们压来，单凭人马的分量便把我们冲得节节败退。与此同时，狙击兵又泼来一阵排枪：我这才开始明白，一个人如何能打完一整仗、挨了败，却一枪未放、一刀未出。

但就在这仓皇溃退之中，我听见了欢呼声。那是约翰·贝克利爵士的骑兵队（此前一直隐蔽在坡下的树篱后）赶来支援我们；叛军惧怕推进过深，想必又撤回了他们的土垒后面，因为过了一阵，压力稍见松动，而令我惊叹的是，刚才还是一团乱糟糟人堆的这支队伍，竟重新排出几分阵形，再度开始向上攀爬。这一回，我前头是个矮壮的长矛兵，后脖颈上生着个大肉瘤，把我整副心神都勾了去。我们向上爬，我数着自己心跳的节拍——那颗心早因这份苦役跳得又重又促；随后，在他们枪炮的爆响与轰鸣声中，我们又停了下来。

===Original===
I had taken no notice of it, but in the confusion of the first repulse the greater part of our men had been thrust past me, so that now I found myself no further back than the fourth rank, and at the very foot of the earthwork, up the which our leaders were flung like a wave; and soon I was scrambling after them, ankle deep in the sandy earth, the man with the wen just ahead, grinding my instep with his heel and poking his pike staff between my knees as he slipp’d.

And just at the moment when the top of our wave was cleaving a small breach above us, he fell on the flat of his pike, with his nose buried in the gravel and his hands clutching. Looking up I saw a tall rebel straddling above him with musket clubb’d to beat his brains out: whom with an effort I caught by the boot; and, the bank slipping at that instant, down we all slid in a heap, a jumble of arms and legs, to the very bottom.

===Chinese===
我先前不曾留意，原来第一次溃退的混乱中，我们大部分弟兄已被挤到了我身后，以致此刻我发现自己离最前不过第四列，正站在土垒脚下；我们的前锋像一朵浪头般向垒顶扑去；很快我也手脚并用地跟了上去，双脚陷进沙土没到脚踝，长肉瘤的汉子就在前头，脚跟碾着我的脚背，身子一滑便把矛杆戳进我两膝之间。

恰在我们这朵浪头楔开头顶一小段缺口的当口，他一头栽在自己的矛杆上，鼻子扎进砾石，双手乱抓。我抬头一看，一个高个子叛军正跨立在他上方，倒抡着火枪要砸碎他的脑袋：我奋力一把攥住那人的靴子；正在这时垒土一滑，我们几个便胳膊缠着腿地滚作一堆，一路滑到了最底下。

===Original===
Before I had the sand well out of my eyes, my comrade was up and had his pike loose; and in a twinkling, the rebel was spitted through the middle and writhing. ’Twas sickening: but before I could pull out my pistol and end his pain (as I was minded), back came our front rank atop of us again, and down they were driven like sheep, my companion catching up the dead man’s musket and ammunition bag, and I followed down the slope with three stout rebels at my heels. “What will be the end of *this*?” thought I. The end was, that after forty yards or so, finding the foremost close upon me, I turn’d about and let fly with my pistol at him. He spun round twice and dropp’d: which I was wondering at (the pistol being but a poor weapon for aim) when I was caught by the arm and pull’d behind a clump of bushes handy by. ’Twas the man with the wen, and by his smoking musket I knew that ’twas he had fired the shot that killed my pursuer.

“Good turn for good turn,” says he: “quick with thy other pistol!”

The other two had stopped doubtfully, but at the next discharge of my pistol they turn’d tail and went up the hill again, and we were left alone. And suddenly I grew aware that my head was aching fit to split, and lay down on the turf, very sick and ill.

===Chinese===
我眼里的沙子还没揉干净，我的同伴已经爬起，抽出了长矛；一眨眼工夫，那叛军已被捅了个对穿，在地上扭动。那光景叫人作呕：可不等我拔出手枪了结他的痛苦（我本有此意），我们的前排又反压回我们头顶，接着他们像羊群一般被驱赶着退下坡去，我的同伴顺手抄起死者的火枪和弹药袋，我则顺着坡往下走，身后紧缀着三个壮实的叛军。「*这*般下去可怎么了局？」我想。结局是：跑了四十码上下，见头一个已近在咫尺，我猛地回身，一枪朝他打去。他原地转了两圈便倒下了：我正自纳闷（手枪这物件本就瞄不准），忽被人一把抓住手臂，拽到了近旁一丛灌木后面。正是那个长肉瘤的汉子，凭他那支还冒烟的火枪，我知道，方才击毙我追兵的那一枪便是他放的。

「好心换好心，」他说，「快使你另一支手枪！」

另外两个迟疑地收住了脚，可等我另一枪一响，他们便掉转尾巴又往山上跑去，只剩我们两个。忽然间，我觉得头痛得像要裂开，便在草皮上躺了下来，病恹恹地难受极了。

===Original===
My comrade took no notice of this, but, going for the dead man’s musket, kept loading and firing, pausing now and then for his artillery to cool, and whistling a tune that runs in my head to this day. And all the time I heard shouts and cries and the noise of musketry all around, which made me judge that the attack was going on in many places at once. When I came to myself ’twas to hear a bugle below calling again to the charge, and once more came the two troops ascending. At their head was a slight built man, bareheaded, with the sun (that was by this, high over the hill) smiting on his brown curls, and the wind blowing them. He carried a naked sword in his hand, and waved his men forward as cheerfully as though ’twere a dance and he leading out his partner.

“Who is that yonder?” asked I, sitting up and pointing.

“Bless thy innocent heart!” said my comrade, “dostn’t thee know? ’Tis Sir Bevill.”

===Chinese===
我的同伴对此毫不理会，只顾去取死者的火枪，装了又打、打了又装，间或停一停，好叫他的「重炮」冷却冷却，嘴里还吹着一支曲子——那调子至今还在我脑中萦绕。这段时间里，我始终听得四周喊杀声、哀号声与火枪声不绝于耳，叫我断定各处攻势正同时展开。待我回过神来，听见山下一支军号又在吹响冲锋号，那两队人马再度向上涌来。走在最前的是个身形瘦削的人，光着头，太阳（此刻已高过山顶）照在他棕色的鬈发上，风把鬈发吹得飘起。他手中提着一柄出鞘的剑，招呼部下前进的那份欢快劲儿，仿佛这是场舞会，他正牵着舞伴领入场中。

「那边那位是谁？」我坐起身来，指着问道。

「保佑你这天真的心肠！」我的同伴说，「你认不得？那是贝维尔爵士。」

===Original===
---

’Twould be tedious to tell the whole of this long fight, which, beginning soon after sunrise, ended not till four in the afternoon, or thereabouts: and indeed of the whole my recollection is but of continual advance and repulse on that same slope. And herein may be seen the wisdom of our generals, in attacking while the main body of the enemy’s horse was away: for had the Earl of Stamford possessed a sufficient force of dragoons to let slip on us at the first discomfiture, there is little doubt he might have ended the battle there and then. As it was, the horse stood out of the fray, theirs upon the summit of the hill, ours (under Col. John Digby) on the other slope, to protect the town and act as reserve.

===Chinese===
---

这场漫长的鏖战，日出后不久开打，直到下午四点上下方才收场；巨细无遗地讲一遍未免乏味：说实在的，对全过程，我的记忆只剩下在同一面山坡上无休止的冲锋与溃退。由此可以看出我们将领们的老成谋算：趁敌方骑兵主力不在时发动攻击——倘若斯坦福德伯爵手里握有一支足够的龙骑兵，可在我们初挫之际放出来扑杀，那么毫无疑问，他当场便能了结这场战役。实际情形是，双方骑兵都置身战团之外：他们的屯在山顶，我们的（由约翰·迪格比上校（Col. John Digby）统领）在另一面山坡，负责护卫市镇、充作后备。

===Original===
The foot, in four parties, was disposed about the hill on all sides; to the west﻿—as we know﻿—under Sir John Berkeley and Sir Bevill Grenville; to the south under General Hopton and Lord Mohun; to the east under the Colonels Tom Basset and William Godolphin; while the steep side to the north was stormed by Sir Nicholas Slanning and Colonel Godolphin, with their companies. And as we had but eight small pieces of cannon and were in numbers less than one to two, all we had to do was to march up the hill in face of their fire, catch a knock on the head, may be, grin, and come on again.

But at three o’clock, we, having been for the sixth time beaten back, were panting under cover of a hedge, and Sir John Berkeley, near by, was writing on a drumhead some message to the camp, when there comes a young man on horseback, his face smear’d with dirt and dust, and rides up to him and Sir Bevill. ’Twas (I have since learn’d) to say that the powder was all spent but a barrel or two: but this only the captains knew at the time.

“Very well, then,” cries Sir Bevill, leaping up gaily. “Come along, boys﻿—we must do it this time.” And, the troop forming, once more the trumpets sounded the charge, and up we went. Away along the slope we heard the other trumpeters sounding in answer, and I believe ’twas a *sursum corda*! to all of us.

===Chinese===
步兵分作四路，自四面围攻这座山丘：西面——如我们所知——由约翰·贝克利爵士与贝维尔·格伦维尔爵士统领；南面由霍普顿将军与莫恩勋爵统领；东面由汤姆·巴塞特上校（Tom Basset）与威廉·戈多尔芬上校（William Godolphin）统领；陡峭的北面则由尼古拉斯·斯兰宁爵士（Sir Nicholas Slanning）与戈多尔芬上校率各自连队强攻。而我们只有八门小炮，人数以一敌二尚且不足，我们所能做的，便是迎着他们的炮火往山上开进，兴许头上挨一记狠的，咧嘴一笑，再来一回。

可到了三点钟，我们已是第六次被打退，正躲在一段树篱后喘气，近旁的约翰·贝克利爵士正伏在一面鼓面上给大营写着什么公文，这时来了个骑马的年轻人，脸上糊满泥土尘灰，打马来到他和贝维尔爵士跟前。他捎来的是（我后来才得知）火药只剩一两桶的消息：但当时，底细只有几位统领知晓。

「好极了，」贝维尔爵士欢快地一跃而起。「跟上，弟兄们——这一回务必拿下。」队伍列定，军号再一次吹响冲锋，我们向山上进发。沿着山坡远远传来别处号手们应和的号声；我相信，对我们全体而言，那便是一声「举心向上」（*sursum corda*）！

===Original===
Billy Pottery was ranged on my right, in the first rank, and next to me, on the other side, a giant, near seven foot high, who said his name was Anthony Payne and his business to act as body-servant to Sir Bevill. And he it was that struck up a mighty curious song in the Cornish tongue, which the rest took up with a will. ’Twas incredible how it put fire into them all: and Sir Bevill toss’d his hat into the air, and after him like schoolboys we pelted, straight for the masses ahead.

For now over the rampart came a company of red musketeers, and two of russet-clad pikemen, charging down on us. A moment, and we were crushed back: another, and the chant rose again. We were grappling, hand to hand, in the midst of their files.

===Chinese===
比尔·波特里排在我右侧第一列，另一侧挨着我的是个巨人，将近七英尺高，自称名叫安东尼·佩恩（Anthony Payne），营生是给贝维尔爵士当贴身仆从。正是他领头唱起一支康沃尔语的歌，调门古怪极了，众人便齐心跟着唱和起来。那歌给他们全体注入的那股火气，简直不可思议：贝维尔爵士把帽子抛上半空，我们便学童一般发足狂奔，直冲前面那几团人马。

因为这时壁垒上冲下来一队红衣火枪兵和两队褐衣长矛兵，直扑我们。一瞬间，我们被压得倒退；又一瞬间，歌声重新昂扬起来。我们已同他们的队列绞作一团，白刃相搏。

===Original===
But, good lack! What use is swordsmanship in a charge like this? The first red coat that encounter’d me I had spitted through the lung, and, carried on by the rush, he twirled me round like a windmill. In an instant I was pass’d; the giant stepping before me and clearing a space about him, using his pike as if ’twere a flail. With a wrench I tugg’d my sword out and followed. I saw Sir Bevill, a little to the left, beaten to his knee, and carried toward me. Stretching out a hand I pull’d him on his feet again, catching, as I did so, a crack on the skull that would have ended me, had not Billy Pottery put up his pike and broke the force of it. Next, I remember gripping another red coat by the beard and thrusting at him with shortened blade. Then the giant ahead lifted his pike high, and we fought to rally round it; and with that I seem’d caught off my feet and swept forward:﻿—and we were on the crest.

===Chinese===
可是，老天爷！在这样一场冲杀里，剑术还有什么用处？头一个撞上我的红衣兵，我一剑刺穿了他的肺，他借着冲势，把我拧得像风车般直转。转瞬间我已被甩在后面；那巨人抢到我身前，把长矛抡得像连枷，在四周清出一片空地。我猛一使劲拔出剑，跟了上去。我看见贝维尔爵士在左近被打得跪倒，朝我这边跌撞过来。我伸手把他拽起来，与此同时，头上挨了一记闷棍，若非比尔·波特里举矛一架卸去力道，我这条命就交代在那里了。接着，我记得自己揪住另一个红衣兵的胡子，收短了剑锋向他戳刺。然后，前头的巨人把长矛高高举起，我们便簇拥着它死战聚合；我只觉得双脚像被人拔了根，整个人被裹挟着向前卷去：——我们已经登上了山顶。

===Original===
Taking breath, I saw the enemy melting off the summit like a man’s breath off a pane. And Sir Bevill caught my hand and pointed across to where, on the north side, a white standard embroider’d with gold griffins was mounting.

“ ’Tis dear Nick Slanning!” he cried; “God be prais’d﻿—the day is ours for certain!”

===Chinese===
我喘了口气，只见敌人正从山顶上消融退散，如同人口中呵出的气从窗玻璃上消散。贝维尔爵士攥住我的手，指向对面北坡：那里，一面绣着金色狮鹫的白军旗正冉冉升起。

「是亲爱的尼克·斯兰宁！」他喊道；「赞美上帝——这一天必定是我们的了！」

## XVII I Meet with a Happy Adventure by Burning of a Green Light / 我遇到一桩美事——点燃绿光

===Original===
The rest of this signal victory (in which 1,700 prisoners were taken, besides the Major-General Chudleigh; and all the rebels’ camp, cannon and victuals) I leave historians to tell. For very soon after the rout was assured (the plain below full of men screaming and running, and Col. John Digby’s dragoons after them, chasing, cutting, and killing), a wet muzzle was thrust into my hand, and turning, I found Molly behind me, with the groom to whom I had given her in the morning. The rogue had counted on a crown for his readiness, and swore the mare was ready for anything, he having mix’d half a pint of strong ale with her mash, not half an hour before.
