So I determin’d to see the end of it, and paying the fellow, climb’d into the saddle. On the summit the Cornish captains were now met, and cordially embracing. ’Tis very sad in these latter times to call back their shouts and boyish laughter, so soon to be quench’d on Lansdowne slopes, or by Bristol graff. Yet, O favor’d ones!﻿—to chase Victory, to grasp her flutt’ring skirt, and so, with warm, panting cheeks, kissing her, to fall, escaping evil days!

How could they laugh? For me, the late passionate struggle left me shaken with sobs; and for the starting tears I saw neither moors around, nor sun, nor twinkling sea. Brushing them away, I was aware of Billy Pottery striding at my stirrup, and munching at a biscuit he had found in the rebels’ camp. Said he, “In season, Jack, is in reason. There be times to sing an’ to dance, to marry and to give in marriage; an’ likewise times to become as wax: but now, lookin’ about an’ seein’ no haughty slaughterin’ cannon but has a Cornishman seated ’pon the touch-hole of the same, says I in my thoughtsome way, ‘Forbear!’ ”

===Chinese===
这场辉煌的胜利（俘虏1,700 人，包括查德利少将；叛军的全部营地、火炮和粮草）的后续，我留给史家去讲述。因为战局方定（山下平原上到处是尖叫奔逃的人，约翰·迪格比上校的龙骑兵在后面追砍屠戮），一匹湿漉漉的马嘴便塞进我手里；我转身一看，莫莉（Molly）就在我身后，身边是早晨我把她托付的那个马夫。这滑头指望着为他的殷勤赚一枚克朗，还信誓旦旦说这母马什么都能干，因为他在不到半个钟头前把半品脱烈性麦酒拌进了她的草料。

于是我决意看个究竟，付了那家伙的钱，翻身上马。山顶上，康沃尔的军官们此刻正相聚一处，热烈拥抱。在此后岁月里回想起他们的呼喊与少年般的笑声，实在令人感伤——这些声音不久便将在兰斯当山坡上或布里斯托尔壕沟边沉寂。然而，哦，受眷顾的人们！——追逐胜利，攥住她飘飞的裙裾，就这样，带着灼热而急促的双颊亲吻她，然后倒下，逃过那些凶险的日子！

他们怎笑得出？至于我，刚才那场激烈的搏斗仍让我因啜泣而颤抖；泪眼朦胧中，我看不见周围的荒原、阳光，也看不见闪烁的海面。我抹去泪水，瞥见比尔·波特里（Billy Pottery）正大步走在我马镫旁，嘴里嚼着他从叛军营地里捡来的一块饼干。他说："该笑的时候笑，该哭的时候哭，杰克，这才有道理。人有该唱该跳的时候，有该嫁该娶的时候；同样也有该像蜡一样融化的时候。可眼下，我四下张望，看见那些威风凛凛、杀人不眨眼的火炮全被康沃尔人按住了火门，我便用我那沉思默想的劲头说：'且慢！'"

===Original===
Presently he pulls up before a rebel trooper, that was writhing on the slope with a shatter’d thigh, yet raised himself on his fists to gaze on us with wide, painful eyes.

“Good sirs,” gasp’d out the rebel, “can you tell me﻿—where be Nat Shipward?”

“Now how should I know?” I answer’d.

“ ’A had nutty-brown curls, an’ wore a red jacket﻿—Oh, as straight a young man as ever pitched hay! ’a sarved in General Chudleigh’s troop﻿—a very singular straight young man.”

“Death has taken a many such,” said I, and thought on the man I had run through in our last charge.

The fellow groaned. “ ’A was my son,” he said: and though Billy pull’d out a biscuit (his pockets bulged with them) and laid it beside him, he turn’d from it, and sank back on the turf again.

We left him, and now, the descent being gentler, broke into a run, in hopes to catch up with Col. John Digby’s dragoons, that already were far across the next vale. The slope around us was piled with dead and dying, whereof four out of every five were rebels; and cruelly they cursed us as we passed them by. Night was coming on apace; and here already we were in deep shadow, but could see the yellow sun on the hills beyond. We crossed a stream at the foot, and were climbing again. Behind us the cheering yet continued, though fainter: and fainter grew the cries and shouting in front. Soon we turn’d into a lane over a steep hedge, under the which two or three stout rebels were cowering. As we came tumbling almost atop of them, they ran yelling: and we let them go in peace.

===Chinese===
不多时，他在一名叛军骑兵面前停住。那骑兵在坡上痛苦扭动，大腿被打碎了，却仍用拳头撑起身子，睁着一双充满痛楚的大眼睛望着我们。

"先生们，"叛军喘着气说，"你们能告诉我——纳特·希普沃德（Nat Shipward）在哪儿？"

"我怎么知道？"我回答。

"他有一头坚果色的卷发，穿红外套——哦，是天底下最挺拔的小伙子！他在查德利少将的骑兵队里当差——一个非常出众的挺拔青年。"

"死亡带走了不少这样的人，"我说，心里想着我在最后一次冲锋中刺倒的那个人。

那汉子呻吟道："他是我儿子。"尽管比尔掏出一块饼干（他口袋里鼓鼓囊囊的全是饼干）放在他身边，他却扭过脸去，重新瘫倒在草皮上。

我们撇下他。这时下坡路平缓了些，我们便跑起来，希望能追上约翰·迪格比上校的龙骑兵，他们早已远在下一道山谷里。我们四周的斜坡上堆满了尸体和奄奄一息的人，十之八九是叛军；他们恶毒地咒骂着我们。夜色正迅速降临；我们这边已陷入深深的阴影，但还能看见远处山丘上那一轮昏黄的落日。我们在山脚下涉过一条溪流，又开始攀登。身后的欢呼声仍在继续，只是微弱了些；前方的呼喊和喧哗声也越来越小。不久，我们拐进一条被陡峭树篱夹住的小路，树篱底下蜷缩着两三个壮实的叛军。我们几乎是跌跌撞撞地冲到他们面前，他们便尖叫着逃跑了；我们放他们安然离去。

===Original===
The lane gradually led us to westward, out of the main line of the rout, and past a hamlet where every door was shut and all silent. And at last a slice of the sea fronted us, between two steeply shelving hills. On the crest of the road, before it plunged down toward the coast, was a wagon lying against the hedge, with the horses gone: and beside it, stretch’d across the road, an old woman. Stopping, we found her dead, with a sword-thrust through the left breast; and inside the wagon a young man lying, with his jaw bound up﻿—dead also. And how this sad spectacle happened here, so far from the battlefield, was more than we could guess.

I was moving away, when Billy, that was kneeling in the road, chanced to cast his eyes up toward the sea, and dropping the dead woman’s hand scrambled on his feet and stood looking, with a puzzled face.

Following his gaze, I saw a small sloop moving under shorten’d canvas, about two miles from the land. She made a pleasant sight, with the last rays of sunlight flaming on her sails: but for Billy’s perturbation I could not account, so turn’d an enquiring glance to him.

“Suthin’ i’ the wind out yonder,” was his answer: “What’s a sloop doing on that ratch so close in by the point? Be dang’d! but there she goes again;”﻿—as the little vessel swung off a point or two further from the breeze, that was breathing softly up Channel. “Time to sup, lad, for the both of us,” he broke off shortly.

===Chinese===
小路渐渐把我们引向西边，离开了溃军的主道，经过一个家家户门紧闭、万籁俱寂的小村。最后，两座陡峭下倾的山丘之间，一片海面迎面展现。在道路最高处、它即将向下直插海岸的地方，一辆马车倚在树篱边，马匹已不见踪影；车旁横着一个老妇人，拦在路中央。我们停下一看，她已经死了，左胸被一剑刺穿；马车里还躺着一个年轻男子，下巴用布包扎着——也已气绝。这场面如此悲惨，却出现在远离战场的地方，实在令人费解。

我正要走开，跪在路中央的比尔偶然抬眼望向大海，随即丢下那老妇的手，爬起来站着，一脸困惑地凝望着。

顺着他的目光望去，我看见一艘小帆船正收帆而行，离岸大约两英里。夕阳的余晖在船帆上燃烧，那景象十分悦目；但我猜不透比尔为何如此不安，便投去询问的一瞥。

"那边有点风向不对，"他答道，"一艘单桅帆船在那航道上、离岬角这么近的地方干什么？见鬼！它又动了——"那小船随着海峡里轻轻吹来的微风又偏移了一两度。"时候不早了，伙计，咱俩都该吃晚饭了。"他干脆地打住了话头。

===Original===
Indeed, I was faint with hunger by this time, yet had no stomach to eat thus close to the dead. So turning into a gate on our left hand, we cross’d two or three fields, and sat down to sup off Billy’s biscuits, the mare standing quietly beside us, and cropping the short grass.

The field where we now found ourselves ran out along the top of a small promontory, and ended, without fence of any sort, at the cliff’s edge. As I sat looking southward, I could only observe the sloop by turning my head: but Billy, who squatted over against me, hardly took his eyes off her, and between this and his meal was too busy to speak a word. For me, I had enough to do thinking over the late fight: and being near worn out, had half a mind to spend the night there on the hard turf: for, though the sun was now down and the landscape grey, yet the air was exceeding warm: and albeit, as I have said, there breath’d a light breeze now and then, ’twas hardly cool enough to dry the sweat off me. So I stretch’d myself out, and found it very pleasant to lie still; nor, when Billy stood up and sauntered off toward the far end of the headland, did I stir more than to turn my head and lazily watch him.

He was gone half an hour at the least, and the sky by this time was so dark, that I had lost sight of him, when, rising on my elbow to look around, I noted a curious red glow at a point where the turf broke off, not three hundred yards behind me, and a thin smoke curling up in it, as it seem’d, from the very face of the cliff below. In a minute or so the smoke ceased almost; but the shine against the sky continued steady, though not very strong. “Billy has lit a fire,” I guessed, and was preparing to go and look, when I spied a black form crawling toward me, and presently saw ’twas Billy himself.

===Chinese===
此时我确实饿得发昏，但在离死者这么近的地方实在吃不下东西。于是我们拐进左边一道门，穿过两三块田地，坐下来就着比尔的饼干当晚餐，那匹母马安安静静地站在我们身旁，啃着短草。

我们此刻所在的田野沿着一个小岬角的顶脊延伸，没有任何围栏，尽头便是悬崖边。我面朝南方坐着，要观察那艘帆船就得转头；但比尔蹲在我对面，眼睛几乎没离开过船，他一边吃饭一边忙着看船，一句话也顾不上说。我呢，则忙着回想方才那场战斗；加上精疲力竭，真想就在这硬草皮上睡一夜：因为虽然太阳已经落山，景色灰蒙蒙的，空气却异常温暖；虽说偶尔有微风拂过，却也不够凉爽，没能把我身上的汗吹干。于是我舒展身体躺下，觉得这样静静地躺着很舒服；比尔起身朝岬角尽头踱去时，我也只是懒洋洋地转过头看他一眼，并未起身。

他去了至少半个小时，天色已经暗得我完全看不见他了。我支起胳膊肘环顾四周，注意到身后不到三百码的地方，草皮断裂处有一抹奇异的红光，一缕细烟袅袅升起，仿佛就从崖壁下方飘出来。大约一分钟后烟几乎散了，但映在天幕上的光依然稳定，虽不很亮。"比尔点了一堆火，"我猜想，正要起身去看，却瞥见一个黑色身影朝我爬来，定睛一看正是比尔。

===Original===
Coming close, he halted, put a finger to his lip and beckoned: then began to lead the way back as he had come.

Thought I, “these are queer doings:” but left Molly to browse, and crept after him on hands and knees. He turn’d his head once to make sure I was following, and then scrambled on quicker, but softly, toward the point where the red glow was shining.

Once more he pull’d up﻿—as I judg’d, about twelve paces’ distance from the edge﻿—and after considering for a second, began to move again; only now he worked a little to the right. And soon I saw the intention of this: for just here the cliff’s lip was cleft by a fissure﻿—very like that in Scawfell which we were used to call the Lord’s Rake, only narrower﻿—that ran back into the field and shelved out gently at the top, so that a man might easily scramble some way down it, though how far I could not then tell. And ’twas from this fissure that the glow came.

Along the right lip of this Billy led me, skirting it by a couple of yards, and wriggling on his belly like a blind worm. Crawling closer now (for ’twas hard to see him against the black turf), I stopp’d beside him and strove to quiet the violence of my breathing. Then, after a minute’s pause, together we pulled ourselves to the edge, and peer’d over.

The descent of the gully was broken, some eight feet below us, by a small ledge, sloping outward about six feet (as I guess), and screen’d by branches of the wild tamarisk. At the back, in an angle of the solid rock, was now set a pan pierced with holes, and full of burning charcoal: and over this a man in the rebels’ uniform was stooping.

===Chinese===
他走到近前停下，把一根手指按在唇边，招了招手，然后转身沿来路领我回去。

我心里嘀咕："这可真够蹊跷的。"但还是丢下莫莉去吃草，手脚并用地跟在他身后爬过去。他回头确认我跟上了，便更快但更轻地朝红光闪烁的方向爬去。

他又停了下来——我估计离崖边大约十二步——考虑了片刻，又开始移动，只是这次稍微向右偏了些。我很快便明白了意图：因为崖壁的边缘恰在此处被一道裂缝劈开——很像我们常去的斯科费尔山（Scawfell）上那条被称为"主教之耙"（Lord's Rake）的裂隙，只是更窄些——裂缝向田野深处延伸，顶部缓缓倾斜，一个人可以轻松地往下爬一段，但当时我无法判断能爬多远。红光正是从这道裂缝中透出来的。

比尔领着我沿裂缝右侧的边缘前进，贴着边缘绕过几码，像一条盲蛇似的匍匐爬行。我离得更近了些（因为在黑色草皮的映衬下很难看清他），在他身旁停下，竭力平息剧烈的喘息。然后，我们等了一分钟，一起爬到崖边，探头向下望去。

峡谷的陡坡在我们下方约八英尺处被一块小小的岩台打断，岩台向外倾斜约六英尺（我估摸着），野柽柳的枝叶遮挡着它。岩台后部，岩石的凹角里放着一个凿了孔的铁盘，里面满是燃烧的炭火；一个身穿叛军制服的男人正弯腰对着炭火。

===Original===
He had a small paper parcel in his left hand, and was blowing at the charcoal with all his might. Holding my breath, I heard him clearly, but could see nothing of his face, for his back was toward us, all sable against the glow. The charcoal fumes as they rose chok’d me so, that I was very near a fit of coughing, when Billy laid one hand on my shoulder, and with the other pointed out to seaward.

Looking that way, I saw a small light shining on the sea, pretty close in. ’Twas a lantern hung out from the sloop, as I concluded on the instant: and now I began to have an inkling of what was toward.

But looking down again at the man with the charcoal pan I saw a black head of hair lifted, and then a pair of red puff’d cheeks, and a pimpled nose with a scar across the bridge of it﻿—all shining in the glare of the pan.

“Powers of Heaven!” I gasped; “ ’tis that bloody villain Luke Settle!”

And springing to my feet, I took a jump over the edge and came sprawling on top of him. The scoundrel was stooping with his nose close to the pan, and had not time to turn before I lit with a thud on his shoulders, flattening him on the ledge and nearly sending his face on top of the live coal. ’Twas so sudden that, before he could so much as think, my fingers were about his windpipe, and the both of us struggling flat on the brink of the precipice. For he had a bull’s strength, and heaved and kicked, so that I fully looked, next moment, to be flying over the edge into the sea: nor could I loose my grip to get out a pistol, but only held on and worked my fingers in, and thought how he had strangled the mastiff that night on the bowling-green, and vowed to serve him the same if only strength held out.

===Chinese===
他左手攥着一个小纸包，正拼命朝炭火吹气。我屏住呼吸，能清楚地听见他吹气的声音，却看不到他的脸——他背对着我们，在炭火的映衬下漆黑一团。炭火升起的浓烟呛得我几乎要咳嗽，这时比尔一手按住我的肩膀，另一手指向海上。

我朝那边望去，看见海上有一点小光亮，离岸很近。那是帆船上的提灯，我立刻断定：此刻我开始隐约猜到是怎么回事了。

但我再次低头看那个摆弄炭火的人时，只见他抬起一头黑发，接着露出两张红彤彤的鼓腮帮子，还有一个长满脓疮、横着一道伤疤的鼻梁——所有这些都在炭火的映照下闪闪发光。

"天上的神明啊！"我倒吸一口气，"正是那个嗜血的恶棍卢克·塞特尔（Luke Settle）！"

我一跃而起，纵身跳下崖边，直扑到他身上。这无赖鼻子紧贴着炭盘弯着腰，还没来得及转身，我便砰的一声砸在他肩上，把他压扁在岩台上，差点让他的脸直接扑到燃烧的炭火上。一切发生得太快，他还没来得及反应，我的手指已经掐住了他的喉咙，两人在悬崖边缘扭打成一团。他力气大得像头公牛，又顶又踹，我眼看下一秒就会被他掀下崖去，摔进大海。我无法松手去掏手枪，只能死死掐住，把手指往他喉咙里抠，心里想着他那天夜里在滚木球场勒死大狼狗的样子，暗暗发誓只要我还有力气，也要让他尝尝同样的滋味。

===Original===
But now, just as he had almost twisted his neck free, I heard a stone or two break away above us, and down came Billy Pottery flying atop of us, and pinned us to the ledge.

’Twas short work now. Within a minute, Captain Luke Settle was turned on his back, his eyes fairly starting with Billy’s clutch on his throat, his mouth wide open and gasping; till I slipp’d the nozzle of my pistol between his teeth; and with that he had no more chance, but gave in, and like a lamb submitted to have his arms truss’d behind him with Billy’s leathern belt, and his legs with his own.

“Now,” said I, standing over him, and putting the pistol against his temple, “you and I, Master Turncoat Settle, have some accounts that ’twould be well to square. So first tell me, what do you here, and where is Mistress Delia Killigrew?”

I think that till this moment the bully had no idea his assailants were more than a chance couple of Cornish troopers. But now seeing the glow of the burning charcoal on my face, he ripped out a horrid blasphemous curse, and straightway fell to speaking calmly.

“Good sirs, the game is yours, with care. S’lid! but you hold a pretty hand﻿—if only you know how to play it.”

“ ’Tis you shall help me, Captain: but let us be clear about the stakes. For you, ’tis life or death: for me, ’tis to regain Mistress Delia, failing which I shoot you here through the head, and topple you into the sea. You are the Knave of trumps, sir, and I play that card: as matters now stand, only the Queen can save you.”

===Chinese===
可就在他几乎要拧脱脖子挣脱的时候，我听到头顶上几块石头松动滚落，接着比尔·波特里从上面飞扑下来，把我们两个钉在岩台上。

接下来的事很快。不到一分钟，卢克·塞特尔上尉已经仰面朝天，比尔掐住他喉咙的手几乎把他的眼珠挤出来，他嘴巴大张着喘气；我把枪口塞进他牙齿之间；这下他再没有机会了，只得认输，温顺得像只羔羊，任凭比尔用他的皮带反绑双臂，又用他自己的带子捆住双腿。

"好了，"我站在他上方，枪口顶着他的太阳穴，"你和我，塞特尔叛徒先生，有些账该算算清了。先告诉我，你在这儿干什么，德利娅·基利格鲁（Delia Killigrew）小姐在哪儿？"

我想直到此刻，这恶棍还满以为袭击他的不过是两个偶然路过的康沃尔骑兵。但此刻看见我脸上映着炭火的红光，他脱口骂出一句骇人的亵渎之词，随即语气平静地开口。

"先生们，这场牌局你们赢了，恭喜。天哪！你们手上的牌可真漂亮——就看会不会打了。"

"该你帮我了，上尉：不过咱们得把赌注说清楚。对你来说，这是生死攸关；对我，这是救回德利娅小姐。救不回她，我就在这里一枪打穿你的脑袋，把你扔下海去。你现在是那张王牌小丑，先生，我正要打这张牌：眼下的局势，只有王后才能救你。"

===Original===
“Right: but where be King and Ace?”

“The King is the Cornish army, yonder: the Ace is my pistol here, which I hold.”

“And that’s a very pretty comprehension of the game, sir: I play the Queen.”

“Where is she?”

For answer, he pointed seaward, where the sloop’s lantern lay like a floating star on the black waters.

“What!” cried I. “Mistress Delia in that sloop! And who is with her, pray?”

“Why, Black Dick, to begin with﻿—and Reuben Gedges﻿—and Jeremy Toy.”

“All the Knaves left in the pack﻿—God help her!” I muttered, as I look’d out toward the light, and my heart beat heavily. “God help her!” I said again, and turning, spied a grin on the Captain’s face.

“Under Providence,” answered he, “your unworthy servant may suffice. But what is my reward to be?”

“Your neck,” said I, “if I can save it when you are led before the Cornish captains.”

“That’s fair enough: so listen. These few months the lady has been shut in Bristol keep, whither, by the advice of our employer, we conveyed her back safe and sound. This same employer﻿—”

“A dirty rogue, whom you may as well call by his name﻿—Hannibal Tingcomb.”

“Right, young sir: a very dirty rogue, and a niggardly:﻿—I hate a mean rascal. Well, fearing her second escape from that prison, and being hand in glove with the Parliament men, he gets her on board a sloop bound for the Virginias, just at the time when he knows the Earl of Stamford is to march and crush the Cornishmen. For escort she has the three comrades of mine that I named: and the captain of the sloop (a fellow that asks no questions) has orders to cruise along the coast hereabouts till he gets news of the battle.”

===Chinese===
"不错：可国王和王牌在哪儿？"

"国王是那边的康沃尔军队；王牌是我手里的这把手枪。"

"你对这局牌理解得很透彻，先生：我打王后。"

"她在哪儿？"

他朝海上一指作为回答，帆船的提灯像一颗漂浮的星，落在漆黑的水面上。

"什么！"我喊道，"德利娅小姐在那艘船上！请问谁跟她在一起？"

"嗯，先是黑迪克（Black Dick）——还有鲁本·盖奇斯（Reuben Gedges）——还有杰里·托伊（Jeremy Toy）。"

"牌堆里剩下的全是小丑——上帝保佑她！"我低声说，目光朝灯光望去，心脏沉重地跳着。"上帝保佑她！"我又说了一遍，转身时瞥见上尉脸上掠过一丝狞笑。

"托天之福，"他答道，"你卑微的仆人或许还够格。可我的赏赐是什么？"

"你的命，"我说，"只要我在康沃尔军官们面前能保住它。"

"这很公平：那么听着。这几个月来，这位小姐一直被关在布里斯托尔城堡里。在我们雇主的授意下，我们把她安全无恙地押送回去。这位雇主——"

"一个卑鄙的恶棍，你不妨直呼其名——汉尼拔·廷科姆（Hannibal Tingcomb）。"

"对，少爷：一个非常卑鄙的恶棍，还吝啬得很——我讨厌这种下作的无赖。嗯，他担心她再次从那座监狱逃走，又跟议会军的人勾结在一起，便把她送上一艘开往弗吉尼亚的单帆船，而他明知斯坦福德伯爵（Earl of Stamford）即将出兵扫荡康沃尔人。押送她的是我刚才提到的那三个同伙：帆船的船长（一个不多嘴多舌的家伙）奉命在这一带沿海巡航，等他得到战役的消息。"

===Original===
“Which you were just now about to give him,” cried I, suddenly enlighten’d.

“Right again. ’Twas a pretty scheme: for﻿—d’ye see?﻿—if all went well with the Earl of Stamford, the King’s law would be wiped out in Cornwall, and Master Tingcomb (with his claims and meritorious services) might snap his thumb thereat. So, in that case, Mistress Delia was to be brought ashore here and taken to him, to serve as he fancied. But if the day should go against us﻿—as it has﻿—she was to sail to the Virginias with the sloop, and there be sold as a slave. Or worse might happen; but I swear that is the worst was ever told me.”

“God knows ’tis vile enough,” said I, scarce able to refrain from blowing his brains out. “So you were to follow the Earl’s army, and work the signals. Which are they?” For a quick resolve had come into my head, and I was casting about to put it into execution.

“A green light if we won: if not, a red light, to warn the sloop away.”

I picked up the packet that had dropp’d from his hand when first I sprang upon him. It was burst abroad, and a brown powder trickling from it about the ledge.

“This was the red light﻿—to be sprinkled on the burning charcoal, I suppose?”

The fellow nodded. At the same moment, Billy (who as yet had not spoke a word, and of course, understood nothing) thrust into my hand another packet that he had found stuck in a corner against the rock.

“Now tell me﻿—in case the rebels won, where was the landing to be made?”

“In the cove below here﻿—where the road leads down.”

“Aye, the road where the wagon stood.”

Captain Luke Settle blink’d his eyes at this: but nodded after a moment.

“And how many would escort her?”

He caught my drift and laughed softly﻿—

“Be damn’d, sir, but I begin to love you, for you play the game very proper and soundly. Reuben, Jeremy, and Black Dick alone are in the plot; so why should more escort her? For the skipper and crew have their own business to look after.”

===Chinese===
"你刚才正要把消息告诉他，"我恍然大悟，脱口说道。

"又猜对了。这计划可真妙：因为——你明白吗？——如果斯坦福德伯爵一切顺利，国王的法律就会在康沃尔被铲除，廷科姆老爷（带着他的功绩和主张）大可对此嗤之以鼻。所以在那种情况下，德利娅小姐要被押上岸带到他面前，任凭他摆布。但若战局对我们不利——果然如此——她就要随帆船去弗吉尼亚，在那里被卖为奴隶。或者更糟的事也有可能；但我发誓这是最坏的下场了。"

"上帝知道这已经够恶毒了，"我说，几乎忍不住要打穿他的脑袋。"所以你得跟着伯爵的军队，负责发信号。信号是什么？"一个迅速的决定已在我脑海中成形，我正在盘算如何付诸实施。

"赢了就发绿光；输了就发红光，警告帆船离开。"

我捡起他被我扑倒时从手里掉落的纸包。纸包已经散了，一种棕色的粉末从里面漏出来，撒在岩台上。

"这是发红光用的——撒在燃烧的炭火上，对吗？"

那家伙点了点头。与此同时，比尔（他至今一句话没说，当然什么也没听懂）把另一个纸包塞进我手里——那是他从岩石角落里捡到的。

"现在告诉我——万一叛军赢了，要在哪儿登陆？"

"就在下面那个小海湾——有路下去的地方。"

"对，就是那辆马车停着的那条路。"

卢克·塞特尔上尉眨了眨眼睛，但过了一会儿还是点了头。

"会有多少人押送她？"

他听出了我的意思，轻轻一笑——

"天哪，先生，我开始喜欢你了，因为你把这局牌打得又规矩又漂亮。鲁本、杰里和黑迪克三人才参与了这事；何必再派更多人押送她？船长和船员还有自己的事要忙呢。"

===Original===
“Then, Master Settle, though it be a sore trial to you, those three Knaves you must give me, or I play my Ace,” and I pressed the ring of my pistol sharply against his ear as a reminder.

“With all my heart, young sir, you shall have them,” says he briskly.

“And this is ‘honor among thieves,’ ” thought I: “You would sell your comrade as you sold your King:” but only said, “If you cry out, or speak one word to warn them﻿—”

Before I could get my sentence out, Billy Pottery broke in with a voice like a trumpet﻿—

“As folks go, Jack, I be a humorous man. But sittin’ here, an’ ponderin’ this way an’ that, I says, in my deaf an’ afflicted style, ‘Why not shoot the ugly rogue, if mirth, indeed, be your object?’ For to wait till an uglier comes to this untravel’d spot is superfluity.”

How to explain matters to Billy was more than I could tell: but in a moment he himself supplied the means. For the rocks here were of some kind of slate, very hard, but scaly: and finding two pieces, a large and a small, he handed them to me, bawling that I was to write therewith. So giving him my pistol, I made shift to scribble a few words. Seeing his eyes twinkle as he read, I stood up.

The charcoal by this time was a glowing mass of red: and threw so clear a light on us that I feared the crew on board the sloop might see our forms and suspect their misadventure. But the lantern still hung steadily: so signing to Billy to drag our prisoner behind a tamarisk bush, I open’d the second packet, and poured some of the powder into my hand.

It was composed of tiny crystals, yellow and flaky: and holding it, for a moment I was possessed with a horrid fear that this might be the signal to warn the sloop away. I flung a look at the Captain: who read my thoughts on the instant.

“Never fear, young sir: am no such hero as to sell my life for that tag-rag. Only make haste, for your deaf friend has a cursed ugly way of fumbling his pistol.”

So taking heart, I tore the packet wide, and shook out the powder on the coals.

===Chinese===
"那么，塞特尔先生，尽管这对你来说是个痛苦的考验，你必须把那三个小丑交给我，否则我就打出王牌。"我把手枪的铁环狠狠顶在他耳朵上作为提醒。

"全心全意，少爷，他们归你了，"他爽快地说。

"这就是所谓'盗亦有道'啊，"我想，"你出卖同伴就像你出卖国王一样痛快。"但我只说了一句："要是你敢叫唤，或者吐出一个字去警告他们——"

我的话还没说完，比尔·波特里就用他那洪钟般的声音插嘴道——

"杰克，按说呢，我也是个幽默人。可坐在这儿，左思右想，我就用我那聋哑人的腔调说：'既然你图的是乐子，干嘛不一枪崩了这丑八怪？'再等更丑的来这鸟不拉屎的地方，纯属多余。"

跟比尔解释清楚眼下怎么回事，我实在没把握；可他自己倒给我找到了办法。因为这里的岩石是一种板岩，很硬，但有层理；他找到两块，一大一小，递给我，嚷嚷着说让我用这来写字。于是我把手枪交给他，凑合着在上面潦草地刻了几行字。看到他读时眼睛闪闪发亮，我站起身来。

炭火此刻已烧成一团炽红的光，把我们照得清清楚楚，我担心帆船上的船员会看见我们的身影，猜到他们的同伙出了岔子。但提灯仍然稳稳地挂着；我示意比尔把囚犯拖到一丛柽柳后面，然后打开第二个纸包，把一些粉末倒进手心。

那是些细小的黄色片状结晶；握着它，我心头突然涌起一阵可怕的恐惧——这会不会是警告帆船撤离的信号？我看了上尉一眼；他立刻读懂了我的心思。

"别怕，少爷：我可不是那种为这帮乌合之众卖命的英雄。快动手吧，你那聋朋友摆弄手枪的样子可真够呛。"

我鼓起勇气，撕开纸包，把粉末撒在炭火上。

===Original===
Instantly there came a dense choking vapor, and a vivid green flare that turned the rocks, the sky, and our faces to a ghastly brilliance. For two minutes, at least, this unnatural light lasted. As soon as it died away and the fumes clear’d, I look’d seaward.

The lantern on the sloop was moving in answer to the signal. Three times it was lifted and lower’d: and then in the stillness I heard voices calling, and soon after the regular splash of oars.

There was no time to be lost. Pulling the Captain to his feet, we scrambled up the gully, and out at the top, and across the fields as fast as our legs would take us. Molly came to my call and trotted beside me﻿—the Captain following some paces behind, and Billy last, to keep a safe watch on his movements.

At the gate, however, where we turned into the road, I tethered the mare, lest the sound of her hoofs should betray us: and down toward the sea we pelted, till almost at the foot of the hill I pull’d up and listen’d, the others following my example.

We could hear the sound of oars plain above the wash of waves on the beach. I look’d about me. On either side the road was now bank’d by tall hills, with clusters of bracken and furze bushes lying darkly on their slopes. Behind one of these clusters I station’d Billy with the Captain’s long sword, and a pistol that I by signs forbade him to fire unless in extremity. Then, retiring some forty paces up the road, I hid the Captain and myself on the other side.

Hardly were we thus disposed, before I heard the sound of a boat grounding on the beach below, and the murmur of voices; and then the noise of feet trampling the shingle. Upon which I ordered my prisoner to give a hail, which he did readily.

“Ahoy, Dick! Ahoy, Reuben Gedges!”

===Chinese===
刹那间，一股浓密呛人的蒸气喷涌而出，接着是一道鲜艳的绿焰，将岩石、天空和我们的脸照成一片惨白的辉光。这诡异的光芒至少持续了两分钟。它一熄灭，烟雾散去，我便朝海上望去。

帆船上的提灯在回应信号。它被举起又放下三次；接着，在寂静中，我听见了人声呼喊，不久便是桨叶规律拍水的声音。

时间紧迫，容不得耽搁。我把上尉拽起来，我们手脚并用地爬上峡谷，翻出崖顶，拼尽全力跑过田野。莫莉听到我的呼唤，小跑着跟在我身边——上尉跟在后面几步远，比尔殿后，严密监视他的一举一动。

然而在拐上大路的那道门前，我把母马拴住了，生怕她的蹄声暴露我们；我们朝海边狂奔，快到山脚时我停下脚步侧耳倾听，其他人也照做。

我们能清晰地听见海浪拍岸声之上的桨声。我环顾四周。道路两旁如今已是高耸的山丘，山坡上覆盖着蕨类和金雀花丛，在暗影中显得黑黢黢的。我让比尔带着上尉的长剑和一把手枪，埋伏在一丛灌木后头，并用手势警告他除非万不得已，不要开枪。然后，我沿路后退四十步，把我和上尉藏在路的另一边。

我们刚布置好，就听见下方海滩上小船靠岸的声音，接着是低语声；然后是脚步踩在卵石上的沙沙声。我命令俘虏喊话，他很爽快地照做了。

"喂，迪克！喂，鲁本·盖奇斯！"

===Original===
In a moment or two came the answer﻿—

“Ahoy, there, Captain﻿—here we be!”

“Fetch along the cargo!” shouted Captain Settle, on my prompting.

“Where be you?”

“Up the road, here﻿—waiting!”

“One minute, then﻿—wait one minute, Captain!”

I heard the boat push’d off, some “Good nights” call’d, and then (with tender anguish) the voice of my Delia lifted in entreaty. As I guess’d, she was beseeching the sailors to take her back to the sloop, nor leave her to these villains. There follow’d an oath or two growl’d out, a short scrimmage, and at last, above the splash of the retreating boat, came the tramp of heavy feet on the road below.

So fired was I at the sound of Delia’s voice, that ’twas with much ado I kept quiet behind the bush. Yet I had wit enough left to look to the priming of my pistol, and also to bid the Captain shout again. As he did so, a light shone out down the road, and round the corner came a man bearing a lantern.

“Can’t be quicker, Captain,” he called: “the jade struggles so that Dick and Jeremy ha’ their hands full.”

Sure enough, after him there came in view two stooping forms that bore my dear maid between them﻿—one by the feet, other by the shoulders. I ground my teeth to see it, for she writhed sorely. On they came, however, until not more than ten paces off; and then that traitor, Luke Settle, rose up behind our bush.

“Set her here, boys,” said he, “and tie her pretty ankles.”

“Well met, Captain!” said the fellow with the lantern﻿—Reuben Gedges﻿—stepping forward; “Give us your hand!”

===Chinese===
片刻之后传来应答——

"喂，在这儿呢，上尉——我们在这儿！"

"把货带过来！"在我的示意下，塞特尔上尉高声喊道。

"你在哪儿？"

"在路上，这边——等着！"

"那就等一分钟——等一分钟，上尉！"

我听见小船撑开了，几声"晚安"传来，接着（带着温柔的痛楚）我听见德利娅哀求的声音。我猜她是求水手们把她送回帆船去，别把她丢给这些恶棍。随之而来的是几声粗暴的咒骂、一阵短暂的扭打，最后，在退去的小船的桨声之上，传来了沉重的脚步踩在路上的声响。

德利娅的声音让我血脉偾张，我费了好大劲才克制住自己没从灌木丛后冲出来。但我还有足够的理智检查手枪的火药，又让上尉再喊一声。他喊了之后，路前方亮起一盏灯，一个提着提灯的男子从拐角处走了出来。

"没法再快了，上尉，"他喊道，"那婆娘挣扎得厉害，迪克和杰里两个人都按不住她。"

果然，在他身后出现了两个弯着腰的人影，他们扛着我心爱的姑娘——一个扛着脚，一个扛着肩。我咬牙切齿地看着，因为她痛苦地扭动着。他们继续走来，离我们不到十步了；这时，那个叛徒卢克·塞特尔从我们的灌木丛后面站了起来。

"把她放这儿，伙计们，"他说，"把她漂亮的脚踝绑上。"

"幸会，上尉！"提灯的那个家伙——鲁本·盖奇斯——走上前说；"拉兄弟一把！"

===Original===
He was holding out his own, when I sprang up, set the pistol close to his chest, and fired. His scream mingled with the roar of it, and dropping the lantern, he threw up his hands and tumbled in a heap. At the same moment, out went the light, and the other rascals, dropping Delia, turn’d to run, crying, “Sold﻿—sold!”

But behind them came now a shout from Billy, and a crashing blow that almost severed Black Dick’s arm at the shoulder: and at the same instant I was on Master Toy’s collar, and had him down in the dust. Kneeling on his chest, with my sword point at his throat, I had leisure to glance at Billy, who in the dark, seem’d to be sitting on the head of his disabled victim. And then I felt a touch on my shoulder, and a dear face peer’d into mine.

“Is it Jack﻿—my sweet Jack?”

“To be sure,” said I: “and if you but reach out your hand, I will kiss it, for all that I’m busy with this rogue.”

“Nay, Jack, I’ll kiss thee on the cheek﻿—so! Dear lad, I am so frighten’d, and yet could laugh for joy!”

But now I caught the sound of galloping on the road above, and shouts, and then more galloping; and down came a troop of horsemen that were like to have ridden over us, had I not shouted lustily.

“Who, in the fiend’s name is here?” shouted the foremost, pulling in his horse with a scramble.

“Honest men and rebels together,” I answered; “but light the lantern that you will find handy by, and you shall know one from t’other.”

===Chinese===
他正伸出手来，我猛然跃起，把枪口抵住他胸口，扣动了扳机。他的惨叫和枪声混在一起，提灯脱手落地，他双手高举，扑通一声栽倒。与此同时灯光熄灭，另外两个恶棍丢下德利娅转身就跑，喊着："中计了——中计了！"

但比尔的吼声紧跟着在他们身后响起，接着是一记震耳欲聋的猛击，差点把黑迪克的胳膊从肩膀上卸下来；同一瞬间，我扑到托伊先生的衣领上，把他摁倒在尘土里。我跪在他胸口，剑尖顶着他的喉咙，抽空瞥了比尔一眼，他在黑暗中似乎正坐在他那被打残的对手的脑袋上。接着，我感到有人碰了碰我的肩膀，一张熟悉的脸凑到我眼前。

"是杰克吗——我亲爱的杰克？"

"当然是我，"我说，"只要你伸出手来，我就吻它，虽然我正忙着对付这无赖。"

"不，杰克，我来亲你的脸颊——就这样！好孩子，我好害怕，却又高兴得想笑！"

===Original===
But now I caught the sound of galloping on the road above, and shouts, and then more galloping; and down came a troop of horsemen that were like to have ridden over us, had I not shouted lustily.

“Who, in the fiend’s name is here?” shouted the foremost, pulling in his horse with a scramble.

“Honest men and rebels together,” I answered; “but light the lantern that you will find handy by, and you shall know one from t’other.”

By the time ’twas found and lit, there was a dozen of Col. John Digby’s dragoons about us: and before the two villains were bound, comes a half dozen more, leading in Captain Settle, that had taken to his heels at the first blow and climb’d the hill, all tied as he was about the hands, and was caught in his endeavor to clamber on Molly’s back. So he and Black Dick and Jeremy Toy were strapp’d up: but Reuben Gedges we left on the road for a corpse. Yet he did not die (though shot through the lung), but recovered﻿—heaven knows how: and I myself had the pleasure to see him hanged at Tyburn, in the second year of his late Majesty’s most blessed Restoration, for stopping the Bishop of Salisbury’s coach, in Maidenhead Thicket, and robbing the Bishop himself, with much added contumely.

But as we were ready to start, and I was holding Delia steady on Molly’s back, up comes Billy and bawls in my ear﻿—

“There’s a second horse, if wanted, that I spied tether’d under a hedge younder”﻿—and he pointed to the field where we had first found Captain Settle﻿—“in color a sad black, an’ harness’d like as if he came from a cart.”

I look’d at the Captain, who in the light of the lantern blink’d again. “Thou bloody villain!” muttered I, for now I read the tragedy of the wagon beside the road, and knew how Master Settle had provided a horse for his own escape.

But hereupon the word was given, and we started up the hill, I walking by Delia’s stirrup and listening to her talk as if we had never been parted﻿—yet with a tenderer joy, having by loss of it learn’d to appraise my happiness aright.

===Chinese===
这时我听见路上方传来马蹄声、呼喊声，接着又是更多马蹄声；一队骑兵疾驰而下，若不是我拼命大喊，他们几乎要从我们身上踏过去。

"这他妈的是谁？"领头的人勒住马，厉声喝问。

"老实人和叛军都在这儿，"我答道，"不过你把那边手边的提灯点上，就能分清谁是谁了。"

等提灯找到并点亮时，约翰·迪格比上校的龙骑兵已有十几人围在我们身边；两个恶棍还没捆好，又来了五六个人，押着塞特尔上尉——他第一下挨打就撒腿跑了，手脚绑着还往山上爬，被逮住时正想翻上莫莉的背。于是他、黑迪克和杰里·托伊都被捆了起来；鲁本·盖奇斯我们丢在路上，权当死人了。然而他没死成（虽然肺部被射穿），居然活了下来——天知道怎么活下来的；而且我有幸在先王陛下最蒙福的复辟第二年，在泰伯恩（Tyburn）刑场亲眼看见他因拦劫索尔兹伯里主教的马车、抢劫主教本人并加以凌辱的罪行被绞死。

但我们准备动身时，我正扶德利娅在莫莉背上坐稳，比尔跑过来在我耳边吼道——

"那边树篱下还拴着一匹马，要是需要的话"——他指着我们最初发现塞特尔上尉的那块田地——"毛色黑黢黢的，挽具像是从马车上拆下来的。"

我看了上尉一眼，他在提灯光下又眨了眨眼。"你这嗜血的恶棍！"我低声骂道，因为我终于读懂了路边那辆马车的悲剧，明白了塞特尔先生是如何为自己逃跑备好坐骑的。

号令一下，我们便朝山上进发，我走在德利娅的马镫旁，听她说话，仿佛我们从未分离过——只是喜悦中多了一份更温柔的滋味，因为曾经历过失去，我才学会了正确地估量我的幸福。

## XVIII Joan Does Me Her Last Service / XVIII 琼恩为我尽最后一次力

===Original===
We came, a little before midnight, to Sir Bevill’s famous great house of Stow, near Kilkhampton: that tonight was brightly lit and full of captains and troopers feasting, as well they needed to, after the great victory. And here, though loth to do so, I left Delia to the care of Lady Grace Grenville, Sir Bevill’s fond beautiful wife, and of all gentlewomen I have ever seen the pink and paragon, as well for her loyal heart as the graces of her mind: who, before the half of our tale was out, kissed Delia on both cheeks, and led her away. “To you too, sir, I would counsel bed,” said she, “after you have eaten and drunk, and especially given God thanks for this day’s work.”

Sir Bevill I did not see, but striding down into the hall, picked my way among the drinking and drunken; the servants hurrying with dishes of roast and baked and great tankards of beer; the swords and pikes flung down under the forms and settles, and sticking out to trip a man up; and at length found a groom who led me to a loft over one of the barns: and here, above a mattress of hay, I slept the first time for many months between fresh linen that smell’d of lavender, and in thinking how pleasant ’twas, dropped sound asleep.

Sure there is no better, sweeter couch than this of linen spread over hay. Early in the morning, I woke with wits clear as water, and not an ache or ounce of weariness in my bones: and after washing at the pump below, went in search of breakfast and Sir Bevill. The one I found, ready laid, in the hall; the other seated in his writing-room, studying in a map; and with apology for my haste, handed him Master Tingcomb’s confession and told my story.

When ’twas over, Sir Bevill sat pondering, and after a while said, very frankly﻿—

“As a magistrate I can give this warrant; and ’twould be a pleasure, for well, as a boy, do I remember Deakin Killigrew. Young sir﻿—” he rose up, and taking a turn across the room, came and laid a hand on my shoulder, “I have seen his daughter. Is it too late to warn you against loving her?”

“Why yes,” I answer’d blushing: “I think it is.”

“She seems both sweet and quaint. God forbid I should say a word against one that has so taken me! But in these times a man should stand alone: to make a friend is to run the chance of a soft heart: to marry a wife makes the chance sure﻿—”

===Chinese===
午夜前不久，我们抵达了基尔汉普顿（Kilkhampton）附近贝维尔·格伦维尔爵士（Sir Bevill Grenville）著名的斯托大宅。宅邸今夜灯火通明，到处是饱餐畅饮的军官和骑兵——打了大胜仗，他们正该如此。尽管心中不舍，我还是将德利娅（Delia）托付给了格蕾丝·格伦维尔夫人（Lady Grace Grenville）照看。格蕾丝夫人是贝维尔爵士美丽温婉的妻子，在我见过的所有淑女中堪称典范，不仅因她忠贞的心，更因她智慧的优雅：故事还未讲到一半，她便亲吻了德利娅的双颊，将她领走了。「先生，您也该歇息了，」她对我说，「用过餐、饮过酒之后，尤其该为今日的胜利感谢上帝。」

我没能见到贝维尔爵士，便大步走进大厅，在饮酒作乐和醉醺醺的人群中穿行；仆人们端着烤肉、面点和大杯啤酒匆匆来去；刀剑和长戟丢在长凳和沙发底下，伸出的柄脚险些绊倒人；最终我找到一个马夫，领我去了谷仓顶上的阁楼。在这里，铺着薰衣草清香的亚麻床单，底下垫着干草床垫，几个月来我第一次睡在如此洁净的床铺上，想着这有多舒坦，便沉沉睡去。

千真万确，再没有比亚麻单铺在干草上更舒适甜美的卧榻了。清早醒来，我神清气爽，骨子里没有一丝酸痛疲惫：在楼下泵边洗过脸，我便去寻早餐和贝维尔爵士。早餐已在大厅备好；爵士则坐在书房里，正对着一张地图出神。我为自己的匆忙道了歉，将廷科姆老爷（Master Tingcomb）的供词呈上，讲述了我的遭遇。

听完之后，贝维尔爵士沉思片刻，非常坦率地说道——

「作为治安官，我可以签发这份拘捕令；这也是件乐事，因为迪肯·基利格鲁（Deakin Killigrew）的音容笑貌，我自幼便铭记在心。年轻人——」他站起身，在房间里踱了一圈，走到我跟前，一只手搭在我肩上，「我见过他的女儿。现在警告你别爱上她，是否为时已晚？」

「哎呀，」我红着脸答道，「我想确实晚了。」

「她似乎既甜美又奇特。上帝不容我诋毁一位如此打动我的女子！但在这个年月，男人应当独善其身：交朋友便有心软的危险；娶妻子更将这危险坐实——」

===Original===
He broke off, and went on again with a change of tone﻿—

“For many reasons I would blithely issue this warrant. But how am I to spare men to carry it out? At any moment we may be assail’d.”

“If that be your concern, sir,” answer’d I, “give me the warrant. I have a good friend here, a seafaring man, whose vessel lies at this moment in Looe Haven, with a crew on board that will lay Master Tingcomb by the heels in a trice. Within three days we’ll have him clapp’d in Launceston Jail, and there at the next Assize you shall sit on the Grand Jury and hear his case, by which time, I hope, the King’s law shall run on easier wheels in Cornwall. The prisoners we have already I leave you to deal withal: only, against my will, I must claim some mercy for that rogue, Settle.”

To this Sir Bevill consented; and, to be short, the three knaves were next morning pack’d off to Launceston: but in time, no evidence being brought against them, regained their freedom, which they used to come to the gallows, each in his own way. Their doings no longer concern this history, and so I gladly leave them.

To return, then, to my proper tale, ’twas not ten minutes before I had the warrant in my pocket. And by eleven o’clock (word having been carried to Delia, and our plans laid before Billy Pottery, who on the spot engaged himself to help us) our horses were brought round to the gate, and my mistress appear’d, all ready for the journey. For though assured that the work needed not her presence, and that she had best wait at Stow till Master Tingcomb was smok’d out of his nest, she would have none of it, but was set on riding with me to see justice done on this fellow, of whose villainy I had told her much the night before. And glad I was of her choice, as I saw her standing on the entrance steps, fresh as a rose, and in a fit habit once more: for Lady Grace had lent not only her own bay horse, but also a riding dress and hat of grey velvet to equip her: and stood in the porch to wish us “Godspeed!” while Sir Bevill help’d Delia to the saddle.

So, with Billy tramping behind us, away we rode up the combe, where Kilkhampton tower stood against the sky; and turning to wave hands at the top, found our host and hostess still by the gate, watching us, with hands rais’d to shield their eyes from the sun.

===Chinese===
他顿住了，换了个语气继续说道——

「出于种种原因，我很乐意签发这份拘捕令。但我如何抽调人手去执行？敌人随时可能来犯。」

「若爵士所虑在此，」我答道，「请将拘捕令交给我。我在此有位好友，是个航海人，他的船此刻正停泊在卢港（Looe Haven），船上水手随时能将廷科姆老爷拿下。不出三日，我们便能将他关进朗塞斯顿（Launceston）监狱，下次巡回审判时，您可坐镇大陪审团审理此案。到那时，愿国王的律法在康沃尔（Cornwall）能更顺畅地施行。已抓的俘虏任您处置：只是，违我本意，我须为那个恶棍塞特尔（Settle）求些宽恕。」

贝维尔爵士应允了。长话短说，三个恶棍次日清晨便被押往朗塞斯顿：但后来因无人指证，他们竟获自由，最终各自走上了绞刑架。他们的行径与本史再无干系，我乐得不再提及。

言归正传，不到十分钟，拘捕令便揣进了我的口袋。十一点钟时（消息已传给德利娅，我们的计划也向比尔·波特里（Billy Pottery）交了底，他当场承诺相助），马匹已牵到门口，我的女主人也整装待发。尽管我一再说明她无需亲往，最好留在斯托，等廷科姆老爷被逼出老巢，但她执意不听，非要骑马随我去亲眼看着这家伙伏法——前夜我已向她详述其恶行。见她站在门阶上，容光焕发如玫瑰，又穿上了合身的骑装，我心中甚慰：格蕾丝夫人不仅借出了自己的枣红马，还提供了一套灰丝绒骑装和帽子为她装扮。爵士夫妇立在门廊下为我们祝「一路顺风」，贝维尔爵士亲自扶德利娅上了马鞍。

于是，比尔跟在我们身后步行，我们策马驶上山谷，基尔汉普顿的塔楼矗立在天际线上；在山顶回身挥手时，发现主人和女主人仍在门口目送，抬手遮着阳光。

===Original===
The whole petty tale of this day’s ride I shall not dwell upon. Indeed, I scarcely noted the miles as they pass’d. For all the way we were chattering, Delia telling me how Captain Settle and his gang had hurried her (though without indignity) across Dartmoor to Ashburton, thence to Lynton in North Devon, and so along the coast of Somerset to Bristol; how they there produced a paper, at sight of which Sir Nathaniel Fiennes, the new Governor, kept her under lock and key. And thus she remained four months, at the end of which time they convey’d her on board a sloop, call’d the *Fortitude*, and bound for the Virginias, with the result that has been told. To all of which I listened greedily, stealing from time to time a look at her shape, that on horseback was graceful as a willow, and into her eyes that, under the flapping grey brim, were gay and fancy-free as ever.

“And did you,” asked I, “never at heart chide me for leaving you so!”

“Why no. I never took thee for a conjurer, Jack.”

“But, at least, you thought of me,” I urged.

“Oh, dear﻿—oh, dear!” She pull’d rein and look’d at me: “I remember now that last night I kiss’d thee. Forget it, Jack: last night, so glad was I to be sav’d, I could have kiss’d a cobbler. Indeed, Jack,” she went on seriously, “I would that some maid had got hold of thee, in all these months, to cure thy silly notions!”

At Launceston, Billy Pottery took leave of us: and now went, due south, toward Looe, with a light purse and lighter heart, undertaking that his ship should lie off Gleys, with her crew ready for action, within eight-and-forty hours. Delia and I rode faster now toward the southwest: and having by this time recover’d my temper, I was recounting my flight along this very road, when I heard a sound that brought my heart into my mouth.

’twas the blast of a bugle, and came from behind the hill in front of us. And at the same moment I understood. It must be Sir George Chudleigh’s cavalry returning, on news of their comrades’ defeat, and we were riding straight toward them, as into a trap.

===Chinese===
这日行程中的琐细之事，我便不赘述了。事实上，里程在脚下流逝，我几乎浑然不觉。一路上我们说个不停，德利娅告诉我塞特尔上尉一伙如何（虽未施暴）押着她穿越达特穆尔（Dartmoor）到阿什伯顿（Ashburton），再到北德文郡的林顿（Lynton），沿海岸经萨默塞特（Somerset）到布里斯托尔（Bristol）；他们在那里出示了一份文件，新任总督纳撒尼尔·费恩斯爵士（Sir Nathaniel Fiennes）见后便将她囚禁起来。她就这样被关了四个月，最后他们将她押上一艘名为「坚毅号」（*Fortitude*）的单桅帆船，驶向弗吉尼亚（Virginias），后来的事前文已述。我贪婪地听着这一切，不时偷眼瞧她骑在马上的倩影，她身姿如柳条般婀娜，帽檐下那双眼睛依然明媚而无拘无束。

「你可曾，」我问道，「在心里怨我丢下你不管？」

「没有。我从没把你当魔术师呀，杰克。」

「可你至少想过我吧，」我追问。

「哎呀——哎呀！」她勒住马看着我，「我倒想起来了，昨晚我还亲了你。忘了吧，杰克：昨晚我劫后余生，高兴得能亲一个补鞋匠呢。真的，杰克，」她正色道，「我倒希望这几个月里有哪个姑娘能治治你那些傻念头！」

在朗塞斯顿，比尔·波特里与我们道别：他轻囊轻心，径直朝南往卢港去了，承诺四十八小时内他的船会停在格莱斯（Gleys）附近海域，船员随时待命。德利娅和我加快了西南方向的行程。此时我的气已消了，正要讲述我沿着这条路逃亡的经历，忽然听到一阵声响，心头猛地一紧。

那是军号声，从前方山丘背后传来。我立刻明白了。必定是乔治·查德利爵士（Sir George Chudleigh）的骑兵闻讯折返——他们的同袍吃了败仗——而我们正朝他们迎面冲去，如同自投罗网。

===Original===
Now what could have made me forgetful of this danger I cannot explain, unless it be that our thorough victory over the rebels had given me the notion that the country behind us was clear of foes. And Sir Bevill must have had a notion we were going straight to Looe with Billy. At any rate, there was no time to be lost: for my presence was a danger to Delia as well. I cast a glance about me. There was no place to hide.

“Quick!” I cried; “follow me, and ride for dear life!”

And striking spur into Molly I turn’d sharp off the road and gallop’d across the moor to the left, with Delia close after me.

We had gone about two hundred yards only when I heard a shout, and glancing over my right shoulder, saw a green banner waving on the crest of the road, and gathered about it the vanguard of the troop﻿—some score of dragoons: and these, having caught sight of us, were pausing a moment to watch.

The shout presently was followed by another; to which I made no answer, but held on my way, with the nose of Delia’s horse now level with my stirrup: for I guess’d that my dress had already betray’d us. And this was the case; for at the next glance I saw five or six dragoons detach themselves from the main body, and gallop in a direction at an acute angle to ours. On they came, yelling to us to halt, and scattering over the moor to intercept us.

Not choosing, however, to be driven eastward, I kept a straight course and trusted to our horses’ fleetness to carry us by them, out of reach of their shot. In the pause of their first surprise we had stolen two hundred yards more. I counted and found eight men thus in pursuit of us: and to my joy heard the bugle blown again, and saw the rest of the troop, now gathering fast above, move steadily along the road without intention to follow. Doubtless the news of the Cornish success made them thus wary of their good order.

===Chinese===
我为何会忘记这危险，实在说不清，许是彻底击败叛军后，我以为身后已无敌人。贝维尔爵士想必以为我们正和比尔直奔卢港。无论如何，不容迟疑：我留在德利娅身边也是个祸害。我环顾四周，无处可藏。

「快！」我喊道，「跟我来，拼命跑！」

我猛踢莫莉（Molly），急转离了大路，纵马向左驰上荒原，德利娅紧随其后。

跑了不过两百码，便听一声呐喊，我扭头望去，只见路脊上飘着一面绿旗，旗下聚着一队先锋——约莫二十名龙骑兵（dragoons）：他们瞥见我们，勒马观望了一瞬。

呐喊声很快又起；我没有回应，只顾催马向前，德利娅的马鼻已与我的马镫齐平：我猜是我的穿着暴露了身份。果然，再一瞥，我看见五六名龙骑兵脱离主力，以锐角方向疾驰而来。他们逼近，喝令我们停下，在荒原上散开拦截。

但我决不愿被赶向东边，便保持直行，指望马儿够快能冲出去，脱离他们的射程。趁他们初见我们时的一愣，我们又偷出了两百码。我数了数，追兵共八人：欣喜的是军号再次响起，主力部队迅速集结在上方，沿大路稳步推进，无意追来。无疑，康沃尔大捷的消息使他们格外珍重整饬的队列。

===Original===
Still, eight men were enough to run from; and now the nearest let fly with his piece﻿—more to frighten us, belike, than with any other view, for we were far out of range. But it grew clear that if we held on our direction they must cut us off: as you may see by these two arrows, the long thin one standing for our own course, the thicker and shorter for that of the dragoons.

Only now with good hope I saw a hill rising not half a mile in front, and somewhat to the right of our course: and thought I “if we can gain the hollow to the left of it, and put the hill between us, they must ride over it or round﻿—in either case losing much time.” So, pointing this out to Delia, who rode on my left (to leave my pistol arm free and at the same time be screen’d by me from shot of the dragoons) I drove my spurs deep and called to Molly to make her best pace.

The enemy divin’d our purpose: and in a minute ’twas a desperate race for the entrance to the hollow. But our horses were the faster, and we the lighter riders; so that we won, with thirty yards to spare, from the foremost:﻿—not without damage, however; for finding himself baulked, he sent a bullet at us which cut neatly through my off rein, so that my bridle was henceforward useless and I could guide Molly with knee and voice alone. Delia’s bay had shied at the sound of it, and likely enough saved my mistress’ life by this; for the bullet must have pass’d within a foot before her.

Down the hollow we raced with three dragoons at our heels, the rest going round the hill. But they did little good by so doing, for after the hollow came a broad, dismal sheet of water (by name Dozmare Pool, I have since heard) about a mile round and bank’d with black peat. Galloping along the left shore of this, we cut them off by near half a mile. But the three behind followed doggedly, though dropping back with every stride.

Beyond the pool came a green valley; and a stream flowing down it, which we jump’d easily. Glancing at Delia as she landed on the further side, I noted that her cheeks were glowing, and her eyes brimful of mirth.

“Say, Jack,” she cried; “is not this better than love of women?”

“In Heaven’s name,” I called out, “take care!”

But ’twas too late. The green valley here melted into a treacherous bog, in the which her bay was already plunging over his fetlocks, and every moment sinking deeper.

“Throw me the rein!” I shouted, and catching the bridle close by the bit, lean’d over and tried to drag the horse forward. By this, Molly also was over hoofs in liquid mud. For a minute and more we heav’d and splashed: and all the while the dragoons, seeing our fix, were shouting and drawing nearer and nearer. But just as a brace of bullets splashed into the slough at our feet, we stagger’d to the harder slope, and were gaining on them again. So for twenty minutes along the spurs of the hills, we held on, the enemy falling back and hidden, every now and again, in the hollows﻿—but always following: at the end of which time, Delia call’d from just behind me﻿—

“Jack﻿—here’s a to-do: the bay is going lame!”

===Chinese===
然而八人也够呛；最近的一个已开火——多半只为吓唬，我们远在射程之外。但形势明摆着，照此直行必被截住：诸君可想象两条箭头，细长的一条是我们的路线，粗短的一条是龙骑兵的。

这时我眼前一亮：不到半英里前方，略偏我们右侧，隆起一座小丘。我想：「若能抢到丘左的洼地，将山丘隔在中间，他们要么翻山要么绕行——无论哪种都耽搁不少时间。」于是指给左骑的德利娅看（我让她在左侧，一来空出右手持枪，二来我可替她挡龙骑兵的子弹），我深深刺入马刺，催莫莉全速奔跑。

敌人看穿了我们的意图：一时间，双方为抢入洼地展开了殊死竞速。但我们的马更快，我们更轻，最终以三十码之差率先抵达——然而并非毫发无损：最前面的追兵恼羞成怒，朝我们放了一枪，子弹正正割断了我的外侧缰绳，马缰从此无用，我只能靠膝盖和口令驾驭莫莉。德利娅的枣红马闻声惊跳，这倒很可能救了女主人一命：子弹距她不过一尺。

我们冲下洼地，三名龙骑兵紧追在后，其余绕山而行。但绕山并无大用，因为洼地尽头是一大片阴沉沉的水域（我后来听说叫多兹米尔湖（Dozmare Pool），方圆约一英里，岸边是黑色泥炭）。我们沿湖左岸疾驰，将他们甩开了近半英里。但身后三人死死咬住，虽每一步都在落后。

湖那边是一片青翠山谷，一条溪流穿谷而过，我们轻松跃过。德利娅在对岸落地时，我瞥见她双颊泛红，眼里盈满笑意。

「杰克，」她喊道，「这不比谈情说爱强多了？」

「看在老天份上，」我大喊，「小心！」

但已迟了。翠谷在此化作一片险恶的沼泽，她的枣红马已陷进蹄踝，越陷越深。

「把缰绳扔给我！」我喊道，抓住靠近马嚼子的辔头，俯身试图把马拽出来。莫莉此时也陷进了稀泥。我们挣扎、溅水，足有一分多钟：龙骑兵见我们受困，一边喊叫一边逼近。就在两颗子弹溅落在脚边泥潭时，我们踉跄上了较硬的斜坡，重新甩开他们。就这样在山脊间奔跑了二十分钟，敌人时而被洼地遮蔽，始终紧追不舍：最后德利娅在我身后喊道——

「杰克——糟了：枣红马瘸了！」

===Original===
There was no doubt of it. I suppose he must have wrung his off hind leg in fighting through the quag. Anyway, ten minutes more would see the end of his gallop. But at this moment we had won to the top of a stiff ascent: and now, looking down at our feet, I had the joyfullest surprise.

’Twas the moor of Temple spread below like a map, the low sun striking on the ruin’d huts to the left of us, on the roof of Joan’s cottage, on the scar of the high road, and the sides of the tall tor above it.

“In ten minutes,” said I, “we may be safe.”

So down into the plain we hurried: and I thought for the first time of the loyal girl waiting in the cottage yonder; of my former ride into Temple; and (with angry shame) of the light heart with which I left it. To what had the summoning drums and trumpets led me? Where was the new life, then so carelessly prevent’d? But two days had gone, and here was I running to Joan for help, as a child to his mother.

Past the peat-ricks we struggled, the sheepcotes, the straggling fences﻿—all so familiar; cross’d the stream and rode into the yard.

“Jump down,” I whisper’d: “we have time, and no more.” Glancing back, I saw a couple of dragoons already coming over the heights. They had spied us.

Dismounting I ran to the cottage door and flung it open. A stream of light, flung back against the sun, blazed into my eyes.

I rubbed them and halted for a moment stock-still.

For Joan stood in front of me, dress’d in the very clothes I had worn on the day we first met﻿—buff-coat, breeches, heavy boots, and all. Her back was toward me, and at the shoulder, where the coat had been cut away from my wound, I saw the rents all darn’d and patch’d with pack thread. In her hand was the mirror I had given her.

At the sound of my step on the threshold she turn’d with a short cry﻿—a cry the like of which I have never heard, so full was it of choking joy. The glass dropp’d to the floor and was shatter’d. In a second her arms were about me, and so she hung on my neck, sobbing and laughing together.

“ ’Twas true﻿—’twas true! Dear, dear Jack﻿—dear Jack to come to me: hold me tighter, tighter﻿—for my very heart is bursting!”

And behind me a shadow fell on the doorway: and there stood Delia regarding us.

===Chinese===
千真万确。我想他定是在冲过沼泽时伤了右后腿。无论如何，再跑十分钟他便撑不住了。但此时我们刚登上一座陡坡的顶头：低头望去，我欣喜若狂。

坦普尔（Temple）荒原如地图般铺展在脚下，夕阳斜照着左侧的残屋、琼恩的小屋屋顶、大路的疤痕，以及上方那座高耸的突岩（tor）。

「再有十分钟，」我说，「我们就安全了。」

我们冲下山坡，我第一次想起那个忠诚的姑娘正在小屋里等候；想起我上次骑马来到坦普尔；又（羞愧交加）想起自己离开时那般轻率。那召唤我的鼓声和号角声将我引向了何方？那被我轻率舍弃的新生活又在哪里？才过了两天，我便跑回来向琼恩求助，像个孩子扑向母亲。

我们挣扎着经过泥炭垛、羊圈、散乱的篱笆——一切都如此熟悉；涉过溪流，策马进了院子。

「跳下来，」我低声道，「我们刚好来得及，再没有余裕了。」回头一瞥，两名龙骑兵已翻过山脊。他们发现了我们。

我跳下马，冲到小屋门口，猛地推开。一道逆着阳光的光芒刺入眼帘。

我揉了揉眼，僵立了片刻。

琼恩就站在我面前，穿着我初遇她那天穿的那身行头——皮甲（buff-coat）、马裤、厚靴，一样不差。她背对着我，肩上那处因我伤口而被裁开的地方，我看到裂口全用麻线缝补过了。她手里拿着我送她的镜子。

听见我踏上门槛的脚步声，她短促地惊叫一声——那叫声我从未听过，满是哽咽的喜悦。镜子滑落在地，碎了。刹那间她双臂抱住了我，挂在我脖子上，又哭又笑。

「是真的——是真的！亲爱的、亲爱的杰克——亲爱的杰克来找我了：抱紧些，再紧些——我的心要炸了！」

我身后门口投下一道身影：德利娅站在那里，望着我们。

===Original===
“Good lad﻿—all yesterday I swore to be strong and wait for years, if need be. Fie on womankind, to be so weak! All day I sat an’ sat, an’ did never a mite o’ work﻿—never set hand to a tool: an’ by sunset I gave in an’ went, cursing mysel’, over the moor to Warleggan, to Alsie Pascoe, the wise woman﻿—an’ she taught me a charm﻿—an’ bless her, bless her, Jack, for’t hath brought thee!”

“Joan,” said I, hot with shame, taking her arms gently from my neck: “listen: I come because I am chased. Once more the dragooners are after me﻿—not five minutes away. You must lend me a horse, and at once.”

“Nay,” said a voice in the doorway, “the horse, if lent, is for *me*!”

Joan turn’d, and the two women stood looking at each other;﻿—the one with dark wonder, the other with cold disdainfulness﻿—and I between them scarce lifting my eyes. Each was beautiful after her kind, as day and night: and though their looks cross’d for a full minute like drawn blades, neither had the mastery. Joan was the first to speak.

“Jack, is thy mare in the yard?”

I nodded.

“Give me thy pistols and thy cloak.” She stepp’d to the window hole at the end of the kitchen, and look’d out. “Plenty o’ time,” she said; and pointed to the ladder leading to the loft above﻿—“Climb up there, the both, and pull the ladder after. Is’t *thou*, they want﻿—or *she*?” pointing to Delia.

“Me chiefly they would catch, no doubt﻿—being a man,” I answer’d.

“Aye﻿—bein’ a man: the world’s full o’ folly. Then Jack do thou look after *her*, an’ I’ll look after *thee*. If the rebels leave thee in peace, make for the Jews’ Kitchen and there abide me.”

She flung my cloak about her, took my pistols and went out at the door. As she did so, the sun sank and a dull shadow swept over the moor. “Joan!” I cried, for now I guess’d her purpose and was following to hinder her: but she had caught Molly’s bridle and was already astride of her. “Get back!” she call’d softly; and then, “I make a better lad than wench, Jack,”﻿—leap’d the mare through a gap in the wall, and in a moment was breasting the hill and galloping for the high road.

===Chinese===
「好姑娘——昨天我还发誓要坚强，等上多少年都成。女人家真没出息，这般不争气！我一整日坐着，坐着，一点活计也不曾干——手没碰过一件家什：日落时我认了命，骂着自己穿过荒原去沃勒根（Warleggan），找阿尔茜·帕斯科（Alsie Pascoe），那位智妇（wise woman）——她教了我一道魔咒（the charm）——愿她受祝福，杰克，因为这魔咒把你引来了！」

「琼恩，」我羞愧难当，轻轻将她的手臂从我颈上拿下，「听我说：我是被追来的。龙骑兵又追上来了——离此不到五分钟。你得借我一匹马，立刻就借。」

「不消借，」门口一个声音说，「那马若要借，也该借给*我*！」

琼恩转过身，两个女人彼此对视；一个眼中满是深沉的惊愕，另一个带着冰冷的轻蔑——我夹在中间，几乎不敢抬眼。她们各具其美，犹如昼夜：目光交锋了足有一分钟，如同出鞘的利刃，却未分高下。琼恩先开了口。

「杰克，你的马驹在院子里吗？」

我点点头。

「把你的手枪和斗篷给我。」她走到厨房尽头的窗口向外张望。「时间充裕，」她说，指着通向阁楼的梯子——「你们俩爬上去，把梯子抽上去。他们是冲*你*来的——还是冲*她*？」她指向德利娅。

「主要是冲我来的，毫无疑问——因为我是男人，」我答道。

「哎——因为是男人：天下净是些蠢事。那么杰克，你照看*她*，我来照看*你*。若叛军饶你太平，便去犹太人的厨房（Jews' Kitchen）等我。」

她将我的斗篷往身上一披，抓起我的手枪出了门。此时夕阳西沉，一道沉郁的阴影掠过荒原。「琼恩！」我喊道，因为我已猜出她的用意，正要追出去阻止：但她已抓住莫莉的辔头，翻身上了马。「回去！」她柔声喊道；接着又说，「杰克，我扮男相可比扮女相强多啦」——纵马跃过墙头缺口，转眼便冲上山坡，朝大路疾驰而去。

===Original===
In less than a minute, as it seem’d, I heard a pounding of hoofs, and had barely time to follow Delia up the ladder and pull it after me, when two of the dragoons rode skurrying by the house, and pass’d on yelling. Their cries were hardly faint in the distance before there came another three.

“ ’A’s a lost man, now, for sure,” said one: “Be dang’d if ’a’s not took the road back to Lan’son!”

“How ’bout the gal?” ask’d another voice. “Here’s her horse i’ the yard.”

“Drat the gal! Sam, go thou an’ tackle her: reckon thou’rt warriors enow for one ’ooman.”

The two hasten’d on: and presently I heard the one they call’d “Sam” dismounting in the yard. Now there was a window hole in the loft, facing, not on the yard, but toward the country behind; and running to it I saw that no more were following﻿—the other three having, as I suppose, early given up the chase. Softly pulling out a loose stone or two, I widen’d this hole till I could thrust the ladder out of it. To my joy it just reach’d the ground. I bade Delia squeeze herself through and climb down.

But before she was halfway down I heard a wild screech in the kitchen below, and the voice of Sam shrieking﻿—

“Help﻿—help! Lord ha’ mercy ’pon me﻿—’tis a black cat﻿—’tis a witch! The gal’s no gal, but a witch!”

Laughing softly, I was descending the ladder when the fellow came round the corner screaming﻿—with Jan Tergagle clawing at his back and spitting murderously. Delia had just time to slip aside, before he ran into the ladder and brought me flying on top of him. And there he lay and bellow’d till I tied him, and gagg’d his noise with a big stone in his mouth and his own scarf tied round it.

“Come!” I whisper’d: for Joan and her pursuers were out of sight. Catching up her long skirt, Delia follow’d me, and up the tor we panted together, nor rested till we were safe in the Jews’ Kitchen.

“What think you of this for a hiding place?” ask’d I, with a laugh.

But Delia did not laugh. Instead, she faced me with blazing eyes, check’d herself and answer’d, cold as ice﻿—

“Sir, you have done me a many favors. How I have trusted you in return it were best for you to remember, and for me to forget.”

===Chinese===
不到一分钟——感觉如此——我听到蹄声隆隆，刚来得及带德利娅爬上梯子并抽上来，两名龙骑兵已策马从屋旁掠过，吆喝着远去。他们的喊声尚未在远处消散，又有三人赶到。

「那小子这下可完蛋了，」一个说，「妈的，他倒跑回朗塞斯顿去了！」

「那姑娘咋办？」另一个声音问，「她的马在院子里。」

「管那姑娘呢！萨姆（Sam），你去逮她：一个老娘们，你一个人够了。」

两人匆匆前行。不一会儿，我听见那个叫「萨姆」的在院子里下了马。阁楼有扇窗洞，不朝院子，而是面向屋后的旷野；我跑到那里望去，发现再无人追来——另外三人想必早已放弃。我轻轻抽出几块松动的石头，将洞口扩大到能把梯子推出去。梯子刚好够到地面。我让德利娅侧身挤出去，顺着梯子爬下。

但她才爬到一半，楼下厨房里便传来一声惨叫，萨姆尖叫道——

「救命——救命！主啊可怜可怜我——是只黑猫——是个巫婆！那姑娘不是姑娘，是个巫婆！」

我轻声笑着正要下梯，那家伙尖叫着转过墙角——扬·特加格尔（Jan Tergagle）正扑在他背上，凶狠地又抓又挠。德利娅侧身躲过，他却撞上梯子，把我掀了下来，正砸在他身上。他躺在地上嚎叫，直到我把他捆住，往他嘴里塞了块大石头，再用他自己的围巾绕上封住。

「走！」我低声道——琼恩和追兵已不见踪影。德利娅提起长裙，跟着我一同气喘吁吁地爬上突岩，直到安全抵达犹太人的厨房才歇下来。

「这藏身处如何？」我笑着问。

但德利娅没有笑。她转过身，目光灼灼地看着我，忍了忍，冷若冰霜地答道——

「先生，您帮了我许多忙。我曾如何信任您，您最好记住，我也最好忘掉。」

===Original===
The dark drew on; the western star grew distinct and hung flashing over against our hiding; and still we sat there, hour after hour, silent, angry, waiting for Joan’s return, Delia at the entrance of the den, chin on hand, scanning the heavens and never once turning toward me; I further inside, with my arms cross’d, raging against myself and all the world, yet with a sick’ning dread that Joan would never come back.

As the time lagg’d by, this terror grew and grew. But, as I think, about ten o’clock, I heard steps coming over the turf. I ran out. ’Twas Joan herself and leading Molly by the bridle. She walk’d as if tir’d, and leaving the mare at the entrance, follow’d me into the cave. Glancing round, I noted that Delia had slipp’d away.

“Am glad she’s gone,” said Joan shortly: “How many rebels pass’d this way, Jack?”

“Five, counting one that lies gagg’d and bound, down at the cottage.”

“That leaves four:”﻿—she stretch’d herself on the ground with a sigh﻿—“four that’ll never trouble thee more, lad.”

“Why? how﻿—”

“Listen, lad: sit down an’ let me rest my head ’pon thy knee. Oh, Jack, I did it bravely! Eight good miles an’ more I took the mare﻿—by the Four﻿—hol’d Cross, an’ across the moor past Tober an’ Catshole, an’ over Brown Willy, an’ round Roughtor to the nor’-west: an’ there lies the bravest quag﻿—oh, a black, bottomless hole!﻿—an’ into it I led them; an’ there they lie, every horse, an’ every mother’s son, till Judgment Day.”

“Dead?”

“Aye﻿—an’ the last twain wi’ a bullet apiece in their skulls. Oh, rare! Dear heart﻿—hold my head﻿—so, atween thy hands. ‘Put on his cast off duds,’ said Alsie, ’an’ stand afore the glass, sayin’ “Come, true man!” nine-an’-ninety time.’ I was mortal ’feard o’ losin’ count; but afore I got to fifty, I heard thy step an’﻿—hold me closer, Jack.”

“But Joan, are these men dead, say you?”

“Surely, yes. Why, lad, what be four rebels, up or down, to make this coil over? Hast never axed after *me*!”

“Joan﻿—you are not hurt?”

In the darkness I sought her eyes, and, peering into them, drew back.

“Joan!”

“Hush, lad﻿—bend down thy head, and let me whisper. I went too near﻿—an’ one, that was over his knees, let fly wi’ his musket﻿—an’ Jack, I have but a minute or two. Hush lad, hush﻿—there’s no call! Wert never the man could ha’ tam’d me﻿—art the weaker, in a way: forgie the word, for I lov’d thee so, boy Jack!”

Her arms were drawing down my face to her: her eyes dull with pain.

“Feel, Jack﻿—there﻿—over my right breast. I plugg’d the wound wi’ a peat turf. Pull it out, for ’tis bleeding inwards, and hurts cruelly﻿—pull it out!”

As I hesitated, she thrust her own hand in and drew it forth, leaving the hot blood to gush.

“An’ now, Jack, tighter﻿—hold me tighter. Kiss me﻿—oh, what brave times! Tighter, lad, an’ call wi’ me﻿—‘Church an’ King!’ Call, lad﻿—‘Church an’﻿—’ ”

The warm arms loosen’d: the head sank back upon my lap.

I look’d up. There was a shadow across the entrance, blotting out the star of night. ’Twas Delia, leaning there and listening.

===Chinese===
夜色渐深；西边的星辰清晰起来，在我们的藏身处对面闪烁；我们仍坐在那里，一小时又一小时，沉默、恼怒，等着琼恩回来。德利娅坐在洞口，手托着下巴，仰望天宇，一次也不曾转向我；我在更里面，双臂交叉，对自己和整个世界怒火中烧，却又被一种令人作呕的恐惧攫住——琼恩再也不会回来了。

时间点滴流逝，这恐惧愈来愈烈。但大约十点钟时，我听见草地上响起脚步声。我冲出去。是琼恩，牵着莫莉的辔头。她步履疲惫，将母马留在洞口，跟着我进了洞。我环顾四周，发现德利娅已悄然离去。

「走了好，」琼恩简短地说，「有多少叛军从这条路过去，杰克？」

「五个，算上那个在小屋里捆着堵嘴的。」

「还剩四个：」——她往地上一躺，长叹一声——「四个再也不会找你麻烦的人了，小伙子。」

「怎么？怎么会——」

「听我说，小伙子：坐下，让我把头搁在你膝上。哦，杰克，我干得漂亮！我骑着母马跑了足足八英里多——过四穴十字（Four-hole Cross），穿荒原经过托伯（Tober）和卡特斯霍尔（Catshole），翻过布朗威利（Brown Willy），绕过罗托尔（Roughtor）往西北——那里有一处最凶险的沼泽——哦，一个漆黑无底的深渊！我把他们引了进去；他们就躺在那儿，每一匹马，每一个人，直到末日审判。」

「死了？」

「死了——最后两个每人脑袋上挨了一枪。哦，痛快！亲爱的——扶住我的头——对，就这样捧在手里。阿尔茜说：'穿上他脱下的破衣服，' '对着镜子，念九十遍"来吧，真汉子！（Come, true man!）"'我生怕数乱了；但还没念到五十遍，就听见你的脚步声了——再抱紧些，杰克。」

「可是琼恩，你说这些人死了？」

「当然死了。哎呀，小伙子，四条叛军的命，死活算得了什么？你倒从没问过*我*！」

「琼恩——你没受伤？」

黑暗中我寻找她的眼睛，凝视之下，我退缩了。

「琼恩！」

「嘘，小伙子——低下头，让我悄悄说。我靠得太近了——有一个跪在地上的，端起火枪开了火——杰克，我只剩一两分钟了。嘘，小伙子，嘘——别哭！你从来没能驯服我——在某种意义上，你更弱些：原谅我这么说，因为我太爱你了，小伙子杰克！」

她的双臂将我的脸拉向她：她的眼眸因疼痛而黯淡。

「摸摸，杰克——这里——右胸上方。我用泥炭草皮堵住了伤口。把它拔出来，血在往里流，疼得厉害——拔出来！」

我犹豫着，她自己伸手进去拔了出来，热血顿时涌出。

「现在，杰克，再紧些——抱紧我。吻我——哦，多么痛快的时光！再紧些，小伙子，跟我一起喊——'教会与国王！'（Church and King!）喊呀，小伙子——'教会与——'」

温暖的双臂松开了：头垂落在我膝上。

我抬起头。洞口有一道身影，遮住了夜空的星光。是德利娅，倚在那里听着。

## XIX The Adventure of the Hearse / XIX 灵车历险

===Original===
The dayspring came at last, and in the sick light of it I went down to the cottage for spade and pickaxe. In the tumult of my senses I hardly noted that our prisoner, the dragoon, had contrived to slip his bonds and steal off in the night.

And then Delia, seeing me return with the sad tools on my shoulder, spoke for the first time:

“First, if there be a well near, fetch me two buckets of water, and leave us for an hour.”

Her voice was weary and chill: so that I dared not thank her, but did the errand in silence. Then, but a dozen paces from the spot where Joan’s father lay, I dug a grave and strew’d it with bracken, and heather, and gorse petals, that in the morning air smell’d rarely. And soon after my task was done, Delia call’d me.

In her man’s dress Joan lay, her arms cross’d, her black tresses braided, and her face gentler than ever ’twas in life. Over her wounded breast was a bunch of some tiny pink flower, that grew about the tor.

===Chinese===
天光终于破晓。在惨淡的晨光里，我下到小屋去取铁锹和镐头。我心神激荡，几乎不曾察觉，我们俘获的那名龙骑兵，竟已在夜里挣脱捆绑，悄悄溜走了。

德利娅见我扛着这几件悲伤的家什回来，第一次开了口：

「先请你，若近处有井，给我提两桶水来，然后让我们独处一个钟头。」

她的声音疲惫而冰冷，我不敢向她道谢，只默默把差事办了。随后，在离琼恩父亲安眠处十几步远的地方，我挖了一个墓穴，铺上蕨草、石楠和荆豆花瓣，它们在清晨的空气里透着罕见的芬芳。我刚把事情做完，德利娅便唤了我一声。

琼恩躺在那里，仍穿着那身男装，双臂交叠，黑发编成了辫子，面容比生前任何时候都更温柔。她受伤的胸口上，放着一小束粉红的小花，那是突岩（tor）一带才有的。

===Original===
So I lifted her softly as once in this same place she had lifted me, and bore her down the slope to the grave: and there I buried her, while Delia knelt and pray’d, and Molly browsed, lifting now and then her head to look.

When all was done, we turn’d away, dry-eyed, and walked together to the cottage. The bay horse was feeding on the moor below; and finding him still too lame to carry Delia, I shifted the saddles, and mending the broken rein, set her on Molly. The cottage door stood open, but we did not enter; only look’d in, and seeing Jan Tergagle curl’d beside the cold hearth, left him so.

Mile after mile we pass’d in silence, Delia riding, and I pacing beside her with the bay. At last, tortur’d past bearing, I spoke﻿—

“Delia, have you nothing to say?”

For a while she seem’d to consider: then, with her eyes fix’d on the hills ahead, answered﻿—

===Chinese===
我轻轻将她抱起，正如当初就在这地方，她也曾这样抱起过我；我抱着她走下坡去，到墓穴旁：我便在那里安葬了她，德利娅跪下祈祷，莫莉在一旁啃草，不时抬头望上一望。

一切完毕，我们转身离去，眼中并无泪水，一同走回小屋。枣红马正在下方荒原上吃草；见它仍旧瘸得驮不动德利娅，我便调换了马鞍，修好断裂的缰绳，扶她骑上莫莉。小屋的门敞着，我们却没有进去；只朝里望了一眼，见扬·特加格尔（Jan Tergagle）蜷在冷了的炉边，便由他去了。

一英里又一英里，我们在沉默中赶路，德利娅骑行，我牵着枣红马走在她身旁。终于，我再忍受不住这煎熬，开口道——

「德利娅，你没有什么要说的吗？」

她似是斟酌了片刻，而后目光凝望着前方的山峦，答道——

===Original===
“Much, if I could speak: but all this has changed me somehow﻿—’tis, perhaps, that I have grown a woman, having been a girl﻿—and need to get used to it, and think.”

She spoke not angrily, as I look’d for; but with a painful slowness that was less hopeful.

“But,” said I, “over and over you have shown that I am nought to you. Surely﻿—”

“Surely I am jealous? ’Tis possible﻿—yes, Jack, I am but a woman, and so ’tis certain.”

“Why, to be jealous, you must love me!”

===Chinese===
「若说得出，倒有许多可说：只是这一切不知怎地改变了我——也许，是我从姑娘长成了妇人——总得习惯过来，好好想一想。」

她说话的口气并不如我预想的那般恼怒，而是带着一种令人痛苦的迟缓，反倒更叫人无望。

「可是，」我说，「你已一再表明，我在你心中一文不值。想必——」

「想必我在吃醋？也未可知——是啊，杰克，我不过是个女人，所以这是必然。」

「哎呀，要吃醋，你总得先爱我呀！」

===Original===
She look’d at me straight, and answered very deliberate﻿—

“Now that is what I am far from sure of.”

“But, dear Delia, when your anger has cool’d﻿—”

“My anger was brief: I am disappointed, rather. With her last breath, almost, Joan said you were weaker than she: she lov’d you better than I, and read you clearer. You *are* weak. Jack”﻿—she drew in Molly, and let her hand fall on my shoulder very kindly﻿—“we have been comrades for many a long mile, and I hope are honest good friends; wherefore I loathe to say a harsh or ungrateful-seeming word. But you could not understand that brave girl, and you cannot understand me: for as yet you do not even know yourself. The knowledge comes slowly to a man, I think; to a woman at one rush. But when it comes, I believe you may be strong. Now leave me to think, for my head is all of a tangle.”

===Chinese===
她直直地看着我，一字一顿地答道——

「这一点，我可远不敢肯定。」

「可是，亲爱的德利娅，等你的气消了——」

「我的气是短暂的；我更多的是失望。琼恩咽气之前几乎还在说，你比她软弱：她比我更爱你，也把你看得更透。你*确实*软弱。杰克」——她勒住莫莉，把手非常温柔地搭在我肩上——「我们同伴（comrade）一场，同行了多少英里的长路，我希望彼此是诚实的好朋友；因此我极不愿说出一句严厉、或似有负义之嫌的话。可你不曾理解那位勇敢的姑娘，也不会理解我：因为连你自己，你都还不了解。我想，这份自知于男人来得缓慢；于女人却是一涌而至。但等它来到时，我相信你会坚强起来。现在让我独自想想，我的脑袋乱成一团麻。」

===Original===
Our pace was so slow (by reason of the lame horse), that a great part of the afternoon was spent before we came in sight of the House of Gleys. And truly the yellow sunshine had flung some warmth about the naked walls and turrets, so that Delia’s homecoming seem’d not altogether cheerless. But what gave us more happiness was to spy, on the blue water beyond, the bright canvas of the *Godsend*, and to hear the cries and stir of Billy Pottery’s mariners as they haul’d down the sails.

And Billy himself was on the lookout with his spyglass. For hardly were we come to the beach when our signal﻿—the waving of a white kerchief﻿—was answered by another on board; and within half an hour a boat puts off, wherein, as she drew nearer, I counted eight fellows.

They were (besides Billy), Matt Soames, the master, Gabriel Hutchins, Ned Masters, the black man Sampson, Ben Halliday, and two whose full names I have forgot﻿—but one was call’d Nicholas. And, after many warm greetings, the boat was made fast, and we climbed up along the peninsula together, in close order, like a little army.

All this time there was no sign or sound about the House of Gleys to show that anyone mark’d us or noted our movements. The gate was closed, the windows stood shutter’d, as on my former visit: even the chimneys were smokeless. Such effect had this desolation on our spirits, that drawing near, we fell to speaking in whispers, and said Ned Masters﻿—

===Chinese===
由于瘸马的缘故，我们走得很慢，直到下午过了大半，格莱斯宅邸（House of Gleys）才映入眼帘。说实话，金黄的阳光给赤裸的墙壁和角楼披上了一层暖意，德利娅的还家倒不显得全然凄凉。而更令我们快慰的是，在远处湛蓝的海面上，望见了「天赐号」（*Godsend*）明亮的帆篷，还听见比尔·波特里（Billy Pottery）的水手们收帆时的呼喝与忙乱。

比尔本人正举着望远镜瞭望。我们刚到海滩，我们约定的信号——挥动一方白手帕——便得到船上另一方手帕的回应；不出半小时，一条小艇放了下来，趁它渐划渐近，我数了数，艇上有八条汉子。

除比尔之外，他们是：船主马特·索姆斯（Matt Soames）、加布里埃尔·哈钦斯（Gabriel Hutchins）、内德·马斯特斯（Ned Masters）、黑人桑普森（Sampson）、本·哈利迪（Ben Halliday），还有两个我记不全姓名的——只知其中一个叫尼古拉斯（Nicholas）。一番热络的寒暄之后，小艇系牢，我们紧挨着排成队形，像一支小小的军队，一同沿着半岛向上攀登。

自始至终，格莱斯宅邸四周没有任何迹象或声响，表明有谁留意我们或察看我们的行踪。大门紧闭，窗板严遮，同我上次来时一般无二：连烟囱都不冒一缕烟。这一片荒凉对我们的心绪影响至深，走近宅邸时，我们不自觉地低声耳语起来，内德·马斯特斯便道——

===Original===
“Now a man would think us come to bury somebody!”

“He might make a worse guess,” I answer’d.

Marching up to the gate, I rang a loud peal on the bell; and to my astonishment, before the echoes had time to die away, the grating was push’d back, and the key turn’d in the lock.

“Step ye in﻿—step ye in, good folks! A sorry day﻿—a day of sobs an’ tears an’ afflicted blowings of the nose﻿—when the grasshopper is a burden an’ the mourners go about seeking whom they may devour the funeral meats. Y’ are welcome, gentlemen.”

’Twas the voice of my one-eyed friend, as he undid the bolts; and now he stood in the gateway with a prodigious black sash across his canary livery, so long that the ends of it swept the flagstones.

“Is Master Tingcomb within?” I helped Delia to dismount, and gave our two horses to a stable boy that stood shuffling some paces off.

===Chinese===
「瞧这光景，人家还当我们是来给谁下葬的呢！」

「他若猜得更糟些，也未可知，」我答道。

我大步走到门前，用力拉响门铃；令我诧异的是，回声尚未消散，门上的格栅小窗便被人推开，钥匙已在锁孔里转动。

「请进——请进吧，好人们！一个伤心的日子——一个抽抽噎噎、泪流满面、鼻子哀哀擤个不停的日子——蚱蜢成了重担，吊丧的走来走去，寻摸着可以吞吃谁家的丧宴。各位老爷，欢迎欢迎。」

是我那独眼朋友的声音，他一面拔着门闩；此刻他站在门洞里，金丝雀黄的号衣上斜挎着一条硕大无比的黑腰带，长得那垂下的两端直扫到石板上。

「廷科姆老爷（Master Tingcomb）在家吗？」我扶德利娅下马，把两匹马交给几步外一个磨磨蹭蹭站着不动的马童。

===Original===
“Alas!” the old man heav’d a deep sigh, and with that began to hobble across the yard. We troop’d after, wondering. At the house door he turn’d﻿—

“Sirs, there is cold roasted capons, an’ a ham, an’ radishes in choice profusion for such as be not troubled wi’ the wind: an’ cordial wines﻿—alack the day!”

He squeez’d a frosty tear from his one eye, and led us to a large bare hall, hung round with portraits; where was a table spread with a plenty of victuals, and horn-handled knives and forks laid beside plates of pewter; and at the table a man in black, eating. He had straight hair and a sallow face; and look’d up as we enter’d, but, groaning, in a moment fell to again.

“Eat, sirs,” the old servitor exhorted us: “alas! that man may take nothing out o’ the world!”

I know not who of us was most taken aback. But noting Delia’s sad wondering face, as her eyes wander’d round the neglected room and rested on the tatter’d portraits, I lost patience.

“Our business is with Master Hannibal Tingcomb,” said I sharply.

===Chinese===
「唉！」老人长叹一声，随即蹒跚着穿过院子。我们满腹狐疑地跟在后面。到了宅门口，他回过头——

「老爷们，有冷盘的烤阉鸡，有火腿，还有精选的上好萝卜，给那些不受胀气之扰的人享用：另有暖身的美酒——唉，可叹这天！」

他从独眼里挤出一颗冷冰冰的泪来，领我们走进一间空旷的大厅，四壁挂着肖像；厅里一张桌上摆满了吃食，角柄刀叉搁在白镴盘旁；桌边坐着一个穿黑衣的人，正在进食。他头发平直，面色蜡黄；见我们进来，抬头望了望，呻吟一声，随即又埋头吃了起来。

「吃吧，老爷们，」老仆劝我们道：「唉！人赤身而来，什么也带不出这世界呀！」

我说不清我们当中谁最惊愕。但一看到德利娅那忧伤而错愕的脸——她的目光扫过这无人经心的大厅，停在那破损的肖像上——我便按捺不住了。

「我们找的是汉尼拔·廷科姆（Hannibal Tingcomb）老爷，」我厉声说道。

===Original===
The straight-hair’d man look’d up again, his mouth full of ham.

“Hush!”﻿—he held his fork up, and shook his head sorrowfully: and I wonder’d where I had seen him before. “Hast thou an angel’s wings?” he ask’d.

“Why, no, sir; but the devil’s own boots﻿—as you shall find if I be not answer’d.”

“Young man﻿—young man,” broke in the one-eyed butler: “our minister is a good minister, an’ speaks roundabout as such: but the short is, that my master is dead, an’ in his coffin.”

“The mortal part,” corrected the minister, cutting another slice.

===Chinese===
那平头发的男人又抬起头来，嘴里塞满了火腿。

「嘘！」——他举起叉子，悲哀地摇了摇头：我心里正纳闷从前在哪里见过此人。「你身负天使的翅膀么？」他问。

「呃，没有，先生；不过穿了魔王的一双靴子——我若得不到答复，你便见识见识。」

「年轻人——年轻人，」独眼的管家插话道：「咱们这位牧师是位好牧师，说起话来弯弯绕绕，牧师都这样：长话短说，我家老爷死了，已经躺进棺材了。」

「是那必朽的部分，」牧师纠正道，一边又切下一片。

===Original===
“Aye, the immortal is a-trippin’ it i’ the New Jeroosalem: but the mortal was very lamentably took wi’ a fit, three days back﻿—the same day, young man, as thou earnest wi’ thy bloody threats.”

“A fit?”

“Aye, sir, an’ verily﻿—such a fit as thou thysel’ witness’d. ’Twas the third attack﻿—an’ he cried, ‘Oh!’ he did, an’ ‘Ah!’﻿—just like that. ‘Oh!’ an’ then ‘Ah!’ Such were his last dyin’ speech. ‘Dear Master,’ says I, ‘there’s no call to die so hard:’ but might so well ha’ whistled, for he was dead as nails. A beautiful corpse, sirs, dang my buttons!”

“Show him to us.”

“Willingly, young man.” He led the way to the very room where Master Tingcomb and I had held our interview. As before, six candles were burning there: but the table was push’d into a corner, and now their light fell on a long black coffin, resting on trestles in the centre of the room. The coffin was clos’d, and studded with silver nails; on the lid was a silver plate bearing these words written﻿—“**Hannibal Tingcomb**, MDCXLIII,” with a text of Scripture below.

===Chinese===
「可不，那不朽的部分这会儿正在新耶路撒冷里跳舞呢：可那必朽的部分，三天前得了一场急病，去得好不凄惨——年轻人，正是你带着血淋淋的威胁找上门来的那一天。」

「一场急病？」

「是啊，老爷，千真万确——就是你亲眼见过的那一种发作。那是第三回了——他喊『哦！』真的，又喊『啊！』——就是那样。『哦！』接着『啊！』这便是他临终的遗言。『亲爱的老爷，』我说，『犯不着死得这般费劲：』可我跟吹口哨一样白费劲，他早就死透了。多体面的一具尸首啊，老爷们，我的老天爷！」

「带我们去见他。」

「乐意效劳，年轻人。」他领路走的正是先前廷科姆老爷与我密谈的那间屋子。和上次一样，屋里点着六支蜡烛：只是桌子被推到了角落，烛光如今照着一口长长的黑棺材，架在屋子中央的支架上。棺材已经钉闭，缀着银钉；棺盖上有一块银牌，镌着这样的字样——「**Hannibal Tingcomb**，MDCXLIII」（汉尼拔·廷科姆，公元 1643 年），下面另有一行经文。

===Original===
“Why have you nail’d him down?” I asked.

“Now where be thy bowels, young man, to talk so unfeelin’? An’ where be thy experience, not to know the ways o’ thy blessed dead in summer time?”

“When do you bury him?”

“Tomorrow forenoon. The spot is two mile from here.” He blinked at me, and hesitated for a minute. “Is it your purpose, sirs, to attend?”

“Be sure of that,” I said grimly. “So have beds ready tonight for all our company.”

===Chinese===
「你们为何把棺材钉上？」我问道。

「年轻人，你的恻隐之心上哪儿去了，说话这般无情无义？你的见识又上哪儿去了，竟不知道蒙福的亡人在暑天里的规矩？」

「你们何时下葬？」

「明天上午。地点离这儿两英里。」他朝我眨眨眼，迟疑了片刻。「诸位老爷，可有意出席？」

「这个自然，」我冷冷地说。「所以今夜要为我们全体备好床铺。」

===Original===
“All thy﻿—! Dear sir, consider: where are beds to be found? Sure, thy mariners can pass the night aboard their own ship?”

“So then,” thought I, “you have been on the lookout;” but Delia replied for me﻿—

“I am Delia Killigrew, and mistress of this house. You will prepare the beds as you are told.” Whereupon what does that decrepit old sinner but drop upon his knees?

“Mistress Delia! O goodly feast for this one poor eye! Oh, that Master Tingcomb had seen this day!”

I declare the tears were running down his nose; but Delia march’d out, cutting short his hypocrisy.

===Chinese===
「全、全体——！亲爱的老爷，体谅体谅：这宅子里哪儿找得出床位？您那些水手，总归可以回自己船上过夜吧？」

「原来如此，」我心想，「你们早有提防；」但德利娅替我答道——

「我是德利娅·基利格鲁（Delia Killigrew），这座宅子的女主人。叫你备床，你便照办。」话音未落，你道那老朽的老罪人做了什么？他竟双膝一屈，跪下了。

「德利娅女主人！哦，我这一只可怜的眼睛可享了眼福！哦，廷科姆老爷要是能见到这一天该多好！」

我敢说，泪水真个顺着他鼻子往下淌；德利娅却径直走了出去，截断了他这出假戏。

===Original===
In the passage she whisper’d﻿—

“Villainy, Jack!”

“Hush!” I answered, “and listen: *Master Tingcomb is no more in that coffin than I.*”

“Then where is he?”

“That is just what we are to discover.” As I said this a light broke on me. “By the Lord,” I cried, “ ’tis the very same!”

===Chinese===
在过道里，她低声道——

「奸恶的把戏，杰克！」

「嘘！」我答道，「且听我说：*那口棺材里根本没有廷科姆老爷，正如里面没有我一样。*」

「那他在哪儿？」

「这正是我们要查明的。」话音出口，我心中忽地一亮。「老天为证，」我叫道，「就是那同一个人！」

===Original===
Delia open’d her eyes wide.

“Wait,” I said: “I begin to touch ground.”

We returned to the great hall. The straight-hair’d man was still eating, and opposite sat Billy, that had not budg’d, but now beckoning to me, very mysterious, whisper’d in a voice that made the plates rattle﻿—

“That’s﻿—a damned﻿—rogue!”

’Twas discomposing, but the truth. In fact, I had just solv’d a puzzle. This holy-speaking minister was no other than the groom I had seen at Bodmin Fair holding Master Tingcomb’s horses.

===Chinese===
德利娅睁大了眼睛。

「等着，」我说，「我心里渐渐有了底。」

我们回到大厅。那平头发的男人还在吃，对面坐着比尔，他始终不曾挪窝，此刻却神神秘秘地向我招手，用一种震得盘碟叮当作响的嗓音悄声道——

「那家伙——是个混账——恶棍！」

这话听着突兀，却是实情。事实上，我恰才解开了一个谜团。这位满口圣言的牧师，不是别人，正是我在博德明集市上见过的、替廷科姆老爷牵马的那个仆人。

===Original===
By this, the sun was down, and Delia soon made an excuse to withdraw to her own room. Nor was it long before the rest followed her example. I found our chambers prepared, near together, in a wing of the house at some distance from the hall. Delia’s was next to mine, as I made sure by knocking at her door: and on the other side of me slept Billy with two of his crew. My own bed was in a great room sparely furnish’d; and the linen indifferent white. There was a plenty of clean straw, though, on the floor, had I intended to sleep﻿—which I did not.

Instead, having blown out my light, I sat on the bed’s edge, listening to the big clock over the hall as it chim’d the quarters, and waiting till the fellows below should be at their ease. That Master Tingcomb rested under the coffin lid, I did not believe, in spite of the terrifying fit that I could vouch for. But this, if driven to it, we could discover at the grave. The main business was to catch him; and to this end I meant to patrol the buildings, and especially watch the entrance, on the likely chance of his creeping back to the house (if not already inside), to confer with his fellow-rascals.

As eleven o’clock sounded, therefore, I tapp’d on Billy’s wall; and finding that Matt Soames was keeping watch (as we had agreed upon), slipp’d off my boots. Our rooms were on the first floor, over a straw yard; and the distance to the ground an easy drop for a man. But wishing to be silent as possible, I knotted two blankets together, and strapping the end round the window mullion, swung myself down by one hand, holding my boots in the other.

===Chinese===
这时太阳已经落山，德利娅很快寻了个由头，回自己房里去了。不多时，其余的人也纷纷学她的样告歇。我见我们的卧房都已备好，彼此相隔不远，在离大厅颇远的一处厢房里。德利娅的紧挨着我的，这是我敲她的门证实的：我另一侧睡的是比尔和他船上的两名水手。我自己的床在一间陈设稀疏的大屋里；床单白得马马虎虎。倒是地板上铺着许多干净的干草——倘若我打算睡觉的话。可我并不打算睡。

我吹灭灯火，坐在床沿上，听着大厅上那座大钟报过一刻又一刻，只等楼下的家伙们安顿下来。要说廷科姆老爷真安息在那棺材盖下，我不信——尽管那回吓人的发作我确曾亲眼目睹。不过真到万不得已，到了坟前自见分晓。要紧的是把他本人逮住；为此我打算巡行各处房舍，尤其守住出入口，多半他还会潜回宅里（若还不在宅里的话），与他的同党恶棍们会商。

于是，十一点的钟声敲响时，我叩了叩比尔的墙壁；探明马特·索姆斯正在守夜（这是我们事先约好的），便脱下靴子。我们的房间在二楼，下面是铺草的院场；那点高度，一个汉子跳下去毫不费力。但为求尽量悄无声息，我把两条毯子系在一起，一头拴在窗棂上，一手抓着毯子荡下去，另一只手提着靴子。

===Original===
I dropp’d very lightly, and look’d about. There was a faint moon up and glimmering on the straw; but under the house was deep shadow, and along this I crept. The straw yard led into the court before the stables, and so into the main court. All this way I heard no sound, nor spied so much as a speck of light in any window. The house door was clos’d, and the bar fastened on the great gate across the yard. I turn’d the corner to explore the third side of the house.

Here was a group of outbuildings jutting out, and between them and the high outer wall a narrow alley. ’Twas with difficulty I groped my way here, for the passage was dark as pitch, and rendered the straiter by a line of ragged laurels planted under the house; so that at every other step I would stumble, and run my head into a bush.

I had done this for the eighth time, and was cursing under my breath, when on a sudden I heard a stealthy footfall coming down the alley behind me.

“Master Tingcomb, for a crown!” thought I, and crouch’d to one side under a bush. The footsteps drew nearer. A dark form parted the laurels: another moment, and I had it by the throat.

===Chinese===
我轻轻落地，四下张望。一弯淡月悬在天上，微光照着草垛；屋子的背阴处却是一片浓黑，我便贴着这黑影潜行。草场通向马厩前的院子，再通进正院。这一路我未听见半点声响，也不曾在任何窗口窥见一星灯火。宅门紧闭，横院的大门上了闩。我转过屋角，去探宅子的第三面。

这里有一群外突的杂用屋，屋子与高高的外墙之间夹着一条窄巷。我在其间摸索前行，好不艰难，因为巷道漆黑如墨，又因贴墙栽着一行凌乱的月桂而愈发逼仄；我隔不上两步便绊一下，一头撞进树丛里去。

这样的跟头我摔到第八回，正低声咒骂，忽然听见身后巷子里传来一阵蹑手蹑脚的脚步声，正朝我这边来。

「廷科姆老爷，赌一克朗，准是他！」我心想，缩身躲进一旁的树丛下。脚步声逼近了。一条黑影拨开月桂：说时迟那时快，我已掐住了他的喉咙。

===Original===
“Uugh﻿—ugh﻿—grr! For the Lord’s sake, sir﻿—”

I loos’d my hold: ’twas Matt Soames. “Your pardon,” whisper’d I; “but why have you left your post?”

“Black Sampson is watchin’, so I took the freedom﻿—ugh! my poor windpipe!﻿—to﻿—”

He broke off to catch me by the sleeve and pull me down behind the bush. About twelve paces ahead I heard a door softly open’d and saw a shaft of light flung across the path between the glist’ning laurels. As the ray touch’d the outer wall, I mark’d a small postern gate there, standing open.

===Chinese===
「呃——呃——嗬！看在主的份上，先生——」

我松开手：竟是马特·索姆斯。「恕罪，」我低声道，「可你为何擅离岗位？」

「黑桑普森在守着呢，所以我斗胆——呃！我可怜的气管！——去——」

话未说完，他猛地抓住我的衣袖，把我拽倒在树丛后。约莫十二步开外，我听见一扇门轻轻开启，一道光线掷过湿亮的月桂之间的通道。光束照到外墙上时，我看见墙上开着一扇小小的角门，正敞着。

===Original===
Cowering lower, we waited while a man might count fifty. Then came footsteps crunching the gravel, and a couple of men cross’d the path, bearing a large chest between them. In the light I saw the handle of a spade sticking out from it: and by his gait I knew the second man to be my one-ey’d friend.

“Woe’s my old bones!” he was muttering: “here’s a fardel for a man o’ my years!”

“Hold thy breath for the next load!” growl’d the other voice, which as surely was the good minister’s.

They pass’d out of the small gate, and by the sounds that follow’d, we guess’d they were hoisting their burden into a cart. Presently they re-cross’d the path, and entered the house, shutting the door after them.

===Chinese===
我们把身子伏得更低，等了约莫能数到五十的工夫。随即传来踩碎砾石的脚步声，两条汉子抬着一只大木箱穿行过道。借着光我看见箱外露出一柄铁锹的把手：从走路的姿态，我认出第二个正是我那独眼朋友。

「苦了我这把老骨头哟！」他一路咕哝着，「这担子，压在我这岁数的人身上，真是要命！」

「省着点气力吧，还有一担呢！」另一个声音咆哮道——那无疑便是那位好牧师的了。

两人出了角门，从随后的动静听来，他们正把那重物搬上一辆车。不多时他们又穿过通道回到宅里，随手把门关上。

===Original===
“Now for it!” said I in Matt’s ear. Gliding forward, I peep’d out at the postern gate; but drew back like a shot.

I had almost run my head into a great black hearse, that stood there with the door open, back’d against the gate, the heavy plumes nodding above it in the night wind.

Who held the horses I had not time to see: but whispering to Matt, to give me a leg up, clamber’d inside. “Quick!” I pull’d him after, and crept forward. I wonder’d the man did not hear us: but by good luck the horses were restive, and by his maudlin talk to them I knew he was three parts drunk﻿—on the funeral wines, doubtless.

I crept along, and found the tool chest stow’d against the further end: so, pulling it gently out, we got behind it. Though Matt was the littlest man of my acquaintance, ’twas the work of the world to stow ourselves in such compass as to be hidden. By coiling up our limbs we managed it; but only just before I caught the glimmer of a light and heard the pair of rascals returning.

They came very slow, grumbling all the way; and of course, I knew they carried the coffin.

===Chinese===
